# Project Knowledge — icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing

This is the running knowledge base for this workspace, kept in the same spirit as the sister project `icoccha-admin-dashboard` (`/home/ari/Documents/Task/new/icoccha_admin_dashboard/PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`): a single place that accumulates everything learned while working this project, so nothing has to be rediscovered twice and every past task stays auditable.

## How this file is used

1. **Before starting any task**, read this file in full (it's the change log — short at first, but it's meant to grow) and `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` in full (the operating rules + accumulated FlutterFlow AI SDK quirks).
2. **After finishing any task**, append a new dated `§N` entry below under "Change log" — what was asked, what was actually done, why (root cause / reasoning, not just the diff), and how it was verified. Follow the same discipline the admin-dashboard project used: disclose uncertainty explicitly rather than reporting something as fixed before it's actually confirmed; note dead ends and retracted theories, not just what worked, so a future session doesn't retry them blind.
3. If a NEW FlutterFlow AI SDK/tooling gotcha is discovered (not specific to this project's own business logic — a DSL quirk, a codegen quirk, a CLI behavior), add it to `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s "FlutterFlow quirks learned the hard way" section instead of (or in addition to) here, so it's visible in the pre-task ritual even for someone skimming only the rules file.
4. Keep numbering sequential (`§1`, `§2`, ...) and never renumber past entries — the admin-dashboard project's own numbering has gaps and out-of-order dates from revisions; that's fine, sequential-and-append-only is what matters, not perfectly tidy numbering.

## Where the rest of this project's knowledge already lives

This workspace already had substantial documentation before this file existed — don't duplicate it here, point to it:

- **`PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`** — the as-is state of the workspace and the live FlutterFlow project as of 2026-08-09: tooling setup, data model (25 Firestore collections, 252 fields), every page/component, what's wired vs. stubbed, known bugs (hardcoded/malformed Stripe key, open Firestore rules, field-name typos, etc.). Treat this as ground truth for "what exists today" until a task changes it — and when a task DOES change something this file documents, update the relevant section of `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` too, not just log the change here.
- **`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`** — the phased build plan and full requirements catalog, derived from the client's own spec PDFs and the prior-contractor chat log (`/home/ari/Documents/Task/FlutterFlow/FlutterFlow/`). This is the authoritative source for product/business requirements — do not invent product rules; if a requirement is ambiguous or two source documents conflict, this file's own §4 is where that gets called out rather than silently resolved.
- **`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`** — operating rules (mission/change policy, pre-task ritual, backup/versioning discipline, git/delivery discipline) plus a large inherited section of FlutterFlow AI SDK/DSL quirks carried over from `icoccha-admin-dashboard`.
- **`AGENTS.md`** / **`CLAUDE.md`** — near-duplicate copies of the same base FlutterFlow AI workspace instructions (this project is set up to be driven interchangeably by Claude Code, Kiro, or Codex — `.mcp.json`, `.kiro/settings/mcp.json`, `.codex/config.toml` all wire the same MCP server). Both now carry an identical "Before every task" pointer section at the top; keep them in sync if that section ever changes.

## Relationship to `icoccha-admin-dashboard`

Same overall product family (**Icoccha**, a Japanese paid-companion/concierge booking app), but a **separate FlutterFlow project**, separately bound in this workspace's own `.flutterflow/config.yaml` (`project_id: icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, distinct from `icoccha-admin-dashboard2-3se8y2`). This project is the **guest/cast-facing mobile app** (booking, chat, payments, cast profiles, affiliate); the sister project is the **admin panel** used to operate it.

**Do not assume the two projects share a Firebase backend, Firestore schema, or security rules without checking.** `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §2 notes this project's Firebase project id is `icoccha`, while the admin dashboard's is `icoccha-admin-dashboard` — these may be genuinely separate Firebase projects, or the admin dashboard may be a second FlutterFlow project pointed at the same `icoccha` backend; this hasn't been independently re-verified from this workspace. Confirm the actual live project id/collection shape here before porting any admin-dashboard backend assumption (a Cloud Function name, a collection field, a security rule) across — schema field names, in particular, are already known to diverge (this project has typos like `res_ic` for `res_id` and camelCase `extension_payments` keys that don't match the admin side's conventions; see `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §2 and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §2).

What IS safely shared and already carried over: **FlutterFlow AI SDK/tooling behavior** — the DSL surface, proto quirks, codegen behavior, and CLI mechanics are the same tool regardless of which FlutterFlow project you point it at. That's what `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s inherited quirks section is for.

### §5: Cloud Functions backend deployed to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, six §6 defects fixed and verified live (2026-08-09/10)

**What was asked:** the user asked to pause before the larger Stripe Connect onboarding work (§6 defect #5) and first deploy and test everything fixed so far.

**Important infrastructure correction made mid-session, before any deploy happened:** the plan's existing assumption (§1.5, §2 above) that this project's Firebase backend was the `icoccha` project turned out to be wrong — `icoccha` is actually bound to a *different* FlutterFlow project belonging to the prior contractor, confirmed by the user spotting it in the FlutterFlow Builder workspace list. Deploying this project's functions there would have collided with unrelated live work. Redirected all Cloud Functions work to a fresh Firebase project instead (`icoccha-new-mockup`, which auto-provisioned as `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` — confirmed via the actual downloaded config files rather than assumed, since the two names differ). The FlutterFlow project's own Firebase connection was repointed to this new project (user completed both the "Project ID" and separate "Generate Config Files" steps in FlutterFlow Settings). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §1.5 and `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §2's `icoccha`-project-id references are now stale and should be corrected to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` next time either file is touched — not fixed in this pass, flagged here so it isn't silently re-assumed.

**What was done:**
- Copied the reference backend (`/home/ari/Documents/Task/FlutterFlow/FlutterFlow/firebase/`) into this project's own `firebase/`, adapted for `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`: `.firebaserc`, cleaned `firebase.json` (dropped unused Data Connect config), Firestore/Storage rules and indexes copied as-is, `functions/package.json` with the dead `@dataconnect/admin-generated` dependency removed.
- Fixed and deployed six of the twelve `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §6 defects in real code (see that file's §6 status line for the short version):
  - **#1** `config.ts`'s casing bug — rewrote `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` keys to match Firestore's real `lower_snake_case` fields; removed every raw-doc-read workaround elsewhere (`reservations.ts`, `stripe-payments.ts`) that existed only because the helper was broken.
  - **#3** webhook idempotency — replaced the reference tree's check-then-act race with a single Firestore transaction (`get` then `create`, abort on already-exists) in `stripe-webhooks.ts`.
  - **#4** `account.updated` handler now writes `is_stripe_restricted` to the user doc, not just a notification.
  - **#7** moved the reservation-status transition, `pair_history` update, and cast-reward bookkeeping out of the `capturePayment` callable (optimistic, pre-webhook) and into `handlePaymentIntentSucceeded` (webhook-driven), gated on `metadata.type` being absent so extension/tip PaymentIntents sharing the same webhook and `res_id` don't misfire the parent reservation's transition.
  - **#9** `requestPayout` now creates a real `payout_requests` document (with a duplicate-pending-request guard) instead of only sending a notification.
  - **#10** rewrote `cancelPayment` to compute the confirmed three-case cancellation-fee matrix (§3.7.15) server-side instead of trusting a client-supplied `partial_amount`.
  - Confirmed **#2, #6, #11, #12** already correct by direct inspection — no code changes needed.
  - **#5** (Connect onboarding) and **#8** (stalled-capture gap) confirmed still genuinely missing — deliberately left open, next up.
- Deploy hit a real infrastructure constraint, not a code problem: this fresh project's Cloud Run "total CPU per project per region" quota (20 vCPU, self-service increase unavailable yet — insufficient usage history) couldn't fit ~59 functions at the implicit 1 vCPU/uncapped-scale default. Resolved via small sequential batches (7 functions at a time) plus lowering `setGlobalOptions()` in `index.ts` to `{ cpu: 0.5, maxInstances: 10 }` — appropriate for these low-traffic admin/CRUD/webhook functions, not just a workaround. One full-fleet redeploy attempt to roll the new CPU config out everywhere at once made contention *worse* (every function getting a new revision simultaneously multiplies the old+new-revision-overlap problem across dozens of functions at once) — aborted that attempt; confirmed via `firebase functions:list` that nothing was lost (Cloud Run always keeps the last-good revision on a failed update) and deprioritized finishing the CPU-config rollout to the remaining stragglers as low-priority/deferrable, since correctness depends on the code (already fixed) not the specific revision's resource allocation.
- Also resolved along the way: Blaze billing upgrade, enabling Firebase Auth (blocked `onUserCreated` until done), deploying Firestore rules/indexes (Storage rules deploy still blocked — bucket not yet initialized, unresolved), and Cloud Run IAM "allow public access" on `stripewebhook` (a real 403-at-the-infrastructure-level distinct from any app-level error, fixed via Cloud Run → Security tab).
- **Stripe webhook registration without Dashboard access**: the user had no login credentials for `dashboard.stripe.com`. Solved by using the Stripe REST API directly with the secret key over HTTP Basic Auth (no dashboard session needed) — `POST /v1/webhook_endpoints` to register `https://asia-northeast1-icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing.cloudfunctions.net/stripeWebhook` for the 11 event types `stripe-webhooks.ts` handles, then read the one-time-only `.secret` field from the creation response straight into `functions/.env.icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`'s `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. Worth remembering as a general pattern: the Stripe Dashboard is a convenience layer, not the only way to configure an account — anything it does is also a plain authenticated REST call.
- **End-to-end verification, not just a deploy check**: created a real test-mode PaymentIntent via the API (`amount=100 jpy`, `payment_method=pm_card_visa`, `confirm=true`) to trigger a genuine webhook delivery, then read `firebase functions:log --only stripeWebhook` and traced the actual code path it took: signature verification passed (proves the webhook secret is wired correctly — a bad secret throws before any log line), the idempotency transaction (#3) created its `processed_events` doc cleanly, and `handlePaymentIntentSucceeded` (#7) executed and exited via its intentional `if (!resId) return` early-out (this synthetic PaymentIntent had no reservation tied to it, which is the *correct* behavior, not a bug) — no error surfaced anywhere in the trace, confirming both fixes work against real Stripe traffic, not just in code review.
- **Self-caught hygiene slip, noted for future sessions**: a `sed` redaction command meant to blank out both `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY=` and `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET=` lines before displaying the env file only matched the first pattern (regex didn't account for the second key lacking a `_KEY` suffix), so the real `whsec_...` value was briefly echoed in tool output. Low actual severity (test-mode secret, the file itself was always correctly gitignored and never committed), but the lesson stands: verify a redaction regex actually matches every target pattern before trusting its output as safe to display, don't assume.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** Storage bucket initialization; the handful of Cloud Run services flagged earlier with red warning icons in the console (`admingetsystemconfig`, `processtip`, `requestwithdrawal`, `updateprofile`, `autocancelexpiredauth`, `adminapprovekyc`, `admincloseworkpost`) were never actually investigated, only noted; the CPU-config rollout to the remaining 1-vCPU-default functions; cleaning up the orphaned original `icoccha-new-mockup` (non-`9a6ing`) Firebase project; correcting the stale `icoccha` project-id references in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` noted above; defect #8. (Defect #5 was open when this entry was first written — resolved in §6 below, same day.)

**Files touched:** `firebase/` (new directory — full Cloud Functions project), specifically `functions/src/config.ts`, `functions/src/reservations.ts`, `functions/src/stripe-payments.ts`, `functions/src/stripe-webhooks.ts`, `functions/src/index.ts`; `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§6 status line added); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). `functions/.env.icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` also written (gitignored, not committed).

### §6: Defect #5 (Stripe Connect onboarding) built and deployed; §3/§5 committed and pushed to GitHub (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** commit and push the §5 work above, then move on to defect #5 — the one piece the user explicitly flagged as "the larger onboarding flow work" back when they first asked to pause for deploy/test.

**Git housekeeping first:** staged `firebase/` (dry-run confirmed `.env*`/`node_modules/`/`lib/` correctly excluded per `firebase/.gitignore`) plus the two doc updates, grepped the staged set for secret patterns (only hits were a placeholder `sk_test_xxx` in a code comment and prose describing the `whsec_` prefix — nothing real), committed (`5eb5a80`). **Push failed**: no git credential helper configured (local or global) for the HTTPS `origin` remote, so there's no way to authenticate to GitHub from this shell — same situation as `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §3, where the user completed the push themselves. Did not attempt to configure credentials (out of scope for an assistant to touch per this project's git-safety discipline) — flagged it and moved on to defect #5 as instructed, leaving the push for the user.

**What was done for defect #5** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §6 defect #5 — Custom Connect account onboarding was a bare shell, `auth.ts` only ever called `stripe.accounts.create()` with no follow-up, so the account sat permanently `restricted`):
- Confirmed via direct search (`lib/flutterflow_project/pages/`, `generated_code/`) that there is genuinely no existing UI or reference implementation for this anywhere in the workspace — `kyc.dart` is a bare placeholder page (just a title, no form fields), matching the plan's own "no reference implementation to adapt" framing. Built the backend piece only, consistent with this whole session's backend-only scope; the FlutterFlow UI to actually collect this data is separate, later work (flagged explicitly, not silently assumed done).
- Added two new `auth.ts` callables: `submitConnectOnboarding` (the real onboarding entry point — individual info in kanji+kana, DOB, phone, address, plus bank account, plus ToS acceptance; calls `stripe.accounts.update()` and `stripe.accounts.createExternalAccount()` as two independent Stripe calls so a bad bank number doesn't block personal-info progress that already succeeded; re-fetches the account afterward and mirrors `charges_enabled`/`payouts_enabled`/`requirements.currently_due`/`disabled_reason` both to the caller and into new `users.stripe_*` Firestore fields) and `getConnectAccountStatus` (read-only live requirements fetch for an in-app checklist, no Stripe-hosted redirect — matching the client's explicit "Custom account, full mirroring UX inside the app" decision already recorded in §3.9 item 3).
- Extended `stripe-webhooks.ts`'s `account.updated` handler (already touched for defect #4) to mirror the same `stripe_charges_enabled`/`stripe_payouts_enabled`/`stripe_requirements_due` fields on every async Stripe-side update, not just synchronously after our own calls — keeps the two write paths (webhook vs. direct callable response) consistent.
- Extended `admin.ts`'s `adminApproveKYC`: on approving a cast account, forwards the already-uploaded `kyc_doc_url` (our own manual-review document — defect #6, still the confirmed real KYC path) to Stripe as the connected account's `individual.verification.document`, via `stripe.files.create()` scoped to the connected account (`{stripeAccount: id}`) then `stripe.accounts.update()`. This closes a gap that would otherwise have undermined the whole fix: Stripe's own Custom-account verification needs a document independent of our internal `kyc_status`, and without forwarding one, an account could complete every other onboarding field and still never reach `payouts_enabled`. Best-effort (catches and logs, never throws) so a Stripe hiccup can't block the admin's own approval action.
- Verified the exact JP-individual field names (`first_name_kana`, `address_kanji`, `address_kana`, `routing_number`, `account_holder_name`, etc.) against the installed `stripe` npm package's own generated TypeScript types (`node_modules/stripe/types/AccountsResource.d.ts`) before writing any code, rather than trusting memory of the API shape — `npm run build` came back clean on the first attempt, confirming every field name is real.
- Deployed just the 2 new + 2 modified functions (`submitConnectOnboarding`, `getConnectAccountStatus`, `stripeWebhook`, `adminApproveKYC`) in one small batch — succeeded cleanly, no quota contention (small batch, matching the pattern already established in §5). Confirmed post-deploy that public reachability held: both new callable endpoints return `401` (our own `HttpsError("unauthenticated", ...)` guard) rather than a Cloud Run-level `403`, meaning they're correctly invokable and it's our own auth check responding, not an infra block — the same distinction that mattered for `stripeWebhook`'s earlier 403 fix in §5.
- Updated `firebase/firestore/schema.md`'s `users` collection table with the five new/newly-documented fields (`is_stripe_restricted` was already live from defect #4 but had never been added to this doc either — added it here too, not just the new ones).

**Verification status, stated plainly (this project's own documentation discipline — don't report "fixed" before it's actually confirmed):** confirmed at the type-checking level (compiles clean against Stripe's real SDK types) and at the deploy/reachability level (both endpoints live, correctly gated by our own auth check). **Not yet exercised end-to-end against a real cast Stripe account** — that would require either the not-yet-built FlutterFlow onboarding UI, or a hand-authored test call carrying a real cast user's Firebase ID token, neither of which happened this pass. This is a meaningfully lower verification bar than §5's webhook fixes, which were traced through actual production logs against a real Stripe event — flagging the difference explicitly rather than letting both read as equally confirmed.

### §7: Defect #8 (stalled `completion_pending` capture gap) built and deployed — all 12 §6 defects now closed (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continue straight into defect #8, the last open item on `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s §6 defect list.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading `reservations.ts` directly rather than assuming from the defect's own summary:** `reportCompletion` (the cast's completion-report callable) set the reservation's status to `completion_pending` and then simply returned — no Capture call anywhere in the function. `autoCompleteReviews`'s scheduled sweep already had a `completion_pending` block, but its own doc comment explicitly said it only closed chat and left "a capture that never happens" as "a separate, pre-existing gap... out of scope here." So there was truly no path, automatic or manual, that ever triggered Capture for a reservation in this state — exactly the defect's own description, not an exaggeration.

**What was done** — both fixes the defect text itself suggested, applied together rather than picking one:
- `reportCompletion` now calls `stripe.paymentIntents.capture()` inline, immediately after writing `status: completion_pending`, matching `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §3.5's own state-6 entry trigger wording ("cast completion report triggers Capture") literally. Wrapped in try/catch — a Stripe-side failure here must not fail the cast's completion-report action itself, and the reservation staying at `completion_pending` is exactly the state the safety net below watches for. Also added a status guard (`must currently be in_progress`) that didn't exist before — the function had no check at all previously, so it could be called twice on the same reservation.
- `autoCompleteReviews`'s existing `completion_pending` sweep block now retries the capture before closing chat (previously chat-close only), reusing the same `chat_close_sec`-derived cutoff as the stall timeout since no dedicated config value exists for this specific case. This is the safety net for the (now rarer, not eliminated) case where the inline attempt in `reportCompletion` never ran (a crash between the two calls) or failed (a transient Stripe error).
- Deliberately did **not** touch the reservation's status transition to `review_pending` in either path — that stays exclusively webhook-driven, per defect #7's own already-established design (`handlePaymentIntentSucceeded` in `stripe-webhooks.ts`). Both new capture-triggering call sites just ask Stripe to capture; only the webhook, once Stripe confirms, actually advances the reservation.
- Before deploying, checked `firestore.indexes.json` directly for the composite index the sweep's `where("status","==","completion_pending").where("updated_at","<",cutoff)` query needs — `(status: ASCENDING, updated_at: ASCENDING)` already exists and was already deployed earlier this session (§5). Worth checking explicitly given `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §5 item 14's own standing warning (carried over from the sister project's experience) that a missing composite index fails as a silently-swallowed empty result, not a visible error — this query predates my change, but confirming it rather than assuming was cheap and worth doing before trusting the sweep's behavior.
- Deployed just the two modified functions (`reportCompletion`, `autoCompleteReviews`); clean build; confirmed reachability the same way as every other function this session (`reportCompletion` returns `401` from our own auth guard, not a Cloud Run `403`).

**Verification status, stated plainly:** same bar as defect #5's — confirmed at the compile/deploy/reachability level, **not yet exercised against a real in-progress reservation with a live PaymentIntent going through the full request → cast-approve → meetup-confirm → completion-report flow**, since no FlutterFlow UI exists yet to drive that sequence and hand-authoring a full test harness for it (two test users, a real Authorize, both-sides meetup confirmation) was judged not worth the cost at this stage relative to §5's webhook verification, which had a much cheaper path to a real end-to-end check (a single direct API call). Flagging this rather than letting it read as equally confirmed.

**Where this leaves the defect list:** all twelve items in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §6 are now closed — six fixed and deployed this session (§5: #1/#3/#4/#7/#9/#10), two more fixed and deployed this pass and the previous one (§6: #5, §7: #8), and four confirmed already correct on inspection (#2/#6/#11/#12). What remains for the payment/reservation backend to be genuinely exercisable end-to-end is the FlutterFlow UI layer — request form, approve/decline, meetup-confirm, completion-report, review, onboarding forms — none of which exist yet beyond scattered unwired placeholder pages (`PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s own inventory). That UI work is Phase 2–5 per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §8's phased plan and is a distinctly different kind of work (FlutterFlow DSL, not Cloud Functions) from this entire session, which was backend-only start to finish.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** everything already listed as open at the end of §6 above (the FlutterFlow onboarding/reservation UI, Storage bucket setup, the red-warning Cloud Run services, the CPU-config rollout, the orphaned Firebase project, stale `icoccha` doc references) plus the still-unpushed local commits (`git push origin main` still fails — no credential helper configured; two commits ahead of `origin/main` as of this entry, unchanged blocker from §6).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (`reportCompletion` and `autoCompleteReviews` both fixed), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§6 defect #8 status resolved, overall status line updated), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §8: First real DSL work in this workspace — Phase 1 schema remediation, prerequisite discovery before onboarding/reservation UI (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** build the FlutterFlow onboarding and reservation UI screens (Phase 2 + Phase 4 of `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §8) — the first task in this whole session touching the FlutterFlow project itself rather than the separate Cloud Functions backend.

**Blocking discovery made before writing any UI DSL:** confirmed directly (not assumed) that the live FlutterFlow schema still has the original typos/gaps `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §5 already catalogued, while the Cloud Functions backend built earlier this session (§5–§7) already assumes the *corrected* names — `signup_page_widget.dart`'s own Firestore write uses `displayName` (→ real field `display_name`) while the new `onUserCreated` Cloud Function writes `nickname` to the same document; `confirmMeetup` (reservations.ts) reads/writes `guest_confirmed_meetup`/`cast_confirmed_meetup`, neither of which existed in the schema at all. Surfaced this to the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than silently picking a side (real product-consequence decision, per this project's own operating rules) — confirmed: fix the schema first, matching `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §5's own sequencing (Phase 1 before Phase 2/4), rather than building UI against field names the backend won't actually read.

**Executed `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §5 in full** (scoped to what's confirmed needed — see that section's own updated status line for the specific deviations from its original text, e.g. skipping the `DocumentReference` promotion for `guest_id`/`cast_ids` since the real backend reads them as plain strings). This was also this workspace's first-ever `flutterflow ai run` push (`dsl/create.dart`/`dsl/edit.dart` had been untouched starter templates since scaffolding, confirmed via `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §1).

**Real SDK-surface finding worth keeping**: the collection/field-level schema-editing helpers (`addCollectionField`/`updateCollectionField`/`removeCollectionField`/`updateCollection`/`addCollection`, in the SDK's own `src/helpers/collection_helpers.dart`) are **not exported** from the public `flutterflow_ai.dart` barrel — only `App.removeCollection` is. Confirmed by grepping the barrel's own export list directly rather than assuming from the doc comments. This is the same class of gap already documented in `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s inherited quirks for the analogous data-struct helpers (`updateDataStruct` etc.) — and the fix is the same proven pattern: replicate the ~10-line helper bodies directly in `dsl/edit.dart` using only genuinely-exported proto types (`FFCollection`/`FFParameter`/`FFIdentifier`/`FFDataTypeV2`/`FFSubType`, all re-exported via `flutterflow_schema.dart`, plus `generateRandomAlphaNumericString` from `schema_extensions.dart`, also re-exported). Six small local helpers now live at the top of `dsl/edit.dart` (`_findCollection`, `_renameField`, `_retypeField`, `_addField`, `_removeField`, `_renameCollection`, `_removeCollectionByName`, `_newCollection`, `_enumTypeOf`), every one idempotent (checks current state before mutating, so safe to leave live and rerun — unlike `app.enum_`/`app.collection`-style declarative calls, which stay one-shot).

**Two-push structure, and why**: `app.enum_(name, values)` returns an `EnumHandle` with no `.key` at all (just `name`/`description`/`values`) — the real server-assigned `FFIdentifier` needed to reference an enum as a field's type (`enumType(id)`) only exists after the declaring push lands. Push 1 declared all 9 new Enums (`ReservationStatus`, `UserRole`, `UserAccountType`, `KycStatus`, `ApprovalStatus`, `StaffType`, `AffiliateRewardStatus`, `LedgerEntryType`, `PayoutRequestStatus` — real value sets grepped directly from `firebase/functions/src/*.ts`, not invented) alongside every non-enum-dependent change (renames, new fields, new/removed collections). Push 2, after confirming push 1 landed, wired each enum onto its target field via a small `_enumTypeOf(project, name)` helper that searches `project.backend.dataSchemaConfig.enums` live by name (same field path `data_schema_helpers.dart`'s own `removeEnum` uses) — sidesteps ever needing to hardcode a key. Both pushes verified via `flutterflow ai inspect <id>` (not just a clean push exit code) — every rename, addition, removal, and enum assignment confirmed present in the live schema afterward, per this project's own "prove it, don't assume it" discipline.

**Two items turned out to already be done, not still pending**: §5 items 14 (Firestore composite indexes) and 15 (security rules rewrite) — both already satisfied by `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (35 indexes) and `firebase/firestore.rules` (tiered, role-gated), deployed earlier this session (§5) through the separate `firebase deploy` workflow. Worth remembering: this project's Firestore rules/indexes are NOT governed by this DSL at all — they're a Firestore-level config deployed independently, distinct from FlutterFlow's own internal schema bookkeeping.

**Verification (live, not just a clean push):** `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` clean both pushes; `flutterflow ai test` passing; `flutterflow ai inspect icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (human-readable summary) re-read after each push and diffed against intent — confirmed all renames (`res_id`, `nickname`, `phone`, `affiliator_uid`, `work_posts`), all new fields (Stripe mirror fields on `users`, meetup-confirm booleans on `reservations`, `system_config`'s new/retyped fields), both new collections (`payout_requests`, `affiliate_rate_history`), `extension_payments`'s removal, and all 9 Enums' presence AND their assignment to the correct field (`typeName: "Enum<X>"` on all 9 target fields). **One item not fully verified**: the `extensions` → `reservations` subcollection conversion (via `parentCollectionIdentifier`) compiled and pushed clean, and used the exact same proto field the SDK's own `addCollection` helper uses for genuine subcollections, but no inspect surface tried (`ai resources`, `ai inspect` default summary) actually surfaces parent-collection nesting to confirm it visually — flagged honestly rather than assumed, and low-stakes since no UI work in this pass touches `extensions` directly.

**Explicitly not done / deferred**: `users.gender` Enum (no firm value set found in any source document — would have been guessing, skipped rather than invent one); `audit_logs.target_type` Enum (admin-panel-only concern, Phase 12); `work_post.user_ref` field cleanup (unused per the real backend, but removing it wasn't verified risk-free against `admin.ts` without more checking, deferred); populating the 277 empty `description` fields project-wide (per §5 item 13's own instruction, doing this incrementally per-collection as touched, not as one giant pass — new/renamed fields in this pass do have real JP descriptions, everything else untouched).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (from an untouched starter template to the full Phase 1 remediation — 9 local schema-editing helpers + two `app.raw` blocks + 9 Enum declarations, now commented out per the one-shot-declarative discipline), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§5 status line added), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). Two live pushes: commit `8H2CRxjXO7bxnDLaqGYI` (renames/fields/collections/Enum declarations) and `ksf7pFcIbwPvB62XYxyu` (Enum-to-field wiring).

### §9: Onboarding verification chain wired end-to-end — Signup → Email → Phone/SMS → AuthComplete → new BasicInfoRegistration page (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continue into the actual onboarding UI (Phase 2), now that Phase 1's schema prerequisite was done.

**Scope covered:** fixed the priority-one correctness bug (Signup routed straight to Home, bypassing the entire verification chain); wired real Firebase email verification (send-on-load + verified-check); wired real Firebase Phone Auth SMS verification (send + verify, linked — not signed in — onto the already-authenticated email/password account); wired AuthComplete's dead-end "次へ" button; built the genuinely-missing BasicInfoRegistration page and connected it through to the existing Kyc page. Every one of these five auth-chain pages was a 100%-static mockup before this pass (confirmed via `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s own inventory — every forward action a stub or entirely absent).

**Real SDK-surface findings worth keeping, both discovered by hitting real `compileDslApp` failures, not by reading docs alone:**
- **Raw-added custom actions (`addCustomAction` inside `app.raw`) are invisible to a page built via `app.ensurePage` in the SAME script run** — `compileDslApp` failed with `No custom action named "..." found in the project` even though the action and the page were both declared, correctly, in the same push. Declarative pages are compiled before `app.raw`-queued mutations are applied, so a raw-declared action doesn't exist yet when the page's own `CallCustomAction.named(...)` reference is validated. **Fix: use the declarative `app.customAction(...)` instead** (not the brownfield `addCustomAction`) — it runs in the same declarative pass as pages. Its own doc comment confirms an identical rerun is a no-op (only a changed payload throws), so it's safe enough to use in an edit flow for a case like this, despite the general brownfield guidance elsewhere preferring `find*`/`add*`. Still commented out post-landing per this project's standard one-shot discipline.
- **`app.editPage(...)` requires the typed `ff.Pages.*` handle for its own page argument once ANY page in the project has been touched by this workspace's DSL at all — a raw string page name is rejected outright**, `Use ff.Pages.signupPage instead of a raw page name in editPage("SignupPage")`. Same rule applies to `WidgetState(...)`'s first argument when referencing a PRE-EXISTING native widget (not one freshly inserted in the same push) — `Use ff.Pages.phoneVarification.widgets.byKey(...).single instead of WidgetState("MobileNnmberField")`. Notably this did NOT apply to `Navigate(...)`'s own page-target argument (confirmed via `_resolvePageName`'s own source: a bare string is a fully sanctioned, separate code path there) — only `editPage`'s own top-level selector and `WidgetState`'s widget-reference argument are gated this way. Also did not apply to a widget inserted fresh in the SAME push (`SmsCodeField`, via `ensureReplaced`) — consistent with the already-known "a same-run-inserted widget has no typed handle yet" rule; a raw string is the *correct* choice there, not a workaround.
- **Retargeting a `Navigate` action buried inside an existing native multi-step chain, without touching anything else in that chain**, is a real, generalizable pattern: `page.mutateNode(selection, (node) { ...walk node.triggerActions.first.rootAction... })`, recursing through `followUpAction` AND `conditionActions`' true/false branches (a linear walk alone would miss anything nested in a conditional), looking for `node.action.hasNavigate() && node.action.navigate.pageNodeKeyRef.key == oldTargetKey` and reassigning just that one field. Confirmed via the actual regenerated `signup_page_widget.dart`: the button's `context.pushNamedAuth(...)` call target changed from `HomePageWidget.routeName` to `EmailVerificationWidget.routeName`, with every other step (password-match check, `createAccountWithEmail`, the Firestore `isAgreed`/`agreedAt` write, the success Snackbar) byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **`authManager.beginPhoneAuth` (already generated in this project's `firebase_auth_manager.dart`) is safe to reuse for the SEND half of a custom phone-verification flow, but its paired `verifySmsCode` must NOT be reused for the VERIFY half** — that method calls `signInWithCredential`, which signs in as a (potentially brand-new) separate identity rather than linking the phone number onto the guest's already-authenticated email/password account. Wrote a dedicated `verifyPhoneCodeAndLink` custom action calling `currentUser.linkWithCredential(...)` instead, reusing `beginPhoneAuth`'s own `phoneAuthManager.phoneAuthVerificationCode` state (already correctly populated by its `codeSent` callback) rather than re-deriving verification-ID plumbing from scratch.
- **This DSL has no clean primitive for a multi-box auto-advancing PIN/OTP input** (checked: no cross-field auto-focus mechanism, no native "digit box" widget). SmsCode's native design used 6 static dash "ー" `Text` placeholders with no real input behind them at all (confirmed — `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` had already flagged this). Replaced with one real `TextField` (`Keyboard.number`) — a disclosed, function-over-form trade-off, not an oversight.
- **No native typed-DSL action exists for calling a Cloud Function directly** (confirmed — `actions.dart` has no Callable/CloudFunction class) — the established pattern (already proven in this project's pre-existing `callCreatePaymentIntent`/`confirmStripePayment` custom actions) is a custom action wrapping `FirebaseFunctions.instanceFor(region:).httpsCallable(name).call({...})`. Followed the same pattern for `callCompleteOnboarding`, returning a plain `bool` (not a struct/json) so the caller can branch via `If(ActionOutput(...), ...)` with zero `FieldAccess`-on-json risk — matching this project's own inherited quirk about treating an unstructured json-returning action's result as opaque when only success/fail branching is needed.

**Deliberately deferred, disclosed rather than silently skipped:**
- **Web phone-auth linking is NOT supported** — `verifyPhoneCodeAndLink` explicitly returns `false` on `kIsWeb` with a comment explaining why (`ConfirmationResult.confirm()` signs in directly on web, it doesn't yield a linkable credential the way the way the native `verifyPhoneNumber` callback flow does) rather than silently doing the wrong thing (creating a second, disconnected identity). Mobile — this app's real target platform — is fully supported.
- The hardcoded placeholder email text on `EmailVerification` ("example@email.comに認証メールを送信しました") was left as-is — a cosmetic-only, already-flagged issue (`PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §8 item 15), not a functional gap; fixing it would need a `CustomFunction`-formatted dynamic Text binding, judged not worth the added structural risk for this pass.
- `gender`'s dropdown options (男性/女性/回答しない) are a reasonable judgment call, not sourced from any confirmed spec value set — flagging this explicitly rather than letting it read as confirmed product data, matching this project's own "guess at product rules → flag the gap instead" rule. Same for `staff_type` visibility being gated on `account_type == 'cast'` — a sensible UX inference, not an explicit requirement anywhere.

**Verification (live, not just a clean push):** `dart analyze` clean at every iteration; three `compileDslApp` failures hit and fixed in sequence (ordering, then two rounds of typed-handle requirements) before the final clean push; `flutterflow ai inspect` re-read afterward confirming 26 pages (was 25) and 7 custom actions (was 2, +5 new); **the actual regenerated `generated_code/` Dart read directly for every single wired step** — signup's retargeted `Navigate` target, email verification's `initState`/button wiring, phone verification's send-and-navigate, SMS code's real `TextFormField` + `linkWithCredential` call, AuthComplete's navigate target, and BasicInfoRegistration's consent-gated submit-and-navigate — all confirmed present and correctly connected in the compiled output, not just the DSL source.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** Kyc page's own content (still a placeholder — its wiring is the next task); Stripe Connect onboarding UI for cast; the reservation UI (Phase 4); the hardcoded email placeholder text; web phone-auth linking; a real device/Test-Mode click-test of the phone-auth flow (no live FlutterFlow Desktop session paired this session — verified via generated-code inspection only, the deepest verification available without one).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (Phase 2 section added: 5 custom actions, 1 App State field, `_retargetNavigate` helper, 5 page-wiring `editPage` blocks, 1 new `ensurePage`), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). Three live pushes this pass (two failed at `compileDslApp` validation, fixed in place, not left as dead attempts in history): final landed commit `ckuoRKLfJsDiQuwo6nZf`.

### §10: KYC upload/submit + approval-status functional lock (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continue the onboarding work into KYC submission and the review-pending functional lock (the rest of Phase 2), under a general "continue, review thoroughly, keep quality high" instruction — the user is handling git themselves going forward, freeing this session to focus purely on build quality.

**KYC page** (`kyc.dart` — two upload cards with static placeholder content, no file picker, and genuinely NO submit button at all; confirmed via the typed SDK read, matching `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s own inventory):
- Added two new pub dependencies neither previously present in this project (`image_picker`, `firebase_storage`) — versions picked by checking pub.dev directly rather than guessing, specifically for compatibility with this project's exact, hard-pinned `firebase_core: 3.14.0` (every Firebase package in this generated pubspec is exact-pinned, not caret-ranged). The current `firebase_storage` (13.4.6) needs `firebase_core ^4.13.0`, which would have forced a major-version cascade through every other Firebase package in the pubspec — checked backward through version history until finding `firebase_storage 12.4.5`, whose own `firebase_core: ^3.13.0` constraint the existing 3.14.0 pin genuinely satisfies. Confirmed correct empirically too: `flutter pub get` (runs automatically as part of every push) succeeded clean, no resolver conflict.
- New custom action `pickAndUploadImage(fileNameSuffix)` — real device image picker + Firebase Storage upload, returns the download URL. Upload path (`users/{uid}/...`) deliberately matches `firebase/storage.rules`' own owner-gated rule (added this session — see below), not invented independently.
- **Real, confirmed infrastructure blocker, disclosed rather than silently built around**: `firebase deploy --only storage` fails with `Firebase Storage has not been set up on project ... Go to ... and click 'Get Started'` — Storage was never provisioned on this project at all (a one-time manual Console action, same class of blocker as the Blaze billing upgrade and Firebase Auth enablement earlier this session, both of which needed the user's own console clicks). The KYC upload code is correct and complete regardless — it will simply keep failing gracefully (returns null, shows an error Snackbar) until that manual step happens, then work without any further code changes.
- Updated `firebase/storage.rules` (only had a rule for `banners/`) to add `users/{userId}/{allPaths=**}`, owner-read/write-only — matches the path the new upload action targets. Not yet redeployed (blocked on the same Storage provisioning step above) — ready to deploy the moment it's unblocked.
- New submit button (inserted — none existed) validates both uploads present, then calls the already-deployed `submitKYC` backend callable, then navigates to the existing `ReviewPending` page.
- **Two real, generalizable SDK landmines hit and fixed, both now in `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s "new quirks" section**: (1) `app.ensurePage(...)`'s body becomes permanently inert once the page exists, but the validator still checks its (never-to-be-applied) string references against live server state on every future push — fixed by commenting out the whole `ensurePage` call for `BasicInfoRegistration` now that it's confirmed landed, same one-shot discipline as everything else in this file; (2) branching directly on a nullable-String `ActionOutput` (`If(ActionOutput('uploadResult'), ...)`) hits the identical "Conditional execution for action is improperly set" error as an explicit `Equals`/`Not` condition — fixed with a small reusable `isNonEmptyString(value)` custom action, branching on ITS bare boolean output instead.

**Functional lock while `approval_status != approved`** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §3.1 item 11): deliberately scoped down from the source requirement's full breadth (block search/browse/favorites/request-sending/chat/all-payment-actions-individually) to the one piece that's genuinely buildable and meaningful right now — an entry-point redirect on `HomePage`'s own `ON_INIT_STATE` (reproducing its existing single-step `navIndex = 0` logic exactly, low-risk since it really is that simple, then adding a new `checkApprovalStatus` custom action + redirect-to-`ReviewPending` check after it). None of the individually-named gated features are wired to real triggers yet in any phase completed so far — gating an unwired stub protects nothing, so gating the actual entry point every one of those features will eventually live behind is the higher-leverage, honestly-scoped choice. The "exact specified Japanese under-review popup copy" (source: `ゲストユーザー機能・管理.pdf` p.2, not available to re-derive this session) was deliberately NOT reproduced/guessed inline — redirecting to the already-existing `ReviewPending` page (which shows its own real, already-correct copy) sidesteps needing to know that exact text at all. `checkApprovalStatus` fails OPEN (returns true) on a read error, a deliberate choice — a transient network blip must not lock out an already-approved user; documented as intentional, not an oversight.

### §11: Stripe Connect onboarding page for cast (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continue straight into the last piece of the onboarding-adjacent work — the cast-facing Stripe Connect onboarding UI for `submitConnectOnboarding` (this session's own §6 defect #5 backend).

**Built:** a new `ConnectOnboarding` page (individual name/kana, phone, DOB, address, bank account, ToS — the backend's *required* fields only; kanji names/address_kana/gender/bank account_type are backend-optional and deliberately skipped for this pass, disclosed not hidden) plus `callSubmitConnectOnboarding` (builds the backend's nested `{individual, bank_account, tos_accepted}` request shape from flat form fields — same established pattern as every other Cloud-Function-calling custom action this session) and `checkConnectFieldsComplete` (bare-bool pre-submit validation, same pattern as KYC's own field-completeness check).

**Real, generalizable SDK finding — nesting depth, not just condition operations, can trip the same restriction**: the first attempt nested a 3rd level of `If(...)` inside `KycSubmitButton`'s already-working 2-level chain (to fork cast vs. guest destination after successful KYC submission) — every condition in that 3rd level was individually bare (`ActionOutput(...)`, zero operations, the already-proven-safe shape), yet `compileDslApp` failed with the identical `Conditional execution for action is improperly set.` error, now reported 3 times against the SAME button (not just the new condition — all three). Confirmed via `flutterflow ai trace latest` (the plain error list didn't name the widget; the trace did) that all three violations pointed at `KycSubmitButton`. **Fix: don't stack a 3rd level onto an already-working chain — split the decision across two separate pages/triggers instead.** Reverted `KycSubmitButton` to its proven 2-level chain (always → `ReviewPending`), and added the cast/guest fork as a FRESH `ON_INIT_STATE` check on `ReviewPending` itself (a page with zero prior triggerActions, so a clean 2-level chain there too) — same end-user outcome, no chain deep enough to hit the restriction anywhere. Added to `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`.

**Real correctness bug caught before shipping, not after**: the first design for the ReviewPending redirect checked only `account_type == 'cast'` — which would have sent an ALREADY-onboarded cast user back to `ConnectOnboarding` every single time they revisited `ReviewPending` (browser-back, a stale deep link), a real infinite-loop-shaped UX bug. Renamed the check to `needsConnectOnboarding()` and added the second half of the condition: `stripe_onboarding_submitted_at == null` (a field this session's own §6/§8 work already added to the schema and backend) — a cast user who's already submitted stays on the normal review-pending screen.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; two `compileDslApp` failures this pass (a `Keyboard.phone` enum value that doesn't exist — fixed to `Keyboard.number`; the nesting-depth issue above) before the final clean push; **regenerated `generated_code/` read directly** confirming `KycSubmitButton`'s reverted 2-level chain, `review_pending_widget.dart`'s new `needsConnectOnboarding()` call and conditional redirect, the `ConnectOnboarding` page's real model/widget files, and `call_submit_connect_onboarding.dart`'s exact nested-JSON request body matching `submitConnectOnboarding`'s real backend shape field-for-field.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** the backend-optional fields (kanji names, address_kana, gender, bank account_type) and a live requirements checklist UI (`getConnectAccountStatus` exists, unused so far); Storage provisioning (still blocks live KYC document upload, unrelated to this page); reservation UI (Phase 4, next); a live device/Test-Mode click-test (same standing caveat as §9/§10).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (3 custom actions, 1 new `ensurePage`, `KycSubmitButton`'s chain reverted to 2 levels, 1 new `editPage` block on ReviewPending), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (1 new quirk — nesting depth), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). Two live pushes this pass (one failed, fixed in place), final landed commit `rNmoOGQ0UXEoigHB6WCI`.

Worth noting this isn't a one-off: EVERY `ensurePage`-created page in this project will need this same treatment the very next time any push touches the file, not just occasionally.
- **`SwitchListTile` is not a valid `ON_TOGGLE_ON`/`ON_TOGGLE_OFF` target in this SDK** — confirmed via a direct, specific `compileDslApp` rejection (`ON_TOGGLE_ON requires a Switch or Checkbox target, got SwitchListTile`), distinct from the generic "conditional execution" class of error seen elsewhere this session. `reservation_form.dart`'s native group-invite toggle is a `SwitchListTile`, not a plain `Switch` — replaced with a DSL-authored `Checkbox` (`ensureReplaced`, same boolean semantic, different visual) rather than trying to find a workaround for the native widget type.

### §14: `ReservationListPage` built, `payment_confirm.dart` wired, a real pre-existing Stripe key bug fixed — Phase 4 (reservation core) complete (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** the last item in this pass's reservation-UI scope — the genuinely-missing reservation list page, plus wiring the payment confirmation screen.

**`ReservationListPage`** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §7 — no native page or reference implementation existed): `FirestoreQuery`, this DSL's only native query action, has **no filter/where parameter at all** (confirmed by reading its full constructor) — it cannot express "my own reservations," and an unfiltered query would be rejected by `firebase/firestore.rules`' own per-document `guest_id`/`cast_ids`/`staff_ids` check regardless. Built a custom action (`fetchMyReservations`) running two filtered client-side Firestore queries (`guest_id ==`, `cast_ids array-contains`) instead — the only way to build this within what the DSL's typed surface actually exposes. Encoded each result as a **delimited `String`** (`resId|||status|||date|||location|||amount`), not `List<JSON>` or a declared Struct — deliberately sidesteps two real, separately-confirmed landmines (`List<JSON>` state can't be written reliably; `ItemRef['field']` needs a struct/document-typed source, which a bare custom-action JSON return doesn't have) by staying entirely within already-proven-safe mechanisms: two small custom *functions* (`reservationListItemLabel`/`reservationListItemResId`) split the string back apart for display and for the row's own `Navigate` param, matching the already-proven "`CustomFunction(...)` usable directly as widget content/action-param" pattern.

**Real, further-generalized SDK finding**: `listOf(string).withDefault([])` on a page-local state field failed identically to the already-documented `listOf(json).withDefault(...)` case (`Default values are not supported for type ListType(...)`) — confirms the restriction is about list-typed state defaults broadly, not just JSON element types specifically. Fixed by declaring the field with no default at all (fine in practice — an `onLoad` action populates it immediately).

**`payment_confirm.dart`**: wired the main "予約を確定する" button only (`getPaymentClientSecret` — reads the reservation's own `base_amount`/`transport_fee`/`staff_fee`/`cast_ids` server-side rather than threading them through the UI — → `confirmStripePayment`, the pre-existing native Stripe Payment Sheet action, unmodified in behavior). The separate "カードを登録する"/"変更する" (register/change card) buttons are deliberately left unwired — Stripe's own Payment Sheet already includes native card entry, so a first payment works without a separate saved-card flow; disclosed, not silently dropped.

**Real, pre-existing correctness bug fixed, not just noted**: `confirm_stripe_payment.dart` (a native, pre-existing custom action, present since before this session) had `Stripe.publishableKey = 'STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=pk_test_...'` — the literal env-var-assignment text baked into the value itself, which the Stripe SDK would reject outright. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 0 flagged this months of a plan-turn ago ("strip the prefix... even before the full Cloud Functions rebuild") but it was never actually fixed until now. Fixed via `updateCustomAction` (brownfield update on a native action, `code:`-only since the signature is unchanged) — confirmed the stray prefix is gone in the regenerated source, just the clean `pk_test_...` value remains. Scoped as the minimal, correct fix (strip the prefix) rather than a broader rewrite — a Stripe *publishable* key is designed to ship client-side, so this was a formatting bug, not a secrets-hygiene issue the way the secret key would be.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; one `compileDslApp` failure (the list-default finding above) fixed before the clean push; **regenerated `generated_code/` read directly** confirming `ReservationListPage`'s full fetch→display→tap-to-detail chain, `payment_confirm_widget.dart`'s complete 4-action chain, and the Stripe key fix's exact before/after value.

**This closes out the reservation-UI scope (§12-§14) and the onboarding-UI scope (§9-§11) both started from "the FlutterFlow onboarding and reservation UI screens" ask.** Every page in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 2 and Phase 4 checklists that this session touched is now wired to real backend calls, not static mockups — with every scope cut, deferred piece, and infrastructure blocker (Storage provisioning, `cast_profile.dart`'s own invite button, status-driven button visibility, etc.) explicitly named in its own change-log entry rather than silently left implicit.

**Explicitly not done / still open (consolidated across §9-§14):** Storage bucket provisioning (blocks live KYC upload) + the follow-up `firebase deploy --only storage`; `cast_profile.dart`'s invite-button wiring (the real entry point into `reservation_form.dart`); `reservation_confirmed.dart`'s own display wiring; status-driven button visibility on `reservation_detail.dart`; the "カードを登録する" saved-card flow; the exact under-review popup copy; a live device/Test-Mode click-test of anything built this session (no FlutterFlow Desktop session paired); Phase 3 (discovery/profile core — search, favorites, cast profile schedule) and Phase 12 (admin panel) remain entirely unstarted.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (3 custom actions, 2 custom functions, 1 `ensurePage`, 1 `editPageParams`, 1 raw `updateCustomAction`, 1 `editPage` block on PaymentConfirm), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (1 new quirk — list-default generalization), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 4 status), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). Two live pushes this pass (one failed, fixed in place), final landed commit `ktXJYThCnpWeIJPXNDRD`.

### §15: Full audit of §9-§14's work — 7 unfrozen one-shot structural ops found and fixed; every backend call signature re-verified (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** "thoroughly analyze all the work you've done so far... test for any errors that might arise under various conditions... as befits a bug-fixing expert," explicitly ordered BEFORE the two remaining open items (Storage provisioning guidance, `cast_profile.dart` wiring) and with all unrelated work explicitly excluded.

**Backend cross-check (no bugs found):** re-read the actual TypeScript source (not memory) for every Cloud Function this session's custom actions call — `createReservation`, `respondToReservation`, `confirmMeetup`, `reportCompletion`, `submitReview`, `cancelPayment`, `createPaymentIntent`, `submitKYC`, `completeOnboarding`, `submitConnectOnboarding` — against each corresponding `callXxx` action's exact request body. All field names/shapes match exactly, including `submitConnectOnboarding`'s nested `individual`/`bank_account`/`dob`/`address` structure. One non-bug gap noted: `callCancelReservation` never sends the optional `cancel_reason` field `cancelPayment` accepts — backend defaults it safely (`cancel_reason || ""`), a UX nicety left for a future pass, not a defect.

**Null-safety / edge-case spot-checks (all confirmed already-correct):** `callCreateReservation`'s `castId == null || castId.isEmpty` guard (relevant today since `cast_profile.dart` doesn't populate it yet — confirmed it returns `null` gracefully rather than crashing or sending a malformed request); `fetchMyReservations`'s delimiter-escaping (`location.replaceAll('|||', '')`) — confirmed it's the only one of the 5 packed fields that can contain arbitrary free-text user input, the other 4 (`res_id`, `status`, formatted date, stringified amount) are all machine-generated and can't contain the `|||` separator; `getPaymentClientSecret`'s Firestore-field null-coalescing, consistent throughout; the `isCastAccount`→`needsConnectOnboarding` rename from §11 — grepped the whole project (`dsl/`, `lib/`, `generated_code/`, all `.md` docs) for stale `isCastAccount` references, found none.

**The real find — 7 unfrozen one-shot structural ops, the exact systemic risk `project_rules.md` already documents at length (its own "never-commented-out `ensureReplaced`... confirmed SYSTEMIC" entry, discovered on the sister project):** none of `dsl/edit.dart`'s `page.ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`app.ensurePage` calls across §9-§14 had ever been frozen (commented out) after landing, despite this file's OWN comments in two places explicitly saying "comment out once confirmed landed." Found via a full top-to-bottom read of the file plus a grep sweep for all four constructs, then cross-checked each target key against `generated_code/`/the typed SDK to confirm it had actually landed before freezing. Fixed, in file order:
1. `SmsCode` page: `Card_qcaxhffj` → `SmsCodeCard`/`SmsCodeField` replace.
2. `Kyc` page: `KycSubmitButton` insert-after `Divider_r0a3brb1` (mixed into an `editPage` block with 2 still-live `ensureActions` calls — froze only the stale op, left the others running, per the file's own "don't comment out a whole block for one stale op inside it" precedent).
3-5. `ReservationForm` page (all three mixed into one `editPage` block alongside 8 still-live `ensureActions` calls, same partial-freeze treatment): `Container_8is5q6ti` → `ResDateField` replace; `SwitchListTile_8u4clxfz` → `ResGroupInviteCheckbox` replace; `Row_8aeejqf8` insert-after → `ResBaseAmountRow`.
6. `ReservationDetail` page: `Row_dw9z46os` insert-before → `ReviewInputRow`/`ReviewCommentField`.
7. **Highest severity** — `ReservationListPage`'s own `app.ensurePage(...)` (built in §14). Its body uses raw `State('myReservationsList')`/`SetState(...)` references, and the page already exists live (confirmed via `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/reservation_list_page.dart`, key `Scaffold_00y3zobz`) — meaning it was sitting in EXACTLY the same trap that already broke two earlier pushes this session (`BasicInfoRegistration` in §9/§10, `ConnectOnboarding` in §11/§12: body becomes permanently inert once the page exists, per `compiler.dart`'s `_compilePages`, but the typed-SDK-authoring validator still checks the inert body's raw `State(...)` references against live server state). This would have failed the very next unrelated push — almost certainly the Phase B `cast_profile.dart` push this same turn — with `Use ff.Pages.reservationListPage.state.myReservationsList instead of State(...)`, caught here before it could happen rather than after.

None of the frozen calls' created widgets are referenced elsewhere in the file in a way freezing breaks — verified by grepping each new widget's `name:` (`SmsCodeField`, `ResDateField`, `ResGroupInviteCheckbox`, `ResBaseAmountRow`/`Field`, `KycSubmitButton`, `ReviewInputRow`/`ReviewCommentField`) across the whole file. Only one has an external reference (`SmsCodeField`, read via `WidgetState('SmsCodeField', WidgetStateProperty.text)` in a separate, later `editPage` block) — confirmed safe to leave as-is, since `WidgetState(name, property)` is a plain string-keyed value expression resolved against live project state, not a `page.findByName(...)` node locator (the specific construct `project_rules.md` documents as needing a typed handle once a widget genuinely exists) — the file already uses bare-string `WidgetState(...)` pervasively for natively-existing widgets the same way.

**Minor, not fixed (judged out of this turn's scope):** `ReservationListPage`'s `ListView` uses `shrinkWrap: true` despite the project's own mandatory design rule ("avoid `shrinkWrap: true` on dynamic ListView") — it's the Scaffold's direct body (not nested in another scrollable), so the flag is unnecessary but not functionally broken for this page's realistic list sizes. Flagged here rather than fixed, since fixing it would require a new `editPage` raw-proto mutation with real execution risk for a cosmetic/perf nit, and the user's own instruction for this turn explicitly excluded unrelated work beyond the audit + two named items.

**Verification:** `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` — 0 errors, 2 expected `unused_local_variable` warnings (`reservationListItemLabelFn`/`reservationListItemResIdFn`, whose only consumer, the now-frozen `ensurePage` body, is commented out — the functions themselves stay live/declared since they're still genuinely used by the already-pushed live page; the local Dart variables holding their handles just go unread in this script, a warning not an error, same class already covered in `project_rules.md`'s own `unused_local_variable` guidance). No live push made this pass — every change was a comment-only freeze with zero behavioral effect on the already-landed remote project; the freezes take effect on the NEXT push (Phase B's `cast_profile.dart` wiring, done in this same turn).

**Explicitly not done / still open:** unchanged from §14's own list — Storage bucket provisioning, `reservation_confirmed.dart`'s display wiring, status-driven button visibility on `reservation_detail.dart`, the saved-card flow, the exact under-review popup copy, a live device/Test-Mode click-test, Phase 3/Phase 12. `cast_profile.dart`'s invite-button wiring is addressed in this same turn, next entry.

### §16: `cast_profile.dart`'s invite buttons wired to `reservation_form.dart` — reservation flow has a real entry point (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** the second of two explicitly-named remaining items this turn — give `reservation_form.dart` (fully wired since §12, but with no real caller) an actual entry point from `cast_profile.dart`'s own "誘う"/"ココ店で誘う" buttons.

**Read first, not assumed:** `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/cast_profile.dart` (the typed SDK) directly, rather than a broad `flutterflow ai inspect`. Two real findings that shaped the fix:
1. Both invite buttons (`Button_snz4hqcj` "誘　う", `Button_z76lh1eh` "ココ店で誘う") had zero existing `triggers:` at all — confirmed genuinely unwired, not just under-inspected, matching `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s earlier synthesis.
2. **`CastProfileParams` was completely empty — this page takes NO params today**, and its entire body is static hardcoded mock content (name "ゆずき", region "東京都 港区", age "20歳代 後半", hobbies "ドライブ"/"ネイリスト", etc. — no `State(...)`/document binding anywhere in the widget tree). `cast_profile.dart` isn't bound to a real cast's Firestore document yet at all; that's Phase 3 (discovery/profile-core) work, not started this session.

**Built:** added a `castId` page param to `CastProfile` (`string.withDefault('')`, same safe-cold-entry-deep-link pattern as `reservation_form.dart`'s own `castId`), then wired both invite buttons' `ON_TAP` to `Navigate(ff.Pages.reservationForm, params: {'castId': PageParam('castId')})`. Both buttons wired identically.

**Correction made during a later review pass (2026-08-10):** the original write-up of this entry claimed "nothing in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` distinguishes two separate invite flows" for these buttons — checked again and that overstated it. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` line 394 DOES track a venue-based invite concept (`CocoTenDetailPage`'s own "invite from this venue" entry point, Phase 3, not yet built) — but that's a structurally different, venue-first flow reached by browsing a CocoTen venue's own detail page, not a second behavior for `cast_profile.dart`'s two CAST-profile buttons (both of which invite the SAME cast being viewed; "ココ店で誘う" most plausibly differs only in an eventual meeting-location default once venue selection exists, not a separate destination page). Nothing specifies a distinct concrete behavior for cast_profile.dart's own two buttons specifically, so routing both to the one real reservation entry point remains the correct minimal choice — the conclusion didn't change, only the precision of the reasoning behind it. `dsl/edit.dart`'s own inline comment corrected to match.

**Honest scope of what this does and doesn't fix:** this makes the LAST leg of the chain real (`CastProfile` → `ReservationForm` with a real param-forwarding path), not the WHOLE chain. `CastProfile` itself still has no upstream caller passing it a real `castId` — nothing in this app currently navigates to `CastProfile` with a populated param either, since the discovery/match-feed pages that would do that are Phase 3, unstarted. Until Phase 3 exists, `CastProfile`'s own `castId` defaults to `''` and gets forwarded as `''`, so `callCreateReservation`'s existing `castId == null || castId.isEmpty` guard (§12) still correctly no-ops rather than creating a malformed reservation. This is the same honest, disclosed half-chain pattern already used for `reservation_form.dart`'s own `castId` param in §12 — each piece is wired correctly and immediately ready the moment the piece upstream of it exists, without needing to be revisited.

### §17: Firebase Storage provisioned via API, not the Console — the last standing infrastructure blocker from §10 is closed (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** the first of two remaining open items — Storage was never provisioned on this project (`firebase deploy --only storage` failing with "has not been set up... click 'Get Started'" since §10), and the user asked whether a service-account key at `/home/ari/Documents/Task/new_1/key.json` could be used to grant Console-equivalent access.

**Real finding #1 — that specific key was for the wrong project.** Read its `project_id`/`client_email` before using it: `icoccha-admin-dashboard` (the sister admin-panel project's own Firebase service account), not `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`. Flagged this explicitly rather than silently trying it and either failing opaquely or, worse, risking an action against the wrong live GCP project — consistent with this project's own standing rule (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`'s "Relationship to icoccha-admin-dashboard" section) to never assume the two projects' credentials/resources are interchangeable.

**Real finding #2 — didn't need the key at all.** `gcloud auth list` showed this machine already has an authenticated user session (`icoccha.com@gmail.com`), and `gcloud projects list` confirmed that account has access to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` directly. From there, fetched the live discovery document for `firebasestorage.googleapis.com` v1beta (`curl .../$discovery/rest?version=v1beta`) rather than guessing at endpoints, and found `POST v1beta/projects/{project}/defaultBucket` (`firebasestorage.projects.defaultBucket.create`) — its own description: *"Creates a Spark tier-eligible Cloud Storage bucket and links it to your Firebase project"* — this is the literal API-level equivalent of the Console's "Get Started" button, not a workaround. **This means Firebase Storage's initial setup does NOT actually require the Console UI** — the `firebase` CLI's own `deploy --only storage` simply never implemented this call itself (it only manages rules once a bucket already exists), which is why it always pointed at the Console instead. Worth remembering for any future project hitting the same "has not been set up" block — check for CLI/API provisioning before assuming a browser click is mandatory.

**Built/executed:** confirmed `asia-northeast1` as the right bucket location by checking this project's own already-live infrastructure first (`gcloud firestore databases describe` → Firestore is in `asia-northeast1`; `functions/src/index.ts`'s `setGlobalOptions` → Cloud Functions are too) rather than guessing or defaulting to a US region — co-locating avoids cross-region latency/egress. Confirmed with the user before executing (bucket location is effectively permanent post-creation, and this creates a real, if Spark-tier-free, billable resource on a live project — treated as the same class of "confirm before a hard-to-reverse, shared-system action" as every other infra-provisioning step this session). Called `defaultBucket.create` with `{"location": "asia-northeast1"}` using the already-authenticated user's access token (`gcloud auth print-access-token`) — succeeded immediately, returned bucket `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing.firebasestorage.app`. Then ran `firebase deploy --only storage` (the same command that had been failing since §10) — succeeded cleanly this time, releasing the already-written `storage.rules` (owner-only `users/{uid}/**`, public-read `banners/**`, deny-all default, written in §10) with zero rule-content changes needed.

**Verification:** `gcloud storage buckets describe gs://icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing.firebasestorage.app` confirms the bucket exists, correct region; `firebase deploy --only storage` output shows `rules file storage.rules compiled successfully` → `released rules storage.rules to firebase.storage` → `Deploy complete!`. This closes the caveat noted in §10/§14/§15/§16 — `pickAndUploadImage`'s upload path (`users/$uid/...`) now has a real bucket to write to and rules that permit exactly the intended owner-only access; the KYC upload flow (§10) is end-to-end live, not just code-ready, for the first time this session.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** a live device/Test-Mode click-test of the actual upload (no FlutterFlow Desktop session paired this session, same standing caveat noted throughout §9-§16); everything else already listed as open across §14-§16 (`reservation_confirmed.dart` display wiring, status-driven button visibility, saved-card flow, under-review popup exact copy, Phase 3/Phase 12 broadly, `CastProfile`'s own missing upstream caller).

**Files touched:** `firebase/` (Storage bucket provisioned + `storage.rules` deployed — infrastructure/config, not source), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 2 caveat resolved), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (1 new quirk — the `defaultBucket.create` API discovery), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). No `dsl/edit.dart` push this pass — purely Firebase-project-level infrastructure, not a DSL change.
