### §19: `MacchaChats` preflight failure fixed — investigated before patching, found the obvious fix would have been wrong (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** fix the `MacchaChats` blocker from §18 before anything else.

**Investigated the root cause instead of blindly satisfying the lint.** Read the SDK's own validator source (`wiring_review.dart`'s `_checkListWiring`/`R10`) directly rather than guessing at what "binds to list item data" requires — confirmed the check is: any `ListView`/`GridView` node with `hasDatabaseRequest() == true` must have at least one descendant `FFVariable` sourced from that same query (`GENERATOR_VARIABLE`/`FIRESTORE_REQUEST`/etc.); a query-less list is explicitly out of scope (`if (!node.hasDatabaseRequest()) continue`). `ListView_84hpa09g` (`MacchaChats.body[0].children[0].children[2]`) has a native `databaseRequest`: an unfiltered Firestore query against a top-level `chats` collection (`chat_room_id`/`created_at`/`sender_id`/`text`, per `schemas.dart`), ordered by `created_at`, streaming — with a single static item-template child showing 2 hardcoded "Hello World" bubbles (a visual mockup, never actually per-item-bound).

**The surface-level fix (bind a Text child to `ItemRef()['text']`, the pattern used successfully elsewhere — e.g. `references/social_feed_data_dsl.dart`) would have been WRONG, not just suboptimal.** Three independent checks, all done before writing any fix:
1. `firebase/firestore.rules` has no `match` block for `chats` at all, and the file has no catch-all/default-allow rule — Firestore denies unmatched paths by default, so this query already returns `permission-denied` for every real client, always.
2. `grep -rn "collection('chats')" firebase/functions/src/*.ts` — zero matches across the entire backend. No Cloud Function has ever written a single document to this collection.
3. `firestore.rules`' own `chat_rooms` rule carries a comment stating the REAL chat storage is the `chat_rooms/{room_id}/messages` SUBcollection (created by `reservations.ts`) — a structurally different, already-established data model from the flat `chats` collection this query targets. `MacchaChatsParams` is also completely empty (no room-scoping param), so even a working query here has no way to filter to one conversation.

Binding to real query data here would have satisfied the linter while staying functionally dead (permission-denied/empty at runtime) — LOOKING wired while being no more functional than the static mockup, and arguably worse (a silently-empty list reads as "no messages yet," misleading, versus an honestly-static placeholder).

**Actual fix:** `page.mutateNode(page.findByKey('ListView_84hpa09g'), (node) { node.clearDatabaseRequest(); })` — removes the orphaned query entirely, which is exactly what the validator's own logic anticipates as a valid state (a list genuinely has no query attached, not "has a query nobody uses"). Leaves the existing static mockup content completely untouched — this page was never functional chat UI to begin with (no room param, no working query, no backend writer for the collection it queried), so nothing observable changes for a user; only the broken, invisible, unscopable query is gone. `clearDatabaseRequest()` is a pure field-clear, safe to leave live permanently (idempotent — clearing an already-empty field is a no-op on every future rerun), unlike `ensureReplaced`/similar structural ops that need freezing after landing.

**Verification:** `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` — 0 issues. `flutterflow ai test` passed. `flutterflow ai run` — `[OK] compileDslApp`, pushed clean, `.flutterflow/workspace.json` confirms `"pushed": true` (commit `roknAgWPvhAAvJXstGVe`) with no `preflight` failure this time. **Regenerated `generated_code/lib/maccha/maccha_chats/maccha_chats_widget.dart` read directly**: no `StreamBuilder`/`FutureBuilder`/Firestore query of any kind remains (confirmed gone), and the 4 "Hello World" placeholder texts are still present unchanged (confirmed zero visual regression). This same push also landed §18's 2 trivial cleanup edits (unused-var removal, comment correction), which had been stuck unpushed since discovering this blocker.

**Explicitly not done / still open, disclosed rather than silently deferred:** real chat functionality for `MacchaChats` — room-scoped reads against `chat_rooms/{room_id}/messages`, a `roomId` page param, real navigation from a specific conversation into this page, sender-based bubble alignment, avatar binding. All of this is a genuine future feature build (the `chat_rooms/{room_id}/messages` subcollection itself is also still ungoverned by any security rule per the existing comment in `firestore.rules` — a separately-flagged, pre-existing gap, not touched here) — explicitly out of scope for "fix the preflight error," which is now resolved on its own terms.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (1 `editPage`/`mutateNode` block clearing the orphaned query, plus §18's 2 pending cleanup edits landed in the same push), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (this fix's own reasoning could inform a future quirk entry if this pattern recurs — not added as a new quirk yet since it's a one-off investigation, not a confirmed SDK-wide gotcha), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). One live push, landed clean: commit `roknAgWPvhAAvJXstGVe`.

### §20: Whole-project sweep for the same orphaned-query pattern — none found (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** check whether any OTHER page has the same pattern that broke `MacchaChats` (§19) — motivated by the fact that `preflight`'s own `R10` wiring check only reviews entities actually TOUCHED by a given push (confirmed by reading `reviewWiring`'s own doc comment: "When `touchedEntities` is provided (edit), only pages/components whose names appear in the set are reviewed... pass `null` to review everything"), meaning a pre-existing issue on an UNTOUCHED page could sit undetected indefinitely, the exact same "systemic, not isolated" risk shape already confirmed for the `ensureReplaced`-freezing pattern in §15/§18.

**Method:** rather than trust an indirect signal (a `flutterflow ai validate` no-op run that might or might not have triggered a full, untouched-entities-included review — inconclusive, since the run trace doesn't record which review mode fired), wrote a script that calls `flutterflow ai inspect <project> --page/--component <name>` for **every single page (28) and component (8)** in the project — the full `--page`/`--component` output (not `--dsl-json`, which omits low-level fields) includes the raw `databaseRequest` field the same way it did for `MacchaChats`, confirmed by testing on `MacchaChats` before it was fixed. For every `ListView`/`GridView` node found with `databaseRequest` present, the script also checked whether ANY descendant carries a `"source"` key (any kind of dynamic variable binding, not just the exact `_childrenBindListItem` source-matching logic from `wiring_review.dart` — deliberately broader/more conservative, so as not to under-report).

**First run crashed silently losing all progress** — an uncaught `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` on page 12 (`HomePageBasic`, which took longer than the 60s per-call timeout) killed the whole script, and since results were only printed/saved at the very end, nothing was recovered. **Real lesson, not just a retry:** any long sequential external-call loop needs (1) generous per-call timeouts (`dart run`'s own JIT/startup overhead here averaged ~55-66s per invocation, not the few seconds a compiled binary would take), (2) per-call exception handling so one slow/failed item doesn't erase all prior work, and (3) incremental result-saving after every item, not just at the end. Rewritten with all three; the second run took ~35 minutes (36 items × ~60s) but completed cleanly with **36/36 succeeded, 0 failures**.

**Result: zero other `ListView`/`GridView` nodes with a `databaseRequest` were found anywhere in the project** — `MacchaChats` (already fixed in §19) was the only instance. Sanity-checked the scan methodology itself before trusting a "zero found" result (a scan that finds nothing could mean "genuinely none" or "the scan is silently broken") — re-ran the same detection logic against 4 pages known to contain real `ListView`/`GridView` widgets (`ReservationListPage`, `CocomisePage`, `MacchaPage`, `WorkPage`) and confirmed it correctly found and reported all 5 of their list/grid nodes (all `has_query: false`, consistent with them being DSL/state-driven rather than native-query-driven, per this session's own established pattern of avoiding native `FirestoreQuery`'s missing-filter limitation) — the detection logic works, this wasn't a false negative from a broken scan.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** this sweep covered only the specific `R10` pattern (`ListView`/`GridView` + `databaseRequest` + no item binding) that broke `MacchaChats` — it did NOT run the other ~10 wiring-review rules (`R11` form-API binding, `R12` navigation params, `R13` page-load data flow, etc.) that `reviewWiring` also checks, since those weren't part of what actually blocked this session's pushes and weren't what was asked. If a future push fails preflight on a DIFFERENT rule, the same "don't assume it's isolated, sweep the whole project" discipline should apply again, scoped to whichever rule actually fired.

**Files touched:** `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry) only — pure investigation, no DSL/infrastructure change (nothing to fix, since nothing else was found).

### §21: R11-R13 checked project-wide — a much faster method found than §20's manual sweep (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** extend §20's coverage to the other wiring-review rules `reviewWiring` runs (`R11` form-to-API field binding, `R12` navigation parameter completeness, `R13` page-load data flow completeness).

**Found a far better method than another custom scan.** §20's manual per-page `inspect` sweep took ~35 minutes for just one rule (R10) because it had to re-implement the check's logic externally. This time, re-read the pipeline code (`flutterflow_ai_pipeline.dart` around line 763) once more and confirmed a reusable trick: `touchedEntities` only gets computed (scoping the review to touched pages) when `protoBeforeTasks != protoAfter` — i.e. when the push actually changes something. Running `flutterflow ai validate dsl/edit.dart` when the file is already a complete no-op against the live project (true right now — nothing changed since the last push) leaves `touchedEntities` at its default `null`, and `reviewWiring(project, touchedEntities: null)` reviews **every page**, running ALL rules (R1, R10-R19), not just R10. Confirmed this actually happened (not just assumed) via the run's own trace: `[info] NO_OP: All tasks completed but no project changes were detected. The project proto is identical before and after task execution` — direct proof the full-project code path fired. The complete, untruncated diagnostics list from that one ~7-second run is authoritative — it's the SDK's own real `wiring_review.dart` logic, not an external re-implementation like §20's script was.

**This is the method that should be used first for any future preflight-rule check** — reach for the manual `inspect`-sweep-and-reimplement approach (§20's own method) only if a no-op validate run isn't possible (e.g. genuinely mid-edit with real pending changes) or if a specific rule needs deeper investigation than the diagnostics summary line offers.

**Results — direct from the full-project diagnostics list:**
- **R11 (form-to-API field binding):** 0 findings anywhere in the project.
- **R12 (navigation parameter completeness):** 1 finding — `R12: Navigates to ReservationDetail but may be missing optional param "castId"`, sourced from `ReservationListPage`'s row-tap `Navigate(ff.Pages.reservationDetail, params: {'resId': ...})` (§14) — genuinely doesn't pass `castId`, since `fetchMyReservations`'s delimited-string encoding (§14) only carries `res_id`/`status`/`date`/`location`/`amount`, not `cast_ids`. **Warning severity, not critical — confirmed via `castId`'s own declaration (`string.withDefault('')`, all 3 occurrences in `dsl/edit.dart`), matching `_checkNavigationParams`' own rule that only a NON-optional (no-default) target param would be critical.** Real-world consequence, already covered by an existing guard: a user reaching `ReservationDetail` via the reservation LIST (as opposed to a request notification) and then tapping "評価する" would have `callSubmitReview`'s `castId` argument arrive empty — `callSubmitReview`'s own `if (castId == null || castId.isEmpty) return false` guard (§13) already makes this fail gracefully (a Snackbar, not a crash or bad data), not a new discovery so much as a confirmed, precisely-located instance of an already-accepted class of gap.
- **R13 (page-load data flow completeness):** 0 findings anywhere in the project.

**Not fixed this pass** — flagged for the user to decide, since fixing the R12 finding properly means extending `fetchMyReservations`'s encoding to also carry `cast_ids` (a real change to an already-shipped, working feature from §14, not a trivial one-liner) and this session's own instruction was to CHECK these rules, not necessarily remediate every non-blocking finding found.

### §22: R12 fixed, then a much more severe pre-existing bug found and fixed while verifying it (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** "correct it right now" — fix §21's R12 finding (`ReservationListPage`'s row navigation not passing `castId` to `ReservationDetail`).

**The R12 fix itself:** extended `fetchMyReservations` (via `updateCustomAction`, since its payload was changing — original `app.customAction` declaration frozen in the same push, same discipline as every other payload-changing update in this file) to also fetch `cast_ids` and append the primary one as a 6th `|||`-delimited field. Added a new `reservationListItemCastId` custom function to extract it, and patched `ReservationListPage`'s row `Card_wf69s6in` (`page.ensureActions`, safe for this one isolated trigger) to pass both `resId` and `castId` into `Navigate(reservationDetail, ...)`. Confirmed `ItemRef()` — read directly from the SDK source (`itemBuilder(const ItemRef())` in `widgets.dart`) — is the literal same expression the original itemBuilder's `item` closure parameter always was, so referencing it from a standalone `page.ensureActions` block (outside any itemBuilder closure) reproduces the original wiring exactly.

**While verifying this push's regenerated code, found something much more serious.** `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/custom_functions.dart` showed the new `reservationListItemCastId` (and the pre-existing `reservationListItemLabel`/`reservationListItemResId` from §14) compiled to a doubly-nested shape:
```dart
String? reservationListItemResId(String? item) {
  String reservationListItemResId(String? item) {
    final parts = (item ?? '').split('|||');
    return parts.isNotEmpty ? parts[0] : '';
  }
}
```
— an inner local function declaration with no invoking call, no return statement. **Verified empirically, not just reasoned about**: ran this exact shape through `dart run` standalone and confirmed it returns `null` unconditionally, every time, regardless of input. Compared against `calculateExtensionPrice` (a genuinely pre-existing, never-DSL-touched native function, confirmed via `grep -n "calculateExtensionPrice" dsl/edit.dart` → zero matches) — it has a clean, single-layer body, no wrapper at all. Then checked every `references/*.dart` example of `app.customFunction(...)`'s own `code:` parameter — **every single one** contains ONLY body statements (`return '...';`, a bare `switch`, etc.), never a repeated function signature. Root cause conclusively identified: `app.customFunction`'s `code:` expects BODY-ONLY, and the SDK auto-wraps it in the correctly-typed outer signature — this file's own 3 customFunction declarations mistakenly included a full redundant `String <name>(String? item) { ... }` wrapper (correct convention for `app.customAction`, wrong for `app.customFunction`), and the SDK dutifully wrapped that already-complete function inside ANOTHER same-named shell.

**Real-world severity: this was not a cosmetic bug.** `reservationListItemLabel`/`reservationListItemResId` have been silently broken since §14 first shipped `ReservationListPage` — meaning the list's own displayed row text has always rendered from a `null` value, AND every row's `resId` navigation param has always been `null`, meaning **every single action on `ReservationDetail` reached via the reservation list — approve/decline/meetup/complete/review/cancel, every one of them gated on a `resId.isEmpty` guard — has silently no-op'd since the feature first launched**, despite §14's own change-log entry claiming this chain was "confirmed via regenerated `generated_code/` read directly." That verification checked that the WIRING (call sites, function names) was correct, but never actually inspected the called function's own internal body-execution correctness — a real, worth-remembering gap in this project's own verification discipline, not just a one-off oversight.

**Fixed all three functions** via `updateCustomFunction(project, name:, code:)` (the exact function-equivalent of `updateCustomAction`, confirmed to exist by reading `custom_code_helpers.dart` directly rather than assuming) with body-only code this time — froze all 3 original `app.customFunction` declarations in the same push (same "changed-payload landmine" discipline as every `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` case in this file). Since `updateCustomFunction` returns `void` (no handle), replaced the two needed `FFIdentifier`-equivalent captures with locally-constructed `CustomFunctionHandle(name:, args:, returnType:)` stubs — confirmed safe by reading the class itself (`types.dart`): a plain value object with no live-proto tie, matched only by `name` for equality, the exact same stub technique this file already uses for a frozen `app.component`.

**Verification, doubled up given the severity:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; push landed clean (commit `tLdpXVtkuqCJ1JvThSzo`); **regenerated `custom_functions.dart` read directly** confirms all 3 functions now have the same clean, single-layer shape as `calculateExtensionPrice`; **re-ran the exact fixed shape through `dart run` standalone again** and confirmed it now correctly returns real extracted values (`abc123`, `castUid789`) instead of `null`; **re-read `reservation_list_page_widget.dart`** confirming the Navigate call still correctly passes both `resId`/`castId` through the now-fixed functions.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** no live device/Test-Mode click-test of the ACTUAL reservation list UI (would be the strongest possible confirmation of a user-facing regression fix; no FlutterFlow Desktop session paired this session, same standing caveat throughout); this bug class (a customFunction `code:` mistakenly written with a full signature instead of body-only) is now understood well enough to be worth a `project_rules.md` quirk entry for future sessions, added alongside this one.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (3 `updateCustomFunction` calls + 3 frozen original declarations + 2 `CustomFunctionHandle` stubs + the R12 navigation fix itself), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (1 new quirk, see below). Two live pushes this pass (R12 wiring fix, then the customFunction body fix), both landed clean: commits `sq3rMjKcokC7coIRauqc` and `tLdpXVtkuqCJ1JvThSzo`.

### §23: Full re-analysis pass — unguarded date parsing fixed (silent wrong data on the backend), BasicInfoRegistration's missing field-validation gate fixed (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** "conduct a thorough re-analysis... identify any issues... incorrectly implemented code... correct them completely," approached "with the mindset of a senior developer." Direct continuation of §22's own lesson (check the CALLED code's actual behavior, not just that it's wired) — applied deliberately broader this time.

**Real finding #1 — unguarded date parsing, worse than a UX gap.** Every date field built this session (`ResDateField` in `reservation_form.dart`, `birthDate` in `BasicInfoRegistration`, `dob` in `ConnectOnboarding`) is a free-text `TextField` with only a hint (`例: 2026-09-01`) — no DatePicker WIDGET exists in this DSL (confirmed by reading `widgets.dart` directly), only a `DatePicker` ACTION (open-a-native-picker-on-tap, output as `dateTime`). Weighed a full DatePicker migration against targeted validation: migration would touch 3 pages' downstream date-consuming logic (`callCreateReservation`'s ISO construction, `callSubmitConnectOnboarding`'s year/month/day split, the backend's own parsing) with real risk of a NEW regression under time pressure, for a benefit (native picker UX) that targeted validation achieves the safety-critical part of anyway — chose the smaller, safer fix. Read the ACTUAL backend consumption of these fields (not assumed): `reservations.ts`'s `createReservation` does `Timestamp.fromDate(new Date(date))` with zero validation; `auth.ts`'s `completeOnboarding` does `const birthDate = new Date(birth_date); const age = now.getFullYear() - birthDate.getFullYear();` — **a malformed/empty `birth_date` makes `age` become `NaN`, and EVERY comparison in the `if (age < 20) ... else` bucket chain is false for NaN, silently falling through to the LAST branch (`ageGroup = "60歳以上"`) with no error thrown at all** — a confirmed silent-wrong-data bug, not just a missing-validation gap. Fixed by adding a real calendar-validity check (`_isValidIsoDate`: regex format + `DateTime(y,mo,d)` round-trip equality, catching both malformed strings AND syntactically-plausible-but-calendar-invalid ones like "2026-13-45" or "2026-02-30", which `DateTime.tryParse` alone does NOT reject — verified empirically: `DateTime.tryParse('2026-13-45')` silently rolls over to `2027-02-14` rather than returning null) into `checkReservationFieldsComplete` and `checkConnectFieldsComplete` (both via `updateCustomAction`, originals frozen same push, same discipline as every other payload-changing update in this file). Deliberately did NOT add a "must be a future date" or age-minimum business rule alongside this — no spec backing found for either, and inventing one would violate this project's own "don't guess product rules" discipline; this fixes the confirmed TECHNICAL defect only.

**Real finding #2 — BasicInfoRegistration had no field-completeness check at all.** Re-read its frozen `ensurePage` body with fresh eyes and confirmed the submit button's ENTIRE gate was `If(State('consentAgreed'), then: [callCompleteOnboarding, ...], orElse: [...])` — every other field (`accountType`, `gender`, `birthDate`, `prefecture`, `city`) could be empty and the API call would still fire. Added `checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete` (new custom action) validating all required fields non-empty AND `birthDate` calendar-valid AND consent given, combined into ONE gate rather than 2 separately-nested `If`s — a 3rd nesting level (fields → consent → api-result) is the EXACT shape that already failed compilation once this session (`KycSubmitButton`'s own 3-level attempt, `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s own documented finding) — collapsing field-completeness and consent into one combined check keeps this at the proven-safe 2-level shape (`checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete` wrapping the api-result check), matching every other submit-button chain in this file. Patched `Button_npmkhs0n` (SubmitBasicInfoButton) via `page.ensureActions` (safe — one isolated widget's own trigger, wholesale replacement is this file's own established-safe pattern for that case).

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean both times; `flutterflow ai test` passed; both pushes landed clean on the first attempt (`compileDslApp` passed immediately) — commits `zWQdQLAXdkYR3AFFidse` (date validation) then `dA6EPWUkh0A8R5CXnX94` (bundled with §24 below in the same push, see there). **Regenerated code read directly for both**: `check_reservation_fields_complete.dart`/`check_connect_fields_complete.dart` show the real `_isValidIsoDate` logic (not just the wiring); `check_basic_info_fields_complete.dart` exists and is genuinely called before `callCompleteOnboarding` in the regenerated `basic_info_registration_widget.dart` (`_model.basicInfoValidResult = await actions.checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete(...); if (_model.basicInfoValidResult!) { ... callCompleteOnboarding ... }`).

**Explicitly not done / still open:** no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual validation UX (same standing caveat throughout this session); phone/postal-code/bank-code fields on `ConnectOnboarding` are STILL free text with no format validation — lower priority than the date fields since a malformed value there fails at the Stripe API layer (a real, well-validated external API returning a clear error) rather than silently producing wrong data the way `new Date()` did, but flagged as a real, smaller, unaddressed gap of the same general class; a genuinely pre-existing, separate inconsistency was ALSO found (not fixed, out of this session's touched-pages scope): `calculateExtensionPrice` (native, pre-existing) checks for `'第3部'`/`'第4部'` but `config.ts`'s own `night_time_slots` default is `["3部","4部"]` (no "第" prefix) — these would never match each other, a pre-existing bug in `extension_payment.dart`'s pricing flow that predates this session and wasn't touched by any UI built this session.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (2 `updateCustomAction` calls + 2 frozen originals + 1 new `checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete` action + 1 new `editPage` block), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §24: Full re-analysis pass, continued — ALL SIX `reservation_form.dart` dropdowns had never contained real content (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continuation of §23's same re-analysis pass — kept looking past the date-parsing fix rather than stopping at the first finding.

**Found while spot-checking whether the group-size dropdown's values would actually parse as integers** (a natural next question after finding the date-parsing gap — the same "does the actual runtime data make sense" scrutiny). Read `reservation_form_widget.dart`'s generated `options: [...]` list directly and found **every one of the page's 6 dropdowns** still had FlutterFlow's own generic scaffold placeholder: literally `['Option 1', 'Option 2', 'Option 3']`. This session's earlier work (§12) wired the `ON_FORM_WIDGET_SELECTED → SetState` mechanism correctly for 5 of them, but never verified the underlying OPTION CONTENT — the wiring was right, the data was fake, for the entire life of this page.

**Confirmed real, silent, differently-shaped consequences per field** (not a uniform bug — three genuinely different failure modes):
- **時間帯 (time_slot)**: reached `createReservation` as `"Option 1"`/`"2"`/`"3"` — compared against `config.night_time_slots` server-side, could never match anything, silently defeating whatever night-slot-specific logic depends on it.
- **交流時間 (duration)**: `callCreateReservation`'s own `RegExp(r'\d+').firstMatch(durationLabel)` extraction DOES find a digit in "Option 1"/"2"/"3" — silently producing `duration_minutes: 1`/`2`/`3` (**minutes**, not a real interaction length) instead of failing loudly. The single most dangerous of the three, since it's a plausible-looking small integer, not an obviously-wrong value.
- **交流開始時間 (start_time)**: fed directly into `'${date}T${startTime}:00'` — "Option 1" is not a valid time component, producing a malformed ISO datetime sent straight into the SAME unguarded `new Date(...)` parse §23 already found unsafe.
- **グループお誘い希望人数 (group_size)**: `int.tryParse('Option 1')` returns `null` → `groupSize` always fell back to its `?? 0` default, meaning **every reservation's `group_size` has been `0` regardless of what the guest actually selected**, for the entire life of this feature.
- **お誘い目的 (purpose)**: lower severity — feeds into free-text `details` via string interpolation, not parsed as a number/date, so "Option 1" was meaningless content but not silently-corrupting the way the other four were.

**Fixed by reconstructing all 6 via `ensureReplaced`** with real options AND (for the 5 wired ones) the `value:`/`onChanged:` binding folded directly into the new widget construction — superseding, not adding to, the pre-existing separate `page.ensureActions(...)` calls (removed, now redundant). Same proven pattern as the review-rating dropdown (`reservation_detail.dart`, earlier this session).

**Option-value confidence, disclosed per field rather than presented as uniformly verified — a real judgment call, not guessed silently:**
- **時間帯 → `1部/2部/3部/4部`**: CONFIRMED against `config.ts`'s own `night_time_slots` naming (not invented — matches the actual server-side comparison target, modulo the separately-flagged `calculateExtensionPrice`/`config.ts` "第"-prefix inconsistency noted in §23).
- **グループお誘い希望人数 → `1`-`10`**: CONFIRMED against `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §3.6 item 8's explicit "(1–10, dropdown)" spec text.
- **交流時間 (`60分`/`90分`/.../`180分`), 交流開始時間 (`18:00`-`22:00`), お誘い目的 (`食事`/`観光`/`イベント同行`/`飲み会`/`その他`)**: NO explicit option list found anywhere in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — used reasonable, format-valid interim values (30-min increments matching the already-confirmed `ratePerThirtyMin` billing granularity; evening hours matching the night-slot business context AND this page's own pre-existing state defaults, e.g. `resStartTime.withDefault('19:00')` already implied evening operation before this fix). **Explicitly flagged as interim, not final business-approved copy** — fixing structural validity (a real parseable time/duration, matching the technical bug class) was mandatory regardless of exact wording; the exact wording is a real, disclosed, still-open product question.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean on the first attempt (commit `dA6EPWUkh0A8R5CXnX94`, bundled with §23's own field-validation fixes in the same push). **Regenerated `reservation_form_widget.dart` read directly**, confirming all 6 `options: [...]` lists now show the real values (not re-trusted from the DSL source alone) — spot-verified the duration regex against the new real values too (`RegExp(r'\d+').firstMatch('60分')` → `60`, correctly parseable now that the input is real).

**Explicitly not done / still open:** the interim-vs-confirmed distinction above for 3 of 6 dropdowns' exact option wording; no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual dropdown UI; a systematic sweep for this SAME "never-populated placeholder options" pattern across OTHER pages' dropdowns was NOT done this pass (this was found by chance while investigating the date-parsing fix, not via an exhaustive check) — **worth treating as its own follow-up sweep**, the same "don't assume this was the only instance" discipline already applied to the `ensureReplaced`-freezing pattern (§15/§18) and the `MacchaChats` preflight pattern (§20).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (6 `ensureReplaced` calls replacing 5 `ensureActions` + fixing 1 previously-unwired dropdown's display), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Follow-up sweep, same pass:** grepped the ENTIRE `generated_code/` tree for the literal placeholder string `'Option 1'` to check whether this bug was isolated to `reservation_form.dart` or systemic. Found exactly one other file: `profile_edit/profile_edit_widget.dart` (2 of its dropdowns) — but confirmed this page has ZERO save/submit wiring of any kind (no `httpsCallable` anywhere in the file), matching `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 3 checklist ("Wire `profile_edit.dart`'s save action" `[MISSING]`, not started). Unlike `reservation_form.dart` (wired, actively consuming the broken values), these 2 dropdowns are genuinely dormant — nothing reads them yet, so fixing their labels now would be cosmetic-only with zero functional effect, and doing the ACTUAL fix (wiring the save action) is a full unstarted Phase 3 feature, not a "correct what's broken" task. Left alone, disclosed rather than silently skipped or overreached into. Also checked every OTHER page this session built dropdowns for directly via the DSL (`BasicInfoRegistration`'s account-type/gender/staff-type, `ReservationDetail`'s review-rating) — all confirmed to already have real, explicitly-specified option values (never touched FlutterFlow's placeholder scaffold, since these were constructed via `Dropdown(options: [...])` from scratch, not wired onto a pre-existing native dropdown) — the placeholder-content bug class only affects NATIVE dropdowns wired without re-checking their existing content, and no other instance of that specific combination was found.

### §25: R19 (shrinkWrap) fixed — the last remaining wiring-review warning in the whole project (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** continuation of the same re-analysis pass; revisited a previously-deferred item now that raw-proto field mutation had been proven safe twice already this session (`clearDatabaseRequest()` in §19).

**Fixed:** `ReservationListPage`'s `ListView_kardnjc6` had `shrinkWrapValue: true` (the native scaffold's own default), violating this project's own Design & Quality Rules ("avoid `shrinkWrap: true` on dynamic ListView") — unnecessary here since this ListView is the Scaffold's direct body, not nested inside another scrollable. Confirmed the exact proto accessor chain by reading the schema directly rather than guessing (`FFNode.props` → `FFWidgetProperties.listView` → `FFListView.shrinkWrapValue`, a plain `FFBooleanValue`) — same class of surgical field-set as `clearDatabaseRequest()`, safe to leave live/rerun. `page.mutateNode(page.findByKey('ListView_kardnjc6'), (node) { node.props.listView.shrinkWrapValue = FFBooleanValue(inputValue: false); })`.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean on the first attempt (commit `rKJSbwPuqsDm1ciYUB47`); confirmed `shrinkWrap` no longer appears at all in the regenerated `reservation_list_page_widget.dart` (codegen omits the param when false, matching Flutter's own default). **Ran a final full-project no-op wiring review** (the same `NO_OP`-triggering trick from §21) — confirmed via the trace that R19 no longer appears, and critically, **zero wiring-review warnings of any kind remain anywhere in the project** — R10 (§19), R11/R13 (§21, always clean), R12 (§22), and now R19 are all resolved; only generic native-compiler advisories remain (iOS min version, auth-navigation, disabled-button, tappable-area-size — none touched by any work this session, pre-existing and out of scope).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (1 `mutateNode` call), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §26: `KycReviewPage` built — IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md's own explicit [NEXT] item (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** "proceed to the next step according to the plan" — confirmed with the user which of several plausible "next steps" (the explicit `[NEXT]`-tagged admin-KYC item vs. remaining Phase 4 items vs. starting Phase 5) they meant before committing effort; they picked the explicitly-tagged item.

**Real finding made BEFORE writing any DSL, not after:** the plan's own note ("backend callable already exists, only the UI trigger is missing") was incomplete. `adminApproveKYC` (the mutation) exists, but there was no client-safe way to fetch the LIST of pending-review users at all — `firestore.rules`' `users` collection rule is strictly owner-only (`allow read: if request.auth.uid == document`, no admin exception whatsoever), so a direct client query for `kyc_status == 'submitted'` across all users would be rejected for every caller, admin included. Checked for an existing fix before assuming a new backend function was needed: `adminGetUsers` (already deployed, used elsewhere in `admin.ts`) already supports a `kyc_status` filter and already returns full user documents — no new Cloud Function required, just call the existing one with the right filter. This also avoided loosening `firestore.rules` for a broader admin-read exception, staying consistent with this codebase's own established pattern (every admin list/read goes through a callable, confirmed by grepping `admin.ts` for the `adminGet*` family — never a relaxed client-facing rule).

**Built:** `checkIsAdminUser` (client-side admin check, UX-only — both callables independently enforce the real check server-side), `callAdminGetPendingKyc` (wraps `adminGetUsers({kyc_status: 'submitted'})`, encodes each result as a delimited string — same pattern as `fetchMyReservations`, `users` being a bare JSON response rather than a typed FirestoreQuery result means `ItemRef['field']` isn't available here either), `callAdminApproveKyc` (wraps `adminApproveKYC`), and 5 extractor custom functions. **Every customFunction body written BODY-ONLY from the start this time** — the §22 lesson applied deliberately rather than rediscovered. `KycReviewPage` (route `/kyc-review`): shows an admin-check-gated list, each row displaying the applicant's doc/selfie images (`CachedNetworkImage`, confirmed via regenerated code) with approve/reject buttons that call the mutation and refetch the list on success — the "mutate then refetch-and-recache, never local list-splicing" pattern `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own admin cross-cutting-patterns section explicitly calls for.

**Real mistake made and caught in the SAME pass, not a later one:** left the new page's `ensurePage(...)` call live/uncommented after it landed — the EXACT `ensurePage`-body-inert failure mode already documented at length earlier this session (§9/§10/§14/§15/§18) — caught immediately via a follow-up `flutterflow ai validate` (the NO_OP-triggering full-project-review trick from §21, now a standing verification habit) failing with `Use ff.Pages.kycReviewPage.state.isAdminUser instead of State(...)`. Fixed in the same pass: froze the `ensurePage` call, dropped the now-unused `CustomFunctionHandle`-equivalent captures on the 5 extractor functions (the exact same cleanup shape as §18). **Genuinely re-made a mistake this session had already written up as a systemic lesson** — worth being honest about rather than glossing over: documenting a quirk doesn't automatically prevent repeating it under the pace of building a large new page; the actual safeguard that caught it was the HABIT of re-validating after every push, not memory of the rule.

**Verification, thorough given the mistake above:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; two pushes (the page itself, then the freeze fix), both landed clean — commits `E6MqezURCGa8VPTvbe7J` then `wfs7sRvt0CjT1Y1GeJTH`. **Regenerated code read directly, not trusted from the DSL source**: confirmed all 5 new custom functions compiled as clean single-layer functions (re-checked the exact §22 bug class specifically, given how recently it was found); confirmed `callAdminApproveKyc`/`callAdminGetPendingKyc`'s bodies match intent exactly; confirmed the approve/reject buttons' full `onPressed` chains in `kyc_review_page_widget.dart` (call → conditional refetch → state update → Snackbar) match the design precisely; confirmed image widgets bind to the URL-extraction functions via `CachedNetworkImage`. **Final full-project no-op wiring review**: `Dry run succeeded`, confirmed `NO_OP` (full-project mode) fired, and zero wiring-review warnings of any kind remain — the same clean state achieved in §25, undisturbed by this addition.

**Explicitly not done / still open, disclosed rather than silently narrowed:** no admin-panel shell/navigation exists — this page is reachable only by direct route, matching "minimal slice" scope (the full Phase 12 admin IA is unstarted); no reject-reason input UI (uses the backend's own sensible default message); no pagination (`adminGetUsers` defaults to `limit: 50`, fine for an MVP review queue, would need a real "load more"/offset param for scale); no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual approve/reject flow (same standing caveat throughout this session — no FlutterFlow Desktop session paired).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (3 custom actions, 5 custom functions, 1 new page + its later freeze), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 2's `[NEXT]` item marked `[DONE]`), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum — first re-review pass (2026-08-10):** found a real crash bug before it reached anyone: `KycReviewPage`'s `ListView` sits inside a `Column` (not the Scaffold's direct body, unlike `ReservationListPage`), so the own code comment claiming shrinkWrap was unneeded was wrong about this page's actual structure — a bare `Column` gives children unbounded main-axis height, which a non-shrinkWrapped `ListView` cannot render under (Flutter's "vertical viewport was given unbounded height" assertion). Fixed via `node.props.listView.shrinkWrapValue = FFBooleanValue(inputValue: true)` (same proto field as §25, opposite value — correct in this genuinely different structural context, not a contradiction of that fix). Weighed and accepted the R19 performance trade-off explicitly (bounded 50-item admin-only queue, not a customer-facing infinite list). Verified via regenerated code and a final no-op full-project wiring review. Commit `OkIMKAZBIHDzck2xAwVW`.

**Addendum — second re-review pass (2026-08-10):** asked to review again; found a SECOND, more serious consequence of the same shrinkWrap fix that the first pass only partially reasoned through — `shrinkWrap: true` sizes the ListView to its full content height, but nothing in this page's ancestor chain (`Scaffold > SafeArea > Padding > Column`, confirmed by reading the regenerated widget directly) provides a scrolling container. With more than a screen's worth of pending KYC cards, the page would overflow with **no way to reach the rest** — not a crash this time, a real usability dead-end. Fixed with the same low-risk single-property-mutation technique (avoiding any need to reconstruct the item template): `Column`'s DSL-level `scrollable:` parameter (already used natively elsewhere in this file, e.g. `reservation_form.dart`) compiles to a plain, non-deprecated `props.column.scrollable` bool — set directly on the page's outer body Column via `mutateNode`. Verified: regenerated code now shows the whole Column wrapped in `SingleChildScrollView`; also checked (not just assumed) whether the shrink-wrapped inner ListView's own scroll physics could conflict with the new outer scroll view — confirmed this is FlutterFlow's own standard, unprompted codegen pattern (`shrinkWrap: true` + `primary: false`, no explicit `physics:` override needed) for this exact nesting shape, not something requiring a further fix. Final full-project wiring review clean (same single accepted R19 warning, nothing new). Commit `OZuTaQaBwkiks3f7MZ8R`.

**Honest pattern across both addenda, worth naming directly:** two consecutive re-review passes each found a real bug the PRIOR pass's own stated reasoning had missed — first pass fixed the crash but only evaluated shrinkWrap's performance cost, not its "no scroll ancestor" consequence; the underlying lesson (§22's "verify the called code's actual behavior, not just that it's wired," extended here to "verify a layout fix's full consequences, not just the one symptom it was aimed at") clearly benefits from a genuine second and third look rather than stopping at the first plausible-looking fix, especially for anything involving nested scrolling/sizing behavior, which has been a recurring source of subtle bugs across this entire session (`ReservationListPage`'s own shrinkWrap in §15/§25 being the first instance).

### §27: Status- and role-driven button visibility on `reservation_detail.dart` (2026-08-10)

**What was asked:** "proceed to the next step" — asked which specific step twice across two turns with no answer, so proceeded with a clearly-stated default: Phase 4's remaining status-driven-visibility item, chosen over starting Phase 5 as the smaller, lower-risk continuation of already-built work.

**Read every relevant backend precondition before designing anything**, rather than assuming a uniform "one rule fits all 6 buttons" shape: `respondToReservation` explicitly rejects unless `status in {authorized, cast_pending}`, cast-only; `reportCompletion` explicitly rejects unless `status == in_progress`, cast-only; `confirmMeetup` and `submitReview` have **no explicit status precondition in the backend at all** (confirmed by reading both functions in full) — gated in the UI anyway to their semantically correct statuses (`confirmed`, `review_pending`), since showing "合流報告" during `completed` would be confusing regardless of backend leniency, even though a stray tap wouldn't corrupt data; `cancelPayment` has no rejecting precondition either (fee percentage varies by status instead) — gated to exclude only the 3 genuinely-terminal statuses.

**Found and closed a second gap the task's own "status-driven" framing didn't name**, while designing the fix: none of the 6 buttons were gated by ROLE either — a guest viewing their own sent request currently saw "承認する"/"断る" (cast-only actions per the backend) and would hit a clean-but-confusing permission-denied error on tap. Fixed together as one coherent per-button rule (status AND role), not status-only, since shipping status-only would leave an equally-real, freshly-noticed gap unaddressed.

**Built:** one shared `fetchReservationVisibility` custom action (single Firestore read on page load, not 6 redundant ones) encoding `status|||isGuest|||isCast` as a delimited string (same established pattern as `fetchMyReservations`/`KycReviewPage`); 5 small customFunctions (body-only from the start — the §22 lesson applied deliberately) each checking one button's (or button-pair's) condition; `respondToReservation`'s approve/decline share one function since they're literally the same backend precondition. Reconstructed all 6 native buttons via `ensureReplaced` (confirmed necessary, not just convenient: `EditWidgetPatch.visible()` only accepts a static `bool` per its own SDK source — no typed patch path exists for a dynamic per-status expression), preserving each button's exact existing onTap chain byte-for-byte and reproducing its native styling (`Colors.hex(...)` matching the exact ARGB values read from the regenerated widget, e.g. `0xFF4461FD` for approve) to minimize visual drift — same "same semantic, different visual" trade-off already accepted elsewhere this session.

**Real bug caught and fixed BEFORE pushing, not after:** replacing all 6 buttons via `ensureReplaced` meant the ORIGINAL `page.ensureActions(page.findByKey('Button_i8a0ibu2'), ...)`-style calls from §13 (still live, wiring the SAME 6 buttons' onTap chains) would become orphaned — `ensureReplaced` assigns each button a fresh key, so those earlier calls would find nothing on the very next push. This is the EXACT failure mode `project_rules.md` already documents at length ("Replacing an existing action chain WHOLESALE... can silently orphan any EARLIER raw-patch block"). Caught by deliberately checking for this BEFORE running the push (not discovered via a failed push) — froze all 6 of the superseded §13 `ensureActions` calls in the SAME push that introduced their replacements.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean on the first attempt (commit `ay5H9sEcZnM3obcbM8uS`). **Regenerated code read directly, not trusted from the DSL source**: confirmed all 5 new customFunctions compiled single-layer (re-checked the §22 bug class specifically again); confirmed the onLoad fetch wires correctly (`_model.resVisibilityData = await ... fetchReservationVisibility(...)`); confirmed each button is now genuinely conditionally BUILT, not just hidden (`if (functions.canRespondToReservation(_model.resVisibilityData) ?? true) FFButtonWidget(...)`); confirmed the `?? true` null-coalescing fallback (FlutterFlow's own defensive codegen for a nullable bool) is never actually reachable in practice — traced the customFunction's own logic for an empty/uninitialized `resVisibilityData` (before onLoad completes) and confirmed it returns a definite `false`, not `null`, so buttons correctly stay hidden during the brief pre-load window rather than accidentally defaulting to shown; confirmed the reconstructed "承認する" button's color/width/height/borderRadius match the original native button exactly (`0xFF4461FD`, 150×40, radius 8). **Final full-project no-op wiring review**: clean, only the same already-accepted `KycReviewPage` R19 warning remains, nothing new introduced by this change.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** per-button independent tracking of "have I (this specific user) already confirmed meetup" — `confirmMeetup`'s own status-based gate (`confirmed`) shows the button through the whole confirmed phase rather than hiding it the instant THIS user's own side is done (a real, disclosed refinement opportunity, not a correctness bug, since re-tapping just re-sets an already-true field); no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual visibility behavior across different statuses/roles (same standing caveat throughout this session).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (1 custom action, 5 custom functions, 1 state field, 1 new ON_INIT_STATE trigger, 6 `ensureReplaced` button reconstructions, 6 superseded `ensureActions` calls frozen), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 4 status), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum — re-review pass (2026-08-10):** asked (a third time, verbatim) to review before proceeding further; treated this as a standing directive rather than re-asking about scope. Found two more real issues by reading the ACTUAL row/container structure directly (`lib/flutterflow_project/pages/reservation_detail.dart`) instead of trusting the button-level design's own stated reasoning:
1. **4 of the 6 button rows contain ONLY that one button** (`Row_cr8et390`/meetup, `Row_qqj87c5s`/completion, `Row_dw9z46os`/review, `Row_ehfeqy1x`/cancel — each a single-child Row). Gating `visible:` on the BUTTON alone (the first-generation design) left an EMPTY Row still occupying its own slot in the surrounding Column, still picking up that Column's own inter-child spacing — a real, confirmed visible-gap cosmetic bug for every status combination where a button is hidden, not a hypothetical edge case (true for MOST status values, since usually only 1-2 of the 6 conditions are true at once).
2. **`Row_58590jz8` (ReviewInputRow — the rating Dropdown + comment TextField) was never gated by anything at all**, missed entirely by the first pass, which only gated the SubmitReviewButton below it. A cast, or a guest at the wrong status, would see and could fill in a review form they can't actually submit — the input and its own submit action were visible on different, disconnected conditions (input: always; button: `canSubmitReservationReview`).

**Fixed both together**: moved `visible:` from each button up to its WRAPPING ROW (removing the now-redundant per-button `visible:`), reconstructing all 6 rows via `ensureReplaced` — approve/decline share one row and one condition (previously duplicated across 2 buttons), the other 4 each get their own row-level gate. Added the SAME `canSubmitReservationReview` condition to `ReviewInputRow` as its own submit button, so the rating form and its submit action now show/hide as one coherent unit, reconstructed with the exact content already proven correct in §13 (preserved verbatim in that section's own frozen comment, not re-derived from memory).

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean on the first attempt (commit `bGUuxAIVPmGWOTOgJCGr`). **Regenerated code read directly**: confirmed exactly 6 `if (functions.can...)` conditionals now exist (down from more scattered button-level ones), each correctly wrapping the ENTIRE `Row(...)` — spot-checked `ReviewInputRow` (now `if (functions.canSubmitReservationReview(...) ?? true) Row(children: [Column([Text('評価'), Dropdown(...), TextField(...)])])`) and `ConfirmMeetupRow` (whole row conditional, not just its button) directly in the generated widget file. Final full-project no-op wiring review clean, same single already-accepted R19 warning, nothing new.

**Pattern holding across three consecutive re-review passes now** (this entry, plus both `KycReviewPage` addenda above): each pass has found something real that the PRIOR pass's own reasoning missed, always by going back to actual generated/typed-SDK structure rather than re-trusting the previous design's stated rationale. Treating "review again" as a standing instruction rather than a one-time gate has been directly productive, not just cautious ritual — three real bugs across two features would have shipped without it.

**Addendum — fourth re-review pass (2026-08-10), including a self-caught mistake made WITHIN the pass itself:** the same review instruction repeated a fourth time verbatim; continued treating it as standing. Found one more real, minor discrepancy: the 6 reconstructed buttons (§27's row-visibility fix) were missing the ORIGINAL native buttons' `padding: EdgeInsetsDirectional.fromSTEB(16.0, 0.0, 16.0, 0.0)` (confirmed via the regenerated widget — the first-generation reconstruction had defaulted to `0.0` on all sides). Attempted a fix (`Button(..., padding: EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 16))`), pushed it, then verified the regenerated output as usual — and the verification itself caught that the "fix" was wrong: `Button.padding` compiles to `props.padding`, the widget NODE's own EXTERNAL wrapper padding (confirmed by reading `UI.button`'s implementation directly in `ui.dart`), not `FFButtonOptions`' internal content padding the original used — there is no DSL-level way to set a `Button`'s internal content padding at all. The "fix" therefore didn't replicate the original; it wrapped each button in an external `Padding`, growing its occupied footprint from 150px to 182px — for the approve/decline row (the only row with 2 buttons sharing `spaceEvenly`), that's 364px combined, a real tightness risk on narrower screens the original design never had. **Reverted rather than compounded** — confirmed via a second regenerated-code read that the external `Padding` wrapper is gone and `FFButtonWidget` is back to a direct Row child, matching the pre-"fix" (already-pushed, already-working) state. Final full-project wiring review clean, same single already-accepted R19 warning.

**Worth stating plainly**: this is the first instance this pass-and-verify discipline caught the REVIEWER'S OWN mistake rather than a pre-existing one — the same "verify the actual generated effect, don't trust the fix's own stated intent" principle applied to work produced only minutes earlier in the same turn. Confirms the verification step matters independent of whose reasoning produced the thing being verified.

**Addendum (2026-08-10, review pass): unbounded-width overflow risk in the date/location rows.** Re-reviewing this push before moving on: both value `Text` widgets had no `maxLines`/`overflow`, and sat as bare (non-`Expanded`) children of a `mainAxisAlignment: spaceBetween` Row — a violation of this project's own Design & Quality Rule, most consequential for `location`, which is unrestricted guest-entered free text from `reservation_form.dart`'s meeting-address field (§12) with no length limit anywhere in the chain. Same failure class already found and fixed in `KycReviewPage` (§26 addenda), via a different structural path (unbounded Row child vs. unbounded Column child). `maxLines`/`overflow` alone would not have been sufficient without also fixing layout: a bare `Text` in a `Row` still gets unbounded main-axis width, so `TextOverflow.ellipsis` has nothing to truncate against until the widget has a bounded width. Fixed by reconstructing each ROW (not just the value `Text`) via `ensureReplaced`, wrapping the value in `Expanded` + `maxLines: 1` + `overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis` + `textAlign: TextAlign.end` — `Expanded` also replaces the need for `spaceBetween`, since it already consumes all remaining row width. Verified via regenerated code: both fields show `Expanded(flex: 1, child: Text(..., overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis))`. Pushed clean, commit `5z1ulSXeDS6IjskKuqHC`.

**Addendum (2026-08-10, next review pass): stale `ensureReplaced` calls left live, caused real key-churn.** The row-level fix above landed correctly, but the ORIGINAL 3 individual-widget `ensureReplaced` calls from this section's own first push (`Button_2j1voyws` status, `Text_ij4v2tka` date, `Text_vvkdealn` location) were never frozen after landing — a violation of this file's own established discipline (every other `ensureReplaced` call elsewhere is frozen immediately once confirmed landed). Left live, all 3 re-executed on the very next push (the row-level fix above) and reassigned a FRESH key to the status button purely as a side effect (confirmed: `Button_2j1voyws` → `Button_m9nhrlbw` with no design change intended), while the date/location calls became doubly dead — superseded by the row-level rebuild that discards their output anyway. Caught not by inspection but by the trace-level no-op check itself: a `flutterflow ai validate` re-run with zero intended changes showed `Change Summary: Modified pages: 1` instead of the expected `[info] NO_OP: ...` full-project confirmation — the first time this session's own standard "re-validate with no changes, expect NO_OP" step actually caught something instead of just confirming cleanliness. Fixed by freezing all 5 `ensureReplaced` calls (3 individual-widget + 2 row-level) and dropping the now-unused `CustomFunctionHandle` local-variable captures for the 3 customFunctions they referenced (same pattern as the §26 `ensurePage` freeze). Re-validated: `NO_OP` confirmation restored, same single already-accepted R19 warning, nothing new. Pushed the freeze itself (a true no-op against the live proto) as commit `qCqShTiIGeCBTvNShahw`. **Lesson reinforced:** the "freeze immediately once landed" rule for `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` has no exceptions, including calls that feel throwaway or destined to be superseded within the same session — an unfrozen call doesn't just risk breaking on some future unrelated change, it can silently reassign keys and produce spurious diffs on every single subsequent push indefinitely, discoverable only by the no-op check itself, not by reading the resulting structure (which looks correct either way).

**Addendum (2026-08-10, mandatory full-file sweep):** `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` already documents this exact failure mode as SYSTEMIC (its own §266, discovered originally in `icoccha-admin-dashboard`) and explicitly mandates a full-file sweep — not just fixing the one instance that broke — whenever this bug is caught even once. Ran that sweep against `dsl/edit.dart` (a small script classifying every `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` occurrence as active vs. already-frozen by checking whether its own line starts with `//`) and found **11 more active instances**, confirmed-landed but never frozen: all 6 `reservation_form.dart` dropdown reconstructions (§24, commit `dA6EPWUkh0A8R5CXnX94` — time-slot/duration/start-time/group-size/purpose/extension-plan) and all 6 `reservation_detail.dart` action-row reconstructions (§27's row-level addendum, commit `bGUuxAIVPmGWOTOgJCGr` — approve/decline, meetup, completion, review-input, submit-review, cancel). Verified each against live typed-SDK/generated-code state before freezing (all confirmed correctly landed, not mid-flight). Froze all 11 (same comment-out convention as every other freeze in this file) and dropped 5 more now-unused `CustomFunctionHandle` captures (`canRespondFn`/`canConfirmMeetupFn`/`canReportCompletionFn`/`canSubmitReviewFn`/`canCancelReservationFn`) that were only referenced inside the now-frozen rows. Re-ran the sweep script after freezing: **0 active occurrences remain out of 23 total** in the file — this is now a checkable invariant, not just a one-off cleanup. `dart analyze` clean, `flutterflow ai test` passed, re-validated to a true `NO_OP` before pushing the freeze itself (a genuine no-op against the live proto) as commit `Wo0UEtHEpKQ9izd3sUz8`. Spot-verified against regenerated code post-push that both pages' structure is unaffected (dropdown options and row visibility conditionals still present exactly as before). **This is the first time this specific systemic-sweep rule has fired inside this workspace** (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`) rather than just in the admin dashboard where it was originally written up — confirms the rule travels correctly across sibling projects sharing the same DSL/SDK, not just the codebase it was discovered in.

### §29: Phase 3 (Discovery & profile core), first slice — real Home ranking query + GPS fallback (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** with Phase 4 fully closed out and verified, chose Phase 3 over Phase 5 (Stripe Connect) as the next phase — confirmed with the user first, since it's a large, multi-page body of work with real design forks ahead, not a simple continuation. Started with the Home ranking query since it's the item other plan sections (Phase 4's remaining bulk-send, Phase 5's `account.updated` webhook note) explicitly reference as a dependency.

**Real design fork found and resolved before writing anything, not decided silently:** HomePage's cast-browsing section was a native `PageView` of 5 hardcoded placeholder cards (`https://picsum.photos/seed/.../600`, confirmed via `home_page_widget.dart`), not a scrollable list. Checked this DSL's own `PageView`/`Carousel` constructors against `ListView`/`GridView` in `widgets.dart` directly: only the latter two accept a `source:`/`itemBuilder:` pair for dynamic binding — `PageView`/`Carousel` only take a static `children:` list, no dynamic-binding path exists at all. There is therefore no way to keep the swipe-carousel shape and make it data-driven in this DSL. Confirmed the 2 per-card icons were pure `PageViewController` navigation arrows (`animateToPage`/`nextPage`), not like/favorite buttons, so nothing functional was actually lost in converting to a `GridView` — only the swipe interaction model itself, which was flagged to the user as a real (if necessary) UX change before proceeding, not silently swapped.

**GPS-fallback design choice, disclosed as interim rather than silently narrowed:** the plan calls for falling back to "the representative lat/lng of the guest's registered residential area," but `activityPrefecture` (§9) is a free-text `TextField`, not a dropdown bound to a fixed list — the real "service-area master data" Phase 3 item (admin-editable prefecture table) doesn't exist yet. Interim choice: substring-match the free-text value against a small static table of the 10 launch prefectures' representative (prefectural-office) coordinates, falling back to Tokyo if nothing matches. Replace this table with a real lookup once the service-area master collection is built (separate, still-open Phase 3 item).

**Built:** `fetchDiscoveryCasts` custom action — tries device GPS via `geolocator` (permission check/request, 5s timeout, any failure falls through silently), else resolves the guest's own prefecture via substring match against the static table; queries `users` where `account_type == 'cast'` AND `approval_status == 'approved'` (limit 100); computes Haversine distance per result (missing `location` → `double.infinity`, sorts last); sorts by (is_online desc, distance asc, last_login_at desc) — the exact §3.3 priority order; returns `|||`-delimited records via the established `List<String>` pattern. 4 body-only extractor customFunctions (id/nickname/photoUrl/isOnline). `geolocator` added as a guarded pub dependency (`findPubDependency`/`addPubDependency`, the documented brownfield idiom from `references/custom_code_pub_package_dsl.dart` — `addPubDependency` throws on collision, unlike `app.pubDependency`'s create-shape no-op). Replaced `Container_z3poslih` (the old carousel's fixed-height 400×150 wrapper, not just the `PageView` inside it) with a `GridView` (2 columns, confirmed to default to `shrinkWrap: true` under the hood by reading `UI.gridView` in `ui.dart` — opposite of `ListView`'s own `false` default — so no extra scrollable-ancestor wrapping was needed, unlike `KycReviewPage`, §26). Each card: image with an online-status dot overlay, nickname, tap → `Navigate(castProfile, castId: ...)` — this also closes the "`cast_profile.dart` has no real upstream entry point" gap flagged in Phase 4's own notes (§16).

**Real, non-obvious SDK bug found and root-caused via isolation testing, not guessed:** `compileDslApp` failed with `Name "X" already in use` / `Action in HomePage has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget` — persisted across THREE different `outputAs` names, proving it wasn't a literal string collision. Isolated via systematic testing (strip the new action, strip just the `SetState`, strip everything down to a pure unmodified reproduction) that the trigger was structural: HomePage's root `ON_INIT_STATE` already had a **prior, already-landed** `page.ensureActions(page.root, ...)` call earlier in this file (the approval-status gate, from earlier this session). Appending a **second**, later `page.ensureActions(page.root, ON_INIT_STATE, ...)` call — even one that faithfully reproduces the original 3 steps plus one new one — fails validation the moment a 4th step is added, regardless of that step's content or name; a pure, unmodified reproduction from a second call site always passed. **Fix: never target the same root+trigger with two separate `ensureActions` calls — there must be exactly one call per trigger, full stop.** Removed the duplicate call and instead modified the ORIGINAL call in place, nesting the new fetch inside the existing `If(...).then:` branch (also more correct: only fetch discovery data when the approval check actually passes, not for a user about to be redirected to `ReviewPending`). Root cause of the underlying validation behavior itself not fully diagnosed (plausibly a name-registration quirk specific to re-targeting an already-materialized trigger from two call sites in one script) — documented as an observed, reproducible pattern rather than a root-caused mechanism, since further digging into SDK internals wasn't warranted once a reliable structural fix was found. Also hit and fixed a plain ordering bug in the same push: the new `discoveryCasts` state field was declared (`state.ensureField`) textually AFTER the block that referenced it in a `SetState`, since DSL scripts execute top-to-bottom — moved the field declaration before its first use.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean once the two bugs above were fixed (commit `0xmcPIsrTvhmqsI55jCz`). **Regenerated code read directly, not trusted from the DSL source**: confirmed `geolocator: ^13.0.1` in `pubspec.yaml`; confirmed `fetchDiscoveryCasts` compiled as a clean single-layer function (no double-signature bug); confirmed all 4 extractor functions compiled clean; confirmed `initState` now calls `fetchDiscoveryCasts` and sets `_model.discoveryCasts` ONLY inside the `if (_model.approvalCheckResult!)` branch, exactly as designed; confirmed the `GridView.builder` in `home_page_widget.dart` matches the design exactly (tap → `pushNamed(CastProfileWidget.routeName, queryParameters: {'castId': ...})`, image + online-dot overlay + nickname); confirmed the pre-existing, unrelated native `GridView` further down the same page (a different section, fixed 400px height, `childAspectRatio: 1.0`) was untouched. Final full-project no-op wiring review: `NO_OP` confirmed, same single already-accepted `KycReviewPage` R19 warning, nothing new. Immediately froze the `ensureReplaced` call and dropped 4 now-unused `CustomFunctionHandle` captures (applying this turn's own freshly-learned lesson right away rather than deferring it) — re-validated to a true `NO_OP` before pushing the freeze as commit `5lCSTHjODqsNX4LxDjHd`. Re-ran the project-wide `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` sweep: 0 active occurrences out of 24 total — invariant still holds.

**Explicitly not done / still open, disclosed rather than silently narrowed:** the prefecture-fallback table is a static, hardcoded interim measure pending the real service-area master collection (a separate Phase 3 item); native Android/iOS location-permission manifest entries (`ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`, `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription`) are outside this DSL's scope and were not added — the action's own try/catch degrades gracefully to the prefecture fallback if the GPS path fails for any reason including a missing manifest entry, so this isn't a correctness bug, but the GPS path itself won't succeed on-device until those are added; no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual grid/GPS behavior (same standing caveat throughout this session).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (1 pub dependency, 1 custom action, 4 custom functions, 1 state field, 1 modified `ensureActions` call, 1 `ensureReplaced` reconstruction + its later freeze), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 3's first checklist item marked done), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): two real defects found by re-scrutinizing the query logic and layout with fresh eyes, not just re-confirming the earlier verification.**

1. **Self-listing bug.** `HomePage` has zero role-based gating anywhere (confirmed via both the typed SDK and generated code — no `account_type` check exists on this page at all), meaning a logged-in **cast** account also lands here, same as a guest. `fetchDiscoveryCasts`'s query (`account_type == 'cast' AND approval_status == 'approved'`) never excluded the CALLER's own uid, so any approved cast browsing Home would see themselves listed in their own discovery grid — a real, always-reproducible bug for that user segment, not a hypothetical edge case. Fixed by hoisting `final uid = currentUserUid;` to the top of the function (previously only read conditionally, inside the GPS-fallback branch) and filtering `snapshot.docs.where((d) => d.id != uid)` before mapping to result rows.
2. **Aspect-ratio mismatch.** The grid card's `childAspectRatio: 0.75` was copied from `media_browser_dsl.dart`'s own reference card, which has a 2-line title+category text block below its image — this card only has 1 line of nickname text. Actual content height (≈130px image + ≈32px text row ≈162px) vs. the ratio-determined cell height at a typical ~173px column width (≈227px) would leave a ~65px blank gap under every card, since the inner `Column` uses `mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min` and doesn't stretch to fill. Not a crash, but a real violation of this project's own Design & Quality visual-quality rule. Fixed to `1.05` (closely matching the actual ≈162px content height at a ~173px column width) via the same raw single-property `mutateNode` technique already used for `ListView.shrinkWrapValue`/`Column.scrollable` earlier this session — confirmed the exact proto field (`props.gridView.childAspectRatioValue`, a plain non-deprecated `FFDoubleValue`) by reading `UI.gridView` in `ui.dart` directly, since `childAspectRatio` has neither a typed `page.update(...)` patch nor a fast-lane op.

**A third issue surfaced while pushing fix #1 — a self-inflicted process lapse, not a new SDK discovery.** Editing fix #1 directly in the ALREADY-CREATED `app.customAction('fetchDiscoveryCasts', ...)` call's `code:` string and re-running failed `compileDslApp` with `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action named "fetchDiscoveryCasts" with a different payload. \`ensure*\` helpers are create-if-missing only.` This momentarily looked like a fresh finding (this session had drifted into assuming `app.customAction`/`app.customFunction` silently update on content changes, since they'd never errored across dozens of reruns) — but checking `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` before writing it up revealed the rule was **already documented there at length, multiple times over** (its own lines ~84, ~100, ~119, ~145–146, ~215: "`app.customAction(...)` left UNCHANGED across many reruns... is empirically confirmed safe... ONLY as long as the payload stays byte-identical"; "Many successful reruns... is NOT evidence it tolerates a changed payload — it only proves the payload hasn't changed yet"). This was a rule I already had access to and simply failed to apply when first writing `fetchDiscoveryCasts`, not a gap in the rules file. Fixed the correct, already-established way: converted to `app.raw((project) { updateCustomAction(project, name: ..., code: ...); })` (the same pattern already proven for `fetchMyReservations` earlier this session), replacing the original `app.customAction(...)` declaration in place rather than leaving both — avoiding the specific landmine line ~119 warns about (an original declaration left live alongside a companion `updateCustomAction` call breaks the NEXT unrelated push). No new rule added to `project_rules.md` for this — re-read the existing entries instead, which already cover this completely; worth internalizing before writing any FUTURE customFunction/customAction that might need editing later, rather than rediscovering the same documented rule a third time.

**Verification of all three fixes:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean once the `updateCustomAction` conversion was applied (commit `uK7jUdSJPy8qU1mkp2Pd`). Regenerated code confirms both the self-exclusion filter (`snapshot.docs.where((d) => d.id != uid)`) and the corrected aspect ratio (`childAspectRatio: 1.05` on `DiscoveryCastGrid`'s `GridView`, line 274 of `home_page_widget.dart` — confirmed the OTHER, unrelated pre-existing native `GridView` further down the same file, line 769, was untouched at its own original `1.0`). Final full-project no-op wiring review: `NO_OP` confirmed, same single already-accepted R19 warning, nothing new. Re-ran the project-wide `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` sweep once more: still 0 active out of 24 total.

**Addendum (2026-08-11, second review pass): one more real gap found — frozen/banned casts were not excluded from the discovery grid.** `users.is_frozen` exists in the schema, defaults to `false` at signup (confirmed in `auth.ts`), and is already a real, used filter elsewhere in the backend (`admin.ts`'s `adminGetUsers`) — but `fetchDiscoveryCasts`'s query never checked it. An admin freezing an abusive/banned cast (an already-built capability) would not actually remove them from guest-facing discovery; guests could still find and request them, defeating the point of freezing. Fixed by adding `&& d.data()['is_frozen'] != true` to the existing client-side self-exclusion filter, deliberately NOT as a Firestore-level `.where('is_frozen', isEqualTo: false)` clause — an equality filter would silently exclude any document where the field happens to be missing entirely (e.g. older seed/placeholder data that predates the field being set at creation) rather than only documents explicitly frozen, which client-side `!= true` avoids (`null`/missing safely reads as "not frozen"). Verified via regenerated code (`.where((d) => d.id != uid && d.data()['is_frozen'] != true)`); pushed clean using the now-corrected `updateCustomAction` path (commit `JiCQtsNZmQiH0XVMKE9O`); final no-op wiring review clean, `ensureReplaced` sweep still 0/24.

**Worth naming directly:** this is the second consecutive review pass to find a real, previously-missed defect in this same feature (self-listing + aspect-ratio in the first pass, frozen-user exclusion in this one) — reinforces the pattern already noted for `reservation_detail.dart` (§27): a feature that "compiles clean and matches its own stated design" is not the same as one that's been checked against every adjacent requirement (role gating, admin moderation state, ban enforcement) a fresh read can still surface.

### §30: Phase 3 — service-area master data, prefecture-level slice (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** continuation of Phase 3 — "service-area master data (admin-editable, 10 prefectures at launch)", the plan item that directly replaces §29's own interim hardcoded prefecture-coordinate table. Scoped down explicitly with the user before building: prefecture-level only this pass, not real municipality data — generating dozens of real Japanese municipality names per prefecture from general knowledge with no live source to verify against would be a genuine risk of shipping wrong product content, the exact thing this project has consistently avoided by flagging gaps rather than guessing.

**Real design correction found before writing any schema:** `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §5 (line 273) already specifies the CONFIRMED shape for this feature — `service_areas` must be an array field on the single `system_config/settings` document (`{name, prefecture, active}`), not a new standalone Firestore collection, which is what a naive reading of "service-area master data" would suggest building. Verified this shape against the real backend directly (`config.ts`'s own `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.service_areas` declaration, `admin.ts`'s `adminGetSystemConfig`/`adminUpdateSystemConfig`/its own `areaActive()` helper matching on `prefecture`) before writing anything, avoiding building a parallel, wrong-shaped data model.

**Backend built:** `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.service_areas` (`config.ts`) seeded with the real 10 launch prefectures (all `active: true`) — no separate seed script or write-back logic needed, since `getSystemConfig()`'s existing `{ ...SYSTEM_DEFAULTS, ...doc.data() }` fallback-merge already surfaces this the moment the live document doesn't explicitly override it. `adminUpdateSystemConfig` (already existed, generic settings-merge) covers the admin-write side with no new function needed. New `getServiceAreas` callable (`auth.ts`) — deliberately NOT admin-gated (unlike `adminGetSystemConfig`), since `system_config` is admin-only under `firestore.rules` and every signed-in user, guest or cast, needs the active-prefecture list during `BasicInfoRegistration`; exposes only the prefecture names, nothing else from `system_config`.

**A significant, unrelated pre-existing deployment blocker was found and investigated, not silently worked around.** Attempting to deploy just `getServiceAreas` failed: `Cannot set CPU on the functions adminGetUsers,adminGetReservations,adminGetAffiliateOverview,adminGetLedger,adminGetStripeLogs,adminUpsertBanner,adminApprovePayout,adminGetDashboardStats because they are GCF gen 1`. Traced into `firebase-tools`' own source (`deploy/functions/validate.js`'s `cpuConfigIsValid`) to confirm this wasn't about the new function's own code. Two fix attempts, both explicitly approved by the user before touching anything, both failed identically:
1. Removed `cpu: 0.5` from `index.ts`'s `setGlobalOptions(...)` (its own comment incorrectly claimed only 3 functions were gen-1 — `onUserCreated`, `adminApprovePayout`, `adminGetPayoutRequests` — when the real deploy error names 8, all genuinely v1-declared via `functionsV1....https.onCall`, confirmed by grep). Same error, byte-identical.
2. Upgraded `firebase-functions` 6.6.0 → 7.3.2 (the version the deploy tool itself was recommending). `tsc --noEmit` passed clean with zero breaking-change fallout, but the deploy failed with the exact same error again.
