Both changes were safely reverted (`package.json`/`package-lock.json` restored, `tsc --noEmit` re-confirmed clean) rather than left in a half-attempted state. **This blocker remains unresolved** — it's a genuine `firebase-tools`/`firebase-functions` interaction affecting HTTPS-callable v1 functions specifically in a mixed v1/v2 codebase, not something fixable from this codebase's own source without either a deeper GCP-console-level investigation (no tool access to do that from here) or accepting one of the two failed approaches' residual risk anyway. **`getServiceAreas` is written, compiles clean, and is ready to deploy — but is NOT yet live.** Flagged as its own distinct follow-up, not conflated with this feature's DSL-side completion.

**A second real DSL constraint was found while designing the picker widget itself, before committing to an implementation:** `Dropdown.options` requires a static `Iterable<String>` at DSL-authoring time (confirmed via `widgets.dart`'s own constructor) — there is no way to bind it to a runtime state value. Only `ListView`/`GridView` support a dynamic `source:`/`itemBuilder:` pair, and building a dropdown-equivalent custom picker on top of one is real additional UI engineering, not a simple widget swap. Checked with the user rather than silently picking either path: shipped a **static** `Dropdown` with the confirmed 10 launch prefectures (same list as §29's own fallback table) for both `prefecture` (required, all-users residential field) and `activityPrefecture` (optional, cast-only "where I work" field) — confirmed via the typed SDK that these are two genuinely separate fields before touching anything, not assumed from naming alone. The `service_areas` backend infrastructure above is real, correct groundwork for a future admin-panel-driven picker, but this specific dropdown does not consume it live — `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own "hardcoding directly into dropdown options would violate the future area expansion requirement" concern is real and stays open for this exact widget, not silently resolved by the backend work alone.

**A genuine bug found and fixed along the way, independent of the service-area work:** §29's `fetchDiscoveryCasts` GPS-fallback read `users.activity_prefecture` (optional, cast-only) when it should read `users.prefecture` (required, all users) — confirmed by reading `BasicInfoRegistration`'s typed SDK closely enough to notice the two distinct field pairs. A guest browsing Home — the primary user of this feature — would almost always have `activity_prefecture` empty, silently defeating the fallback for most guests even though the GPS path itself was correct. Fixed via `updateCustomAction` (commit `kcEiKtqEF5X1amIrBgsf`), verified against regenerated code.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; DSL-side dropdown work pushed clean (commit `rt9ODnajzYTKeu6KulJl`), regenerated code confirms both `FlutterFlowDropDown<String>` widgets bound correctly to `_model.prefecture`/`_model.activityPrefecture` (the same fields the existing submit handler already reads — unaffected by the widget-type swap). Final full-project no-op wiring review clean, same single already-accepted R19 warning. Froze both `ensureReplaced` calls immediately in the SAME pass (commit `pz7wH0mfP4jIgIT1ZCdX`) rather than deferring, applying the lesson from §28/§29's own freeze-discipline lapses; `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` sweep still 0 active, now out of 26 total.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** the `getServiceAreas`/`adminUpdateSystemConfig` backend infrastructure is not yet deployed (blocked, see above) and not yet consumed by any live UI; municipality-level data (both fields' city half stay free text, by explicit scope decision); no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual dropdown behavior (same standing caveat throughout this session); the `firebase-tools` gen-1/CPU deploy blocker itself is unresolved and will block ANY future backend deploy attempt, not just this feature's, until separately investigated.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/config.ts` (seeded `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.service_areas`), `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (new `getServiceAreas` callable, fixed `fetchDiscoveryCasts`'s field bug via `dsl/edit.dart`), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (exported `getServiceAreas`; removed `cpu` from `setGlobalOptions`), `dsl/edit.dart` (2 `ensureReplaced` dropdown reconstructions + freeze), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11): the deploy blocker above is fully root-caused, fixed, and `getServiceAreas` is now LIVE.** Requested explicitly by the user to keep digging rather than accept it as unresolved. Full diagnosis, in order:

1. **Ruled out this project's own code as the cause.** Spawned `firebase-functions`' own discovery binary directly (`node_modules/.bin/firebase-functions`, with `FUNCTIONS_CONTROL_API=true`) and queried its HTTP manifest endpoint (`/__/functions.yaml`, which actually returns JSON) to see the RAW endpoint definitions firebase-tools receives from this codebase. Confirmed via both a manual run and the real `firebase deploy --debug` log (`Got response from /__/functions.yaml ...`) that **no endpoint — v1 or v2 — has a `cpu` field at all** once `setGlobalOptions`'s own `cpu` was removed (§30's earlier addendum). The manifest is clean; the bug is NOT in this codebase.
2. **Traced the real source into `firebase-tools`' own compiled source** (`deploy/functions/prepare.js`, both the currently-installed 15.23.0 AND the latest 15.26.0 — byte-identical, confirmed unfixed upstream). `inferDetailsFromExisting(want, have, ...)` — the step that merges a function's DESIRED (`want`) config with its EXISTING LIVE (`have`) config fetched from GCP — contains:
   ```js
   if (typeof wantE.cpu === "undefined" && haveE.cpu) {
       wantE.cpu = haveE.cpu;
   }
   ```
   No platform check. GCP's Cloud Functions API apparently reports SOME non-null `cpu`-shaped value even for genuinely gen-1 functions (plausibly an informational/computed field derived from their fixed memory-tier CPU allocation, not a real settable property) — this gets blindly copied onto `wantE.cpu`, and a LATER validation step (`cpuConfigIsValid`, `validate.js`) then correctly rejects it because the endpoint's `platform === "gcfv1"`. Confirms this project's own 8 gen-1 admin functions (`adminGetUsers` etc.) were never the problem themselves — GCP's own API response about them is what trips a validator elsewhere in the SAME tool. Tellingly, a SEPARATE function in the exact same file, `resolveCpuAndConcurrency`, already has the correct guard (`if (e.platform === "gcfv1") { continue; }`) for a related concern — this one call site is just missing the same guard.
3. **Fix applied and empirically verified**: `wantE.platform !== "gcfv1" && typeof wantE.cpu === "undefined" && haveE.cpu` — the exact same guard style already used elsewhere in the file. Applied to a session-local npm cache copy of `firebase-tools@15.26.0` first (via `npx --yes firebase-tools@15.26.0`, then patched the cached `prepare.js` directly — NOT the project, NOT the system-wide install), then deployed `getServiceAreas` using that patched binary directly: `node <npx-cache-dir>/node_modules/firebase-tools/lib/bin/firebase.js deploy --only functions:getServiceAreas --project icoccha`. **Deploy succeeded cleanly, first try** — `Deploy complete!`, `getServiceAreas` confirmed live via `firebase functions:list --project icoccha`, all 8 previously-blocked admin functions confirmed still present/healthy (untouched by this targeted deploy, as expected with `--only`).
4. **This is a real upstream `firebase-tools` bug**, not a workaround-around-a-mystery — worth reporting to the `firebase-tools` GitHub repo if the user chooses to (not done automatically; posting externally needs the user's own call).

**Two patch locations exist, with different scope/durability — know which one is active before assuming `firebase deploy` works:**
- **Session-local (applied, confirmed working):** the npx-cached copy under `~/.npm/_npx/<hash>/node_modules/firebase-tools/lib/deploy/functions/prepare.js` (hash is npx's own resolution cache key for `firebase-tools@15.26.0` at the time it was fetched — NOT guaranteed stable long-term; if missing, re-run `npx --yes firebase-tools@15.26.0 --version` to repopulate the cache, then reapply the one-line guard above, then locate it via `find ~/.npm/_npx -iname firebase-tools -type d`). **The plain `firebase deploy` command does NOT use this patched copy** — it uses the system-wide install. To deploy using the patched copy, invoke it directly: `node <that-dir>/node_modules/firebase-tools/lib/bin/firebase.js deploy ...` instead of the bare `firebase` command.
- **System-wide (`/usr/lib/node_modules/firebase-tools`, root-owned, requires `sudo`):** NOT yet applied as of this entry — `sudo` needs an interactive password prompt this environment can't provide non-interactively. The user was given the exact command to run themselves (via the `!` prefix) to make plain `firebase deploy` work normally again project-wide. **Check whether this has been done before assuming the bare `firebase deploy` command works** — if not yet applied, continue using the session-local patched-binary invocation above for any further backend deploys.

**Files touched (this addendum):** no project files — the fix lives entirely in `firebase-tools`' own installed source (session-local npm cache, confirmed patched and working; system-wide install, patch command handed to the user, not self-applied). `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): re-verified the fix's blast radius, and caught a real hygiene issue in the process of doing so.** Re-ran the patched deploy with `--debug` specifically to confirm the other 8 previously-blocked functions were validated-against but never actually re-pushed (not just "still listed," which alone doesn't prove they weren't touched) — confirmed via the run's own summary (`1 Functions Deployed`, `0 Functions Errored`) and a follow-up `firebase functions:list` showing all 9 functions (8 existing + `getServiceAreas`) present and unchanged. **While doing this, the `--debug` log (written to `/tmp`) turned out to contain a full Cloud Functions API response body for an unrelated function (`submitReview`), including its live `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` environment variables in plaintext** (test-mode keys — `sk_test_...`/`pk_test_...` — not production, but real credential material regardless). A `grep` over that log for verification purposes also surfaced a preview of this content into the session transcript before the issue was noticed. Immediately deleted both `--debug` log files from `/tmp`, then swept every OTHER temp file created this session (`flutterflow ai run/validate/trace` outputs) for the same secret patterns — none found; the exposure was isolated to these 2 Cloud-Functions-API debug dumps specifically, not a broader pattern. Disclosed to the user directly rather than silently cleaned up. **Lesson for any future backend debugging**: `firebase deploy --debug` logs full raw API request/response bodies, including function environment variables — treat any such log as sensitive by default, avoid writing it to disk when avoidable, and never `grep`/`cat` it in a way whose output could get echoed back into a visible transcript. A background process (the manually-spawned discovery server from the earlier root-cause investigation) was also confirmed cleanly terminated, no lingering processes.

**Addendum (2026-08-11, another review pass): found and closed a real source-vs-live gap, not previously known.** Re-verifying the earlier claim "8 gen-1 functions" against source turned up a discrepancy worth chasing: `grep "= functionsV1"` across `src/*.ts` finds **10** matches, not 8 — `onUserCreated` (auth-triggered, different trigger shape, correctly not part of the CPU-error set) and `adminGetPayoutRequests` (identical `functionsV1.region(...).https.onCall(...)` shape to the 8 flagged ones, exported in `index.ts`) account for the other 2. Checked `firebase functions:list --project icoccha` and confirmed `adminGetPayoutRequests` was **not deployed at all** — this is why it never hit the CPU bug (`inferDetailsFromExisting`'s copy only fires when `haveE` — the existing remote state — exists at all; with no prior deploy, there was nothing to copy). This function reads the `payout_requests` collection for admin withdrawal review, a real, needed feature per this plan's own earlier schema notes — anything that tried to call it would have failed with a not-found error. Deployed it now using the same proven, patched CLI path (`--only functions:adminGetPayoutRequests`); succeeded on the first attempt (`Successful create operation`, `Deploy complete!`). Confirmed live via `functions:list` and confirmed the other 9 functions (8 admin v1 + `getServiceAreas`) remain unaffected. One curiosity, not chased further since it doesn't affect anything actionable: `functions:list` displays `adminGetPayoutRequests` as `v1` but the other 8 (same source-level `functionsV1...` syntax) as `v2` — the display column may reflect actual original deploy-time GCF generation rather than current SDK-syntax, which would mean those 8 were deployed as gen-2 via some earlier process despite their v1-syntax source, while this one, freshly deployed via the same syntax just now, became a genuine gen-1 function. Not investigated further; doesn't change anything about the fix's correctness or this function's own working state.

### §31: `profile_edit.dart`'s save action wired (§3.6.1) (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "proceed with profile_edit.dart's save action" — the next Phase 3 checklist item.

**Backend needed zero new work**: `updateProfile` already existed, already deployed and live, and is fully generic (accepts any subset of a fixed `allowedFields` list, writes only the fields provided) — confirmed via direct source read (`auth.ts`) and `firebase functions:list` before writing anything. This was purely a DSL/UI task.

**A significant, blocking DSL constraint was found and checked with the user before designing anything**: `TextField` has no way to display a dynamic/state-bound initial value in this DSL — confirmed via its own constructor (no `value:` parameter, unlike `Dropdown`, which genuinely does support one), confirmed `hint:` is a plain static `String?` (not a bindable expression either, so it can't be (ab)used as a workaround), and confirmed no `SetTextField`-style action exists (`ClearTextField` does, with no fill counterpart). The one theoretical path — `FFTextField.initialText`, a real non-deprecated proto field reachable only via hand-compiling a dynamic `FFText` expression through raw `mutateNode` — was correctly assessed as too risky for an uncertain payoff and not attempted, matching this project's own standing guidance to prefer typed builders. **Decided with the user**: every free-text field now shows the CURRENT value as a read-only `Text` plus a SEPARATE `TextField` for a new value; empty new-value input at save time means "keep the existing value" (computed server-call-side, not just client-side, so the effective value is always well-defined). `Dropdown`-backed fields (gender/prefecture/drinking/smoking) have no such problem — genuinely pre-filled directly via `value: State(...)`.

**Two real, pre-existing bugs found while mapping every native field to its backend counterpart** (not introduced this session): (1) two fields were both labeled "飲　酒" (drinking) — the second was clearly meant to be "喫　煙" (smoking), a copy-paste label bug, fixed as part of wiring; (2) the self-introduction field's static placeholder was identical to the one-line-message field's own placeholder — naturally resolved by the current-value/new-input redesign, since each field now shows its own real fetched value instead of any shared static placeholder.

**Two fields deliberately left unwired, disclosed rather than guessed**: 年齢層 (age bracket) is not in `updateProfile`'s `allowedFields` — per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §3.1.5 it's meant to be auto-computed from birth date, and this page has no birth-date field to compute it from anyway; 職　業 (occupation) has no corresponding backend field at all — inventing a mapping to the semantically-unrelated `atmosphere` field would be guessing product content, so left untouched, matching the established `reservation_form.dart` 延長予定の有無 precedent. Gallery (§3.1.12's "1 main photo + up to 10 gallery photos" requirement) is explicitly NOT built — this page has no gallery UI at all, only the single profile photo; building one is a genuinely separate, larger UI undertaking, not a wiring gap. Self-intro's OWN §3.1.12 requirement (≥50 chars) IS enforced, computed against the EFFECTIVE value (new input if provided, else existing) inside `callUpdateProfile` so the constraint holds for the actually-saved state, not just freshly-typed text.

**Built:** `fetchMyProfile` (single Firestore read on load, 12-field delimited string, same established pattern as every other page-summary this session) + a single parameterized `profileField(data, index)` extractor reused for all 12 fields, rather than 12 near-unique extractor functions — a deliberate simplification given this page's unusually high field count compared to prior uses of this pattern (typically 3–6 fields). `callUpdateProfile` — one large custom action taking 19 parameters (7 new/orig pairs for text fields + 4 direct dropdown values + photo URL), computing each field's effective value, validating self-intro length, then calling `updateProfile`; returns `'ok' | 'too_short' | 'error'` (a 3-way string result, not a bare bool, so the UI can distinguish a validation failure from a real save failure). `callUpdateProfilePhoto` for the separate, immediate-save photo-change flow (reusing `pickAndUploadImage`, already built for KYC — §10 — no new upload plumbing needed). `CircleImage` (no DSL constructor, confirmed via `widgets.dart`) reconstructed as `Avatar` (confirmed to support a dynamic `imageUrl:` expression, unlike `CircleImage`/the typed `imagePath()` patch which is static-only) at `size: 80`, matching the original's confirmed 80×80 dimensions from generated code.

**Two real mistakes made in this same pass and self-caught before pushing successfully, not shipped broken:**
1. Used a nonexistent `Concat([...])` function to prefix "現在: " onto each current-value display, and misapplied the `profileField` delimited-string extractor as if it were a generic string-passthrough helper — caught immediately by `dart analyze` (`undefined_function`), not discovered later. Fixed by splitting into two separate `Text` widgets (a static "現在の設定:" label + the raw `State(...)`-bound value) — simpler, and avoids relying on unconfirmed API surface entirely.
2. Guessed a `Conditional.string(...)` API for branching the save button's 3-way string result that doesn't exist in this SDK — caught the same way (`undefined_identifier`). The real, established construct is `Switch(value, cases: [SwitchCase(...), ...], orElse: ...)`, found by reading `actions.dart` directly rather than guessing a second time.
3. **Also hit and fixed a genuine `compileDslApp`-level validation failure** (not caught by `dart analyze`, since it's Dart-syntax-valid): passed a nullable `String` `ActionOutput` directly as an `If(...)` condition (`If(ActionOutput('newPhotoUrl'), ...)`), assuming implicit truthiness — this DSL requires an actual boolean expression. The established, correct idiom for "is this string output non-empty" (confirmed via an existing comment referencing the exact pattern for `kycDocUrl`, `dsl/edit.dart`'s own §2-era KYC work) is `If(Not(Equals(ActionOutput(...), '')), ...)`. Fixed on the second push attempt; the FIRST push failed cleanly with a precise, correctly-scoped error (`Button [key: Button_adgkk84a] — Conditional execution for action is improperly set`) rather than silently landing something broken.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; pushed clean on the corrected second attempt (commit `iZH4CQ6a8UCe5ezQ5vgP`; a first attempt failed cleanly on a missing `name:` on several reconstructed `Column`s — `ensureReplaced requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name` — fixed before the successful push, not after). **Regenerated code read directly, not trusted from the DSL source**: confirmed the `initState` chain matches exactly (fetch → 12 sequential field assignments); confirmed `_model.origNickname!` renders as the current-value `Text`; confirmed the smoking dropdown's corrected "喫　煙" label is present; confirmed the save button's full `callUpdateProfile(...)` call passes all 19 arguments in the right order and the 3-way `if/else if/else` branch on `saveResult` matches exactly; confirmed the Avatar's `imageUrl: _model.editProfileImageUrl!` binding and the photo-change flow's `_model.editProfileImageUrl = _model.newPhotoUrl` update. Final full-project no-op wiring review: `NO_OP` confirmed, same single already-accepted R19 warning, nothing new. Froze all 12 `ensureReplaced` calls immediately in the SAME pass (commit `8BZ2jyBxSwowIuQ2o8Ra`), applying the freeze-discipline lesson from earlier in this session rather than deferring it — re-verified `NO_OP` before that push too; `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` sweep still 0 active, now out of 38 total.

**Explicitly not done / still open, disclosed rather than silently narrowed:** gallery UI (see above); age-bracket display/computation; occupation field (no backend counterpart); drinking/smoking option wording is a reasonable, undocumented interim choice (no explicit option list found in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` for either field), same disclosure discipline as every other guessed-vs-confirmed distinction in this file; no live device/Test-Mode test of the actual form behavior (same standing caveat throughout this session).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (2 custom actions + 1 photo-save action, 1 parameterized extractor function, 19 state fields, 1 new ON_INIT_STATE trigger, 12 `ensureReplaced` widget reconstructions + freeze, 2 new button wirings), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§3.6.1 marked done), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): a real, significant scoping bug in the self-intro validation, found and fixed before it could affect a real user.** The original implementation validated the EFFECTIVE self-intro length (new input if provided, else the existing value) on EVERY save, regardless of which field the user was actually trying to change — meaning any existing user whose self-intro happened to be under 50 characters (very plausible, since this validation was never enforced before this session) would be unable to save ANY profile edit at all — even just changing their nickname — until they separately, specifically fixed a self-intro field they weren't even trying to touch. A brand-new user who hadn't written a self-intro yet would hit the exact same lock-out on their very first edit. Checked with the user rather than silently deciding either way (keep the strict all-saves gate vs. only validate when self-intro itself changes) — chosen: only gate when `newSelfIntro` is non-empty (the user is actively submitting a new one). Fixed via `updateCustomAction` (not re-declaring `app.customAction`, applying §30's own freshly-learned lesson immediately rather than re-discovering it) since the parameter list/count was unchanged, only the function body — `code:`-only update, matching the documented rule. Verified via regenerated code (`if (newSelfIntro != null && newSelfIntro.isNotEmpty && effSelfIntro.length < 50)`); pushed clean (commit `hzpaug0PUsG3fVD0BXTT`); final no-op wiring review clean; `ensureReplaced` sweep still 0 active out of 38.

**Addendum (2026-08-11, next review pass): a real, if narrow-window, data-loss risk found and closed.** `callUpdateProfile` unconditionally sends `gender ?? ''`/`prefecture ?? ''`/`drinking ?? ''`/`smoking ?? ''` — these 4 fields have no "orig" fallback the way text fields do (`Dropdown` pre-fills them directly into ONE state field, not a new/orig pair), because they don't need one under normal use. But if the save button were tapped before the page's own async on-load fetch (`fetchMyProfile`) resolves, those 4 state fields would still hold their default empty-string value, and saving would silently OVERWRITE the user's real gender/prefecture/drinking/smoking with empty strings — genuine data loss, not just a UX rough edge, in a real (if narrow) timing window. Fixed generally rather than patched around just these 4 fields: added a `profileLoaded` bool state field, set `true` only as the LAST step of the on-load fetch chain (after all 12 other fields are populated), and gated the save button's entire action behind `If(State('profileLoaded'), then: [...], orElse: [Snackbar('読み込み中です...')])` — protects every field this page can save, not only the ones lacking their own orig-value fallback. Verified via regenerated code: `_model.profileLoaded = true` confirmed as the genuinely-last line of the init chain (after all 12 field assignments), and the save button's own code confirmed wrapped in `if (_model.profileLoaded!) { ... }`. Pushed clean (commit `vxM7PA9p2ZwjOFVjfjM8`); final no-op wiring review clean; `ensureReplaced` sweep still 0 active out of 38.

### §32: Full-project review — re-read the whole change log, dispatched two targeted sweeps, fixed one real pre-existing defect (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "thoroughly review the entire project... identify any parts implemented incorrectly, mistakes, omissions... fix these bugs completely" — broader than the standing per-task review directive (§28 onward), this one explicitly scoped to the WHOLE project's accumulated history, not just the most recent unit of work.

**Method:** re-read this file's own full change log (§1-§31) end to end rather than relying on memory of the most recent entries alone — surfaced several "explicitly not done / still open" items already disclosed in earlier entries, worth re-checking for whether any had quietly become more urgent. In parallel, dispatched two background research agents (not a Workflow — this wasn't an explicit multi-agent-orchestration request, just ordinary background delegation) to sweep for the two bug CLASSES this session has already proven recur, rather than re-inventing an audit approach from scratch:
1. **Field-name/semantic mismatches** (the class behind §29's `activity_prefecture`-vs-`prefecture` bug) — swept all 34 files in `generated_code/lib/custom_code/actions/` against `schemas.dart` and the real backend source.
2. **Unguarded async-load-then-save race conditions** (the class behind §31's `profileLoaded` bug) — swept every page with an `initState`-async-fetch pattern for a save/submit action reading the same not-yet-loaded fields without a loaded-flag guard.

**Sweep #1 result: clean.** Beyond the already-fixed `prefecture` bug, no new confirmed field-name mismatches found — every near-duplicate field pair checked (`prefecture`/`activity_prefecture`, `city`/`activity_city`, `approval_status`/`kyc_status`, the `role`/`role_admin`/`roleAdmin` triple-check in `checkIsAdminUser`, matching `admin.ts`'s own identical compat check by design) reads the semantically-correct field at every site. One low-confidence item surfaced and independently investigated (see below).

**Sweep #2 result: clean.** `profile_edit.dart` (§31) is confirmed the ONLY page with the vulnerable "async on-load fetch populates multiple fields, a later save button reads them unguarded" shape anywhere in the project — every other async-load page either only displays read-only data, only submits route params / freshly-typed user input (not the loaded fields themselves), or loads only a single field used purely as a visibility gate. No further fix needed from this thread.

**One real, already-diagnosed-but-never-fixed defect found and fixed**: `calculateExtensionPrice` (native, pre-existing, never DSL-declared — confirmed via grep) compares its `timeSlot` argument against `'第3部'`/`'第4部'`, but `config.ts`'s own `night_time_slots` (the confirmed real source of truth, already used to populate `reservation_form.dart`'s own 時間帯 dropdown correctly in §24) is `["3部","4部"]` — no "第" prefix. This was FOUND and explicitly flagged as unfixed all the way back in §23's own entry ("a pre-existing bug in `extension_payment.dart`'s pricing flow that predates this session and wasn't touched by any UI built this session") but never actually corrected in the ~9 entries since. Fixed now via `updateCustomFunction` (`code:`-only, signature unchanged) — a low-risk fix specifically because `extension_payment.dart` remains entirely unwired (Phase 6/11, not started this session), so nothing currently reachable changes behavior; this only corrects a genuine latent defect for whenever that page eventually gets wired. Distinguished explicitly from `profile_edit.dart`'s own dormant dropdown option CONTENT (§24's follow-up sweep, correctly left alone) — that would have meant inventing new product wording; this is a pure correction against an already-established, confirmed source of truth, a categorically lower-risk class of fix.

**One minor, disclosed-not-fixed finding**: `reservations.location` and `reservations.meeting_point` are two genuinely separate fields (venue vs. specific meeting point), both correctly captured on `reservation_form.dart`'s own two distinct fields (`resMeetingAddress`/`resMeetingPoint`, §12) and both correctly written by `createReservation` — but `fetchMyReservations` (§14) and `fetchReservationSummary` (§28) both only read/display `location`, never `meeting_point`. Classified as a display-completeness NICETY, not a defect, by the same standard already applied to the "`callCancelReservation` never sends the optional `cancel_reason` field" gap (§15: "a UX nicety left for a future pass, not a defect") — no data is lost (both fields are correctly written to Firestore regardless), nothing crashes or shows wrong data, this is purely "the summary view could show one more real field it currently omits." Left unfixed, disclosed rather than silently addressed or silently ignored — worth a follow-up pass if the product owner confirms guests need to see the specific meeting point (not just the venue) in the reservation list/confirmation screens.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; the `calculateExtensionPrice` fix pushed clean (commit `Z9tBjpoXVdz1JWiP2L8U`); confirmed via regenerated `custom_functions.dart` (`if (timeSlot == '3部' || timeSlot == '4部')`); final full-project no-op wiring review clean, same single already-accepted R19 warning; `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` sweep still 0 active, unchanged at 38 total (this fix touched no structural widget ops, only a native customFunction body).

**Explicitly not done / still open:** the `location`/`meeting_point` display gap above; every item already carried forward as open across §14-§31 (Storage-adjacent items already resolved in §17; remaining: the saved-card flow, exact under-review popup copy, `ConnectOnboarding`'s free-text phone/postal/bank-code fields with no format validation (§23, lower priority than the date-parsing fix already made since a malformed value fails at Stripe's own well-validated API layer rather than silently corrupting data), Phase 3 items beyond what §29-§31 covered (cast schedule tab, `CocoTenDetailPage`, `FavoritesPage`, search/filter panels), Phase 12 admin panel (only `KycReviewPage` exists), and a live device/Test-Mode click-test of anything built this entire session (no FlutterFlow Desktop session ever paired) — this last one remains the single largest category of "not yet verified" across the whole project, disclosed consistently throughout rather than claimed as done.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (1 `updateCustomFunction` call), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §33: MAJOR bug found and fixed on a second full-project review pass — the entire Home ranking query feature was silently non-functional since it was built (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** the user repeated the exact same "thoroughly review the entire project... fix these bugs completely" request a second time, verbatim. Rather than re-running §32's same two sweeps, looked for a genuinely different angle this pass — specifically, whether any client-side Firestore query built this session could be silently blocked by `firestore.rules` itself, a class of bug neither §32's field-mismatch sweep nor its race-condition sweep would have caught (both operate on already-successfully-compiling Dart code; a rules-rejection is a RUNTIME failure invisible to any static check).

**The bug, confirmed precisely, not assumed:** `firestore.rules`' `users` collection rule is `allow read: if request.auth.uid == document` — strictly identity-based, no admin/role exception, no content-based alternative. `fetchDiscoveryCasts` (§29, Home ranking query) ran `firestore.collection('users').where('account_type', isEqualTo: 'cast').where('approval_status', isEqualTo: 'approved')...get()` **directly from the client**. Firestore's security-rules engine denies a QUERY outright (before it ever touches real data) unless the rule can be PROVEN satisfied for every possible result without per-document evaluation — an identity rule like this one can only be proven for a query that constrains results to the caller's own document ID; a query filtering on `account_type`/`approval_status` (fields with no relationship to `request.auth.uid`) can match arbitrarily many OTHER users' documents, so Firestore rejects the whole query with `permission-denied`. The action's own try/catch (a defensive, "fail gracefully" pattern applied consistently throughout this session) silently absorbed this into an empty `<String>[]` return — **meaning the Home discovery grid has shown zero casts to every single user since §29 first shipped it**, with no error surfacing anywhere client-visible. This is qualitatively different from every other bug found this session (wrong field name, wrong validation scope, wrong widget property) — it's a fully-built, seemingly-correct, compiles-clean, pushes-clean feature that was **never functional at all**, and neither `dart analyze`, `flutterflow ai test`, `compileDslApp`, nor any of this session's regenerated-code read-throughs could have caught it, because the failure only happens against LIVE Firestore at runtime, a layer none of those checks touch.

**Real fix, not a workaround:** moved the query server-side into a new Cloud Function, `getDiscoveryCasts` (`auth.ts`) — Admin SDK bypasses Firestore rules entirely, the exact same reason `getServiceAreas` (§30) already exists as a callable rather than a direct client read of admin-only `system_config`. The new function takes `{lat, lng}` (the CLIENT's already-resolved coordinates — GPS or the prefecture fallback, both unchanged) and does the full query/filter (`account_type`, `approval_status`, excluding the caller's own uid, excluding `is_frozen`)/Haversine-sort/format server-side, returning the exact same `|||`-delimited item format the client already expected — so only `fetchDiscoveryCasts` itself needed to change (now just resolves coordinates, then calls the new callable), not the discovery-grid UI or its extractor functions. Deployed via the same proven patched-CLI path from §30 (session-local `firebase-tools` cache copy, since the system-wide gen-1/CPU bug is still unresolved pending the user's own `sudo` command) — succeeded on the first attempt.

**Swept for the same bug class elsewhere before declaring this fixed, not just patching the one instance found:** checked `firestore.rules`' other collections with content-based (not identity-based) rules — `reservations`' rule (`resource.data.guest_id == request.auth.uid || resource.data.cast_ids.hasAny([request.auth.uid]) || ...`) is the kind of rule Firestore CAN prove satisfiable for a query, and confirmed `fetchMyReservations`' own two queries (`.where('guest_id', isEqualTo: uid)`, `.where('cast_ids', arrayContains: uid)`) match the rule's own conditions exactly — safe by construction, a genuinely different (and correct) pattern from what broke `fetchDiscoveryCasts`. Grepped the whole file for every other `.collection('users')` usage — found none doing a filtered QUERY (every other `users` access is a `.doc(uid).get()`/`.doc(uid).update()` against the CALLER's own document, which trivially satisfies the identity rule) — confirmed `fetchDiscoveryCasts` was a genuinely isolated instance, not a systemic pattern across this session's Firestore usage.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on the new backend function; `dart analyze`/`flutterflow ai test` clean on the DSL change; deploy succeeded (`Successful create operation`, `Deploy complete!`); DSL push succeeded clean (commit `X9PReBNL8sL8CtXLMPJB`); regenerated code confirms `fetchDiscoveryCasts` now calls `FirebaseFunctions.instanceFor(...).httpsCallable('getDiscoveryCasts')`; final full-project no-op wiring review clean, same single already-accepted R19 warning.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** a live device/Test-Mode test that the discovery grid now actually populates with real cast data (same standing caveat as everything else in this session — no FlutterFlow Desktop session ever paired; this fix is verified correct by construction and against the deploy/compile layer, not by an actual observed populated grid); the system-wide `firebase-tools` gen-1/CPU patch is still pending the user's own `sudo` command from §30 — this deploy used the session-local patched-cache workaround again, which remains fully functional but is a per-session detail worth remembering if a fresh session needs to deploy again.

**A broader lesson worth naming**: this session has now found bugs at every layer this project's own verification discipline explicitly built up over time — wrong data (§9), double-wrapped function bodies invisible to any compile check (§22), nested-conditional SDK limits only discoverable by hitting them (§11), unfrozen one-shot ops causing silent key churn (§28), and now a Firestore-rules rejection invisible to every layer of static/compile verification this project uses. The standing discipline of "verify against regenerated code, not just a clean push" (established early, §14 onward) catches an entire CLASS of bug — but it does not and cannot catch RUNTIME security-rule rejections, since regenerated code and a clean compile only prove the code is well-formed and calls the right functions, not that those calls will be ALLOWED against live infrastructure. Worth treating "does this client-side Firestore query's own `.where()` clauses match what the target collection's security rule can actually prove" as its own explicit checklist item for any FUTURE feature that reads Firestore directly from a custom action, especially anything querying data belonging to OTHER users (discovery/search/browse-style features are the highest-risk category, by nature).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (new `getDiscoveryCasts` callable), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (export), `dsl/edit.dart` (`fetchDiscoveryCasts` rewritten to call the new callable instead of querying directly), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §34: Third full-project review pass — cast Stripe Connect onboarding has been completely broken since §11, two functions were never actually live (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** the user repeated the "review thoroughly, check for further issues" request a third time. Deliberately avoided re-running §32/§33's own sweeps a third time (diminishing returns on the same method) and picked a genuinely new angle: §33 found a bug where deployed CODE was correct but a RUNTIME dependency (Firestore rules) silently blocked it — this pass asked the mirror question, "is every function the client-side code CALLS actually live?", a check no prior pass in this session had ever explicitly performed.

**Method:** extracted every literal `httpsCallable('...')` name referenced anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart` (14 distinct names) and cross-referenced each against `firebase functions:list --project icoccha`'s actual live output. Separately, extracted every function NAME exported from `index.ts` and diffed the full set against the live list, to catch anything beyond just what THIS project's own DSL happens to call directly.

**The bug: `submitConnectOnboarding` and `getConnectAccountStatus` were NOT LIVE**, despite `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §11 explicitly documenting them as deployed and verified back on 2026-08-10 ("Deployed just the 2 new + 2 modified functions... in one small batch — succeeded cleanly... Confirmed post-deploy that public reachability held"). Both functions' SOURCE was completely correct — present in `auth.ts`, correctly exported from `index.ts`, compiled clean — they simply were not present on the live `icoccha` project when checked just now. Root cause of the disappearance not conclusively determined (not investigated further once source-correctness and the fix path were both confirmed — the practical remedy is identical either way), but it does NOT indicate a code defect: this is the SAME class of gap as §32's `adminGetPayoutRequests` finding (correct source, never/no-longer actually deployed), not a repeat of §33's class (code that's live but silently rejected at runtime). **Real-world severity: this makes ALL of `ConnectOnboarding` (§11) — the entire cast Stripe payout onboarding page, fully built, fully wired, with a real form UI — completely non-functional.** A cast user filling out and submitting this form would get a callable "not found" failure on every attempt, unable to ever complete onboarding, unable to ever receive payouts. This sat undiscovered for the entire session between §11 and now, since no push, `dart analyze`, or regenerated-code read since then had any reason to re-verify live deployment status of functions that were already confirmed live once — the same "verified once, trusted forever" gap this session's own review discipline exists specifically to catch, just applied to backend deploy state rather than DSL source this time.

**Fixed:** redeployed both functions via the same proven patched-CLI path (`--only functions:submitConnectOnboarding,functions:getConnectAccountStatus`) — succeeded on the first attempt, no code changes needed at all.

**A broader, real gap found in the same sweep, deliberately NOT treated as an equal-priority bug:** 16 more functions (`adminCloseWorkPost`, `adminCreateWorkPost`, `adminDeleteBanner`, `adminDeleteCocotenShop`, `adminGetAuditLogs`, `adminGetCocotenShops`, `adminGetProcessedEvents`, `adminGetReportChatLog`, `adminGetReservationExtras`, `adminGetSystemConfig`, `adminGetWorkPosts`, `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`, `adminManualRefund`, `adminUpdateReservationLocation`, `adminUpdateUserProfile`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`) are ALSO exported in source but not live on `icoccha`. Explicitly checked whether any is actually called from anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart` — none are (confirmed via the same `httpsCallable(...)` name extraction). These are the REMAINDER of the sister admin-dashboard project's own admin-panel backend, shared via the same reference source tree (per §1.4/§1.5's own documented history) — this project's own Phase 12 admin panel is still almost entirely unbuilt (only `KycReviewPage` exists, §26), so nothing in THIS project's UI currently depends on any of them. Left undeployed, disclosed rather than silently deployed unprompted — deploying 16 functions nothing calls would be scope creep beyond "fix what's currently broken," the same restraint already applied to `getServiceAreas` (§30, built correctly, deliberately not yet consumed by the static-dropdown decision). **Worth redeploying each one the moment any future Phase 12 admin UI work actually calls it** — don't assume it's already live just because it's already in source, per this entry's own finding.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (no code changed); both functions confirmed live via `firebase functions:list` post-deploy. No DSL push needed this pass — the bug and its fix were entirely backend-deployment-state, not DSL source.

**Pattern worth naming across all three review passes today (§32, §33, §34):** each pass deliberately picked a DIFFERENT layer to interrogate rather than re-running the same check a third/fourth time — DSL-level field-name correctness (§32), Firestore security-rule satisfiability (§33), and now Cloud Functions live-deployment state (§34). None of the three would have caught either of the OTHER two — a lesson in its own right: "review thoroughly" is best served by asking a genuinely different verification question each pass, not repeating the same one with more scrutiny.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** why the two functions disappeared from live deployment between §11 and now (not investigated — the practical fix doesn't depend on the answer, but a future session hitting the same "function I know I deployed isn't live" surprise should consider checking `.flutterflow/runs.jsonl`/deploy history around whatever change might have triggered a broader `firebase deploy` without `--only`, which DOES prune server-side functions absent from the local deploy set — no such deploy was identified in this session's own history, so the cause may predate this session or be external); the 16 dormant admin functions above; a live device/Test-Mode test that `ConnectOnboarding` now actually completes successfully end-to-end (same standing caveat as everything else this session).

### §35: Fourth full-project review pass — a confirmed, currently-active, hourly-crashing bug in the auto-cancellation payment safety net, caught on live logs (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** the user repeated the "review thoroughly, check for further issues" request a fourth time. §32/§33/§34 had each deliberately picked a different verification layer (DSL field-name correctness, Firestore security-rule satisfiability, Cloud Functions live-deployment state); this pass needed a fifth, genuinely new angle rather than re-running any of those three.

**Method:** started broad — grepped `generated_code/lib/custom_code/actions/*.dart` (not just `dsl/edit.dart`) for every `httpsCallable(...)` name, to catch any NATIVE (non-DSL-authored) custom action calling a function §34's DSL-only sweep might have missed. Came back as a strict subset of §34's already-checked list — clean, no new names. Pivoted to a angle none of the prior three passes had touched: **Firestore composite index coverage.** Server-side Admin SDK queries (the exact mechanism §33 used to FIX the Home ranking query, and the pattern several other backend functions already use) bypass security rules entirely, but they do not bypass Firestore's composite-index requirement — a query filtering/ordering on 2+ different fields without a matching index throws `FAILED_PRECONDITION` at query time. Since almost every call site in this codebase wraps its query in a defensive try/catch (the same pattern that made §33's rules-rejection invisible), a missing index produces the exact same client-visible symptom: silent empty/no-op results, zero user-facing error. Grepped every `.where(` across `firebase/functions/src/*.ts` (94 occurrences), manually classified each query chain as pure-equality-only (safe — Firestore's automatic single-field indexes cover this via zigzag-merge, confirmed correct for `getDiscoveryCasts` itself, re-derived from first principles rather than assumed) vs. combining an equality/array-contains/in/not-in filter with a range filter or `orderBy` on a DIFFERENT field (requires an explicit composite index), then cross-referenced every query in the second group against the entries actually present in `firestore.indexes.json`.

**The critical bug, confirmed via live production logs, not inferred:** `autoCancelExpiredAuth` (`reservations.ts`) — an `onSchedule("every 1 hours", ...)` function, no client call site, fires purely on its own cron — runs `.collection('reservations').where('status','in',['authorized','cast_pending']).where('created_at','<',cutoff).get()`. No composite index existed for `status`+`created_at` on `reservations` (the file had `status`+`scheduled_at` and `status`+`updated_at`, but never `status`+`created_at`). `firebase functions:log --only autoCancelExpiredAuth` showed this query throwing `Error: 9 FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index` on **every single hourly invocation** visible in the log window (going back 9+ hours, i.e. since at least whenever this function was last deployed) — an uncaught exception at the top of the function body, before the per-document try/catch further down even runs, meaning the function does zero work on every firing. Real-world impact: this is the mechanism that's supposed to auto-release a guest's payment authorization and expire the reservation when a cast never responds within 24 hours — it has never successfully run even once. Reservations stuck in `authorized`/`cast_pending` past 24h simply stay that way forever; guests get no automatic resolution of an unanswered booking.

**Fixed:** added the composite index (`reservations`: `status` ASC + `created_at` ASC) to `firestore.indexes.json`, deployed via plain `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes --project icoccha` (index-only deploys don't touch the `deploy/functions/prepare.js` code path, so the still-unresolved gen-1/CPU deploy blocker from §30 doesn't apply here — no patched-CLI workaround needed for this deploy). **Verified byte-exact, not just "looks right"**: Firestore's own error message embeds a `create_composite` URL with a base64-encoded protobuf describing the exact index it wants; decoded it directly (`base64.urlsafe_b64decode` + inspect raw bytes) and confirmed it names `reservations` / `status` (ASCENDING) / `created_at` (ASCENDING) / `__name__` (ASCENDING, Firestore's own implicit tie-breaker) — an exact match to what was added, given the safety-critical nature of this specific fix.

**A second, same-class bug found in the same sweep, not yet empirically observed failing but certain to fail identically:** `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` (`affiliate.ts`, `onSchedule({schedule: "0 3 5 * *", timeZone: "Asia/Tokyo"})` — runs the 5th of every month) calls `countUniqueWorkDays`, which queries `.where('cast_ids','array-contains',castUid).where('status','==','completed').where('updated_at','>=',start).where('updated_at','<=',end)` — array-contains + equality + range on a fourth field, no matching index existed. Unlike `autoCancelExpiredAuth`, this one IS wrapped in a per-affiliator try/catch one level up (`processAffiliatorPayment`'s caller logs and continues to the next affiliate), so a failure here doesn't crash the whole scheduled run — but it silently leaves `workDays` uncomputed, so the `workDays < minDays` gate is never reached, so the affiliate's reward for that month never resolves to either "paid" or "forfeited." Because the query that finds pending rewards also filters on the specific `month` string, an orphaned reward from a failed run is never picked up by a later run — it's stuck in `pending` permanently, not just delayed. Confirmed this function IS live and scheduled (`firebase functions:list`); the next real occurrence would have been 2026-09-05 03:00 JST. Fixed the same way: added `reservations`: `cast_ids` (arrayConfig CONTAINS) + `status` ASC + `updated_at` ASC to `firestore.indexes.json`, deployed together with the fix above.

**Two more gaps in the same category found and deliberately left disclosed-not-fixed, both currently unreachable from any live code path in this project:** (1) `adminGetUsers` accepts `approval_status` and `prefecture` as optional equality filters (alongside `account_type`/`is_frozen`/`kyc_status`), each combinable with either a `nickname` prefix-range+orderBy or a `created_at` range+orderBy — `firestore.indexes.json` only covers combinations built from `account_type`/`is_frozen`/`kyc_status`; any combination touching `approval_status` or `prefecture` has no matching index and would throw. Checked the ONLY live call site (`callAdminGetPendingKyc` in `dsl/edit.dart`) — it sends `{'kyc_status': 'submitted'}` alone, which IS covered, so this is currently dormant, not active. (2) `requestWithdrawal` (`auth.ts`, exported, callable, never invoked from this project's DSL — no account-withdrawal UI exists yet) runs two `reservations` queries combining `guest_id`/`cast_ids` with `status not-in [...]` — no matching index exists. Both left undeployed rather than speculatively pre-building indexes for filter combinations nothing currently sends, matching this session's established restraint on the 16 dormant admin functions (§34) and unconsumed `getServiceAreas` (§30) — **worth revisiting the moment either gets an actual caller.**

**One false alarm, caught before it became a wasted fix — worth naming as a methodology note.** `adminGetUsers`'s nickname prefix-search range (`nickname_prefix + ""`, the standard Firestore "starts-with" idiom) rendered as `nickname_prefix + ""` in every terminal tool output (`Read`, `grep`) during this investigation — U+F8FF is a Private-Use-Area character with no visible glyph in the terminal/tool rendering pipeline, not an actually-empty string. Nearly flagged and "fixed" this as a broken prefix search. Caught the discrepancy by dumping the raw bytes (`sed -n '138p' | xxd`) before editing anything, which showed the real UTF-8 bytes `ef a3 bf` (exactly U+F8FF) sitting right where a correct implementation would put them. The code was already correct; no fix was needed or made. Worth remembering for any future session: **never trust a visual "this string looks empty" read on Japanese-adjacent or CJK-range source without checking raw bytes first** — this codebase already deals in real Unicode content (Japanese UI strings throughout) where an invisible-but-present character is a live risk of a false-positive bug report.

**Verification:** both composite-index deploys succeeded (`firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes`, "Deploy complete!"); the `autoCancelExpiredAuth` index confirmed byte-exact against Firestore's own diagnostic as described above; `firebase firestore:indexes --project icoccha` lists all 6 `reservations` composite indexes including both new ones. No DSL/TypeScript source changed this pass (the affiliate/withdrawal/admin-search findings were left as disclosed gaps, not fixes) — `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` and `npx tsc --noEmit` both remain clean from their last-verified state, unaffected by an indexes-only change.

**A broader lesson worth naming, extending §34's own closing observation:** four passes, four different layers, four different bugs — DSL field-name correctness (§32), Firestore security-rule satisfiability (§33), Cloud Functions live-deployment state (§34), and now Firestore composite-index coverage (§35). This pass is also the FIRST time this session verified a bug against actual live runtime behavior (`firebase functions:log`) rather than reasoning from source code, rules text, or deployment listings alone — and it's precisely because of that direct observation that this bug is reported with full confidence rather than "probably fails" hedging. The general principle for future sessions: any Cloud Function combining a filter on one field with a range/orderBy/inequality on a DIFFERENT field needs its composite index verified against `firestore.indexes.json` BEFORE being trusted, especially for `onSchedule` functions — nothing in this project's compile/push/test loop exercises a scheduled function's query at all, so a missing index there can and did go undetected through every prior verification pass this session performed.

**Explicitly not done / still open:** a live device/Test-Mode confirmation that `autoCancelExpiredAuth` now actually processes a real expired reservation end-to-end (same standing caveat as everything else this session — no FlutterFlow Desktop session ever paired; this fix is verified via live error logs going silent on the next run, not yet re-observed post-fix since that requires waiting for the next hourly firing); the two disclosed-not-fixed index gaps above (`adminGetUsers` approval_status/prefecture, `requestWithdrawal`); everything already carried forward as open through §34.

**Files touched:** `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (2 new composite indexes: `reservations` status+created_at, `reservations` cast_ids+status+updated_at), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Files touched:** none in source — `firebase/` deployment state only (2 functions redeployed, zero code changes), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§6 defect #5 status note corrected), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §36: Phase 6 built — chat send/receive, マッチャ 5-category history list, notifications page (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "Start Phase 6, chat & notifications" — the first genuinely new FEATURE build since the four review passes (§32-§35), covering §3.5.7-9 (chat open/close mechanics, message history filters), §3.9.10 (chat-open/close cross-ref), and §3.9.16 (Stripe-webhook-to-notification mirroring, the 5-category notification set).

**Pre-build discovery, before writing anything:** read `firestore.rules`' own 2026-08-03 comment on the `chat_rooms` rule, which had already explicitly flagged and left open the exact gap this phase needed to close ("the `chat_rooms/{room_id}/messages` subcollection is NOT governed by this rule at all... flagged as a separate follow-up"), and found an EARLIER-this-session comment block (already landed, clearing a dead native `databaseRequest` on `MacchaChats`) that had independently arrived at the same conclusion and explicitly deferred it: "Real chat wiring... is a genuine future feature build, not something to improvise here." Both of these confirmed the real message-storage shape (`chat_rooms/{roomId}/messages`, fields `sender_id`/`text`/`created_at`) via `admin.ts`'s own `adminGetReportChatLog` (already reading exactly this path) rather than guessing — the schema also has vestigial top-level `chats`/`messages` collections from FlutterFlow's default starter scaffold, confirmed via grep to have zero backend references anywhere, correctly left untouched.

**Backend (`firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts`, 3 new callables):**
- `sendChatMessage({res_id, text})` — writes to `chat_rooms/{roomId}/messages` + mirrors `chat_rooms.last_message`/`last_message_time`. Posting-lock (§3.5.8a) is enforced via the EXISTING `chat_rooms.active` flag rather than a new field — traced through `respondToReservation` (sets `active:true` exactly at 確定決済済, §3.5.7's open trigger), `submitReview` (sets `active:false` instantly on review submission), and `autoCompleteReviews` (sets `active:false` on the `chat_close_sec` timeout) and confirmed every path that should block posting already flips this one flag, so no separate lock field was needed.
- `getChatRoomInfo({res_id})` / `getMyMatchaList()` — both Admin-SDK-required specifically to resolve the COUNTERPART's nickname/photo, since `users/{uid}`'s rule is identity-only (§33's own lesson: a client can never read another user's doc regardless of query shape). `getMyMatchaList` derives §3.5.9's 5 history categories (すべて/新しい/未交流/交流済み/断られた) from RESERVATION status, not chat_room existence — "断られた"/"新しい" both cover reservations with no chat room at all. Mapping confirmed against the REAL status values this backend writes (traced through `respondToReservation`/`confirmMeetup`/`reportCompletion`/the Capture webhook/`submitReview`), including the `cancelled_by: "cast"` field (distinct from a guest-initiated cancellation) that makes "断られた" precisely identifiable.

**Two new Firestore rules** (`chat_rooms/{roomId}/messages`, `users/{uid}/notifications`) — both closed pre-existing "no rule = deny-all" gaps, both content-based via a `get()` on the parent doc (same technique `isAdmin()` already used), both read-only for clients (write stays Cloud-Functions-only so the posting-lock and `last_message` mirroring stay atomic). Notification `read`-marking is the one client-writable exception, restricted via `diff().affectedKeys().hasOnly(['read'])` to that single field.

**DSL — real dynamic lists via `ListView(source:, itemBuilder:)`, not this session's usual hand-unrolled-Column pattern.** Investigated first (via the frozen `ReservationListPage`/`myReservationsList` precedent, §14) and confirmed this is the actual native, working, previously-proven pattern in this project — just never reused since §14. Built: `MacchaChats` (real message thread, header showing real counterpart nickname/photo, working send with a `roomActive` UX gate mirroring the server-side check), `MacchaPage` (real マッチャ list replacing 5 hardcoded static cards, with the 5-category filter row), and `NotificationsPage` (brand new — the first genuinely new page authored this session, 6-tab filter for the 5 spec categories + "all", tap-to-mark-read).

**Two real mistakes found and fixed within this same build, before calling it done — both now permanent lessons (added to `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`):**
1. **Ordering**: `MacchaPage`'s dynamic list navigates to `MacchaChats` with a `resId` param — but that param was declared (`editPageParams`) AFTER the block that referenced it, and the compiler validates a `Navigate(...)` call's params against whatever's ALREADY been declared at that point in the operation sequence, not the final state. Fixed by moving the whole `MacchaChats` params/state/wiring block before `MacchaPage`'s.
2. **Overflow risk from copy-pasting a "nested list" pattern onto lists that weren't nested.** `MatchaItemsListView` genuinely IS nested inside `MacchaPage`'s own outer ListView, so `shrinkWrap: true` there is correct — but `ChatMessagesListView` and `NotificationsListView` are each just one of several siblings in a plain (non-scrolling) `Column`, where a shrink-wrapped list sizes to its own content instead of filling available space, risking a real `RenderFlex` overflow once there are enough items. Caught via `flutterflow ai trace latest`'s `VALIDATION_LIST_VIEW_SHRINK_WRAP`/R19 warnings (deliberately checked as a review-pass step, not assumed clean from a passing compile), not visually — no live device session exists to have seen it render. Fixed by wrapping both in `Expanded` and reverting to the default `shrinkWrap: false`.

**A third, purely mechanical lesson, hit while applying fix #2**: once `ensureReplaced` consumes a widget key, EVERY other reference to that old key anywhere else in the script breaks too — not just a second `ensureReplaced` attempt on it. An earlier-session `mutateNode(page.findByKey('ListView_84hpa09g'), ...)` call, explicitly documented as "safe to leave live and rerun on every future push" (a real, correct claim AT THE TIME, since `clearDatabaseRequest()` on an already-empty field is genuinely idempotent), broke the instant this phase's `ensureReplaced` replaced that same key — confirmed via `Bad state: page MacchaChats findByKey("ListView_84hpa09g") found no matches.` Also (re-)confirmed the already-documented `ensurePage`-body-inert failure mode (§9/§10/§26's own precedent) a second time on `NotificationsPage`'s first follow-up edit, despite having just read all three prior write-ups — a live reminder that documenting a quirk doesn't prevent re-making it under the momentum of a large new-page build.

**Explicitly not done / disclosed, not guessed:** no "new message" push/in-app notification is sent to the other chat participant — none of §3.5's 10 chat items nor this phase's own build checklist actually specify one, so none was invented. Message sender identity in the chat UI is reduced to "自分"(me)/"相手"(them) rather than the counterpart's real per-message nickname (already shown once in the room header; resolving it again per-message would need the same Admin-SDK round trip for no new information) — and chat bubbles are a uniform sender-labeled row, not left/right-aligned, since this DSL's `itemBuilder:` produces one fixed widget shape per list with no per-item structural branching (confirmed by how every other dynamic list in this project already binds properties/text per item but never swaps layout). `adminGetReportChatLog`'s own report-review path was read for the messages schema but not touched — report/block (§3.6.17/§3.7.14) is a separate, not-yet-started feature. A live device/Test-Mode click-test of the actual send/receive/filter flow remains the same standing, session-wide unverified gap as everything else built without a paired FlutterFlow Desktop session.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean across 3 iterations (2 real compile/validation errors caught and fixed: missing `args:` on 4 new custom actions, the ordering bug above); `flutterflow ai test` passed; 3 successful `flutterflow ai run` pushes (commits `AWDITj8yituydZap6Kmf`, then two follow-up fixes landing as `NNRChPiiy5J2yMklk9fI`); regenerated code read directly and confirmed for all three pages — `ListView.builder`/`Expanded` compiled exactly as intended, the `roomActive` gate, the `sendChatMessage`/refetch flow, all 5+6 filter tabs with correct category values, and `getChatRoomInfo`/`getMyMatchaList`/`fetchMyNotifications` all correctly wired; `firebase functions:list` confirms `sendChatMessage`/`getChatRoomInfo`/`getMyMatchaList` live; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; final `flutterflow ai trace latest` shows only the deliberately-accepted nested-list shrinkWrap warning (`MatchaItemsListView`) plus the project's pre-existing, already-known warning corpus (tappable-area-size, auth-navigation, custom iOS version) — nothing new or unaddressed.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (+3 callables), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (exports), `firebase/firestore.rules` (2 new rules), `dsl/edit.dart` (custom functions/actions, 3 page rewires, 1 new page, 2 mid-build fixes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): a real, confirmed category-mapping bug found and fixed in `getMyMatchaList`, before it could mislead a real user.** The original category logic only mapped `status === "cancelled" && cancelled_by === "cast"` to "断られた" (declined) — written against a partial view of the state machine (only `respondToReservation`/`confirmMeetup`/`reportCompletion`/the Capture webhook/`submitReview` were traced at design time). A full sweep of every `reservations.status` write site across the whole backend (`grep -n 'status: ["\']' *.ts`, not assumed complete from memory) found 3 more paths that write `status: "cancelled"` — `cancelPayment`'s own guest/cast fee-matrix cancellation (`cancelled_by` can be `"guest"` as well as `"cast"`), `adminForceCancel` (`cancelled_by: "admin"`), and `handlePaymentIntentFailed` (no `cancelled_by` field at all) — plus a wholly separate `status: "expired"` write (`autoCancelExpiredAuth`, the same function whose missing composite index §35 fixed earlier today). None of these 4 real, reachable status/cancelled_by combinations matched the original condition, so all fell through to the code's default bucket — meaning a guest-cancelled, admin-force-cancelled, payment-failed, or 24h-timed-out-expired reservation would have shown up under "新しい" (new) instead of anywhere resembling "this didn't happen," directly contradicting what a guest or cast would expect to see in that tab. Fixed by broadening the condition to `status === "cancelled" || status === "expired"` (regardless of `cancelled_by`) — since §3.5.9 specifies only 5 fixed categories with no dedicated cancelled/expired bucket, folding every terminal "didn't happen" outcome into the closest existing category ("断られた") is a reasonable, disclosed interpretation, not a literal-only "cast declined" reading; the doc comment on `getMyMatchaList` itself now states this explicitly as an interpretation needing eventual client confirmation, not a silently-assumed-correct-forever choice. Also added `request_pending` explicitly to the "new" bucket's condition list (previously relied on the same default-fallthrough that just proved unsafe) rather than leaving it implicit.

Also reviewed and deliberately left as a disclosed, not-fixed simplification: for a group-invite reservation with multiple `cast_ids`, both `getChatRoomInfo` and `getMyMatchaList` resolve "counterpart" as only the FIRST cast in the array — a real simplification for what should eventually be a multi-party display, but the group-invite acceptance flow itself (§3.6.9/§3.7.6) is still `[MISSING]` elsewhere in this plan, so no currently-reachable path in this app can actually put multiple real cast participants into a confirmed group chat room yet; fixing multi-party chat display now would be speculative work on a feature that doesn't exist yet, not a live bug.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; redeployed `getMyMatchaList` only (`firebase deploy --only functions:getMyMatchaList`, "Successful update operation") — a pure server-side logic fix, no DSL/client change needed since the DSL only displays whatever `category` string the callable returns, and no new Firestore query shape was introduced (pure in-memory filtering on already-fetched documents), so no new composite-index or rules risk from this fix.

**Addendum (2026-08-11, second review pass): a real, user-visible UI defect found and fixed — `NotificationsPage` displayed the literal text "true"/"false" next to every notification's title.** `fetchMyNotifications`'s delimited item format encodes the `read` boolean as its Dart string form (index 4), and the card's `itemBuilder` fed that straight into a `Text` widget with no translation step — every OTHER status-ish value shown anywhere in this project goes through a label-mapping function first (e.g. `matchaListItemFieldFn`'s category values are never shown raw, always via the filter tabs' own Japanese labels), but this one slipped through in the original build. Fixed with a new `readStatusLabel(value)` custom function (`"true"` → `既読`, else `未読`), applied via `ensureReplaced` on the list's current live key (`ListView_fev2xlkv` — the PRIOR `ensureReplaced` from the Expanded-wrap fix earlier today had already consumed `ListView_bt6qn7dp`, confirmed via the regenerated typed SDK before targeting it, applying this file's own freshly-documented "consumed keys are gone, check the regenerated SDK for the current one" lesson immediately rather than re-discovering it a third time). Also spot-checked the other two new pages' item cards for the same class of "raw value shown untranslated" defect — `MacchaPage`'s card only displays nickname/last-message (never the raw `active` boolean), `MacchaChats`' message row has no boolean field in its format at all — both clean, this was an isolated instance.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean; pushed clean (commit `reRmhoqqZb2wdvldH91J`); regenerated code confirms `functions.readStatusLabel(functions.splitField(visibleNotificationItem, 4))!` compiled exactly as intended.

### §37: Phase 7 built — review viewing, tips, report/block, minimal admin report review (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "Start Phase 7, reviews, tips, and safety" (§3.6.14-17, §3.7.13-14).

**Backend audit first, before any DSL work — and a real documentation-drift finding.** `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own `[MISSING]` markers for §3.6.14/§3.7.13 (review submission) were stale — `submitReview` was already fully wired to `reservation_detail.dart`'s "評価する" button back in Phase 4, confirmed directly against `generated_code/` (the ground truth), but the plan document was never updated when that landed. Beyond that, `processTip`, `blockUser`, `reportUser`, `adminGetReports`, and `adminResolveReport` all already existed as complete, correct Cloud Functions — this phase turned out to be almost entirely UI wiring, not backend building, once the actual state was checked rather than trusted from the plan's own markers.

**Two real backend gaps found and fixed before writing any DSL, both matching bug classes this session already knows well:**
1. `adminGetReportChatLog` existed in source, correctly exported, but was missing from live deployment — the exact §34 bug class (documented ≠ deployed). Redeployed.
2. `reviews` had no Firestore rule at all — the exact "no rule = deny-all" gap class as `chat_rooms/{id}/messages` (§36) and `users/{uid}/notifications` (§36). Fixed with a public-read rule (reviews are meant to be browsable on a cast's profile by any guest, matching the already-established `cocoten_shops`/`banners`/`prefectures` pattern for guest-facing browsable content — a Cloud Function would have been unnecessary indirection for content that's supposed to be public).

Also patched `getDiscoveryCasts` (§33's own fix) to exclude the viewer's own `blocked_users` from results — §3.6.17's literal wording is unidirectional ("the blocker's search results"), so only that direction was implemented; the reverse case (hiding a guest from a cast who blocked them) is a separate, undecided question, disclosed rather than assumed.

**Built:**
- `CastProfile`: a reviews section (average rating + review list, `reviews` collection now public-read) plus report/block buttons, appended as a new section after the existing 3-tab container — additive, no disruption to the invite buttons or tabs.
- `ReservationDetail`: a tip section (amount input + send button, wired to `processTip`), inserted between the existing review row and cancel row, visible for the guest when status is `review_pending` or `completed` (broader than `canSubmitReservationReview`'s narrower review-only window, since a tip is reasonable to send even after already reviewing).
- `AdminReportReviewPage` (new): minimal Phase-12 slice pulled forward for testability, same precedent as Phase 2's `KycReviewPage` — list pending reports, view the underlying chat log, resolve. Deliberately NOT combined with a "resolve + freeze" action (the sister-project-proven pattern §3.8a documents for Phase 12 proper) since `adminResolveReport` itself doesn't implement a freeze side-effect today (confirmed by reading it — its `action` param is accepted but only ever passed to the audit log) — building that now would mean extending `admin.ts` speculatively ahead of the real Phase 12 design.

**One real compile-blocking issue found and worked around, not fully root-caused.** `ClearTextField('X')` failed validation ("trying to clear a widget that doesn't exist") for all 3 new TextFields (`ReportReasonField`, `TipAmountField`, `AdminNoteField`) — despite the name matching exactly, and despite the identical pattern (`ClearTextField('ChatMessageInput')`) working correctly in Phase 6. The likely difference: Phase 6's working instance had the `TextField` created via one `ensureReplaced` call and the `ClearTextField` action wired via a SEPARATE `ensureActions` call on a different, pre-existing widget; all 3 of this phase's new instances have the `TextField` AND the button that clears it created together in the SAME `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensurePage` call — a same-batch self-reference the validator may not resolve. Not conclusively confirmed (no way to test the hypothesis directly this session). Worked around by dropping the 3 `ClearTextField` calls — `SetState(field, '')` already correctly clears the underlying value (so a resubmit doesn't send stale data), the only loss is that the input box visually retains its old text until the user starts typing again, a minor disclosed UX gap, not a functional one.

**Same R19-class shrinkWrap-without-Expanded overflow risk as Phase 6, found again on review and fixed the same way.** `PendingReportsListView` and `SelectedReportChatLogListView` on the new admin page are both siblings in a plain, non-scrolling Column (not nested inside another scrollable) — wrapped both in `Expanded`, reverted to the default `shrinkWrap: false`. `CastReviewsListView` was checked and confirmed genuinely nested (`CastProfile.body[0].children[0]` is itself a `ListView`), so its `shrinkWrap: true` is correct and was left alone — checked before assuming every new list needed the same fix, not applied reflexively.

**Explicitly not done / disclosed, not guessed:** no confirmation dialog before blocking (fires immediately); the "no chat log" sentinel in the admin chat-log viewer renders with a blank nickname prefix before the reason text (`"  チャットログがありません。"`) — legible but not polished; `processTip`'s backend (pre-existing, not touched this phase) doesn't verify any relationship between the caller and the target cast before accepting a tip — any authenticated guest could tip any cast_id directly via the API, a pre-existing design choice noted but out of this phase's scope to change; a live device/Test-Mode click-test of the actual review/tip/report/block flow remains the same standing, session-wide unverified gap as everything else.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `dart analyze` clean across 2 iterations; `flutterflow ai test` passed; 2 successful `flutterflow ai run` pushes (commits `ouC8YorO9ZIHAjqsemCW`, then the Expanded-wrap fix as `Uze1B2XRe5SFddcy3SOZ`); regenerated code read directly and confirmed for all three pages — `functions.averageRatingLabel`/`callReportUser`/`callBlockUser` on `CastProfile`, the `canSendTip`-gated tip section calling `callProcessTip` on `ReservationDetail`, and `checkIsAdminUser`/`fetchPendingReports`/`fetchReportChatLog`/`callResolveReport` all correctly wired on `AdminReportReviewPage`; `firebase functions:list` confirms `adminGetReportChatLog`/`getDiscoveryCasts` live; final `flutterflow ai trace latest` shows only the 3 deliberately-accepted nested-list shrinkWrap warnings (`CastReviewsListView`, `MatchaItemsListView`, and MacchaPage's own outer list) plus the project's pre-existing warning corpus — nothing new or unaddressed.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (`getDiscoveryCasts` blocked-user filter), `firebase/firestore.rules` (`reviews` rule), `dsl/edit.dart` (custom functions/actions, 2 page insertions, 1 new page, 1 mid-build fix), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (stale `[MISSING]` markers corrected, Phase 7 marked done), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): a MAJOR bug found and fixed — the entire review-viewing feature this phase just built was completely non-functional, silently, from the moment it shipped.** `fetchCastReviews`'s query (`reviews.where('reviewee_id', isEqualTo: castId).orderBy('created_at', descending: true)`) combines an equality filter with an `orderBy` on a different field — exactly the composite-index-requiring shape §35 already established a checklist for — but `reviews` had ZERO composite indexes in `firestore.indexes.json` (confirmed by reading the file directly, not assumed). Every call would have thrown `FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index`, silently absorbed by the action's own try/catch into an empty list — meaning `CastReviewsListView` would always render empty and `averageRatingLabelFn` would always show "レビューなし", for every cast, regardless of how many real reviews existed. This is the same severity class as §33 (Home ranking query) and §35 (`autoCancelExpiredAuth`) — a feature that compiled clean, pushed clean, and read correctly in the regenerated code, while being entirely dead at runtime.
