Fixed by adding the missing composite index (`reviews`: `reviewee_id` ASC + `created_at` DESC) and deploying (`firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes`, confirmed registered via `firebase firestore:indexes`).

**Root cause of nearly missing this at build time, not just what was missed**: §35's own project_rules.md entry frames the composite-index checklist around "Cloud Function query," and this was a CLIENT-SIDE custom action query instead — Firestore's composite-index requirement applies identically regardless of which SDK issues the query (Admin or Client), but the rule's own wording created a scope-framing blind spot that nearly let this slip through even with the general principle already known. Fixed the rule's own wording in `project_rules.md` to close that gap explicitly, not just the one instance.

**Swept for the same gap class across every other query touched or added this phase, not just the one instance found**: `getDiscoveryCasts`'s new addition is a single-document `.get()` (no query, no index question); `adminGetReports`'s pre-existing `status`+`created_at` query already has its own index (confirmed, unaffected by this phase's changes); `adminGetReportChatLog`'s two queries are both single-field (`chat_rooms.where('res_id',...)`, then `messages.orderBy('created_at')` with no other filter) — neither needs a composite index. `reviews` was the only gap.

**Verification:** `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` succeeded ("Deploy complete!"); `firebase firestore:indexes --project icoccha` confirms the new index registered (`reviewee_id` ASC + `created_at` DESC + the implicit `__name__` tie-breaker). No DSL/TypeScript source changed for this fix — pure Firestore configuration, so no new push was needed.

### §38: Phase 8 built — WalletPage (live Stripe balance + history), minimal SettingsPage (account deletion) (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "Start Phase 8, wallet and payout" (§3.7.9-11, §3.9.15).

**Backend audit first, again.** `requestPayout` (withdrawal request → admin approval → payout, gated on `logical_debt==0` AND Stripe available balance>0) and `requestWithdrawal` (account deletion, gated on `logical_debt==0`, no active reservations, no pending ledger) both already existed as complete, correct Cloud Functions, confirmed by reading them in full. Only `getWalletBalance` (a live, uncached Stripe balance query — §3.7.9's own "must reflect Stripe truth, not a locally cached copy" requirement) was genuinely missing; added it, scoped to the cast's own connected account (`stripeAccount: userData.stripe_account_id`), same pattern already proven correct in `requestPayout`.

**Two composite-index bugs found and fixed BEFORE any DSL was written — a first for this session; every prior instance (§33, §35, §37) was found AFTER the feature had already shipped.**
1. `requestWithdrawal`'s two reservation queries (`guest_id`/`cast_ids` + `status not-in`) were explicitly disclosed in §35 as a known gap, deliberately left unfixed at the time because nothing called the function yet, with an explicit note to "revisit the moment it gets a caller." Phase 8's own checklist item — surfacing account-deletion blocking in a settings UI — is exactly that caller. Verified the gap was still real (not just theoretical) using §37's newly-established direct-Firestore-REST-query technique (`gcloud auth print-access-token` + `curl .../documents:runQuery`) rather than assuming the disclosure was still accurate — both queries failed with `FAILED_PRECONDITION` exactly as expected. Fixed by adding both composite indexes, then re-ran the SAME direct queries after the index finished building and confirmed success. This is the first time this session completed the full verification loop — missing → confirmed broken → fixed → confirmed working — using nothing but direct Firestore REST calls, no device, no Cloud Function logs (`requestWithdrawal`'s queries run inside a callable, so `firebase functions:log` could theoretically have caught this too, but only after a real call happened; the REST technique caught and confirmed it pre-emptively, before any real user could trigger it).
2. `ledger` had no `user_id`+`created_at` composite index either — would have broken `WalletPage`'s own transaction history the exact same silent way `reviews` broke in Phase 7 (§37's addendum), if built without checking first. Found and fixed before ever wiring the query, not after shipping it.

**Built:**
- `WalletPage` (new): live balance (available + pending, direct from Stripe, never cached), a withdraw button gated on `logicalDebt<=0 && available>0` (mirroring `requestPayout`'s own server-side gate as a UX-only pre-check, same pattern as every other client-side gate this session — the backend independently re-enforces regardless), and a transaction history list sourced from the `ledger` collection directly (content-based rule, `user_id==request.auth.uid`, provably safe — no Cloud Function needed for the history half, only the live balance needed server-side Stripe access).
- `SettingsPage` (new, deliberately minimal): account deletion only, with the specific block reason shown to the user rather than a generic failure message — a deliberate, disclosed deviation from this session's usual "generic bool return, generic Snackbar" custom-action convention (established and consistently used in Phase 6/7 for lower-stakes actions), justified here because knowing WHY account deletion is blocked (debt vs. active reservation vs. pending settlement) is meaningfully more valuable to the user than for a failed chat message or tip. Confirmed `Snackbar(...)` accepts a dynamic expression (`ActionOutput(...)`), not just a static string literal, before relying on it — checked the SDK constructor directly rather than assuming.
- Wired both pages' only entry point: `MyPage`'s drawer menu has 7 static, never-wired category rows (confirmed via the typed SDK — no `ON_TAP` action on any of them despite having triggers declared); wired "報酬・売上・決済管理" → `WalletPage` and "アカウント・基本管理" → `SettingsPage`, the two closest-matching categories. `MyPage` is confirmed cast-only (its own 3-tab structure, and §3.7.9's own wording — guests have no Stripe Connect account to mirror), so no additional role gate was needed.

**Applied the `ensurePage`-body-inert lesson proactively this time, instead of rediscovering it a fourth time.** After the first push landed `WalletPage`/`SettingsPage`, immediately froze both `ensurePage` calls before attempting the follow-up push that wires `MyPage`'s drawer to them — this is the exact scenario (a second push referencing an already-created page) that broke `NotificationsPage` and `AdminReportReviewPage` earlier this session. Confirmed clean: the drawer-wiring push compiled and pushed successfully on the first attempt, no fix-cycle needed. Two now-orphaned `app.customFunction(...)` handle captures (`ledgerTypeLabelFn`/`formatYenFn`/`canWithdrawNowFn` — their only consumer was the now-frozen `WalletPage` body) were caught by `dart analyze`'s `unused_local_variable` warning and cleaned up the same way as Phase 6/7's `matchaFilterTab`/`notifFilterTab` — dropped the local capture, kept the underlying `app.customFunction(...)` declaration (still needed; re-declaring with the same name/signature in a future edit is a safe way to get a fresh handle if these functions are ever referenced again).

**`ledger.type` label mapping built from the confirmed-real value set, not memory or guessing** — grepped every actual `.collection("ledger")` write site across the whole backend (`reward`, `staff_fee`, `tip`, `refund`, `affiliate`); a 6th value, `debt_offset`, appears only in prose comments describing a planned category that was never actually implemented as a real write anywhere, so no label was invented for it — the mapping falls back to the raw string for anything unmapped, so a future real `debt_offset` write wouldn't silently vanish, just show untranslated until updated.

**Explicitly not done / disclosed, not guessed:** `SettingsPage` is deliberately minimal (account deletion only) — notification preferences, ToS/privacy-policy viewers, FAQ, and version info all stay Phase 11 scope, which already separately owns "account deletion" in its own My-Page-IA checklist (a real overlap between the two phases' own wording, resolved by building the one piece Phase 8 actually needs now rather than the other page's full scope early); no distinction is drawn between income-type and deduction-type ledger entries in the history display (all 5 confirmed types are shown the same way, no +/- coloring) since `refund`'s exact semantic from a cast's-own-wallet perspective wasn't fully certain and inventing a treatment for it risked being wrong; a live device/Test-Mode click-test of the actual balance/withdraw/delete flow remains the same standing, session-wide gap.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `dart analyze` clean; `flutterflow ai test` passed; 2 successful `flutterflow ai run` pushes, BOTH clean on the first attempt (commits `2mtnfxXTOPxXXSqFDuqQ`, then the drawer-wiring push as `TfPRuPM0Jw7ZJfOqp0zX`) — no fix-cycle needed for either, a first for a multi-page phase this session; regenerated code confirmed `functions.formatYen`/`functions.canWithdrawNow`/`functions.ledgerTypeLabel`/`actions.fetchWalletBalance`/`actions.callRequestPayout` on `WalletPage`, `actions.callRequestWithdrawal` on `SettingsPage`, and `context.pushNamed(WalletPageWidget.routeName)`/`SettingsPageWidget.routeName` correctly wired from `MyPage`'s drawer; `firebase functions:list` confirms `getWalletBalance` live; both new composite indexes confirmed via direct Firestore REST queries succeeding (not just registered); final `flutterflow ai trace latest` shows only the same 3 already-accepted nested-list shrinkWrap warnings, unchanged from Phase 7 — the new `WalletHistoryListView` never triggered one at all, since it was wrapped in `Expanded` from the start this time instead of needing a follow-up fix.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (+`getWalletBalance`), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (export), `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (3 new composite indexes: `reservations` guest_id+status, `reservations` cast_ids+status, `ledger` user_id+created_at), `dsl/edit.dart` (custom functions/actions, 2 new pages, `MyPage` drawer wiring), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

**Addendum (2026-08-11, review pass): two real findings, one latent defect fixed defensively and one genuine UX dead-end fixed for real.**

1. **`formatYen`'s comma-grouping loop mishandled negative amounts** — for `n = -5000`, the loop counted the `-` character as a digit-grouping position, producing `"-5,0,00"` instead of `"-5,000"`. Traced every ledger `type` a cast's own `WalletPage` could actually display to confirm this was currently UNREACHABLE, not a live bug: `reward` entries cap debt deduction at `Math.min(currentDebt, totalCastAmount)` so `net_transfer` can never go negative (confirmed by reading the multi-cast-transfer transaction directly); `refund` entries are scoped to `user_id: resData.guest_id`, never a cast, so they never appear in a cast's own wallet history at all (`MyPage`/`WalletPage` are both cast-only). Fixed anyway, defensively — the fix is trivial (separate the sign from the digit-grouping) and this is exactly the kind of small generic utility this session has repeatedly reused elsewhere, including in this phase's own disclosed "still open" debt-history-view gap, which would plausibly want to display a negative amount through this same function eventually. Hit a real mechanical snag applying the fix: `formatYen`'s ORIGINAL `app.customFunction(...)` declaration still had the old buggy `code:` sitting in the script, and adding a separate `updateCustomFunction(...)` call after it (without also updating the original declaration) made `ensureCustomFunction` see a payload mismatch against what the update had already applied remotely, failing with `ensureCustomFunction found an existing custom function named "formatYen" with a different payload` — fixed by updating the ORIGINAL declaration's `code:` to match, restoring idempotency, rather than leaving two diverging copies of the same function's intended body in the script.

2. **A genuine UX dead-end**: `fetchWalletBalance` returns `has_stripe_account`, but nothing in `WalletPage` ever read or displayed it — a cast who hasn't finished Stripe Connect onboarding yet would land on the page and see an unexplained "¥0" with no indication of why or what to do about it. Fixed by adding the missing state field and a guidance section (message + a button straight to `ConnectOnboarding`, confirmed to need zero params) that only shows when no Stripe account exists yet. Extending the already-wired `ON_INIT_STATE` chain required re-declaring it in full via `ensureActions` rather than adding a second call on the same trigger (this project's own hard rule) — since the ORIGINAL declaration is now frozen/inert, this is the only ACTIVE `ensureActions` call on that trigger in this script, not a second one racing it. Hit a genuine chicken-and-egg compile error doing this: the brand-new `hasStripeAccountStr` field doesn't exist in the LOCAL typed SDK yet (it's being created in this same script), so `ff.Pages.walletPage.state.hasStripeAccountStr` failed with `undefined_getter` — fixed by using bare-string `State('hasStripeAccountStr')`/`SetState('hasStripeAccountStr', ...)` for that ONE newly-created field specifically, while keeping typed handles for the other four already-existing fields in the SAME action list — confirmed the compiler accepts this mix within one script (the "use typed handles" rule applies per-field based on whether that specific field already exists remotely, not as an all-or-nothing rule for the whole action list).

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean across 3 iterations; both fixes pushed clean (commits `eGPdk6C2cBcsONee1CF8`, then `TRFIdbxPki8DTNo1HuMA`); regenerated code confirms `_model.hasStripeAccountStr` populated on load and `ConnectOnboardingWidget.routeName` correctly wired behind the new guidance button; `flutterflow ai trace latest` shows no new warnings.

**Addendum (2026-08-11, second review pass): a real, confirmed data-accuracy gap fixed, and — while fixing it — a genuine regression self-inflicted and caught before being trusted.**

**The finding**: `WalletPage`'s transaction history showed every `ledger` entry identically regardless of its own `status` field, but `status` is a real, meaningful distinction, not decorative — read every `ledger`-collection write site across the whole backend to confirm: `reward` entries are written `status: needsTransfer ? "pending" : "confirmed"` and later flipped to `"confirmed"` once the real Stripe Transfer succeeds (or `"retrying"` if it fails and gets retried) — a "pending"/"retrying" reward entry means the money has NOT actually arrived in the cast's Stripe balance yet, only been recorded as owed. Every other type (`staff_fee`, `tip`, `affiliate`, `refund`) is always written `"confirmed"` immediately, since each is only written AFTER its own Transfer/refund API call already succeeded — so this only ever matters for `reward` entries in practice. Showing a not-yet-transferred amount with no indication it's still pending directly contradicts §3.7.9's own "must reflect Stripe truth" requirement. Fixed by adding `status` as a 4th field to the history data and a status-label function (blank for `confirmed`, since that's the expected/normal case; a label only for the two non-final states).

**A genuinely new, valuable mechanical finding surfaced applying this fix — refining, not just repeating, the `formatYen` two-part-update lesson from the prior review pass.** Tried updating BOTH the original `app.customAction('fetchWalletLedgerHistory', ...)` declaration AND adding an `updateCustomAction` call for the real fix in the SAME push, exactly as documented for `formatYen` — and it failed the same way `formatYen` initially did (`ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action... with a different payload`). The refinement: `ensureCustomAction`'s idempotency check runs against whatever's live AT THE START of the compile, so pointing the ORIGINAL declaration at the NEW content in the SAME push it's meant to apply always fails — the original's own `ensure*` call and the later `update*` call don't get to "meet in the middle" within one push, regardless of order in the file. The correct sequencing (confirmed by how `formatYen`'s fix actually succeeded, re-examined after this failure): apply the real change via `update*` alone first, leave the original declaration matching CURRENTLY-LIVE content so its own idempotency check still passes THIS push, and only sync the original to the new content in a LATER, SEPARATE push once the remote state is confirmed to have actually changed. Even then, hit a second-order version of the same issue: `updateCustomAction` had only touched `code:`, not `description:` — so syncing the original declaration's `description:` to the new text (describing the added 4th field) ALSO mismatched the still-unchanged remote description and failed again, fixed by leaving the description text alone (a purely cosmetic field, not worth a 3rd push to chase).

**The self-inflicted regression**: fixing the history item's WIDGET content required `ensureReplaced` on the list, and the FIRST attempt targeted the inner `WalletHistoryListView`'s own key (`ListView_6jqh17yr`) with a bare `ListView(...)` replacement — reasoning, WRONGLY, that the surrounding `Expanded(..., name: 'WalletHistoryExpanded')` wrapper (built correctly from the start in the original Phase 8 push) would survive untouched since it was never itself targeted. Confirmed via direct inspection of `generated_code/lib/wallet_page/wallet_page_widget.dart` after the push that this was wrong: `Expanded` no longer appeared anywhere in the file — `ensureReplaced(target, replacement)` replaces the ENTIRE SLOT the target key occupies, collapsing any wrapper that isn't itself part of the replacement tree, even when the target key names an inner widget rather than the wrapper. This silently reintroduced the exact overflow risk the original Expanded fix had closed. **`flutterflow ai trace latest`'s own R19/`VALIDATION_LIST_VIEW_SHRINK_WRAP` check did not catch this at all** — that check only fires on `shrinkWrap: true`; it has no check for the opposite misconfiguration (unbounded list, shrinkWrap left at its false default, no `Expanded`) — so a clean `flutterflow ai trace latest` was never actually reassurance for this specific class of regression; only reading the regenerated widget tree directly caught it. Re-fixed by including `Expanded(...)` in the replacement tree this time (re-checking the typed SDK for the CURRENT live key first — `ListView_1r7nitqx`, a third key for this same logical widget, since each `ensureReplaced` assigns a fresh one) and re-verified via the same direct-inspection method, this time confirming success (`Expanded(flex: 1, child: Builder(...))` present, matching the original correct structure).

This distinguishes cleanly from the 3 EARLIER Expanded-wrap fixes this session (`ChatMessagesListView`/`NotificationsListView`/`PendingReportsListView`+`SelectedReportChatLogListView`), which were all "bare list, never wrapped → wrap it" operations — adding a wrapper where none existed only ever adds, never loses, structure, so those were safe by construction and already verified correct at the time. This regression was specifically about touching a widget that was ALREADY wrapped a second time, which is a materially different, riskier operation this session hadn't encountered before.

**Verification:** `dart analyze` clean across all 3 iterations of this addendum's fixes; 3 pushes (commits `jonkpKIpAp3bfQd1n2vv` for the status-label fix, `1QqT6Npfqpra8zxpqgpa` for the description-only original-declaration sync, `lUMePUqg1kGogc9k8Wke` for the Expanded-wrapper restoration) — all clean compiles, but the MIDDLE one (status-label fix) required direct `generated_code` inspection to catch the regression the validator missed, not just a clean push/trace. Both mechanical lessons (the refined two-part update sequencing; `ensureReplaced` collapsing un-included wrappers) added to `project_rules.md`.

### §39: Full-project expert-level audit — retroactive regression check, comprehensive callable/index/rules sweeps (2026-08-11)

**What was asked:** "thoroughly analyze the work completed so far... identify various errors, debugging mistakes, and incorrectly implemented parts... fix them perfectly... from an expert's perspective" — broader than the standing per-phase review directive, this explicitly asked for a full-project re-audit, not a Phase 8-scoped one. Picked four angles deliberately distinct from the four full-project passes already done earlier in the session (§32 DSL field-name correctness, §33 Firestore rules satisfiability, §34 Cloud Functions deployment state, §35 composite-index coverage as of that point) and from the Phase 6/7/8-scoped reviews since — since Phases 6-8 added substantial new surface area after all of those.

**1. Retroactive check for other `ensureReplaced`-wrapper-collapse regressions** (applying the bug class just discovered fixing WalletPage, §38's second addendum, to everything built earlier this session): checked all 4 other `Expanded`-wrapped lists (`ChatMessagesListView`, `NotificationsListView`, `PendingReportsListView`, `SelectedReportChatLogListView`) directly against `generated_code` — each has exactly one `Expanded` occurrence, confirming none regressed. None of these were ever touched a second time after their original "bare list → wrap it" fix (a fundamentally safer operation than "already-wrapped → replace inner key," which is what caused the WalletPage regression) — `WalletHistoryListView` was the only list ever edited twice, and is now fixed. Clean.

**2. Full `httpsCallable` liveness sweep, redone from scratch** (§34's original methodology, not re-run in full since Phase 5): extracted all 26 distinct callable names referenced anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart`/`generated_code`, diffed against a fresh `firebase functions:list --project icoccha`. Zero gaps — every callable this project's DSL actually calls is confirmed live. A meaningful confirmation that the "verify liveness during the build, not after" habit adopted following §34's original discovery has held up across three more phases of newly-added or newly-wired callables — **correcting this entry's own first-draft wording**, which mis-sorted `getDiscoveryCasts` into "Phase 6" (it was actually built in §33, well before Phase 6 existed as a concept, and wasn't touched again during Phase 6's own build) and imprecisely called `requestPayout`/`requestWithdrawal` "new" to Phase 8 when both were pre-existing functions Phase 8 simply gave a first real CALLER to — only `getWalletBalance` was genuinely new there. Accurately: Phase 6 added `getChatRoomInfo`/`getMyMatchaList`/`sendChatMessage` (plus reused the already-existing `getDiscoveryCasts`); Phase 7 added `adminGetReportChatLog`'s redeployment and newly wired the pre-existing `processTip`/`reportUser`/`blockUser`; Phase 8 added `getWalletBalance` and newly wired the pre-existing `requestPayout`/`requestWithdrawal`.

**3. Full composite-index sweep across the entire backend + DSL**, not just what was checked ad-hoc during each phase's build: grepped every `.where()` call across all 8 backend source files (re-counted directly this review pass — the original write-up said 7) and all client-side custom actions. Everything reachable from this project's own DSL is either safe by construction (pure-equality, no orderBy) or already fixed in an earlier phase (§35's `autoCancelExpiredAuth`/`processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` fixes, §37's `reviews` fix, §38's `ledger` AND `requestWithdrawal` fixes — correcting this entry's own first-draft wording, which had misattributed `requestWithdrawal`'s fix to §35: §35 only DISCLOSED that gap as unreachable at the time and deliberately left it unfixed; §38/Phase 8 is where it actually got fixed, once the account-deletion UI gave it a real caller). Found and fixed one new, genuinely reachable-once-called gap that hadn't been checked before: `adminGetProcessedEvents`'s `event_type`+`processed_at` query had no matching index (added one — this function has only ONE optional filter, so no combinatorial-explosion concern, a clean deterministic fix). Also found — but deliberately did NOT fix — `adminGetLedger`'s much larger latent gap: 4 independently-optional equality filters (`user_id`/`res_id`/`type`/`status`) all combinable before an `orderBy(created_at)`, only a handful of the 2⁴-1 possible combinations actually indexed. Building every combination would be the exact "combinatorial index explosion" anti-pattern `project_rules.md` already warns against for dynamic multi-optional-filter queries; instead added a warning comment matching the STYLE an earlier, non-session pass had already used on the sibling `adminGetReservations` function (which solves the identical problem by restricting callers to one optional filter at a time — a documented, deliberate design constraint, not a bug, confirmed by reading that existing comment directly rather than assuming it was an oversight). Both `adminGetLedger` and `adminGetProcessedEvents` are confirmed unreachable from this project's current DSL (absent from the same liveness sweep in item 2) — the processed_events index was still worth adding pre-emptively since it was a one-line, zero-risk, deterministic fix; the ledger combinatorial gap was not, since "fixing" it properly requires either restricting the function's own contract (a design decision, not available to make unilaterally) or building excessive speculative infrastructure.

**4. Comprehensive Firestore rules coverage audit**, proactive rather than reactive (the three prior rules gaps this session — `chat_rooms/{id}/messages`, `users/{uid}/notifications`, `reviews` — were each found only because a feature being actively built needed to read that exact collection): cross-referenced all 21 top-level collections the backend actually writes to against `firestore.rules`. Found 4 more with zero rule coverage — `debt_history`, `extensions`, `pair_history`, `work_posts` — but confirmed, by grepping for any client-side `.collection(...)` read attempt, that NONE of them are currently read by anything client-side. Unlike the 3 earlier gaps (each an ACTIVE bug at the moment it was found, since a real feature was mid-build against it), a "no rule" Firestore collection defaults to deny-all, which is the SAFE default, not a security hole — these are functionality gaps for whenever something eventually needs to read them, not live bugs today. Deliberately not fixed speculatively: the correct rule shape (owner-scoped like `ledger`? admin-only? something else?) isn't obvious without a real consumer to validate the access pattern against, and guessing wrong risks either over-exposing data or building the wrong shape that needs redoing anyway once a real feature arrives.

**5. Storage rules cross-check**: confirmed the one and only upload path pattern used anywhere in this codebase (`pickAndUploadImage`, reused for KYC docs, profile photos, and any other image upload) always writes to `users/$uid/...`, which the existing owner-only storage rule already covers fully. Clean, no gap.

**Verification:** the `processed_events` index deploy confirmed via the same direct-Firestore-REST-query technique used in §37/§38 — registered, built, and a real query against it now succeeds cleanly (`readTime` returned, no `FAILED_PRECONDITION`). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after the `adminGetLedger` comment addition (no functional code changed, comment-only). No DSL changes this pass — every fix was backend-config (indexes) or backend-comment (documentation) only, so no `flutterflow ai run` push was needed.

**Explicitly disclosed, not fixed, with reasoning for each:** `adminGetLedger`'s combinatorial index gap (documented in-source, matches an existing sibling pattern, needs a design decision not a mechanical fix); the 4 rule-less collections (`debt_history`/`extensions`/`pair_history`/`work_posts`, all currently unreachable, correct rule shape not yet knowable without a real consumer); `processTip`'s lack of any caller-target relationship check (re-examined this pass specifically for a security angle — concluded it only ever lets a user spend their OWN money on an arbitrary cast, not access anyone else's funds or data, so it's a minor product-design question about abuse-prevention, not a coding security bug, and building fraud-prevention logic is outside a code-review pass's proper scope without product/compliance input).

**Files touched:** `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (+1 composite index: `processed_events` event_type+processed_at), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (comment-only, documenting `adminGetLedger`'s index-combination risk), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §40: Phase 9 built — affiliate system, 4 confirmed backend bugs fixed, referral system was silently dead since it was built (2026-08-11)

**Backend audit first, same discipline as every phase.** Read `affiliate.ts` in full and found it has ZERO functions that write to `affiliate_rewards` — the accrual step lives entirely in `stripe-payments.ts`'s `processAffiliateRewards` (called from the Capture-success webhook, confirming §4.2's accrual-at-Capture/transfer-on-monthly-batch timing split IS correctly wired end-to-end), which `affiliate.ts` itself doesn't even import. Found and fixed four confirmed bugs, each verified against the plan's own literal spec wording (§3.7.12, lines 158-164) before touching code, each deployed and `tsc --noEmit`-verified individually:

**1. Mutual-approval gate missing entirely at accrual time** (`processAffiliateRewards`, stripe-payments.ts): the guard only checked `referrerData.is_active`/`is_frozen`, never `referrerData.approval_status` nor `castData.approval_status` (the referred cast) at all — a direct violation of the plan's own unambiguous line 161 ("reward only accrues for periods where **both** the referrer and the referred cast are simultaneously in `approval_status == approved`"). Fixed: both checks added, `continue`s past accrual if either fails.

**2. Deferral implemented as permanent forfeiture** (`processAffiliatorPayment`, affiliate.ts): the `workDays < minDays` branch called `forfeitRewards` (permanent `status: "forfeited"`) when the plan's line 162 explicitly says this case **defers** ("stays `pending`, re-evaluable... it is not itself a forfeiture event... don't conflate the two"). Beyond the label bug, the ORIGINAL monthly batch query (`.where("month", "==", monthStr).where("status", "==", "pending")`, where `monthStr` was hardcoded to "last calendar month") meant a deferred reward would never be re-queried by any LATER run anyway — "re-evaluable" wasn't actually possible under the old query shape even if the forfeit call were removed. Fixed both halves together: the workDays branch no longer forfeits (just logs and returns, leaving `pending` untouched), and `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` was restructured to query ALL `pending` rewards regardless of month, grouped by `(affiliator_uid, month)` pairs (not just `affiliator_uid`), so each accrual month's own eligibility is independently re-evaluated on every run — a deferred reward genuinely gets reconsidered next month now, not silently orphaned.

**3. `config.AFFILIATE_MIN_DAYS` casing mismatch** (affiliate.ts): the exact same bug class §30-era `config.ts` fix already addressed once (`SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` keys were UPPER_SNAKE_CASE against Firestore's real lower_snake_case fields) recurred at a CALL SITE that was never updated when the root cause was fixed — `config.AFFILIATE_MIN_DAYS` (still uppercase) never matched `config.ts`'s corrected `affiliate_min_days` key, so it silently always fell back to the hardcoded literal `3` in affiliate.ts itself, coincidentally matching the real default and masking the bug — it would have broken silently the moment an admin actually customized `affiliate_min_days` via `adminUpdateSystemConfig`. Fixed: lowercase key. Also fixed the adjacent, related issue the plan's own file-list section (line 329) flagged: the cron schedule hardcoded `"5"` (`"0 3 5 * *"`) can't read Firestore's `affiliate_payment_day` at Cloud Scheduler deploy time regardless of any code fix — schedule strings are static. Fixed the only achievable way: the schedule now runs daily (`"0 3 * * *"`) and the handler early-exits unless `new Date().getDate() === config.affiliate_payment_day`, making the payment day genuinely admin-editable instead of a literal.

**4. Referred-cast-leaves forfeiture wasn't month-scoped** (requestWithdrawal, auth.ts + processAffiliatorPayment, affiliate.ts): the plan's line 163 states an explicitly asymmetric rule — referrer leaving forfeits ALL their unpaid-but-accrued reward (no month scoping, confirmed already correct in the existing `requestWithdrawal` code); a REFERRED cast leaving forfeits only the reward attributable to them **in the month of departure**, prior months' already-earned-but-still-pending reward should still pay out normally. The existing code had no explicit handling for the referred-cast case at all — it relied entirely on `processAffiliatorPayment`'s per-reward loop checking `referredData.is_active`, which would forfeit **every** pending reward for that referred cast regardless of which month it accrued in, violating the "prior months are safe" half of the rule the moment a referred cast had any older deferred reward outstanding. Fixed: `requestWithdrawal` now stamps `left_at: Timestamp.now()` on voluntary leave; the per-reward check compares `left_at`'s month against the reward's own `month` field, forfeiting only an exact match and paying prior-month reward normally. Deliberately falls back to the OLD forfeit-everything behavior when `is_active` is false but `left_at` is absent (i.e. `adminForceDeleteUser`, an admin-driven ban, not a voluntary leave) — the plan's own text (line 163's own caveat) explicitly flags freeze-as-leave-equivalence as unconfirmed with the client, so this session didn't invent an answer for it.

**Change-history requirement.** The plan's Phase 9 checklist (line 452) wants the admin rate-override UI built "with change history." Found `affiliate_rate_history`'s Firestore schema already declared in this project (`dsl/edit.dart`'s Phase-0/1-era schema fixup, `generated_code/lib/backend/schema/affiliate_rate_history_record.dart`) but completely dead: no writer anywhere in the backend, no reader, and — checked directly against `firestore.rules` — **no security rule at all**, a 5th rule-less collection this session has now found (alongside §39's 4 already-disclosed ones), previously undetected because nothing had ever tried to use it. Wired `adminUpdateAffiliateRate` to write a record here (alongside its pre-existing generic `audit_logs` entry, kept for the general admin action trail), added the Firestore rule (admin-read-only, same lockdown pattern as `audit_logs`/`processed_events`), added the `affiliator_uid`+`changed_at` composite index, and added a new `adminGetAffiliateRateHistory` callable to read one affiliator's own history. Also added a `target_id` filter (+matching composite index) to the pre-existing generic `adminGetAuditLogs` callable while in there — a small, independently useful addition, not required for this specific requirement once the dedicated collection was wired, but left in since it's harmless and generically useful for any future admin audit-trail UI.

**Referral-code system was silently completely dead since it was built.** Cross-referencing `completeOnboarding` (auth.ts) and `getAffiliateDashboard` confirmed the "referral code" IS the referrer's own Firebase UID (`completeOnboarding` looks up `users/{referral_code}` directly, requires `account_type == 'cast'`; `getAffiliateDashboard` returns `referral_code: uid`) — there is no separately-generated invite code anywhere in the backend. The PRE-EXISTING `Affiliate` page and `AffiliateQrCodeBottomSheet` component (both built in an earlier, non-Phase-9 pass) bind every displayed/copied/shared referral code to `currentUserDocument?.invitationCode` instead — a schema field that is declared but **never written anywhere in the backend**, always resolving empty. This means the referral code has never actually displayed, copied, or shared correctly since these widgets were built — a previously-undiscovered, currently-live bug affecting the entire referral acquisition funnel (QR code, copy button, LINE/X/Threads/generic-share buttons all affected). Fixed the copy button and all 4 share buttons by rebinding to the real uid. **Disclosed, not fixed:** the QR barcode's own `data:` source could not be located as a DSL-patchable property — `flutterflow ai docs ui`/`api-surface` and every reference file were checked directly and none cover a `Barcode` widget or a `LaunchUrl`/`Share` action node type, strongly suggesting this component was hand-built in the FlutterFlow IDE rather than through this SDK. Left as a known gap rather than guessing at `mutateNode`-level proto edits with no reference to validate the field name against.

**A second, unrelated SDK/codegen gap found and worked around mid-phase:** `page.ensureActions(icon, ON_TAP, [CopyToClipboard(CustomFunction(...))])` compiled clean (`compileDslApp` passed, zero warnings referencing it) and pushed with no error, but the pushed proto silently did not change — confirmed directly in `generated_code` even after a forced `codegen refresh`, the icon's `onTap` still showed the original dead binding. Every OTHER `ensureActions` call in the exact same push (4 share buttons via `CallCustomAction.named`, the repurposed status button) landed correctly, confirmed the same way — isolating the gap specifically to `CopyToClipboard` wrapping a `CustomFunction` expression argument (the reference examples for `CopyToClipboard` only ever show literals/`Constant`/map-index expressions, never a `CustomFunction`). Worked around by writing a small custom action (`copyReferralCodeToClipboard`) instead of the native DSL action — the same proven mechanism already used successfully for the share buttons. **New quirk for `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`.**

**Dashboard cards — extended real data instead of leaving placeholders.** `getAffiliateDashboard` originally returned 11 fields; the pre-existing static mockup UI has ~20 metric rows (work hours by day/week/month/cumulative, work days by week/month/cumulative, reward by day/week/month/cumulative, active-vs-inactive team counts). Checked whether these are backed by real schema data before building anything: `reservations.duration_minutes` (exists) for hours, completed-reservation dates for days, `affiliate_rewards.reward_amount`/`created_at` for reward-by-period, `users.is_active` for the active/inactive split — all real, none fabricated. Extended the callable to compute and return all of it (one unbounded fetch of the affiliator's own completed reservations, bucketed client-side by day/week/month/cumulative boundaries, rather than four separate range queries).

**"申請する" (apply) button — resolved a mockup-vs-backend conflict the same way §4.2 resolved its own.** The static mockup shows a manual payout-request affordance with a "振込可能金額 5,000円以上〜" minimum-balance gate. This directly conflicts with the confirmed backend design: affiliate Transfer execution is fully automatic via the monthly batch job, gated only on the 3-active-day rule and mutual-approval — no manual-request concept and no minimum-payout-amount config exist anywhere in `affiliate.ts`. Building a fake manual-request callable would have contradicted the already-reconciled §4.2 design rather than completed it. Resolved by privileging the Affiliate document's own confirmed automatic design over the inherited UI element (the identical resolution principle §4.2 itself used) — repurposed the button into a real status display (current eligibility + the actual admin-configured payment day) instead of wiring it to a nonexistent endpoint.

**New admin page: `AdminAffiliateManagement`** (`/admin-affiliate-management`, not yet linked from any menu — matches `AdminReportReviewPage`'s own precedent from §37, route-reachable, nav wiring deferred). Affiliator roster (from the pre-existing `adminGetAffiliateOverview`, no backend change needed there), tap-to-select, a 6-option `Dropdown` constrained to exactly `5/10/15/20/25/30` (matches the plan's own discrete-steps-only requirement, not a free-form input), wired to `adminUpdateAffiliateRate`, with the new change-history list underneath.

**Two-push sequencing, both fully verified against `generated_code` before moving on:** the first push landed 17 of 18 intended widget bindings plus the new admin page and the QR component's 4 share-button fixes; direct inspection of `generated_code` caught the `CopyToClipboard` gap (item above) before considering the phase done. Fixed with a second push: froze all 17 already-landed one-shot `ensureReplaced` calls and the `ensurePage('AdminAffiliateManagement', ...)` call (same `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage`-one-shot discipline as every other phase), added the `copyReferralCodeToClipboard` custom action, re-verified the specific fix in `generated_code` directly (not just a clean push) before reporting done.

**Explicitly disclosed, not fixed:** the QR barcode's `data:` binding (no DSL mechanism found, see above); freeze-as-leave-equivalence for the referred-cast forfeiture rule (plan's own text flags it as unconfirmed with the client); `users.invitation_code` and `users.password_hash`-class dead-field cleanup (the field is now confirmed permanently unused going forward, but removing a declared schema field is a separate, unrelated cleanup task, not touched here to keep this phase's diff scoped to the affiliate system itself).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (mutual-approval accrual gate), `firebase/functions/src/affiliate.ts` (deferral fix, query restructure, config casing, cron→daily+config-day-check, month-scoped leave forfeiture, dashboard extension), `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (`left_at` stamp), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (`affiliate_rate_history` write, `adminGetAffiliateRateHistory`, `adminGetAuditLogs` `target_id` filter), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (new export), `firebase/firestore.rules` (+`affiliate_rate_history`), `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (+`affiliate_rate_history` affiliator_uid+changed_at, +`audit_logs` action+target_id+created_at), `dsl/edit.dart` (Affiliate page wiring, QR component fix, new admin page, 2 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §41: Phase 9 review pass — a self-introduced critical bug in the SAME push that would have made the payment processor never fire at all (2026-08-11)

**Standing review directive, applied to §40's own work.** Re-read the entire diff fresh rather than re-confirming prior findings. Found two real issues, one severe:

**1. CRITICAL — the daily-schedule-plus-date-check fix from §40 (item 3) was itself broken by a UTC-vs-JST mismatch, and would have silently prevented `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` from EVER actually processing a payment.** The fix replaced the hardcoded `"0 3 5 * *"` cron with `"0 3 * * *"` (daily) plus an in-handler `if (new Date().getDate() !== paymentDay) return;` guard. This looked correct and compiled/deployed clean, but `new Date()` inside a Cloud Function reads the CONTAINER's own runtime clock (UTC), not the `timeZone: "Asia/Tokyo"` value configured on the `onSchedule` trigger — that option only controls WHEN Cloud Scheduler fires the trigger (wall-clock JST), not what the function's own `Date` object reports once running. Since JST is UTC+9, a 3:00 AM JST firing happens at 18:00 UTC the PREVIOUS calendar day — meaning `new Date().getDate()` would read one day EARLIER than the JST day the admin actually configured, and the guard would never match, permanently no-op'ing the function every single day, forever. This would have been a silent, total failure of the entire affiliate payout mechanism — no error, no log noise, just money never moving — exactly the kind of bug this session's review discipline exists to catch before it ships. Confirmed the failure mode with a direct Node repl check (`new Date().getDate()` vs. `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {timeZone:'Asia/Tokyo', day:'numeric'}).format(new Date())` — the two diverge for roughly 9 hours out of every 24). **Fixed**: replaced the bare `new Date().getDate()` check with the `Intl.DateTimeFormat`-based JST extraction, which is immune to the runtime's own local timezone. Re-verified `tsc --noEmit` clean and redeployed `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` (and `getAffiliateDashboard`, batched in the same deploy for the second fix below) before considering the phase done.

**2. `getAffiliateDashboard`'s own `current_month_min_days`/`eligible_for_payment` fields were still hardcoded to the literal `3`**, even though `config` (fetched via `getSystemConfig()`) was already in scope in that exact function, and the identical hardcoding-vs-config bug had JUST been fixed one function away in `processAffiliatorPayment` in this same phase — an inconsistency introduced by not applying the same fix everywhere it was needed within one phase's own diff. If an admin ever changes `affiliate_min_days`, the dashboard shown to affiliators would display a stale threshold and a wrong eligibility flag while the real payment processor used the correct one. Fixed: both now read `config.affiliate_min_days || 3`. While fixing this, also noticed `eligible_for_payment` only ever checked the work-day count, never the affiliator's own `is_active`/`is_frozen`/`approval_status` — meaning a frozen or unapproved affiliator could see "eligible: true" despite `processAffiliatorPayment` forfeiting their entire reward at payment time regardless of work days. Extended the check to match the actual payment-time gate exactly (including matching the EXACT truthiness convention `processAffiliatorPayment` uses for `is_active` — `!!userData.is_active`, not `!== false`, since those differ for an `undefined` value and the two checks need to agree for the dashboard to be honest about what will actually happen).

**Also checked and ruled out as in-scope-but-not-actionable**: a `VALIDATION_DISABLED_ACTION` warning ("Action on Button is disabled, and will not be performed") present in the trace both before and after this phase's pushes — grepped `dsl/edit.dart` in full for `enabled:` on any `Button(...)` (zero matches, across all 23 buttons including every one built this phase), confirming this warning refers to a button built entirely outside this DSL (pre-existing, not attributable to Phase 9's own diff) — disclosed here rather than silently ignored, but correctly out of scope for a fix within this review.

**Also disclosed, not fixed — a narrower, PRE-EXISTING instance of the same UTC-vs-JST class of bug**: every "which calendar month does this belong to" computation across the affiliate system (`processAffiliateRewards`'s `month` field, `getAffiliateDashboard`'s `currentMonth`, the new `work_hours_today`/`_week`/`_month` boundaries added in §40) uses bare `new Date().getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`/`getDate()` — the same UTC-reads-as-if-JST pattern, just for MONTH/DAY boundaries rather than a single day-of-month equality check. Unlike item 1 above, this doesn't cause a total failure — it only misattributes activity that happens within roughly 9 hours of a JST day/month boundary to the adjacent UTC day/month, a narrow edge case, not a guaranteed-always-wrong condition. This pattern predates this session's changes (already present in `processAffiliateRewards`'s pre-existing `month` computation before Phase 9 touched the file) and is used consistently throughout the wider backend, not something Phase 9 introduced as a deviation — fixing it properly would mean auditing and changing date arithmetic across the entire codebase, well outside this phase's scope. Flagged here and in `project_rules.md` as a reusable lesson rather than fixed piecemeal.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` + `getAffiliateDashboard` redeployed and confirmed via `firebase functions:list`. No DSL changes needed for either fix (both are backend-only; the JSON field shapes `dsl/edit.dart`'s custom actions consume were unchanged).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/affiliate.ts` (JST-aware payment-day check, config-driven min-days in the dashboard, full eligibility check), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §42: Second Phase 9 review pass — one edge case rigorously proven unreachable (not just assumed), two pre-existing forfeiture-logic gaps discovered and disclosed (2026-08-11)

**Standing review directive, applied again.** No code changes this pass — every thread investigated either confirmed §41's fix is already correct, or surfaced a pre-existing (not Phase-9-introduced) gap that's a product-policy question, not a coding bug. Documented rather than silently dropped, matching this session's "disclosed, not guessed" discipline.

**1. Rigorously verified (not just assumed) that §41's month-scoped referred-cast-forfeiture fix has no post-departure-accrual gap.** Re-examined whether `processAffiliateRewards` could ever accrue a NEW reward for a referred cast dated AFTER their own `left_at` — which would slip past the `leftMonthStr === rewardData.month` equality check (paying reward for post-departure activity that shouldn't exist). Traced the actual reachability rather than assuming: `requestWithdrawal`'s own precondition blocks withdrawal outright while ANY reservation involving that cast is in a non-terminal status (`status not-in ["completed","cancelled","expired"]`), and affiliate reward accrual fires at Capture success — an earlier point in the reservation status machine than "completed". So by the time a cast's withdrawal can succeed, every Capture-triggered accrual for their own reservations has already happened; there is no code path today that can produce a reward dated after `left_at`. Confirmed the equality check is correct as written — extending it to `rewardData.month >= leftMonthStr` would be defensive code for a scenario this codebase's own existing guarantees already rule out, which this project's own conventions treat as unwarranted complexity, not caution.

**2. Discovered `adminForceDeleteUser` (pre-existing, not touched by this session) already forfeits the AFFILIATOR's own pending rewards immediately on admin force-deletion** (`affiliate_rewards.where("affiliator_uid","==",user_id).where("status","==","pending")` → batch-forfeit) — a piece of affiliate-forfeiture logic this session had not previously read in full. This means the codebase ALREADY treats "admin force-delete" as equivalent to "referrer voluntarily leaves" for the purposes of §3.7.12's affiliator-side forfeiture rule — precisely the same "is a freeze/ban equivalent to a leave?" extrapolation the plan's own text (line 163) explicitly flags as unconfirmed with the client, not something to assume. Notably, §40/§41's own fix for the OTHER (referred-cast) side of this same asymmetric rule deliberately did NOT make this assumption (kept the conservative default rather than newly asserting leave-equivalence) — so there is now a real inconsistency between the two sides: the affiliator-side already assumes force-delete≈leave (pre-existing), the referred-cast-side deliberately does not (this session's own choice). **Not changed**: this is a product-policy question requiring client confirmation, not a coding defect this review pass can resolve unilaterally, and the current behavior (forfeit on force-delete) is the safer default from a fraud/abuse-prevention standpoint — reversing it would be the riskier direction to change toward without that confirmation. Flagged here for whoever eventually takes the "confirm freeze/ban-equivalence with the client" action item the plan already calls out.

**3. Discovered a pre-existing asymmetry in how thoroughly each side of a reward is checked at payment time.** `processAffiliatorPayment`'s referrer-side gate (pre-existing, in `processAffiliateRewards`) checks both `is_active` AND `is_frozen`; the referred-cast per-reward forfeiture check (pre-existing, unchanged by §40/§41's month-scoping fix) checks only `is_active` — `adminToggleFreeze` (confirmed by reading it) sets `is_frozen` alone, never touches `is_active`, so a referred cast who is FROZEN but not force-deleted/withdrawn would still have `is_active: true` and their attributed reward would still be paid out normally. Checked the plan's own literal §3.7.12 wording before deciding whether to fix this: it explicitly calls out "frozen" only for the referrer's own gate ("a frozen or not-yet-approved referrer must not accrue"), not for the referred cast's side — so extending the referred-cast check to also require `!is_frozen` would be inventing a stricter rule than the spec literally asks for, not implementing an omission. **Not changed**, disclosed instead — matches the general principle this session has applied consistently: don't guess at broader symmetry the spec didn't request, even when an asymmetry looks suspicious at first glance.

### §43: Third Phase 9 review pass — a real cross-pass inconsistency between §40's DSL and §41's backend fix (2026-08-11)

**Standing review directive, applied a third time.** Found one genuine, fixable bug this pass: the "申請する" status button's "not eligible" `Snackbar` message (written in §40) hardcoded "insufficient work days" as the ONLY explanation — accurate at the time, since `eligible_for_payment` was then purely a work-days check. §41's fix (same phase, LATER pass) broadened `eligible_for_payment` server-side to also require `is_active`/`!is_frozen`/`approval_status === "approved"`, but never revisited the DSL-side message text that assumed the OLD, narrower computation. Net effect: a frozen or not-yet-approved affiliator tapping the status button would see "work days insufficient" — actively wrong, since their real blocker has nothing to do with work days. A concrete example of why "review the work you've just completed" needs to re-check EARLIER work in the same phase against LATER changes to the same phase, not just the most recent diff in isolation — the bug was invisible to §41's own review because §41 only looked at the backend function it had just changed, not at every DSL consumer of that function's output written in an earlier pass.

**Fixed**: generalized the message to name both possible reasons ("稼働日数（3日以上）・アカウントの承認状況をご確認ください。" — check your work days and your account's approval status) rather than adding a new backend field just to disambiguate a status message, since the underlying boolean already correctly gates the actual eligibility. Confirmed no other DSL site references `eligible_for_payment` (index 8) before considering this closed — this was the only consumer.

**Verification:** `dart analyze`/`flutterflow ai test` clean, pushed, confirmed the new string landed in `generated_code/lib/payment/affiliate/affiliate_widget.dart` directly (not just a clean push).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (Snackbar message text), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §44: CRITICAL — a 4th Phase 9 review pass found that every `firebase` CLI deploy this session (Cloud Functions, Firestore rules, Firestore indexes) had been targeting the WRONG Firebase project, silently, for an extended, session-spanning window (2026-08-11)

**How this was found:** re-verifying (not assuming) that the two composite indexes added in §40 had actually finished building — using this session's own established direct-Firestore-REST-query technique — both queries failed with `FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index`, even though `firestore.indexes.json` had both indexes declared correctly and `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` had reported success. `gcloud firestore indexes composite list --project=icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` confirmed neither index existed on that project AT ALL, not even in a building state.

**Root cause**: every `firebase`/patched-`firebase.js` CLI invocation this session passed `--project icoccha` — but `icoccha` and `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` are two ENTIRELY SEPARATE Firebase/GCP projects (confirmed via `firebase projects:list`: project numbers 983232490881 vs 625233988069 — different projects, not an alias). `.firebaserc` in this workspace's own `firebase/` directory declares `"default": "icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing"`, and `firebase projects:list` marks `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` as `(current)` for this directory — but the explicit `--project icoccha` flag on every command OVERRODE that correct default, silently, on every single deploy. **Confirmed which project is actually real** by reading the FlutterFlow-generated app's own live Firebase config directly — `generated_code/android/app/google-services.json` and `generated_code/ios/Runner/GoogleService-Info.plist` both hard-code `project_id`/`PROJECT_ID`: `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`. The compiled app that real users would run has NEVER pointed at `icoccha`.

**Scope, established by diffing the complete function list of both projects** (`firebase functions:list --project icoccha` vs `--project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`): this was NOT a Phase-9-only mistake. `getChatRoomInfo`, `getMyMatchaList`, `sendChatMessage` (Phase 6), `getWalletBalance` (Phase 8), `getDiscoveryCasts` (§33), and `getServiceAreas` existed ONLY on the wrong project — meaning those functions, despite being extensively built, reviewed, and reported as "deployed and verified" across multiple earlier phases of THIS session, were never actually live for the real app to call. The wrong-project habit was already documented as established convention BEFORE this visible session segment began (the pre-existing `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` gen-1/CPU-deploy-blocker entry itself shows `--project icoccha` as the example invocation) — meaning this had likely been silently happening across a large, indeterminate portion of the project's build history, not just this session's visible Phase 6-9 work. Every "deployed and verified via `firebase functions:list`" confirmation in this session's own prior review passes (§34, §35, §37, §38, §40, §41) was **verifying against the wrong project** — the verification step itself was real and rigorous, it was just pointed at the wrong target the entire time, so it never had a chance to catch this.

**Why nothing else caught it**: `dart analyze`/`flutterflow ai test`/`compileDslApp` never call a real backend, so they couldn't see this. `firebase functions:list --project icoccha` legitimately succeeded and legitimately showed the deployed function — because it WAS correctly deployed, just to the wrong place, and every verification command in this session ALSO used `--project icoccha`, so it consistently, self-confirmingly agreed with itself while being wrong about which project mattered. The direct-Firestore-REST-query technique (this session's own established "don't trust a clean push/deploy alone" discipline, §37/§38) is what finally caught it — because that technique's whole point is bypassing the tool's own self-report and checking the actual resource — but even that technique had been run against `--project`-implied context without an explicit `--project` flag matching what the CLI deploys used, until this exact review pass which happened to target `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` directly by habit while investigating the index-freshness question.

**Fix in progress**: redeployed Firestore rules + indexes to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (confirmed correct project this time — both new composite indexes now show `CREATING` state on the real project, will finish building shortly), redeployed all 7 Phase-9-touched Cloud Functions individually, then launched a FULL `firebase deploy --only functions --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (all 55+ functions) rather than trying to reconstruct exactly which functions have stale code from an indeterminate history of wrong-project deploys — cherry-picking risks silently missing one. **This entry will be updated with the full deploy's outcome and any errors encountered.**

**Every future `firebase`/patched-`firebase.js` command in this project MUST use `--project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, never `--project icoccha`** — this is now the single most important operational correction from this entire session. `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated with a prominent, impossible-to-miss warning about this.

### §45: §44 follow-up — remediation outcome, a genuine infrastructure blocker, and a correction notice for this log's own historical accuracy (2026-08-11/12)

**Remediation outcome.** The full `functions` deploy to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` succeeded for ~35 functions (spot-checked two — `getDiscoveryCasts`, `getWalletBalance` — via `gcloud functions describe ... --format="value(updateTime)"`, both show an update timestamp matching the deploy window, confirming genuinely fresh code landed, not a stale no-op). `firebase/storage.rules` was checked too (in case the same wrong-project habit had affected it): `firebase deploy --only storage --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` reported "latest version already up to date, skipping upload" — storage rules were already correctly on the real project, unaffected by this bug. No gap there.

**~25 functions remain on stale code on the real project**, blocked by a genuine GCP infrastructure constraint, not a code or deploy-command error: `completeOnboarding`, `capturePayment`, `submitReview`, `processTip`, `respondToReservation`, `createSetupIntent`, `createExtensionPayment`, `updateLastLogin`, and ~17 `admin*` functions. Root cause, confirmed by direct inspection (`gcloud run revisions list`): 178 total Cloud Run revisions exist for this project in `asia-northeast1`, and dozens are stuck in a non-serving (`status: False`) state — broken remnants of the failed deploy attempts themselves — still holding CPU quota allocation that a healthy revision would have released on cleanup. Retrying the SAME deploys (even one function at a time) fails identically, confirming this is a sustained quota ceiling from accumulated dead revisions, not a transient burst limit that clears on its own between attempts.

**Deliberately NOT resolved unilaterally**: clearing this requires bulk-deleting ~29 specific non-serving Cloud Run revisions via `gcloud run revisions delete`. This is a genuinely destructive, wide-blast-radius action across many GCP resources — correctly blocked by this session's own auto-mode safety classifier, and correctly not something to route around. Asked the user directly for explicit authorization before proceeding; as of this entry, awaiting their answer. **Until that cleanup happens, the ~25 functions listed above remain on whatever code was live before this session's wrong-project discovery** — for the ones touched by Phase 6-9 work specifically, this could include real, already-identified-and-fixed bugs still being served to production. This is the single most consequential open item carried out of this session.

**Correction notice for this log's own historical accuracy.** Every "deployed and verified via `firebase functions:list`"-style claim in §30 through §43 (and likely earlier, undated entries from before this visible session segment) was verified using `--project icoccha` — the wrong project, per §44's finding. This does NOT mean the CODE described in those entries is wrong — the source fixes themselves were correctly written and are correctly reflected in the current `firebase/functions/src/*.ts` files, confirmed by this session's own `tsc --noEmit` checks throughout. It means the DEPLOYMENT/LIVENESS half of each of those entries' claims — "confirmed live", "redeployed and verified", "functions:list shows it deployed" — was checked against a project the real app never talks to, and cannot be trusted as evidence the fix was ever actually serving real users, independent of whatever §44/§45's remediation now establishes. Readers relying on this log for "is X actually live in production" should treat any pre-§44 liveness claim as unconfirmed until independently re-checked against `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` explicitly, not as false — the underlying code changes are real and correct, only their deployment provenance is now in question.

**Files touched:** no code changes this entry (storage rules confirmed already correct, no edit needed); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §46: §44/§45 fully resolved — wrong-project remediation complete, every Cloud Run service on the real project confirmed healthy (2026-08-12)

**With explicit user authorization, deleted the specific non-serving Cloud Run revisions blocking redeployment.** Not all 29 candidates could be deleted directly — Cloud Run refuses to delete a service's own most-recently-created revision even when it's unhealthy and carries no traffic (`FAILED_PRECONDITION: The latest created Revision "..." cannot be directly deleted` — a real Cloud Run constraint not previously encountered in this project). 6 of the 29 (ones that weren't their service's "latest") deleted cleanly; that alone freed enough CPU quota for the remaining ~25 function deploys to succeed once retried in small batches (5 at a time) — each fresh deploy created a new, healthy "latest" revision, which is what actually resolved the services whose latest revision itself had been the stuck one, not the direct deletion.

**Full resolution confirmed, not assumed**: all ~25 previously-blocked functions redeployed successfully across 5 sequential batches. Specifically re-verified the 3 functions (`submitReview`, `createSetupIntent`, `updateLastLogin`) that §45 flagged as showing `traffic: None`/ambiguous health — all three now show `{'latestRevision': True, 'percent': 100, ...}` with `Ready: True`, confirming they are genuinely healthy now, not just re-deployed. Final sweep: `gcloud run services list` across every service in the project shows zero non-`True` statuses — every Cloud Run service backing every Cloud Function in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` is confirmed healthy.

**Net effect of §44-§46 together**: this session discovered and fully corrected a session-spanning (likely longer) deployment misconfiguration that had every backend change verified against a Firebase project the real app never connects to. All functions, Firestore rules, Firestore indexes, and storage rules for this project are now confirmed — via direct resource inspection, not just deploy-command exit codes — to be live and healthy on `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, the actual project the compiled app uses.

**Files touched:** no code changes this entry (infrastructure remediation only — Cloud Run revision cleanup + redeploys); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §47: Phase 10 built — work/staff board, staff-fee-split was hardcoded to zero, new client-facing work-posts backend (2026-08-12)

**Backend audit first, same discipline as every phase.** Read `reservations.ts`, `admin.ts`'s work_posts functions, and `stripe-payments.ts`'s payout split before writing anything. Found real gaps:

**1. `createReservation` hardcoded `staffFee = 0` and `staff_ids: []` unconditionally** — never accepted a staff selection at all, even though the downstream payout logic (`recordCastRewardsAndProcessOthers`, stripe-payments.ts, pre-existing and untouched) already correctly subtracts `staff_fee` before computing cast reward and splits it evenly across `staff_ids`. The split math was ready and correct; the input side simply never fed it anything real, so §3.9.11's staff-fee-first-then-cast-split has never actually applied to a single real reservation. Fixed: added an optional `staff_selections` param (`{staff_id, role}[]`, mirroring `cast_ids`' own direct-selection shape), validating each staff member's `staff_type` against the requested role (must match or be `"both"`) and computing the fee from `config.security_staff_fee`/`transport_staff_fee`.

**2. No client-facing way to apply to a `work_posts` doc existed at all.** `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`'s own pre-existing comment explicitly said the apply-side write was expected but missing — confirmed by grepping the whole backend before writing anything. Built a new file, `work-posts.ts`: `applyToWorkPost` (type-appropriate eligibility: any approved cast for `partner_recruit`, matching `staff_type` for `security`/`transport`), `selectWorkApplicant` (poster-facing, not admin-only — distinct from `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`), `fetchWorkPosts`, `getWorkPostDetail`, `fetchMyWorkPosts`. `selectWorkApplicant` also creates the dedicated cast-to-cast chat room (§3.7.6) for `partner_recruit` posts specifically — a separate room from the reservation's own guest+cast room, reusing the same `chat_rooms` shape.

**3. `WorkPostsFields`' typed schema was missing `description`** (the real field every backend write uses) **and carried 7 decoy fields** (`category`, `content`, `title`, `is_active`, `user_name`, `user_photo`, `user_ref`) that nothing in the backend ever writes — leftover from an earlier, abandoned generic "job board" schema draft. Fixed via the project's own `_addField`/`_removeField` helpers.

**Composite indexes**: added `work_posts` `poster_id`+`created_at` and `applicants`(array-contains)+`created_at` for `fetchMyWorkPosts`'s two queries; `status`+`created_at` already existed (from `adminGetWorkPosts`) and covers `fetchWorkPosts`. Deliberately did NOT add a `status`+`type`+`created_at` index for `fetchWorkPosts`' optional `type` filter — filtered in-memory instead, avoiding the same combinatorial-index-explosion pattern already documented for `adminGetLedger`/`adminGetReservations` in this backend.

**New pages**: `MyWorkContent` (§3.7.4's self-service screen — user-type/work-type read-only, staff-moonlighting dropdown editable, wired to the pre-existing `updateProfile` via a new dedicated `callUpdateStaffType` action rather than the much heavier ProfileEdit-specific `callUpdateProfile`, which has no `staff_type` param at all and would need every other profile field re-supplied just to touch one). `WorkPostDetailPage` (confirmed `[MISSING]` in the plan) — post detail, apply button for non-poster/non-applied/open posts, applicant list + select button for the post's own poster. `WorkPage`'s job feed (previously 4 static fake cards) is now a real dynamic list; the security/transport category cards show real open-post counts appended to their existing copy — the girls/boys cards are general cast-recruiting marketing text with no corresponding `work_posts` data, left unchanged, disclosed not silently skipped.

**Review pass caught a real, significant gap in this phase's own first push**: `MyWorkContent` was built but nothing anywhere navigated to it — confirmed by re-reading `WorkPage`'s own AppBar/body, which never referenced it. A built-but-unreachable page is effectively dead from a real user's perspective, the same class of problem as Phase 9's referral-code-bound-to-a-dead-field bug, just at the navigation layer instead of the data layer. Compounding it: the page had no `account_type` gate at all, hardcoding "キャスト" as a label and exposing a functional `staff_type` editor to ANY signed-in user — `WorkPage` itself (the entry point being added) is one of the 5 universal bottom-nav tabs per the plan's own IA, not cast-scoped, so a guest could have reached it. Fixed both: added a "マイワーク" nav button to `WorkPage`'s AppBar, and gated `MyWorkContent`'s real content behind a computed `isCast` check (same pattern as `AdminReportReviewPage`/`AdminAffiliateManagement`'s own role gates), showing a permission message otherwise. Hit `patch.visible(...)`'s literal-bool-only limitation while building the gate (confirmed by compile error, not assumed) — worked around by consolidating into one `ensureReplaced` on the body's root Column rather than 9 individual per-widget patches.

**Explicitly disclosed, not built this phase:** the actual reservation-creation client UI (`callCreateReservation`, pre-existing, untouched) has no staff-selection UI at all — confirmed by reading it directly, it still only ever sends a single `cast_id` (no multi-cast either, a separate pre-existing simplification). §3.9.11's calculation is now correct and reachable via `createReservation`'s new `staff_selections` param, but nothing in the client currently populates it, so every real reservation created today will still show `staff_fee: 0` until a future pass extends the reservation form itself — reading and safely extending that already-complex, unaudited page was judged too large an undertaking to rush within this phase's own review pass, unlike the MyWorkContent fix above which was a small, contained, same-phase omission. `fetchMyWorkPosts` (posted/applied lists) is built and deployed but not wired to a dedicated UI — a post's own poster can already find and manage it via the main open-posts feed (their own post appears there too), which covers the checklist's core "application/response management" ask; the gap is narrower — once a post leaves "open" status it drops out of that feed, losing easy revisit access.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout, all backend changes deployed to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (confirmed via `firebase functions:list` against that exact project, not assumed after §44-46's lesson) and composite indexes confirmed `READY` + a live query returning cleanly via the direct Firestore REST technique. DSL pushed across 3 rounds (initial build, one-shot-call freezing, review-pass fix), every one verified against `generated_code` directly, not just a clean push — the reachability/gating fix specifically confirmed via `context.pushNamed(MyWorkContentWidget.routeName)` appearing in `work_page_widget.dart` and `_model.isCast` gating every content branch in `my_work_content_widget.dart`.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (staff_selections + fee calc), `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (new file), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (new exports), `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (+2 work_posts composite indexes), `dsl/edit.dart` (schema fix, WorkPage wiring, MyWorkContent + WorkPostDetailPage, reachability/gating fix — 3 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §48: Phase 10's disclosed staff-selection gap closed — redesigned around a flat-fee/role-flag model, plus a confirmed SDK compiler caching bug that cost several pushes to isolate (2026-08-12)

**Redesigned rather than reusing §47's `staff_selections` (direct staff-ID) param.** Rereading the actual client surface confirmed there is no staff-browsing/discovery UI anywhere in this app for a guest to obtain a specific `staff_id` — building one would be a separate, large feature. Key realization: `security_staff_fee`/`transport_staff_fee` are FLAT, role-level `system_config` values, not per-individual — so the fee is fully determined by role alone, before any specific person is chosen. Replaced the direct-selection UI plan with `needs_security`/`needs_transport` checkboxes on `ReservationForm`, mirroring the already-proven `group_invite`→`partner_recruit` auto-post pattern exactly: guest flags the role at booking time (fee correctly included in `total_amount`/Stripe authorization immediately), and once the cast accepts (`respondToReservation`), the backend auto-creates a "security"/"transport" `work_posts` entry tied to the reservation for staff to apply to via §47's own `applyToWorkPost`. Extended `selectWorkApplicant` (§47) so selecting an applicant for a reservation-linked security/transport post now also appends them to that reservation's own `staff_ids` — completing the loop back to `recordCastRewardsAndProcessOthers`' pre-existing, untouched fee-split logic. `staff_selections` (§47) is left in the backend, unused by any client today, available without further backend work if a future "invite a specific staff member directly" feature is ever built.

**Confirmed SDK bug, not a scripting mistake — cost 4 pushes to isolate.** Rewiring `ReservationForm`'s existing submit button to call a new custom action with 2 extra arguments repeatedly failed to take effect, across three structurally different approaches: (1) `updateCustomAction` with a manually-reconstructed `arguments: List<FFParameter>` — failed with "argument X is not specified" for ALL 14 params simultaneously (not just the 2 new ones), inconsistent with a genuine per-parameter mismatch; (2) declaring a brand-new custom action (avoiding `updateCustomAction` entirely) and calling it from a second, later `app.editPage` block for the same page — compiled and pushed clean, checkboxes landed correctly, but `generated_code` still showed the OLD action being called; (3) moving the same fix into the page's ORIGINAL `editPage` block instead of a second one — this time the push itself reported **`NO_OP: All tasks completed but no project changes were detected. The project proto is identical before and after task execution`** — a claim directly contradicted by the source diff, confirmed by re-reading the script (the new action name and arguments were genuinely present). `flutterflow ai refresh-context` (re-fetching from the server, not just local cache) still showed the old action afterward, confirming the REMOTE project itself never received the mutation — this was not a local-cache-only artifact. **What actually broke the deadlock**: adding one more, entirely unrelated change (a Snackbar error-message text tweak) to the SAME action chain in the SAME push — that push succeeded for real, landing BOTH the unrelated text change AND the button-action fix that had been silently rejected 3 times prior. This strongly suggests the compiler's own change-diffing incorrectly treated the specific action-chain mutation as already-applied/no-op across multiple independent pushes, and only a genuinely-different accompanying change in the same trigger's action list was enough to force it to re-diff and apply the whole chain correctly. **Practical lesson**: if `ensureActions` (or any trigger-chain mutation) on an EXISTING widget produces a clean push/compile but `generated_code` doesn't reflect the change, don't assume the script is wrong — try bundling in a second, trivial, unambiguous change (e.g., alter a literal string) to the SAME action chain in the SAME push as a forcing function before concluding the approach itself is broken. Confirmed working: the diagnostic marker text and the real fix landed together; a follow-up push then cleanly removed the marker text alone (no further no-op recurrence).

**Verification:** all 3 backend functions (`createReservation`, `respondToReservation`, `selectWorkApplicant`) confirmed deployed and healthy via `gcloud run services list` against `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (zero non-`True` statuses). Final DSL state confirmed directly in `generated_code`: submit button calls `actions.callCreateReservationWithStaff` with all 14 arguments including `_model.resNeedsSecurity`/`_model.resNeedsTransport`; diagnostic marker text confirmed fully removed in the cleanup push.
