**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (needs_security/needs_transport params + auto-post creation on confirm), `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (selectWorkApplicant now links back to reservation.staff_ids), `dsl/edit.dart` (reservations schema fields, ReservationForm checkboxes, new callCreateReservationWithStaff action — 4 pushes including 1 diagnostic + 1 cleanup), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (new SDK-caching-bug quirk entry), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §49: Dedicated error-resolution phase — full-project audit across backend/rules/indexes/DSL/generated_code; found and fixed the single most severe bug of the whole project (the booking flow never actually authorized payment) plus 15+ other confirmed real bugs (2026-08-12)

**Scope of this phase, per explicit user instruction**: pause new-feature work, thoroughly analyze the entire project for errors/incorrectly-implemented sections/overlooked details, and eliminate everything found — not a new phase of the build plan, a dedicated correctness pass across everything built in Phases 1–10.

**Method**: infra ground-truth check first (deployed functions vs. source, Cloud Run health, live composite indexes vs. `firestore.indexes.json`, live Cloud Functions logs) — all clean, no drift found. Then three parallel full-file audits (backend Cloud Functions logic, `dsl/edit.dart` structural review, `generated_code` cross-check against what recent phases claimed was "DONE"), plus a manual `firestore.rules`/indexes read against every collection the backend actually queries. This combination — read the real deployed state, don't trust prior "DONE" markers — is what caught the two most serious findings below; neither would show up from reading `dsl/edit.dart` or the plan doc alone.

**CRITICAL — the entire Stripe payment step was disconnected from the booking flow.** `ReservationForm`'s submit button navigated straight to `ReservationConfirmed` after `createReservation` succeeded, completely skipping `PaymentConfirm`. `createReservation` always writes `payment_intent_id: ""`; the only code path that ever populates it (`createPaymentIntent`, via the `getPaymentClientSecret` custom action) was only ever called from `PaymentConfirm`'s own submit button — which nothing in the app ever navigated to. Confirmed via `generated_code`, not inferred: every reservation created through the live UI was permanently stuck with no PaymentIntent, meaning no guest was ever actually charged and `reportCompletion`'s own defensive `console.error("...no payment_intent_id")` path would fire for every single one. `PaymentConfirm`'s own payment-submit action chain (`getPaymentClientSecret` → `confirmStripePayment` → `isPaymentSuccess`) was already correctly wired from a prior phase — the page was just never reached, and once reached, its 5 price/date/location fields still showed FlutterFlow's literal default "Hello World" placeholder text (never wired to real data, a pre-existing gap `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` had flagged from the very start of the project but no phase had circled back to close). Fixed: edited the two ORIGINAL, already-live `app.editPage` blocks in place (ReservationForm's submit chain now navigates to `PaymentConfirm`; PaymentConfirm's payment-success branch now navigates to `ReservationConfirmed` instead of just popping back) — both are exactly the kind of "re-wire an already-live trigger" edit that hit the confirmed SDK caching bug in §48, but editing the ORIGINAL block in place (the proven-safe pattern from that same section) worked cleanly this time, confirmed directly in `generated_code`. Added a new `fetchPaymentConfirmDetails` custom action (same established pattern as `fetchReservationSummary`) and replaced the 5 "Hello World" `Text` widgets in place with values bound through the existing shared `splitField` helper.

**CRITICAL — extension payments were authorized but never captured; money was never actually collected.** `createExtensionPayment` creates a real `capture_method: manual` Stripe PaymentIntent per extension, but grepping every `.ts` file confirmed no code path anywhere ever called `.capture()` on an extension's `payment_intent_id` — the authorization hold would just silently expire after ~7 days, so even a guest who "successfully" bought extra time was never charged and the cast/platform never received that revenue. Fixed with a new `captureAuthorizedExtensions(resId, resData)` (stripe-payments.ts), called from the same two places the main reservation payment is captured (`reportCompletion`'s inline capture, and `autoCompleteReviews`'s stalled-completion retry sweep, plus defensively in the review_pending timeout loop) — captures each authorized extension PaymentIntent and writes `type: "reward"` ledger entries against the SAME `res_id`, so the pre-existing `transferPendingCastRewards(resId)` call right after it transfers extension rewards together with the base reservation's, no new transfer path needed. **Not fixed this phase, explicitly disclosed**: `extension_payment.dart`'s own UI is still hardcoded to a fake `'test_res_001'` reservation ID (confirmed pre-existing — it was wired directly in the live FlutterFlow project before this AI-tooling session ever started; no `app.editPage(ff.Pages.extensionPayment, ...)` block exists anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart` to edit in place) and has no navigation entry point anywhere in the app. Deliberately did not attempt a full rebuild of its pricing pipeline this pass — doing so blind (without the original action-chain source, only a paraphrase of what `generated_code` shows) risked breaking an already-partially-correct dropdown pricing computation, and adding a navigation entry point without also fixing the hardcoded ID would have newly exposed a broken flow to real users. The backend danger (money silently never collected) is closed regardless of when the UI gets fixed — a future pass should read this page via `flutterflow ai inspect --dsl-json` first to get the real existing action-chain expressions before touching it.

**Also found and fixed in `firebase/functions/src/`** (each independently confirmed by reading the code directly, not assumed from a comment claiming something already works):
- `handleAmountCapturableUpdated` (stripe-webhooks.ts) had no `metadata.type` guard, unlike its sibling handlers — authorizing an extension mid-session could silently stamp the PARENT reservation back to `status: "authorized"`, breaking `reportCompletion`'s status check and making an active in-progress reservation eligible for the 24h auto-cancel sweep. `handlePaymentIntentFailed` had the same gap on its cancel-and-notify path. Both now gated behind the same `!paymentIntent.metadata?.type` check `handlePaymentIntentSucceeded` already used correctly.
- `submitReview` had no ownership check (`cast_id` came straight from client input, never checked against `resData.cast_ids`), no status guard (could jump a reservation straight to `completed` before any meetup/capture), and no duplicate-review check. All three added.
- `adminGetReservations` filtered/sorted on `scheduled_at` — a field `createReservation` never writes (the real field is `date`) — so the function returned an empty list on every call, unexercised until now since no admin UI calls it yet. Fixed to use `date`; the 3 deployed composite indexes built around the wrong field name (`status+scheduled_at`, `guest_id+scheduled_at`, and a `cast_id+scheduled_at` index for a singular field name nothing has ever queried by) replaced with `status+date`/`guest_id+date`.
- Multi-cast reservations: `recordCastRewardsAndProcessOthers` computed each cast's `platform_profit`/`tax_amount` per-row as if that cast were the reservation's ONLY cast (never subtracting any other cast's own reward), so on a 2+ cast reservation every ledger row overstated platform profit — `adminGetLedger`'s own res_id-dedup summary silently picked one arbitrary (wrong) figure. Fixed with a two-pass computation: sum every cast's reward first, then derive one correct, shared profit figure applied identically to every row. The underlying reward-split FORMULA itself (each cast independently claims `rewardBase * theirOwnRate`, rather than the pool being divided across casts the way the transport-fee pool already correctly is) is unchanged and disclosed as a genuine open product question, not silently resolved — combined cast rates approaching/exceeding 100% is a real possible state today.
- `adminForceCancel` swallowed Stripe errors (try/catch that only logged) and marked the reservation `cancelled` regardless of whether the Stripe call actually succeeded; its partial-capture path also created no `ledger` entry at all for the captured amount. Fixed: rethrows as a real `HttpsError` (reservation is NOT marked cancelled unless Stripe genuinely succeeded), partial captures now write a `refund`-type ledger entry. Also added a status guard rejecting already-`completed`/`cancelled`/`expired` reservations.
- `adminHireWorkPostApplicant` (admin-facing hire flow) never appended the hired applicant to the linked reservation's `staff_ids`, unlike the client-facing `selectWorkApplicant` (§48) which does — a staff member hired via the admin panel instead of the normal apply flow would never be paid their share. Fixed to mirror `selectWorkApplicant`'s logic exactly.
- No status/idempotency guards existed on `capturePayment` (could be called by anyone right after cast-approval, before any meetup — premature real capture), `createPaymentIntent` (repeat calls orphaned prior PaymentIntents and could re-authorize an already-progressed reservation), `createExtensionPayment` (could be bought on a non-`in_progress` reservation), `confirmMeetup` (could reopen an already-cancelled/completed reservation), `cancelPayment` (attempted to touch an already-finalized PaymentIntent on a terminal reservation — failed loudly via Stripe, not silently wrong, but now rejected up front with a clear reason). All five now guard on the correct reservation status.
- `autoCancelExpiredAuth`'s 24h sweep matched `status in ["authorized", "cast_pending"]` — `cast_pending` is a dead status value nothing in this codebase ever writes (confirmed via grep), and `request_pending` (a guest who submitted a request but never got to authorize payment) had NO expiry path at all, since `respondToReservation` only accepts `authorized`/`cast_pending`, so a stuck `request_pending` reservation could never even be approved/declined and just lingered forever. Fixed to sweep `["request_pending", "authorized"]`.
- `firestore.rules`'s `isAdmin()` helper checked `data.role_admin` — a field never written anywhere in this project (confirmed against `schemas.dart`'s real field definition, `role`, and `auth.ts`'s actual writer). `role_admin` leaked in from the shared reference tree's OWN schema (a different, sister project). Every `isAdmin()`-gated rule (system_config, stripe_logs, audit_logs, and every admin-OR-owner fallback) silently evaluated as permanently false for every user, admins included — zero current observable impact only because no admin-panel UI exists yet to exercise a direct-client read gated by it (every admin Cloud Function uses the Admin SDK, which bypasses rules entirely), but a real, deployed defect waiting for the first admin-panel feature to hit it. Fixed to check `role`.
- `work_posts`, `debt_history`, `pair_history` — three real, actively-queried collections with NO Firestore rule of their own at all (same "no rule = deny-all" gap class already found and fixed 3 times in earlier phases for chat messages/notifications/reviews). Added rules matching each collection's actual access pattern (poster-or-applicant-or-admin for work_posts, owner-or-admin for debt_history, admin-only for pair_history) — currently unexercised by any live client read (everything goes through Cloud Functions today), added proactively rather than waiting to rediscover this same bug class a 4th time.
- `kyc.dart` had an undisclosed 6th "Hello World" (2 occurrences, one per upload card) beneath its real description text — not one of the 5 already-tracked `payment_confirm.dart` occurrences. Rebound to a new `kycUploadStatusLabel` function reading the page's own pre-existing `kycDocUrl`/`kycSelfieUrl` state (already set on real upload completion from an earlier phase), turning meaningless placeholder text into a genuinely useful upload-status line.
- **Client-confirmed decision, implemented**: freezing a referred cast now counts the same as that cast leaving for the affiliate forfeiture rule (§3.7.12), asked directly since the plan's own text flagged this as unconfirmed. Added `frozen_at` (stamped by `adminToggleFreeze`, freeze-only, never cleared on unfreeze) giving the same month-scoping precision `left_at` already gives the voluntary-withdrawal path; `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments`'s referred-cast loop now forfeits only the specific month a freeze happened in, mirroring the existing leave-rule logic exactly. The REFERRER side of this same freeze-equivalence already existed correctly before this phase (confirmed by reading the code, not assumed) — only the referred-cast side had the gap.

**Also confirmed, explicitly NOT changed (real findings, deliberately low-priority)**: ~9 Firestore rules/indexes entries (`cocomise`, `job_board_posts`, `kyc_reviews`, `payment_ledger`, `withdrawal_applications`, `affiliates`, `system_settings`, `prefectures`) protect collections that don't exist in this project's real schema at all — leftover artifacts from the shared reference tree's OWN (sister-project) schema; already properly locked down (admin-only/owner-scoped, not open), so not a security hole, just dead weight — left alone rather than spending deploy cycles pruning something harmless. `selectWorkApplicant` deployed with GCF v2 default `cpu:1/concurrency:80` instead of the rest of the fleet's `cpu:0.5/concurrency:1` (a side effect of the `cpu` field being dropped from `setGlobalOptions` in an earlier session per `index.ts`'s own comment) — minor cost/quota inefficiency, not a correctness issue. Android's `MainActivity.kt` sits under a stale `com/example/my_project/` directory path while its own `package` declaration correctly says `com.mycompany.icoccha` — confirmed harmless (Gradle compiles off the file's own package declaration, not the directory path) and outside this DSL's control regardless (a `generated_code/` artifact of FlutterFlow's own project scaffolding, not something Cloud Functions/DSL work can reach). Three dead-`cast_pending` read-sites remain in `reservations.ts`/`respondToReservation` (checking membership of a value nothing ever writes, harmless no-op) and a few doc-only DSL declaration/comment drifts (`callUpdateProfile`/`fetchDiscoveryCasts` have no `app.customAction` declaration left in `dsl/edit.dart` to sync against post-`updateCustomAction`, purely an audit-trail readability gap, zero functional risk) — none fixed, all cosmetic.

**Still open, unchanged from before this phase**: `fetchMyWorkPosts` (§47) still not wired to a dedicated UI; the Affiliate QR code's own visual `data:` binding — re-checked this phase via `flutterflow ai docs ui`/`docs api-surface`/`references/`/`patterns/`, confirmed again no `Barcode`-widget data-binding path exists anywhere in this DSL; no live device/Test-Mode click-test has been done all session (`live.status` reconfirmed `paired: false`).

**Verification, same discipline as every prior phase — never trust a clean push/deploy alone**: backend — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across two separate deploys, all 16 touched functions confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`, all Cloud Run services confirmed healthy via `gcloud run services list`, all 48 composite indexes confirmed `READY` (46 pre-existing + 3 new − 1 removed net, cross-checked local `firestore.indexes.json` count against the live `gcloud firestore indexes composite list` count, exact match). DSL — pushed once, then verified directly in `generated_code` (not the push's own "success" message): `reservation_form_widget.dart` confirmed navigating to `PaymentConfirmWidget.routeName`; `payment_confirm_widget.dart` confirmed navigating to `ReservationConfirmedWidget.routeName` on payment success (not a leftover bare `pop()`) and confirmed zero remaining `"Hello World"` occurrences; `kyc_widget.dart` confirmed zero remaining `"Hello World"` occurrences.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-webhooks.ts`, `stripe-payments.ts`, `reservations.ts`, `admin.ts`, `affiliate.ts` (bug fixes, guards, new `captureAuthorizedExtensions`); `firebase/firestore.rules` (`isAdmin()` fix, 3 new collection rules); `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (removed 1 dead index, renamed 2 to the real field, added 3 new); `dsl/edit.dart` (2 in-place edits to already-live blocks, 1 new section — PaymentConfirm data binding, kyc.dart placeholder fix); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside (see their own change markers).

### §50: §49 follow-up — `extension_payment.dart` fully rebuilt and verified (was calling the wrong Cloud Function entirely, not just a fake ID); Affiliate QR barcode reattempted and a genuine SDK bug confirmed via isolation, not just re-disclosed (2026-08-12)

**User asked directly whether the two items §49 left open were actually fixable or genuinely blocked** — worth resolving that distinction explicitly rather than leaving both bundled under one "still open" note.

**`extension_payment.dart`: fixable, and fixed.** Reading the actual compiled Dart (not just the earlier agent's paraphrase) surfaced this was worse than "hardcoded ID" — the submit button called `actions.callCreatePaymentIntent(...)`, the MAIN reservation's own authorize function, not `createExtensionPayment` (the dedicated endpoint with cap enforcement this project already has); it wrote a new doc directly to a client-side `payments` collection, disconnected from `captureAuthorizedExtensions` (§49, which reads the `extensions` subcollection `createExtensionPayment` actually writes); and it never called `confirmStripePayment` at all — it showed "決済が完了しました" (payment completed) unconditionally the instant the (wrong) Cloud Function call returned, regardless of whether the guest ever authorized a real charge. Rebuilt the submit chain following the same proven get-client-secret → present-Payment-Sheet → check-success pattern `payment_confirm.dart` already uses correctly; added a `resId` param, a `fetchExtensionTimeSlot` action (real `time_slot` on load, replacing the permanently-hardcoded `'第2部'` default the pricing dropdown was silently keying off), and fixed the time-slot banner (was a hardcoded "第2部　20:00〜23:00" literal — now shows the real slot; the fixed clock-range portion was dropped rather than guessed, since no verified slot→clock-range mapping exists anywhere in this backend). Added the missing navigation entry point: a "延長する" button on `ReservationDetail`, gated guest-only + `in_progress`-only (matching `createExtensionPayment`'s own status guard from §49) via the same `resVisibilityData`/`CustomFunction`-gate pattern every other lifecycle button on that page already uses, inserted between `ConfirmMeetupRow` and `ReportCompletionRow` (its own `in_progress` window sits exactly between them). The dropdown's own pricing computation (`calculateExtensionPrice`) was untouched — it was already correct, it only needed real `timeSlot` data fed into it.

**Two typed-SDK validation errors hit and fixed during this push, both a repeat of an already-known class of mistake**: `SetState('timeSlot', ...)` and `page.findByName('ConfirmMeetupRow')` both failed — `timeSlot` is a PRE-EXISTING field (native, pre-session) and `ConfirmMeetupRow` is a widget that was RENAMED by an earlier phase's own `ensureReplaced` — both needed the typed handle form (`SetState(ff.Pages.extensionPayment.state.timeSlot, ...)`, `page.findByKey('Row_qv779nx8')`, the row's real current key per the regenerated typed SDK) instead of a bare string, matching the exact rule this file's own affiliate-page section already documents ("re-running [findByName-style lookups] would fail since the OLD keys no longer exist... must go through app.editPage(...) targeting the NEW keys/names instead").

**Affiliate QR barcode: a confirmed, isolated SDK bug — not a re-statement of the earlier "no path found" conclusion.** Re-reading the `Barcode` widget's own constructor (`widgets.dart`) showed `data:` takes an arbitrary `DslExpression` via `normalizeExpression`, the same mechanism every other dynamic-bindable widget property goes through — §49's "no DSL-authorable path" conclusion was based on `docs`/`references` not covering Barcode, which turned out to be incomplete investigation, not a real limitation. Rebound the 4 dead-field share buttons' sibling — the QR itself — to a `https://icoccha.com/signup?ref=$uid` URL (matching what the share buttons already send), fetched via a new component-local state field + component-root `ON_INIT_STATE`. This failed `flutterflow ai validate` with two errors. Isolated via two further `validate`-only (no push) attempts: a literal string with no state binding at all made the "Widget class state field not found" error disappear completely (pointing at the component-root `ON_INIT_STATE` approach specifically — likely unsupported on components, only ever used on page roots elsewhere this whole session), but "Barcode Type in Barcode Widget is not properly set" persisted across all three variants tried, including a fully literal, minimal reconstruction with an explicit, valid `type: BarcodeKind.qrCode`. `compiler.dart`'s own Barcode-compilation code looks correct on direct inspection, and reuses the same general-purpose `_compileStringValue` helper Text/Button/Image already use successfully with dynamic bindings elsewhere in this exact file — so this isn't a blanket "dynamic data doesn't work" issue, it's specific to reconstructing a Barcode's `type` field via `ensureReplaced`, independent of what's bound to `data:`. Matches this project's own standing rule (an error surviving multiple distinct, plausible fixes means the bug is in the SDK, not the script) — stopped iterating rather than guessing further blindly. Left commented out in `dsl/edit.dart` with the full isolation trail preserved (not deleted) so a future session doesn't have to re-derive it. **Nothing was pushed for this part** — both isolation attempts used `flutterflow ai validate` specifically to avoid touching the live project with a known-broken state; the live QR code is unchanged from before this pass (still shows the dead `invitationCode` field, per §40's original finding — the 4 share buttons next to it were already fixed then).

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` clean before push; pushed once (extension_payment + reservation_detail only, QR section commented out to unblock); confirmed directly in `generated_code`, not the push's own success message — `extension_payment_widget.dart` calls `actions.callCreateExtensionPayment(widget!.resId, ...)` then `actions.confirmStripePayment(...)` (zero remaining references to `callCreatePaymentIntent`/`'test_res_001'`), `initState` calls `fetchExtensionTimeSlot(widget!.resId)`; `reservation_detail_widget.dart` has a real `延長する` button gated by `functions.canExtendReservation(_model.resVisibilityData)`, navigating to `ExtensionPaymentWidget.routeName` with the real `widget!.resId`. No backend redeploy needed — `createExtensionPayment`/`captureAuthorizedExtensions` were already live from §49.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (extension_payment.dart full rebuild + ReservationDetail entry point — 1 push; QR barcode section, isolated via 2 `validate`-only calls, left commented out with full diagnostic trail — no push); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside.

### §51: Mandatory review pass on §50's own work — found and fixed 2 real bugs the rebuild itself introduced/exposed, plus a documentation structure error and a genuine DSL-tooling lesson learned the hard way (2026-08-12)

**User's standing instruction: review completed work for errors before moving on, don't just declare done.** Re-traced §50's extension-payment rebuild end-to-end (not just re-reading what was written) and found two real issues, both severe enough to be worth the extra push cycle.

**1. CONFIRMED BUG — payment failure never rolled back the optimistic reservation mutation.** `createExtensionPayment` (stripe-payments.ts) increments the reservation's `extension_count` and `duration_minutes` IMMEDIATELY on Stripe PaymentIntent creation — before the guest has done anything in the Payment Sheet that follows. This is pre-existing backend design, not something §50 introduced, but it was never reachable before §50's fix (the page previously called a completely different, broken function) — so this is the first time it's actually live. Without a rollback, a guest whose card is declined (or who cancels the Payment Sheet) permanently loses one of their 3 extension slots and shows inflated duration for a reservation, for a payment that never happened; 3 failed attempts would exhaust the cap with zero successful extensions ever granted. Fixed with a new transactional `cancelExtensionPayment` Cloud Function — re-reads the extension doc's own `duration_minutes` (not a guessed value), only decrements if its status is STILL `"authorized"` (a no-op if a race means it was already captured, so a real successful payment can never be incorrectly reversed), and best-effort cancels the abandoned Stripe PaymentIntent. Wired into the client's existing `isPaymentSuccess == false` branch, before the failure snackbar. `callCreateExtensionPayment` now returns `client_secret|||extension_id` (delimited, matching this project's established pattern) so the extension_id is available for the rollback call.

**2. CONFIRMED BUG — `createExtensionPayment` trusted the client-supplied `amount` with zero server-side validation.** Same vulnerability class already fixed once before in this exact file, for `createPaymentIntent`'s `transport_fee` — but the equivalent fix was never applied to `createExtensionPayment`'s own `amount` param, which came straight from `request.data` with only an `>0` check. A crafted call (or even an innocent UI default-value mismatch — the extension-minutes dropdown's pre-selected value and the price-state fields' seeded defaults don't actually correspond to the same duration, a separate minor pre-existing quirk noted but not chased further since the fix below makes it moot) could request 5 hours of extension for ¥1. Fixed: `amount` is now always server-computed from `duration_minutes` and the reservation's own real `time_slot`, using the identical rate formula (`calculateExtensionPrice`'s constants, mirrored in TypeScript) the client already uses to display the price — so what the guest sees matches what they're actually charged, and the charge can never be manipulated below the real cost.

**3. Cosmetic but real — new button had no explicit sizing, inconsistent with every sibling button on the page.** Caught by directly comparing the compiled output against neighboring buttons (`合流報告`/`お誘いを断る`, both `width: 150, height: 40, borderRadius: 8`) rather than assuming a "successful push" meant "looks right" — the project's own Design & Quality Rule mandates explicit button sizing and this violated it. Fixed.

**4. A genuine DSL-tooling lesson, confirmed the hard way.** Re-issuing the SAME `page.ensureInsertedAfter(...)` call a second time (to add styling to the button it had already inserted in the previous push) compiled and pushed clean with ZERO error — but `generated_code` showed the styling never applied. This confirms `ensureInsertedAfter` belongs in the SAME one-shot/idempotent-unsafe category as `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage` (already documented for those two) — silently ignored once its target already exists, not just "errors if content differs" the way `ensureCustomAction` does. Fixed by leaving the original `ensureInsertedAfter` call frozen (commented out) and using a fresh `ensureReplaced` directly on the button's own real key instead — the same recovery pattern already established for this whole class of bug. **Separately**, attempting to "sync" `callCreateExtensionPayment`'s original `app.customAction(...)` declaration to match its already-`updateCustomAction`-mutated live content (the documented "part 2" of the two-part sequencing rule) FAILED with the exact same `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action... with a different payload` error, even with content that appeared byte-identical to what `generated_code` showed as live, across two separate attempts. Root cause not isolated. Resolved by following the pattern already proven elsewhere in this exact file for other post-`updateCustomAction` targets (`checkReservationFieldsComplete`, `fetchMyReservations`, `reservationListItemLabel`): comment out the original live declaration entirely rather than trying to keep it in sync — `updateCustomAction` alone is sufficient going forward, a live `app.customAction()` call for an already-mutated name is apparently never safe to keep re-asserting, not just something to defer.

**5. Documentation hygiene**: §49's own "Files touched" line had been displaced to the very end of the file (after §50's own) by how §50 was appended in the previous turn — a real structural error in the audit trail, not just a formatting nit, since it made §49's file list read as if it belonged to §50. Fixed by moving it back to directly follow §49's own verification paragraph.

**Verification:** backend — `npm run build` clean, both `createExtensionPayment` and the new `cancelExtensionPayment` confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`, all Cloud Run services healthy. DSL — 3 total pushes this round (rollback logic; a failed resync attempt reverted; the working comment-out-declaration fix), each verified directly in `generated_code`: the failure branch now calls `actions.callCancelExtensionPayment(widget!.resId, functions.splitField(_model.extCreateResult, 1))` before the failure snackbar; the extend button now shows `width: 150.0, height: 40.0, borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(8.0)` matching its siblings exactly.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (new `cancelExtensionPayment`, server-computed `amount` in `createExtensionPayment`), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (export), `dsl/edit.dart` (rollback wiring, button-styling fix via fresh `ensureReplaced`, `callCreateExtensionPayment` declaration commented out per the established post-`updateCustomAction` pattern — 3 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry + a structural fix to §49/§50's own file-list placement).

### §52: Second review pass, this time on §51 itself — found the SAME client-trust vulnerability class in the MAIN reservation payment function, more severe than the extension instance since it affects every real booking (2026-08-12)

**Same standing instruction, asked again.** Rather than re-reading §51's diff, checked whether the exact bug class just fixed there (§51 item 2, `createExtensionPayment` trusting a client-supplied `amount`) also existed in `createPaymentIntent` — the sibling function for the MAIN reservation payment, which §51's fix was directly modeled on a precedent from. It did, and worse: `amount` (base reservation price), `staff_fee`, and `cast_ids` all came straight from `request.data` with only a `>0` check on `amount` — Cloud Functions are callable directly by any authenticated client regardless of what this app's own UI sends, so a guest could call `createPaymentIntent` directly with `amount: 1` and pay almost nothing for ANY real reservation, not just an extension. This is more severe than the §51 instance purely by exposure — every single booking in the app goes through this function, versus extensions being an optional add-on.

**Fixed**: unlike extensions, there's no formula to recompute a correct base reservation price from (guest self-reports it at `createReservation` time — a disclosed, already-accepted simplification, no pricing catalog exists). But that value is already durably stored server-side on the reservation document the instant it's created, and the document was ALREADY being fetched in this function for the ownership check. `amount`/`staff_fee`/`cast_ids` are now read from `resDataForGuard` (the fetched reservation) instead of trusted from the client — zero new reads, no schema change, and no client-visible behavior change for legitimate callers (the app's own `getPaymentClientSecret` custom action already read these same values from the same reservation document before sending them, so a non-malicious call already sent the correct values; they're just no longer trusted).

**Found and fixed one more instance in the same function while there**: `transport_fee`'s client-supplied value was already reduced to a boolean SIGNAL by an earlier fix (only the amount was server-computed, not eligibility) — but `createReservation` already computes and stores that exact eligibility correctly at creation time (from the reservation's own `time_slot`/`night_time_slots`), so there was no reason to trust the client for the signal either. Now reads `resDataForGuard.transport_fee` directly. Two now-orphaned references to the removed `transport_fee` client variable (in the `thirty_min_rule_applied` computation, both the Firestore write and the return value) were caught immediately by `tsc` failing the build — fixed to reference the same server-side value.

**Swept for other instances of this same class, confirmed no more exist**: grepped every `amount`-from-`request.data` destructure across `stripe-payments.ts`. The only other hit, `processTip`'s `amount`, is NOT the same bug — a tip is an inherently guest-chosen, voluntary amount with no correct server-computable value to validate against (unlike a service charge with a real price), so client-trusting it is appropriate, not an oversight. Did not extend this sweep to admin-only functions (`adminManualRefund`, `adminForceCancel`'s `refund_amount`, etc.) — those are gated by `verifyAdmin()`, so the "any authenticated client can call it directly" exposure this whole bug class depends on doesn't apply; an admin-supplied amount is expected to be manually entered and trusted by design.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean after fixing the two orphaned references; deployed `createPaymentIntent` alone (only function touched this round), confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`. No DSL changes needed — the client already sent correct values for legitimate use, this was a pure backend-hardening fix.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (`createPaymentIntent`'s `amount`/`staff_fee`/`cast_ids`/`transport_fee` now server-sourced), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §53: Third review pass in this same chain — swept the rest of `stripe-payments.ts` for the same vulnerability class, found and fixed one more (dead code, but still exploitable), confirmed the rest of the file clean (2026-08-12)

**Same standing instruction, asked a third time.** Rather than re-reading §52's diff again, finished what §52 started: swept every remaining function in `stripe-payments.ts` for the same "client-trusted money/ID field on a directly-callable function" pattern, function by function, instead of stopping at the two instances already found.

**Found and fixed: `confirmPaymentIntent` had no ownership check at all.** Took `payment_intent_id`/`payment_method_id` straight from the client and called `stripe.paymentIntents.confirm()` with zero verification the caller owns that PaymentIntent — any authenticated user could call it with an arbitrary ID. Confirmed via grep that this callable is genuinely UNUSED by this app's real flow (`confirm_stripe_payment.dart` confirms payments entirely client-side through `Stripe.instance.presentPaymentSheet()`, which never calls this function) — but it's still deployed and directly callable by anyone with a Firebase Auth token regardless of what the app's own UI reaches, which is the exact same exposure every other fix in this chain has been closing, so it gets the same fix rather than being left as a theoretical gap: now retrieves the PaymentIntent first and verifies its `metadata.guest_uid` (already stamped by `createPaymentIntent` on every PaymentIntent it creates) matches the caller before allowing confirmation.

**Confirmed clean, no changes needed**: `capturePayment`/`cancelPayment` take no client-supplied amounts (operate on whatever the already-validated PaymentIntent already holds, or compute the cancellation-matrix split entirely server-side from `resData.total_amount` and hardcoded percentages). `processTip`'s client-chosen amount is legitimate by design (voluntary, no correct value to validate against — already confirmed in §52, re-verified here). `createSetupIntent`/`requestPayout`/`getWalletBalance` all operate exclusively on the CALLER's own `users/{request.auth.uid}` document and Stripe account — no arbitrary target-ID or amount parameter exists for any of them to trust in the first place, so this vulnerability class structurally doesn't apply. `recordCastRewardsAndProcessOthers`/`captureAuthorizedExtensions`/`transferPendingCastRewards`/`processAffiliateRewards` are internal functions never directly callable by a client (no `onCall` wrapper) — not exposed at all.

**Also reconsidered whether `createReservation` itself has the same bug for `base_amount`** — it doesn't, and this is a real design distinction, not an oversight: the guest setting their own `base_amount` at reservation-creation time is a disclosed, intentional simplification (no pricing catalog exists yet — see `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §12), not something to "validate" against a formula that doesn't exist. The actual bug this whole chain (§51/§52/§53) closed was a SEPARATE, later opportunity to inject a different value — a client could set an honest `base_amount` at reservation time, then supply a DIFFERENT (lower) `amount` at the payment step, which is what `createPaymentIntent`'s fix specifically closes by locking payment to whatever was already stored on the reservation.

**This sweep is now considered complete** for `stripe-payments.ts` — every `onCall` function in the file has been individually checked against this specific vulnerability class (client-trusted money/target-ID field on a function callable directly by any authenticated user), not just spot-checked. A similar sweep of `reservations.ts`/`admin.ts`/`affiliate.ts` for the same pattern was NOT done this round (scoped to the file this review chain started in) — worth a dedicated pass if this exact vulnerability class is suspected there too, but not assumed clean by extension.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean; `confirmPaymentIntent` deployed alone, confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`; all Cloud Run services healthy; no DSL changes needed (confirmed dead code, zero legitimate callers to affect).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (`confirmPaymentIntent` ownership check), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §54: Full comprehensive re-audit, explicitly requested after several rounds each found new bugs — 4 parallel deep-dive agents plus manual sweeps, found and fixed a genuine SSRF vulnerability, an identity/authorization bug in the core booking flow, a multi-cast review bug, a premature-status bug, and 8 other confirmed issues; one reported finding was a false positive, caught by verifying at the byte level rather than trusting the report (2026-08-12)

**Scope**: not "review the last change" — a from-scratch, systematic pass across everything: `reservations.ts`, `admin.ts`, `affiliate.ts`, `auth.ts`, `work-posts.ts`, `dsl/edit.dart`, and a `generated_code` cross-check of every page touched in §49-§53. Four parallel agents plus direct manual review (auth.ts/work-posts.ts, and triage/fixing of every finding).

**CRITICAL — SSRF via unvalidated `kyc_doc_url`.** `admin.ts`'s KYC-approval flow does `fetch(docUrl)` on `users.kyc_doc_url` with zero validation, to forward the document to Stripe. That field is set by the cast's own `submitKYC` callable with no URL validation, AND `firestore.rules` lets a user write ANY field on their own `users/{uid}` doc directly from the client SDK — bypassing `submitKYC` entirely. A malicious cast could set `kyc_doc_url` to an internal/cloud-metadata URL via a direct Firestore write, then simply wait for a routine admin KYC approval to trigger a server-side fetch of an attacker-chosen URL. No admin compromise required. Fixed by allowlisting the URL to Firebase Storage's own hosts (`firebasestorage.googleapis.com`/`storage.googleapis.com`) before ever fetching it, at the point where the dangerous fetch actually happens — closes both the `submitKYC` path and the direct-Firestore-write path with one fix, since neither can produce an allowed URL.

**HIGH — `createReservation` never checked `account_type === "cast"` on `cast_ids`.** The validation loop checked existence/`approval_status`/`is_frozen`/`blocked_users` but never the account type — and `approval_status` is a shared field present on every user doc regardless of type, so ANY approved, non-frozen, non-blocking user (including another GUEST) passed every check. A guest could create a reservation naming another guest as the "cast," and since `respondToReservation`/`confirmMeetup`/`reportCompletion`/`getChatRoomInfo` all authorize purely via `cast_ids.includes(uid)` with no account_type check of their own, that guest's account could drive the whole reservation lifecycle — approve, chat, complete, get reviewed — entirely outside the guest↔cast model. Fixed by adding the missing `account_type === "cast"` check (matching the correct pattern already used in `work-posts.ts`), plus a `typeof castId === "string"` guard for the same loop (closes a separate crash risk the same audit found).

**HIGH — multi-cast reservations could only ever get ONE cast reviewed.** `submitReview`'s duplicate-check was scoped to `res_id + reviewer_id` only, not `reviewee_id`/`cast_id` — so the guest's first review for ANY cast on a multi-cast reservation both created a doc that permanently blocked a second review call (wrong scope) AND flipped the reservation to `"completed"` (unconditional), which independently failed the status guard for any later call. Net effect: 2+ cast reservations could only ever have exactly one cast rated; the others silently never got a review. Fixed: duplicate-check now scoped per-cast too, and the reservation only flips to `"completed"` once every cast on it has actually been reviewed (payment transfers were already correctly per-reservation, not gated on this — unaffected).

**HIGH — `createPaymentIntent` wrote `status: "authorized"` before Stripe ever confirmed anything.** The write happened the instant `stripe.paymentIntents.create()` returned — before the guest even opened the Payment Sheet, let alone before the `amount_capturable_updated` webhook confirmed a real hold. `handleAmountCapturableUpdated` (already fixed in §49) ALSO correctly writes `status: "authorized"` when Stripe genuinely confirms it — meaning the field was being set twice, once prematurely. If a guest abandoned the Payment Sheet or their card failed, the reservation was left at `"authorized"` with zero real money on hold, and `respondToReservation`'s own status guard would let a cast accept a "confirmed" booking with no payment security behind it. Fixed by removing the premature write, mirroring the exact same webhook-driven-not-optimistic principle this project already deliberately chose for the Capture step (§6 defect #7).

**HIGH — a single malformed `affiliate_rewards` doc could permanently stall an entire affiliator's monthly payment.** `db.collection("users").doc(rewardData.referred_uid)` throws SYNCHRONOUSLY if `referred_uid` is missing (confirmed against the installed Admin SDK source, not guessed) — and admin.ts's own comments confirm at least one `_seed` placeholder doc already exists in this collection missing exactly that field. The throw was never caught locally, only by the OUTER per-(affiliator,month) try/catch in `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments`, which aborts the WHOLE batch — so every other legitimately-valid pending reward for that same affiliator+month never got paid or forfeited, stuck "pending" forever, re-failing at the same bad doc every single day the cron fired. Fixed by wrapping the per-reward loop body in its own try/catch — one bad doc is now logged and skipped (left pending for admin investigation, not silently forfeited), the rest of the batch proceeds normally.

**HIGH — `adminApprovePayout` silently approved a real payout for ANY unrecognized `action` value.** Only `"on_hold"`/`"rejected"` were explicitly checked; anything else — `undefined`, a typo, garbage — fell through to the branch that retrieves the Stripe balance and creates a real `stripe.payouts.create()`. Currently unreachable (no caller exists anywhere in this app's DSL — the admin panel UI, Phase 12, isn't built), but the function is still deployed and directly callable by any admin account. Fixed to require the exact literal `"approve"`, rejecting anything else.

**MEDIUM-HIGH — mutual-approval rule not re-checked for the referred cast at payment time.** `processAffiliatorPayment` re-checks the REFERRER's `approval_status` (forfeits if not approved) but never checked the REFERRED cast's — only `is_active`/`is_frozen`. A referred cast whose KYC gets revoked after their reward already accrued but before the monthly batch would still be paid in full, violating IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §4.2's "both parties remained approved throughout." Fixed by mirroring the referrer-side treatment exactly (forfeit, not defer — no `left_at`-equivalent timestamp exists for an approval_status change to month-scope it more precisely).

**MEDIUM — 6 functions in `reservations.ts` had no `res_id` presence/type validation**, so an omitted or malformed `res_id` produced an opaque `HttpsError('internal', 'INTERNAL')` (Firestore's `.doc(undefined)` throws synchronously) instead of a clean `invalid-argument` message. Fixed across `respondToReservation`, `confirmMeetup`, `reportCompletion`, `submitReview`, `sendChatMessage`, `getChatRoomInfo`. `createReservation` itself got the same treatment for `date`/`time_slot`/`duration_minutes`/`base_amount` (missing `date` throws inside `Timestamp.fromDate`; missing `time_slot`/`base_amount` don't throw but silently write `NaN`/incorrect values into a real reservation). Also fixed: `respondToReservation`'s `accept` param wasn't strictly boolean-checked (a string `"false"` is truthy in JS, would have silently flipped a decline into an accept); `submitReview`'s `rating` accepted a boolean/numeric-string via loose comparison instead of requiring an actual number.

**LOW — `getDiscoveryCasts` never excluded withdrawn casts.** `requestWithdrawal` sets `is_active: false` but never touches `approval_status` — so a cast who left the platform remained fully visible and bookable in Home discovery indefinitely (only `is_frozen`/blocked-users were excluded). Fixed the same way those are — in-memory filter, matching the existing pattern.

**LOW — age computed as a bare year subtraction, no month/day adjustment.** `completeOnboarding`'s `age = now.getFullYear() - birthDate.getFullYear()` overcounts age by 1 for anyone whose birthday hasn't occurred yet this calendar year relative to signup — wrong for roughly half of all signups depending on birth-date distribution, directly feeding `age_group`, a real search/filter field. A DIFFERENT, already-known issue on the same line (malformed/empty `birth_date` producing `NaN` and silently falling through to the last `age_group` branch) was already disclosed and mitigated client-side in `dsl/edit.dart` — but that mitigation only prevents the NaN case, not this off-by-one-year case for well-formed dates, so this is a genuinely new, non-redundant fix. Corrected with a proper month/day-aware calculation.

**A confirmed FALSE POSITIVE, caught before wasting a fix cycle.** One agent report flagged `admin.ts`'s nickname-prefix search as broken (`nickname_prefix + ""` instead of the standard `nickname_prefix + ""` trick, which would make it an exact-match filter, not a prefix search). Before editing, checked the actual file at the byte/codepoint level (Python, not just grep/Read) — the file already correctly contains `""` (U+F8FF), a private-use-area Unicode character with no visible glyph in most terminals/fonts, which is almost certainly why the agent's own tool output rendered it as if the string were empty. No bug existed; no edit was made. Worth remembering: when a finding involves a string that could contain non-printing or private-use Unicode characters, verify at the codepoint level before trusting a text-rendered report, including this session's own agent reports.

**DSL findings — mostly latent risk, not active bugs, confirmed via `.flutterflow/history/` push-history cross-reference (a technique not used before this pass).** The DSL re-verification agent proved the current `dsl/edit.dart` is byte-for-byte identical to the archived source of the last successful push — so nothing in the live file was actively broken. Two categories of genuine finding, both fixed:
1. **3 more cases of "live declaration co-exists with a later `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` for the same name"** (`fetchMyWorkSettings`, `fetchWalletLedgerHistory`, `formatYen`) — the exact anti-pattern already confirmed to throw a false mismatch for `callCreateExtensionPayment` (§51/§53), just not yet triggered for these three since their content happened to still match. `fetchMyWorkSettings`/`fetchWalletLedgerHistory` commented out per the established safe pattern; `formatYen` COULD NOT be commented out the same way — its Dart variable (`formatYenFn`) is referenced directly in later widget expressions, so removing the declaration would break Dart compilation, not just the FlutterFlow-side registration. Left live with an explicit warning: if `formatYen` is ever changed, both the declaration and the `updateCustomFunction` call must be edited together, in the same push, kept byte-identical.
2. **A stale comment on `callCreateExtensionPayment`** claiming a live `updateCustomAction` call still existed for it — it had actually been removed in the same push that commented out the original declaration, leaving genuinely NO live declaration or update path for that action anywhere in the script (not broken today since the action is already correctly live server-side, but the script itself can no longer reproduce or modify it without adding a fresh `updateCustomAction` call first). Comment corrected to state this accurately.
3. **NOT fixed, deliberately deferred, clearly disclosed**: 15 live `ensureReplaced` calls target widget keys already consumed by their own prior successful run (stale keys, e.g. `payment_confirm`'s 5 Text replacements, `ExtendReservationButton`, several ListViews). Confirmed via push-history that `ensureReplaced` does NOT throw on a stale key the way `mutateNode` does — these 15 blocks are currently dead/inert, not crash risks, and the file has already pushed successfully in this exact state. The real risk is only for a FUTURE edit to these specific blocks' content (which would be silently ignored, per the already-documented `ensureReplaced` one-shot-unsafe behavior). Not fixed this round given zero current impact and the volume of higher-priority, actively-exploitable findings above — flagged here so a future session doesn't have to rediscover it, and doesn't mistake "not yet frozen" for "safe to re-edit."

**Verification:** all backend changes — `npm run build` clean throughout; deployed in two batches (11 functions: createReservation/respondToReservation/confirmMeetup/reportCompletion/submitReview/sendChatMessage/getChatRoomInfo/createPaymentIntent/adminApprovePayout/processMonthlyAffiliatePayments/adminApproveKYC, plus completeOnboarding/getDiscoveryCasts deployed individually during the manual auth.ts sweep) — all confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`, all Cloud Run services healthy via `gcloud run services list`. DSL — pushed once (comment-only, no functional widget/action change), confirmed via `generated_code` that `fetchWalletLedgerHistory`/`fetchMyWorkSettings`/`formatYen` all still produce identical output to before the cleanup (no regression from removing the redundant declarations).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (SSRF fix, adminApprovePayout action validation), `affiliate.ts` (mutual-approval gap, malformed-doc crash isolation), `reservations.ts` (account_type check, res_id/field validation across 7 functions, multi-cast review fix, accept boolean check, rating type check), `auth.ts` (age calculation, getDiscoveryCasts is_active exclusion), `stripe-payments.ts` (premature authorized-status fix), `dsl/edit.dart` (3 declaration-cleanup fixes, 1 comment correction — 1 push), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside.

### §55: Follow-up on §54 — the user asked whether the items §54 explicitly deferred/disclosed could be fixed too. All 5 were genuinely fixable and are now fixed; one fix attempt (removing 3 "dead" DSL functions) was caught as a false positive by the compiler itself before it could push, and reverted (2026-08-12)

**Audit-log `reason` gaps closed (`admin.ts`, 11 functions).** 8 functions never captured a `reason` from the client at all (`adminUpdateReservationLocation`, `adminUpsertBanner`, `adminDeleteBanner`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`, `adminDeleteCocotenShop`, `adminCloseWorkPost`, `adminCreateWorkPost`, `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`) — added `reason` to each function's destructured `request.data`/`data` and threaded it into `createAuditLog`'s `details`/`reason` params (backward-compatible: still defaults to `""` if the caller — whichever admin app ends up calling these — doesn't send one yet). 3 more (`adminApproveKYC`, `adminToggleFreeze`, `adminForceCancel`) already accepted `reason` but the audit log itself defaulted to bare `""` while a MORE descriptive fallback was already being used one line away, in the guest-facing notification body or the `cancel_reason` field written to the reservation — e.g. `adminApproveKYC` told the rejected user "却下理由: 書類に不備があります。" but logged `reason: ""` for the same action. Fixed by computing the resolved reason once and reusing it in both places, so the audit trail is never less informative than what the affected user was actually told.

**JST/UTC month-boundary bug fixed (`affiliate.ts`).** `monthStrFromDate`, `countUniqueWorkDays`, and `getAffiliateDashboard`'s today/week/month stat boundaries all used bare `Date.getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`/`getDate()`, which read the Cloud Functions runtime's own UTC day, not Japan's — a reservation completed between 00:00-08:59 JST landed in the previous UTC calendar day, so it could be counted toward the wrong month (affecting the `affiliate_min_days` eligibility threshold that gates whether a reward pays out that month) or the wrong day-bucket on the affiliate dashboard, for a ~9-hour window every single day. Fixed by adding `jstParts`/`jstMidnightUtc`/`jstDateKey` helpers that mirror the identical, already-correct `JST_OFFSET_MS`-shift pattern already established in `admin.ts`'s `adminGetDashboardStats` (found and reused rather than inventing a second convention) — every date-bucketing call site in the file now goes through them, including the week-boundary's day-of-week computation (built from the JST calendar date's UTC-midnight equivalent, since weekday doesn't depend on time-of-day).

**`affiliate_payment_day` validation added (`admin.ts`, `adminUpdateSystemConfig`).** The sole write path for this field (a generic `settings` merge-set with zero field-specific validation) could previously accept 29/30/31, which never matches in every month lacking that date (Feb, and Apr/Jun/Sep/Nov for 31) — `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments`'s own `jstDay !== paymentDay` gate would then silently skip that month's run entirely. Fixed by validating `affiliate_payment_day` (when present in the update) is an integer in `[1, 28]` before the write — 28 guarantees the day exists in every calendar month, the standard fix for a day-of-month config field, rather than trying to special-case month lengths.

**15 stale-key `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter` blocks frozen (`dsl/edit.dart`).** All 15 (macchaChats' `ChatMessagesListView`, notificationsPage's `NotificationsListView`, adminReportReviewPage's 2 lists, walletPage's `WalletHistoryListView`, myWorkContent's body Column, paymentConfirm's 5 data-bound Text fields, kyc's 2 upload-status Text fields, extensionPayment's time-slot banner, reservationDetail's `ExtendReservationButton`) were re-verified against `generated_code/` before freezing (not just trusted from §54's own report) and confirmed already correctly landed. Commented out per the file's own established freeze discipline — each has no dedup guard and would otherwise keep reassigning fresh keys to its subtree on any future unrelated push. Two of these blocks (`notificationsPage`'s `readStatusLabelFn`, `kyc`'s `kycUploadStatusLabelFn`, `extensionPayment`'s `extensionTimeSlotBannerFn`) were each that block's ONLY caller — their `app.customFunction(...)` declarations were deliberately left live/uncommented (the function itself is still genuinely deployed and in use by the live widget; only the widget-authoring call site moved into a comment), so `dart analyze` now flags these 3 as unused local variables. Confirmed this is a harmless warning, not a build error — `flutterflow ai test` passes clean with them present.

**A second confirmed false positive this review cycle — caught by the compiler, not by inspection, before anything pushed.** §54's DSL audit had flagged `canConfirmReservationMeetup`/`canReportReservationCompletion`/`canCancelReservationNow` as dead code ("zero live references anywhere in the file") and queued them for removal via `app.removeCustomFunction(...)`. That check was accurate about `dsl/edit.dart`'s own text — nothing in the current script calls any of the three by name — but wrong about the live project: `flutterflow ai run` rejected the removal outright (`Validation: Custom function not found` / `Row 'ConfirmMeetupRow' ... Custom function not found` etc., ×3), and a direct grep of `generated_code/lib/reservation/reservation_detail/reservation_detail_widget.dart` confirmed why — all three genuinely gate live `visible:` conditions on ReservationDetail's meetup-confirm/completion-report/cancel buttons, wired through an earlier, since-frozen `ensureReplaced`-class block whose own DSL text no longer names the functions it calls. **The generalizable lesson**: "zero references in this script's text" is not the same claim as "zero references in the live project" — a function's only caller can live in a PAST push that has since been frozen/commented out of the current script (removing the DSL source of a widget doesn't undo what it already built server-side). Before removing ANY custom function/action as "dead," grep `generated_code/` for its name, not just `dsl/edit.dart` — the DSL script is a build recipe, not an inventory of what's currently live. Restored both declarations exactly as they were (byte-identical to before the incorrect edit) and re-pushed; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded and `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/custom_functions.dart` confirms all three functions are present post-push with the ReservationDetail widget still calling them.

**Deployment:** 14 Cloud Functions redeployed (`adminUpdateReservationLocation`, `adminApproveKYC`, `adminToggleFreeze`, `adminForceCancel`, `adminUpsertBanner`, `adminDeleteBanner`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`, `adminDeleteCocotenShop`, `adminCloseWorkPost`, `adminCreateWorkPost`, `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`, `adminUpdateSystemConfig`, `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments`, `getAffiliateDashboard`), all confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`. DSL pushed once (the freeze + the revert-in-place of the incorrect removal, net zero functional widget change beyond the 15 freezes). `npm run build` clean throughout; `flutterflow ai test` passes; all Cloud Run services healthy.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (11 reason-gap fixes, `affiliate_payment_day` validation), `affiliate.ts` (JST helpers + 4 call sites), `dsl/edit.dart` (15 freezes, 1 attempted-then-reverted removal), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §56: Phase 11 slice 1 — MyPage drawer IA wiring, the two stub quick-action buttons, a real own-rating display, and a new SystemInfo page (version info + logout) (2026-08-12)

**Context.** Phases 0–10 are all `[DONE]`. Phase 11 ("My Page IA completion & polish") bundles ~10 distinct sub-features (drawer wiring, card management, block-list, notification prefs, phone/auth self-service, a cast work-calendar, legal-document viewers, an inquiry form, version info) — too large for one push. A pre-implementation audit (reading `my_page.dart` and `generated_code/lib/mypage/my_page/my_page_widget.dart` directly, not the stale `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` description) found the highest-severity, lowest-risk finding first: `Affiliate` (Phase 9, fully built, real ~21-field dashboard) and `MyWorkContent` (Phase 10, fully built) were **100% unreachable from anywhere in the app** — the exact same "built but nobody can get to it" bug class Phase 10 already caught and fixed for `MyWorkContent` via `WorkPage`. 5 of MyPage's 7 drawer rows had zero `onTap` at all; both quick-action buttons ("プロフィール編集"/"ワーク編集") were literal `print('Button pressed...')` stubs despite their destinations being fully backend-wired since Phase 3. User confirmed: build this fast-wins slice now (wire what already works, zero new backend risk), scope the remaining ~8 sub-features in follow-up turns; for the not-yet-drafted legal content (ToS/business-commission/privacy-policy — confirmed absent from this repo entirely, flagged in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 0.5 as a legal drafting deliverable, not a coding task), use explicit placeholder content when that slice is eventually built, not fabricated legal text.

**Wired (all in `dsl/edit.dart`, one push, extending the existing `app.editPage(ff.Pages.myPage, ...)` block from Phase 8):**
- Drawer row 2 ワーク・活動管理 → `Navigate(ff.Pages.myWorkContent)`.
- Drawer row 4 集客・シェア → `Navigate(ff.Pages.affiliate)`.
- Drawer row 7 システム・情報 → `Navigate(systemInfoPage)` (new page, below).
- "プロフィール編集" button (was a stub) → `Navigate(ff.Pages.profileEdit)` (Phase 3, already fully backend-wired).
- "ワーク編集" button (was a stub) → `Navigate(ff.Pages.myWorkContent)` — same destination as row 2; `MyWorkContent` already self-gates non-casts with a "この画面はキャスト専用です。" message (confirmed in this session's own §54/55 freeze work), so no extra guard needed even though MyPage is cast-only by convention rather than enforcement.

**Own-rating rebind — zero new backend.** MyPage's "評価：3.0" was a static placeholder. Fixed by reusing the exact `fetchCastReviews`/`averageRatingLabelFn` pair already live on `cast_profile.dart` (Phase 7, public-read `reviews` collection query by `reviewee_id`), called with `AuthUser(AuthUserField.userId)` — a built-in DSL expression for the current user's own uid (confirmed via the SDK's `references.dart`; compiles to `currentUserUid`) — instead of `PageParam('castId')`. Added a `myReviewsList` state field and an `ON_INIT_STATE` fetch. **Two DSL/tooling findings from doing this:**
1. `page.root`'s `ON_INIT_STATE` already carried a real native action (`FFAppState().navIndex = 4`) — reproduced it verbatim as the first step of the new `ensureActions` call rather than adding a second, separate `ensureActions` call on the same root+trigger, per the already-documented finding (HomePage's own history, this file) that a second call targeting the same root+trigger fails `compileDslApp` outright (`Name ... already in use`) even when reproducing an unchanged chain.
2. `EditWidgetPatch.text(...)` (the `page.update(...).text(...)` path) **only accepts a literal String, not a dynamic expression** — confirmed by compile error (`CustomFunctionInvocation can't be assigned to String`) when first attempted. Same drift class already documented for `patch.visible(...)` (literal-bool-only). Fixed by using `page.ensureReplaced(...)` on the `Text` node instead, matching the established workaround for this exact limitation. The native `RatingBar` widget itself (`RatingBar_xph2043g`) has no DSL constructor at all (grepped the whole SDK — not in `widgets.dart`), so its 5 stars stay a static `initialRating: 3.0` — only the adjacent numeric/count label is now real. Disclosed, not a gap worth chasing given the RatingBar's stars are decorative anyway once the real `★X.X (N件)` text sits right next to them.

**New page: `SystemInfo`.** Title "システム・情報", a version-info line, and a real, labeled "ログアウト" button using the DSL's built-in `Logout()` action (confirmed via `references/auth_shell_dsl.dart` — handles sign-out and the router's own auth-gate redirect, no manual `Navigate` needed after it). Logout already technically worked before this — a stock FlutterFlow AppBar-logo `ON_TAP` present on ~16 pages project-wide (confirmed not built by any session phase, a native scaffold pattern) — but it was an undocumented gesture, not a labeled UI element; left as-is (harmless) while this page adds the real, discoverable path. Version info required a new `package_info_plus` pub dependency (`^10.2.1`, confirmed current stable via direct pub.dev fetch before writing the version string, per this project's own "pub.dev discovery is your job" rule) and a zero-arg `fetchAppVersionLabel` **custom action** (not a custom function — `PackageInfo.fromPlatform()` is inherently async I/O, and every custom function in this project is a pure/synchronous computation; confirmed this distinction by reading the SDK's own `app.customFunction`/`app.customAction` doc comments before choosing, not guessed).

**A third DSL/tooling finding this session**: `app.ensurePage(...)`'s returned `PageHandle` **must** be captured in a local variable and referenced directly (`Navigate(systemInfoPage)`) rather than via `ff.Pages.systemInfo` when the page is created and navigated-to within the **same** script run — `ff.Pages.*` reflects the typed SDK as of the START of the run, so a page declared earlier in the same script has no typed handle yet (confirmed by compile error: `Member not found: 'systemInfo'`). This is the same underlying "can't reference something just-created in the same run by its typed/string name" family already documented for widgets (`project_rules.md` line ~85) — now confirmed to apply to pages too. Fixed by declaring `SystemInfo` BEFORE `MyPage`'s drawer-wiring block and using its captured handle.

**Explicitly left out of scope this slice, to be picked up in follow-up turns:** drawer row 5 対人・実績管理 (needs the not-yet-built block-list feature — no sensible destination exists yet, left unwired rather than pointed at a throwaway placeholder page), drawer row 6 サポート・法的項目 (needs the legal-content slice), the 4 static illustration-button placeholders (ご利用規約/ガイドライン×2/Q&A — need both real destinations and the client's SVG asset zip), the work-calendar tab (`Calendar_5lwqxdoh`, decorative, needs a real data model + custom grid UI — the single largest remaining Phase 11 item), the Affiliate QR barcode graphic (confirmed genuine SDK bug, already disclosed §50, not reattempted), card/payment-method management (backend `createSetupIntent` already exists, zero UI), notification prefs, phone/auth self-service settings, general inquiry form (distinct from the existing `reportUser`).

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` passed before push; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded on the second attempt (first attempt caught both compile errors above before any push happened — validation, not a live break). Read `generated_code/lib/system_info/system_info_widget.dart` directly and confirmed: version label loads via `actions.fetchAppVersionLabel()` on init, logout button performs a real `authManager.signOut()` + navigates to Login. Read `generated_code/lib/mypage/my_page/my_page_widget.dart` and confirmed all 5 new navigations (`MyWorkContentWidget`/`AffiliateWidget`/`SystemInfoWidget`/`ProfileEditWidget`/`MyWorkContentWidget` routeNames), the `navIndex = 4` native action preserved, `fetchCastReviews(currentUserUid)` called on init, and the rating area now calling `functions.averageRatingLabel(_model.myReviewsList.toList())` instead of a literal `'3.0'`.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (MyPage drawer wiring, own-rating rebind, new SystemInfo page + `fetchAppVersionLabel` custom action + `package_info_plus` pub dependency — 2 pushes, first caught by validation before landing), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` updated alongside.

**Review-pass correction (same day, immediately after the above landed).** Asked to review this slice's own work before moving on. Found and fixed a real, self-inflicted bug: this slice's own newly-landed `app.ensurePage('SystemInfo', ...)` and `page.ensureReplaced(page.findByKey('Text_a7eis4fi'), ...)` calls were left LIVE in the script — violating this project's own extensively-documented freeze-immediately-after-landing discipline (§15, §54/55, and the dedicated `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureCustomAction` sections in `project_rules.md`), which this same slice's own commit messages and comments correctly *cited* but then didn't actually *apply* to its own new additions. Confirmed via direct inspection (not assumed):
- `Text_a7eis4fi` no longer existed in the regenerated `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/my_page.dart` — `ensureReplaced` had already reassigned it a fresh key (`Text_8ipm8e8m`) the moment the first push ran. Left live, the very next unrelated push to this file would have failed outright (`findByKey('Text_a7eis4fi')` finding nothing) — a genuinely higher-severity finding than the `SystemInfo` one below, since this one would have broken a future push, not just silently no-op.
- `app.ensurePage('SystemInfo', ...)` is genuinely idempotent (confirmed via the SDK's own doc comment — no-ops if the page already exists) so leaving it live would not have errored, but doing so risked the same "silent no-op trap" already documented for every other `ensurePage`'d page in this file: a future edit to its inline `body`/`state`/`onLoad` would silently have no effect. Freezing it required also fixing the `Navigate(systemInfoPage)` call in the drawer-wiring block below it — that local-variable capture was only ever necessary to reference a page within the SAME run it's created in; now that `SystemInfo` is genuinely live and `ff.Pages.systemInfo` exists as a real typed handle (regenerated after the first push), the reference was switched to the standard `ff.Pages.systemInfo` form, matching every other already-landed page reference in this file.

Both fixed by commenting out the two calls (with the real replacement key documented inline for the Text node, and a pointer to `app.editPage(ff.Pages.systemInfo, ...)` for any future page edit), re-tested, re-pushed, and re-verified against `generated_code` to confirm zero regression (the rating label and SystemInfo page both render identically to before — only the script's own future-safety changed, not any live behavior). **Lesson for next time**: when a task explicitly involves landing a NEW `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensurePage` call, freeze it in the SAME push's own review step, immediately after confirming via `generated_code` that it landed — don't treat "I know this rule and cited it correctly for pre-existing code" as equivalent to "I applied it to my own new code in the same turn." A dedicated self-review pass, even directly following a push that already felt complete, is what caught this.

### §57: Phase 11 slice 2 — block-list viewer + unblock, closing the drawer row 5 (対人・実績管理) gap left open in slice 1 (2026-08-12)

**Context.** `blockUser` (Phase 7) is add-only — no `unblockUser`, no page listing a user's own `blocked_users`. Before planning, confirmed the real scope: `blocked_users` lives on the caller's own doc (client-readable directly per firestore.rules' owner-only rule), but resolving each blocked UID's nickname/photo requires reading OTHER users' docs, which the same rule blocks — genuinely needs a new callable, not just client-side Firestore reads. Also investigated card/payment-method management as an alternative next slice first: `createSetupIntent` already exists but only lets a guest attach a card to their Stripe customer — the shared `confirmStripePayment` Payment Sheet action (used by `payment_confirm.dart`/`extension_payment.dart`) never requests a customer/ephemeral-key, so a saved card would never actually surface for reuse without also touching that shared action and adding an ephemeral-key backend piece. Surfaced this to the user before picking a slice rather than understating the scope; user chose block-list as the more genuinely contained option.

**Backend (`firebase/functions/src/auth.ts`, next to `blockUser`):**
- `unblockUser({target_uid})` — mirrors `blockUser` exactly, `FieldValue.arrayRemove` instead of `arrayUnion`.
- `getBlockedUsersDetails()` — reads the caller's own `blocked_users`, batch-fetches each via `Promise.all` (mirroring `admin.ts`'s existing nickname-batch-resolve pattern), returns `{items: ["uid|||nickname|||photo_url", ...]}` — the exact same pipe-joined-string convention `getDiscoveryCasts` already uses, specifically so the DSL side could consume it with the existing generic `splitFieldFn` extractor rather than inventing new single-purpose ones. Skips (doesn't crash on) a blocked account that's since been deleted.
- Both deployed individually, confirmed `ACTIVE`.

**DSL (`dsl/edit.dart`):** `callUnblockUser`/`fetchBlockedUsers` thin callable wrappers (mirroring `callBlockUser`/`fetchDiscoveryCasts` exactly); a new `isListEmpty(items)` custom function (genuinely reusable — didn't exist, checked first) gating an empty-state message; new `BlockList` page (list + per-row "ブロック解除" button that unblocks then refetches, the same "act, then refetch" pattern used throughout this file); wired drawer row 5 to it.

**Caught one mistake before it ever reached a push**: while writing the empty-state `visible:` binding, first referenced a `listLengthFn` that doesn't exist anywhere in this file — invented it without checking, same class of error this project's own discipline exists to catch. Caught by grepping for it BEFORE running `flutterflow ai test` (not after a compile failure), and replaced with a real, newly-added `isListEmptyFn` since no equivalent existed under any name.

**Applied the freeze lesson from slice 1's own review pass, this time in the SAME turn instead of a follow-up**: per the approved plan, froze `BlockList`'s `ensurePage` call immediately after confirming (via `generated_code`) that the first push landed, in the same turn — not deferred to "review later." Switched the drawer's `Navigate(blockListPage)` to `Navigate(ff.Pages.blockList)` at the same time, exactly mirroring the `SystemInfo` correction. Re-tested, re-pushed, re-verified zero regression.

**Verification:** `unblockUser`/`getBlockedUsersDetails` both confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`. `flutterflow ai test` passed before each push; both pushes succeeded on the first attempt. Read `generated_code/lib/block_list/block_list_widget.dart` and confirmed: empty-state text gated on `functions.isListEmpty(...)`, list items call `functions.splitField(...)` for nickname, unblock button calls `actions.callUnblockUser(...)` then `actions.fetchBlockedUsers()` and updates state. Read `generated_code/lib/mypage/my_page/my_page_widget.dart` and confirmed drawer row 5 calls `context.pushNamed(BlockListWidget.routeName)`.

**Review-pass correction (same day, immediately after the above landed).** Asked to review this slice's own work before moving on. Re-read `getBlockedUsersDetails` fresh against the exact function it claimed to mirror (`getDiscoveryCasts`, both in `auth.ts`) and found a real inconsistency: `getDiscoveryCasts` sanitizes BOTH `nickname` and `photoUrl` by stripping any `|||` before pipe-joining them (`.replace(/\|\|\|/g, "")` on both fields — confirmed by reading its actual source, not assumed) — `getBlockedUsersDetails` only sanitized `nickname`, leaving `photoUrl` unstripped. Low practical risk (Firebase Storage download URLs essentially never contain `|||`) and currently dormant (the `BlockList` page doesn't even display the photo field today, only nickname), but a genuine correctness gap relative to the pattern this function explicitly claimed to follow — if a photo URL ever did contain the delimiter, it would corrupt the pipe-parsed shape for any current or future consumer. Fixed by applying the same `.replaceAll("|||", "")` to `photoUrl`, matching `getDiscoveryCasts` exactly; redeployed `getBlockedUsersDetails`, reconfirmed `ACTIVE`. Also checked and ruled out one other suspicion during this review — the generated `BlockList`/`MyPage` widget code emits TWO consecutive `safeSetState(() {})` calls at the end of the unblock button's action chain, which looked at first glance like a possible codegen artifact worth investigating; confirmed via `generated_code/lib/notifications_page/notifications_page_widget.dart` that this exact double-call pattern already appears ~8 times in previously-verified, already-live code — standard FlutterFlow codegen behavior, not something introduced by this slice, no fix needed.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (`unblockUser`, `getBlockedUsersDetails`), `index.ts` (exports), `dsl/edit.dart` (2 custom actions, 1 custom function, new BlockList page — frozen same-turn, drawer wiring — 2 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` updated alongside.

### §58: Full comprehensive re-audit round 3 — 5 parallel deep-dive agents covering ground never audited before (stripe-webhooks.ts, firestore.rules/indexes cross-referenced against every real query, app-wide navigation-reachability sweep, index.ts/config.ts, an independent re-review of the last two Phase 11 slices) plus personal resolution of 4 previously-deferred items — found and fixed 2 CRITICAL bugs (every returning user's cold launch landing on the wrong page; chat messages silently unreadable for every real user since the feature shipped), 1 money-critical bug (cancellation fee captures double-paying casts and un-cancelling reservations), plus 9 more confirmed fixes across security, correctness, and UX (2026-08-12)

**Context.** Third round of "stop and audit everything" this session (following §54/55 and its own follow-up).
