Rather than re-covering files already deeply audited (reservations.ts, admin.ts, affiliate.ts, auth.ts, work-posts.ts, stripe-payments.ts, the DSL freeze sweep), scoped this round at genuinely uncovered ground: `stripe-webhooks.ts` (touched reactively many times, never given a dedicated top-to-bottom read), `firestore.rules`/`firestore.indexes.json` (patched incrementally as gaps were found, never cross-referenced against every real query in one pass), a systematic sweep for the "built but unreachable" bug class across the WHOLE app (not just MyPage, which was already fully checked), `index.ts`/`config.ts` (never audited at all), and an independent, fresh-eyes re-review of Phase 11 slices 1-2 specifically to catch anything missed by two rounds of self-review already. 5 parallel background agents, plus personal resolution of 4 `ensureInsertedAfter` blocks that an earlier pass had noticed as unfrozen but explicitly deferred as out-of-scope at the time.

---

#### CRITICAL — every logged-in user's cold app launch landed on ExtensionPayment, not Home

Confirmed directly in `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/nav/nav.dart`: `initialLocation: '/'`'s route builder AND `errorBuilder` both resolved `appStateNotifier.loggedIn ? ExtensionPaymentWidget() : LoginPageWidget()` — every returning user's fresh app open landed on the extension-payment screen (which needs a `resId` param that's empty on a cold launch, since nothing sets it) instead of `HomePageWidget()`. Almost certainly a stale project-level "Initial Page" setting left over from whenever `ExtensionPayment` was being actively developed (§50), never reverted — nothing in `dsl/edit.dart` ever declared this (`app.page`/`app.ensurePage` both default `isInitial: false`, confirmed by re-reading every ExtensionPayment-related block).

Fix required real investigation, not a one-line change: the obvious lever (`setInitialPage(project, pageName:)`, found in the SDK's own `project_helpers.dart`) turned out NOT to be part of the public DSL export surface (`flutterflow_ai.dart`'s barrel only exports `findPage`/`findComponent` from that file) — confirmed by a real compile failure (`Method not found: 'setInitialPage'`) caught by `flutterflow ai run`'s validation before anything pushed. The actual mechanism is `FirebaseAuthDeclaration.homePageName`/`configureFirebaseAuth`, but rewriting the WHOLE Firebase Auth config was deliberately avoided — it requires the current `providers` list as a required param, which wasn't discoverable through any tool available this session, and passing the wrong list risks silently disabling a live auth provider. Fixed surgically instead: `project.ensureAuthPageInfo().homePageNodeKeyRef = FFNodeKeyReference(key: findPage(project, name: 'HomePage')!.node.key)` via `app.raw(...)`, touching only that one field — `findPage`/`FFNodeKeyReference` are both already part of the public export surface. Verified post-push: `nav.dart` now correctly resolves `HomePageWidget()`.

#### CRITICAL — chat messages have been unreadable by every real guest/cast since the feature shipped

`fetchChatMessages` (backing `MacchaChats`) queried `chat_rooms` filtered only on `res_id`. The collection's own rule requires `resource.data.participants.hasAny([uid])` — but Firestore's rule engine can only prove that condition for a *query* (not a get-by-id) when the query's own `where()` clauses already mirror it; a filter on `res_id` alone can't be statically proven safe, so the query was denied outright for every non-admin caller, silently swallowed by the action's own `try/catch` into an empty message list. This is the exact same mechanism as the already-fixed Home-discovery bug (§29/§33) — confirmed independently by the auditing agent citing that fix's own comment as the mechanism it was relying on. Fixed by adding `.where('participants', arrayContains: uid)` to the same query (mirrors the rule's condition so it's now provable) plus a new composite index (`chat_rooms`: `participants CONTAINS` + `res_id ASC`). Landing this required going through `updateCustomAction` (the action already existed live) rather than editing the `app.customAction` declaration in place, per this file's own established rule.

#### MONEY-CRITICAL — guest cancellation-fee captures were double-paying casts and un-cancelling the reservation

`cancelPayment`'s guest-charged cancellation path calls `stripe.paymentIntents.capture(id, {amount_to_capture: chargedAmount})` with no `metadata` — so the resulting `payment_intent.succeeded` webhook event still carried the ORIGINAL PaymentIntent's type-less metadata, and `handlePaymentIntentSucceeded`'s `!paymentIntent.metadata?.type` guard (which correctly distinguishes extension/tip captures from the main reservation) treated this cancellation-fee capture as if it were a normal full-service completion. On nearly every guest cancellation with a fee: the webhook overwrote `status: "cancelled"` (which `cancelPayment` had JUST written) back to `"review_pending"` — letting a review be submitted for a cancelled reservation — wrote a bogus `pair_history.last_capture_at`, and called `recordCastRewardsAndProcessOthers` using the reservation's FULL `total_amount`, paying the cast a SECOND time on top of what `recordCancellationCastRewards` (called synchronously in `cancelPayment` itself, using the correct reduced `chargedAmount`) already correctly paid moments earlier — at the wrong, much larger, full-service rate. Affiliate rewards were double-triggered on the same wrong base too. This was the DESIGNED cancellation-with-fee path, not a rare edge case — it fired on nearly every guest-initiated cancellation carrying a fee (100% or 50% of the total, per the existing cancellation-fee-matrix policy).

Fixed by tagging the capture with `metadata: { type: "cancellation" }` (Stripe metadata updates merge by key, so `res_id`/`guest_uid`/etc. are untouched) — the webhook's existing guard now correctly skips its whole reservation-mutating block for this event, exactly like it already does for extensions/tips, with zero loss of correct behavior since `recordCancellationCastRewards` already fully handles the reward/ledger bookkeeping. Also fixed the adjacent, directly-related issue this exposed: the webhook's notification text unconditionally said "決済が完了しました" (payment completed) regardless of `metadata.type` — genuinely misleading for what is actually a cancellation FEE charge. Now says "キャンセル料が確定しました" for the cancellation case specifically.

---

#### HIGH — the entire post-booking reservation-management flow was an orphaned island

`ReservationListPage` (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §7 — the client's own explicitly-requested "reservation list, distinct from the Maccha match feed") was fully built (§14) but had **zero incoming navigation anywhere in the app** — confirmed by a systematic reachability sweep of all 38 pages and 8 components, cross-checked against `generated_code` (not just DSL text) to rule out false negatives. Worse than the already-fixed MyWorkContent/Affiliate/BlockList instances of this same bug class: `ReservationDetail` (approve/decline/meetup/complete/rate/cancel) and `ExtensionPayment` are ONLY reachable from this page's own row-tap, so the whole post-booking management flow was unreachable, not just one page. No existing AppBar icon fit semantically (HomePage's/MacchaPage's "検索"/"お知らせ" slots are either already wired or a poor fit for "my reservations") — added a new "予約一覧" icon to HomePage's AppBar, matching the exact precedent already proven for this class of gap (WorkPage's "マイワーク" nav button, Phase 10). Caught a real authoring mistake mid-fix: `Column` has no `onTap:` parameter in this DSL (only NATIVE pre-existing scaffold Columns carry an onTap-capable trigger) — switched to `Button` with an `icon:` param instead, matching `MyWorkNavButton`'s own exact shape.

#### MEDIUM — 8 more confirmed fixes

1. **`affiliate_min_days || 3`** (affiliate.ts, 3 call sites) silently coerced a legitimate admin-set `0` (no minimum working days) back to 3 — unlike `affiliate_payment_day`, which is validated at write time, this field had zero validation. Fixed with an explicit `typeof === "number"` check, matching the codebase's own established pattern for this exact falsy-zero class of bug.
2. **`processAffiliateRewards`'s loop** (stripe-payments.ts) had no per-iteration try/catch, unlike its two sibling loops (cast-reward, staff-fee) in the same file — one bad affiliate lookup aborted every LATER cast in the same reservation's loop, and since `processed_events` is created before dispatch (deliberate idempotency design), Stripe's retry of the same event was treated as a duplicate and skipped, so the missed rewards were never retried. Fixed with the same try/catch-continue shape already used by its siblings.
3. **Webhook signature verification failed OPEN** on a missing `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — silently trusted an entirely unsigned `req.body` as a genuine Stripe event rather than refusing to process anything. Currently mitigated in practice (the secret is confirmed set), but the code itself had no guard against this ever regressing. Fixed to fail CLOSED (500, refuses all requests) when the secret is absent.
4. **5 orphaned firestore indexes removed**: `audit_logs` `admin_uid`+`created_at` (wrong field name — the real field is `admin_id`) and `target_type`+`created_at` (real field, but the function's own comment documents deliberately never filtering by it); `users` `is_active`+`created_time`, `role`+`created_time`, `role_admin`+`created_time` (`created_time` has zero occurrences anywhere in the codebase — the real field is always `created_at`; `role_admin` is never used in a `.where()` at all). The 5 stale indexes remain live server-side (Firebase requires `--force` to actually delete an index no longer in `firestore.indexes.json`, not attempted — harmless to leave, just minor unused storage).
5. **`reservations/{resId}/extensions` had no firestore.rules coverage at all** — the 4th of an already-known group of 4 rule-less collections (`debt_history`/`pair_history`/`work_posts` were fixed on 2026-08-12 in an earlier pass; this one was missed in that same cleanup). Currently harmless (confirmed zero client-side access, everything goes through Admin SDK) but closes the latent trap. Added, matching `pair_history`'s exact admin-only treatment.
6. **BlockList's unblock button gave zero success/failure feedback** — inconsistent with the sibling `callBlockUser`/`callReportUser` wiring on `CastProfile`, which correctly shows a Snackbar either way. Found by an independent, fresh-eyes review agent specifically tasked with re-checking Phase 11 slices 1-2 without the original author's own context. Fixed to match the sibling pattern.
7. **BlockList's nickname Text had no overflow protection** — unlike every other dynamic list-item Text in this file (reviews, notifications, chat previews). Fixed with `maxLines`/`overflow: ellipsis`, wrapped in `Expanded` (required for either to actually take effect inside a `Row`).
8. **`getBlockedUsersDetails`'s `photoUrl` field wasn't `|||`-sanitized**, unlike `nickname` in the same function and unlike `getDiscoveryCasts` (the function it explicitly claimed to mirror) — found in a dedicated self-review of §57's own just-landed work, before this round even started. Already fixed and documented in §57's own addendum; listed here only for completeness of this round's full picture.

#### 4 previously-deferred `ensureInsertedAfter` blocks, resolved

An earlier pass (§54's audit) noticed 4 live, unfrozen `ensureInsertedAfter` calls (CastProfile's reviews/report/block section, ReservationDetail's tip section, WalletPage's Stripe-account guidance, WorkPage's MyWork nav button) but explicitly deferred them as out-of-scope at the time. This round resolved all 4: verified each landed correctly via `generated_code` (confirmed by CONTENT — e.g. grepping for `callProcessTip`/`fetchCastReviews` — not by searching for the DSL's own internal `name:` label, which is NOT reliably preserved as a literal string in compiled Dart for a nested widget the way page-level class names are; a first verification attempt using the wrong method briefly looked like a false regression before this was caught and corrected), then froze all 4.

#### Findings investigated and explicitly NOT fixed this round — disclosed, not silently dropped

- **`handlePaymentIntentCanceled` is a no-op stub** despite firing on every cast decline, full cancellation, and abandoned extension — all of which write `status`/similar optimistically, the same "API succeeded, Firestore write crashed" risk class already fixed for the Capture path (§6 defect #7), never mirrored here. Fixing properly means redesigning the response flow of 3 call sites (`respondToReservation`'s reject branch, `cancelPayment`, `cancelExtensionPayment`) to defer their status writes to this webhook instead of writing synchronously — judged too large and risky to rush within this already-large round; the current optimistic-write behavior is a known, narrow race window, not a confirmed active bug like the cancellation-capture one above.
- **`payout.paid`/`payout.failed` are no-op stubs**, and `adminApprovePayout` never writes `metadata`/correlates the Stripe payout ID back onto the `payout_requests` doc — even a rewritten handler couldn't match an event to a request today. `payout_requests.status` is permanently stuck at `"approved"` regardless of whether the real Stripe payout later succeeds or fails. Needs new correlation infrastructure (write `stripe_payout_id` at approval time, look it up in the handler), a real but non-trivial build — not attempted this round.
- **`mirrorStripeNotification` writes a second, raw-JSON debug-grade notification** (`JSON.stringify(obj).substring(0, 500)`) into the end-user-facing notification feed for every one of the 11 handled webhook event types, in addition to each handler's own human-readable one. Reads like a debugging aid that ended up on the wrong channel, but intent isn't verifiable from the code alone — not removed without confirming this wasn't a deliberate design choice.
- **`isAdmin()`'s `role_admin`/`roleAdmin` fallback branches may be unreachable in practice** — `firestore.rules`' own comment claims `role_admin` "is never written anywhere in this project," but `admin.ts`'s `verifyAdmin`/`verifyAdminV1` and the DSL's `checkIsAdminUser` both check it "by design," with an inline comment saying "Flutter admin login uses role_admin." No Cloud Function anywhere ever writes `role`/`role_admin`/`roleAdmin` (real admin accounts are provisioned out-of-band) — genuinely unverifiable without live Firestore data access this session doesn't have. If any real admin account has `role_admin` set but not `role`, every `isAdmin()`-gated DIRECT-CLIENT rule (not the Cloud-Function admin operations, which bypass rules and are unaffected) would silently deny that admin. Zero current observed impact. **Flagging for the user specifically** — this is worth a direct look at the real admin accounts' actual field values, not something this session can verify or safely resolve alone.
- **`RegistrationPopupComp` and `WorkFilterSelectComp`** (2 more built-but-unreachable components, found by the same reachability sweep that found `ReservationListPage`) have no identifiable clear trigger — unlike `ReservationListPage`, where the client's own spec unambiguously described what should exist, these two have no source-of-truth backing for WHERE/WHEN they should fire, and guessing would mean inventing product behavior. Disclosed, not wired.
- **`AdminAffiliateManagement`/`AdminReportReviewPage`/`KycReviewPage`** unreachable — but self-acknowledged in their own DSL comments as deferred to the real Phase 12 admin-panel navigation shell, not a surprise this round uncovered.
- **`banners: is_active + sort_order` index** — no query in Cloud Functions or DSL custom-action code uses it, but `banners` is public-read, so a native FlutterFlow-bound query widget (invisible to code-based grep) remains a real possibility. Not removed without higher confidence.
- **2 dead `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` fields**: `features_enabled` (zero references anywhere) and `cancel_fee_rates` (read only by `adminGetSystemConfig` for display — the REAL cancellation-fee policy is fully hardcoded in `stripe-payments.ts`'s `cancelPayment`, so changing this value via the admin panel would silently do nothing). `corsOptions` (config.ts) is also fully dead — defined, never imported anywhere. None removed — all confirmed harmless-dead rather than actively wrong, and removing a config surface the (not-yet-built) admin panel might expect is a product call, not made unilaterally.

**Verification:** all backend changes — `npm run build` clean throughout; `unblockUser`/`getBlockedUsersDetails` (from §57, redeployed this round for the photoUrl fix), `cancelPayment`, `stripeWebhook` (deployed twice this round — once for the cancellation-metadata fix, once for the fail-closed signature hardening) all confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`; all Cloud Run services healthy. `firestore.rules`/`firestore.indexes.json` deployed twice (chat_rooms index, then the extensions rule + orphaned-index cleanup together), rules compiled clean. DSL pushed 4 times this round (initial-route fix, chat-messages fix, the 4-block freeze sweep, ReservationListPage nav + BlockList UX fixes) — every push validated by `flutterflow ai run` before landing; 2 of the 4 caught real compile errors on the first attempt (`setInitialPage` not being exported; `Expanded(child: ...)` using a named param that's actually positional) before anything pushed, both fixed and re-verified. `flutterflow ai test` passes; `git status` shows exactly the expected file set.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (cancellation-metadata fix, `processAffiliateRewards` resilience), `stripe-webhooks.ts` (notification text, fail-closed signature check), `affiliate.ts` (`affiliate_min_days` falsy-zero fix), `index.ts` (gen-1 comment correction), `dsl/edit.dart` (initial-route fix, chat-messages fix, 4-block freeze, ReservationListPage nav button, BlockList UX fixes — 4 pushes), `firebase/firestore.rules` (`extensions` rule), `firebase/firestore.indexes.json` (chat_rooms index added, 5 orphaned indexes removed), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside.

### §59: Phase 11 slice 3 — legal-content viewers + inquiry form, closing the last remaining unwired drawer row (row 6: サポート・法的項目) (2026-08-12)

**Context.** Slice 1 left drawer row 6 unwired because none of its target content existed: a ToS viewer, a Privacy Policy viewer, and a general inquiry/contact-operator form (distinct from the existing user-to-user `reportUser`). Confirmed again this session that no real ToS/privacy-policy text exists anywhere in this repo — per the user's own earlier decision, built with explicit `[法務レビュー待ち]`-style placeholder content, not fabricated legal text, so swapping in the client's final copy later is a data update, not a rebuild.

Investigated and deliberately ruled out before starting: the client's illustration-button/icon-font assets (`mypage-button-svg.zip`, `flutter-icons-67b174d0.zip`) exist on this machine (outside this repo, alongside a copy the sister project also references) — but uploading custom fonts/SVGs into a live FlutterFlow project is a capability never exercised this session, and there's no live FlutterFlow Desktop pairing to verify the visual result. Given the explicit instruction this round to avoid time-costly mistakes, this slice stayed scoped to page/backend authoring — proven, repeatedly-verified competence — and did not attempt the asset upload. The 4 illustration-button placeholders on MyPage remain untouched.

**Backend:** `submitInquiry` (`auth.ts`, modeled directly on `reportUser`'s exact shape — self-scoped write, `HttpsError` validation) writes to a new `inquiries` collection (`{uid, subject, message, status: "pending", admin_note: "", created_at}`). New firestore.rule matching `reports`' exact shape (`create` limited to the authenticated submitter, `read: if isAdmin()`, no client update/delete). No composite index needed (the function only ever `.add()`s, no queries).

**DSL:** `callSubmitInquiry` (mirrors `callReportUser`); 4 new pages — `TermsOfService`, `PrivacyPolicy` (both static placeholder text), `InquiryForm` (subject + message fields, submit button), `SupportLegalHub` (the drawer row 6 destination, 3 rows linking to the above). Wired `Row_gnymcn1g` (confirmed still unwired, verified before touching it) to `SupportLegalHub`.

**One real DSL-authoring gap found and worked around**: the inquiry form's submit button needed to validate BOTH `subject` and `message` are non-empty before submitting. Initially wrote `Not(Equals(...)).and(Not(Equals(...)))` — invented a `.and()` method that doesn't exist. Confirmed by reading `references.dart` directly: this DSL has NO `And`/`Or` boolean combinator at all, only `Not`. Fixed with nested `If`s (check subject → check message → submit → check result), matching this file's own established multi-step validation pattern elsewhere. Caught before ever running `flutterflow ai test`, by checking the SDK source first rather than guessing a Flutter-shaped API would carry over.

**Applied the freeze-same-turn lesson for a 3rd time**: all 4 `ensurePage` calls (with 3 of them cross-referencing each other — `SupportLegalHub` navigates to the other 3 — plus one more cross-reference from `MyPage`'s drawer) captured as local variables for the creating push, then frozen and switched to `ff.Pages.*` in an immediate same-turn follow-up push, exactly mirroring slices 1 and 2. Both pushes succeeded on the first attempt — zero compile errors this time, unlike slices 1/2's first attempts.

**Verification:** `submitInquiry` confirmed `ACTIVE`. `flutterflow ai test` passed before each push; both `flutterflow ai run` pushes succeeded first-try. Read all 4 pages' `generated_code` directly and confirmed: `TermsOfService`/`PrivacyPolicy` render the placeholder text, `InquiryForm`'s submit button's nested-If validation compiled exactly as authored (subject-empty → message-empty → submit → success/failure, each with its own Snackbar), `SupportLegalHub` navigates to all 3 sub-pages, `MyPage`'s drawer row 6 navigates to `SupportLegalHub`. Re-verified zero regression after the freeze push.

**Phase 11 status**: with this slice, drawer rows 1-7 are ALL wired (row 5 in slice 2, row 6 here — the two rows deliberately held open since slice 1). Still open for future slices: notification settings, phone/auth self-service, card/payment-method management (scoped properly per slice 2's own investigation — needs an ephemeral-key backend piece, not just UI), the cast work-calendar (the single largest remaining item), and the 4 illustration-button placeholders (blocked on the asset-upload capability question above, not on content/backend work).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/auth.ts` (`submitInquiry`), `index.ts` (export), `firebase/firestore.rules` (`inquiries` rule), `dsl/edit.dart` (`callSubmitInquiry`, 4 new pages — frozen same-turn, drawer row 6 wiring — 2 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` updated alongside.

Explicitly flagged: `mypage-button2/` must NOT be used — the client withdrew this set (already documented in IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §12).

When that happens, the next step is wiring each confirmed `Image`/icon reference through the normal `dsl/edit.dart` → `flutterflow ai run` → `generated_code` verification loop, not a new investigation.

### §61: `style/` asset wiring completed — user's manual upload verified via screenshots, every reference wired, one SDK gap found and worked around (2026-08-13)

**Context.** Following §60's manifest, the user performed the manual Media Assets upload in the FlutterFlow builder and sent 8 full-panel screenshots plus 4 targeted content screenshots when specific filenames/mappings were ambiguous. Verified the upload thoroughly before wiring anything, rather than trusting the user's summary — per this session's established discipline.

**Deletion-safety check.** Cross-referenced the 25 asset filenames the live app was already depending on (extracted via `grep -rohE "assets/images/..." generated_code`) against the screenshots. `icoccha_logo.png` and all `image0_(8)`–`image0_(17)` files were confirmed present — nothing critical was lost in the user's "insufficient storage" cleanup. Three bare filenames (`image1.jpeg`, `image0.png`, `image0_(18).png`) were confirmed genuinely absent by the user searching for them directly; two of the three were already going to be fixed by this round's wiring (`image1.jpeg` → HomePage/MyPage photos, `image0_(18).png` → ReservationConfirmed), the third (`image0.png`, used by PaymentConfirm/ExtensionPayment) has no corresponding delivered asset and is disclosed to the user as a separate, still-open broken reference — not fixed, not silently dropped.

**Upload naming discovery.** The user's bulk upload renamed every file after its **parent folder**, not its own filename (e.g. `home_image/image1.jpeg` → `home_image1.jpeg`, `macchaChatsimage/icoccha_noimage.png` → `macchaChatsimage.png`) — confirmed directly by the user ("I updated the names... modeling them after the parent folder"). This meant zero filename collisions with the old numbered placeholders, so nothing auto-wired itself — every widget needed an explicit DSL path update, none were "free."

**Two ambiguous mappings resolved by inspecting actual file content, not filenames:**
- `kyc_image_man.png`/`kyc_image_woman.png` — opening both revealed the "man" file depicts the ID-document upload flow, the "woman" file depicts the selfie-upload flow; mapped to Kyc's two cards by content, ignoring the gendered names entirely.
- `mypage-button-svg1-4.svg` — thumbnails rendered as flat unlabeled color circles (unlike `woorkpage-button-svg1-4.svg`, whose thumbnails render their Japanese label baked into the image). Opening svg4 at full resolution revealed "お問い合わせメール" (inquiry email) text — it is NOT a second ガイドライン image as the slot count suggested. MyPage's 2nd ガイドライン placeholder (`Text_sswmld4n`) was deliberately left unwired; svg4 is held for a follow-up wiring it to the `SupportLegalHub`/`InquiryForm` entry point instead of being force-fit into a slot it doesn't match.

**WorkPage's 4 CircleImage slots needed no user input**, unlike MyPage's — the delivered SVGs self-label their recruitment category in the image itself (送迎スタッフ/イコッチャガールズ/セキュリティスタッフ/イコッチャボーイズ募集中！), so any left-to-right assignment to the 4 structurally-identical placeholder slots is correct by construction.

**New SDK finding: `EditWidgetPatch.imagePath(path, source: ImageSource.asset)` works correctly on both `Image` and `CircleImage` nodes** (confirmed via `_ensureFFImage`'s explicit switch over both types) — used for ~25 simple path swaps across 12 pages + 1 component in a single push, first attempt, zero compile errors.

**New SDK gap found: static `patch.visible(false)` silently no-ops.** WorkPage's 4 CircleImage slots each have a redundant "募集画像表示" placeholder `Text` overlaid in the same `Stack` (design intent was clearly "this text disappears once a real image is set" — no such conditional existed). Tried `patch.visible(false)` (the typed `EditWidgetPatch` path) — compiled and pushed with zero error, but `generated_code` showed the Text still rendered completely unconditionally, and `flutterflow ai inspect --selector-key --dsl-json` showed no `visibility` field on the node at all afterward. Tried the raw `page.mutateNode` escape hatch instead, writing `node.props.ensureVisibility().visibleValue = FFBooleanValue(inputValue: false)` directly — the exact same proto write `widget_helpers.dart`'s own `setVisibility` performs internally. Same result: compiled, pushed, zero error, zero effect. **Confirmed via two independent mechanisms, not one attempt** — this is a genuine SDK/codegen gap with static (non-dynamic) visibility on a `Text` nested inside a `Stack`, not a scripting mistake. Worked around by blanking the text content instead (`patch.text('')`) — `.text()` is proven reliable elsewhere in this file and achieves the identical end-user-visible result (no leftover label) without depending on the broken field. Documented as a new drift finding in `project_rules.md` rather than re-discovering this on the next visibility need.

**MyPage's 3 `ensureReplaced` Text→Image swaps (利用規約/ガイドライン/Q&A・ヘルプ) frozen same-turn**, immediately after confirming via `generated_code` they landed — new keys `Image_a2t7auha`/`Image_g4f2674l`/`Image_4pso7u43` recorded in the freeze comment for any future edit to target instead of the stale `Text_*` keys.

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` before every push; 3 pushes total (main wiring sweep — first-attempt success; visibility-patch attempt — pushed clean but confirmed ineffective via `generated_code` + `inspect --dsl-json`; text-blank fix + MyPage freeze — confirmed effective). Final sweep of every `assets/images/*` reference across `generated_code` confirms all ~35 new filenames present, zero stale `image0_(N)`-style references remain except the one disclosed pre-existing gap (`image0.png`), and MyPage's untouched 2nd ガイドライン placeholder is still intact exactly as intended.

**Still open, explicitly deferred, not silently dropped:** `mypage-button-svg4.svg` (お問い合わせメール icon) → needs wiring to `SupportLegalHub`/`InquiryForm`, a structural insert not attempted this round; `PaymentConfirm`/`ExtensionPayment`'s broken `image0.png` reference, no replacement asset delivered; the 8 "needs new UI work first" items from §60 (CocomisePage ad carousel — now moot, wired directly since the widgets turned out to already be genuine `Image` nodes, not `Image.network`; MacchaPage empty state; the 5 remaining `icoccha_noimage`-class fallback locations beyond ReservationDetail's 2; HomePage promo banner) — MacchaPage/fallback-rollout/HomePage-banner remain unbuilt, CocomisePage's carousel is now done; the 4 unclassified `その他_image/` files, still unassigned.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (~25 `imagePath` patches across 12 pages + 1 component, 3 `ensureReplaced` swaps frozen, 4 `text('')` blanks — 3 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside with the static-visibility SDK gap.

### §62: New UI built for 3 of the 4 remaining style-folder slots — SupportLegalHub restyle, HomePage banner, MacchaPage empty state; one more SDK docstring/behavior mismatch found and fixed (2026-08-13)

**Context.** User asked to build the UI for §61's disclosed "needs new UI work" leftovers, styled consistently with the app's existing design (theme tokens confirmed via `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/flutter_flow_theme.dart` — `Colors.primary`/`secondaryBackground`/`primaryText`/`secondaryText` etc., used exactly as elsewhere in this project).

**Built, all confirmed live via `generated_code`:**
- **SupportLegalHub**: its 3 rows (built plain in Phase 11 slice 3) restyled from bare `Button`s to `Card` rows (icon + label + trailing chevron), matching this app's established card-list visual language. The 3rd row (お問い合わせ) uses the real `mypage-button-svg4.svg` illustration instead of a generic icon, since that asset was delivered specifically for this slot (§61). Each row's `onTap` reproduces the exact `Navigate` target the replaced `Button` already had — confirmed via `generated_code` before touching anything, so no regression to the working navigation.
- **HomePage promo banner**: a new `Container`(rounded, shadowed) + `Image` inserted as the first item in the body's scroll column, above `DiscoveryCastGrid`, using `home_image2.png` (the version with the "乾杯！icoccha OPENING EVENT" copy baked in — `home_image1.png`, the unlabeled variant, stays unused pending client direction on a possible 2nd banner slot).
- **MacchaPage empty state**: a new `hasMatches` bool state field, set once at `ON_INIT_STATE` from a new tiny custom function `stringListIsNotEmpty` (no `Length`/`isEmpty` expression exists anywhere in this DSL — confirmed by grepping `references.dart`), driving `app.ensureEmptyState(...)` to swap between the real tabs+list content and a new empty-state block using `macchapage_conditionalBuilder_else_image.png`.

**Deliberately scoped to avoid a real risk, not an oversight**: MacchaPage's 5 filter tabs (すべて/新しい/未交流/交流済み/断られた) each recompute `visibleMatchaList` via a `matchaFilterTab` local-helper pattern that was authored directly in the FlutterFlow builder — confirmed by finding its call sites frozen in `dsl/edit.dart` with an explicit prior note that the helper's own implementation was deleted as orphaned dead code once its only call sites landed. Its exact filtering expression (which field of the `|||`-delimited match record it reads, in what order) can't be reconstructed from this file. Rather than guess and risk breaking working per-tab filtering, the empty-state condition was scoped to `myChatRoomsList` — the ONE list this DSL script itself owns end-to-end (set once, at `ON_INIT_STATE`, by an action chain this file already fully controls) — which correctly covers the real target scenario (a brand-new user with zero matches at all). An empty *filtered* sub-view (e.g. zero "断られた" items while other matches exist) is a different, lower-stakes case this pass does not cover.

**New SDK finding: `app.ensureEmptyState(...)`'s `visibleWhen` behaves opposite to its own docstring.** The docstring reads: "The `content` target remains visible when `visibleWhen` is true; the new `emptyState` is shown for the inverse condition." The actual implementation (`edit.dart`) does `bindVisible(emptyState, visibleWhen)` then `bindVisible(content, Not(visibleWhen))` — i.e. **emptyState gets the direct condition, content gets the negation** — backwards from the docstring. Caught by verifying `generated_code` after the first push (per this session's own standing discipline — always verify a claim against generated code, not just "it compiled and pushed"): with `visibleWhen: State('hasMatches')`, the empty-state graphic rendered for users WHO HAD matches, and the real list only rendered for users with none. Fixed by passing `Not(State('hasMatches'))` instead, re-verified correct on the second push. Documented in `project_rules.md` so the next `ensureEmptyState` use trusts the verified behavior, not the docstring.

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` before every push; 2 pushes (main build — first-attempt success on SupportLegalHub/HomePage/MacchaPage structure; `ensureEmptyState` inversion fix). Read `generated_code` for all 3 pages directly after each push — confirmed the 3 SupportLegalHub `context.pushNamed(...)` calls target the correct pages, the HomePage banner Image renders `home_image2.png` at the correct position, and MacchaPage's conditional (`if (!hasMatches!) → emptyState`, `if (hasMatches) → content`) is correct after the fix.

**Still open, deliberately not attempted:** the shared `macchaChatsimage.png` no-image fallback for the 5 locations beyond ReservationDetail (MacchaChats, Home recommended-cast, MacchaPage match-list avatar, ProfileEdit, KycReviewPage) — each needs a genuine per-item conditional (real photo OR fallback) inside a dynamically-bound `CircleImage`/`Image`, which in turn needs knowing the exact expression each page uses to extract a photo URL from its list item; at least one of these (MacchaPage's `MatchaItemCard` avatar) sits behind the same kind of removed-local-helper pattern that blocked the filter-tab logic above. Not attempted this round rather than guessed. `PaymentConfirm`/`ExtensionPayment`'s broken `image0.png` reference and the 4 unclassified `その他_image/` files remain open from §61, unchanged.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (SupportLegalHub restyle, HomePage banner insert, MacchaPage `hasMatches` field + custom function + `ensureEmptyState` — 2 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside with the `ensureEmptyState` inversion finding.

### §63: No-photo fallback rolled out to all 5 remaining locations; `ConditionalBuilder` found broken, worked around with `Stack` (2026-08-13)

**Context.** User asked to finish the last deferred item from §62 — the shared `macchaChatsimage.png` fallback for MacchaChats, HomePage's discovery-cast grid, MacchaPage's match-list avatar, ProfileEdit, and KycReviewPage — all 5 confirmed via `grep -rln "CachedNetworkImage" generated_code` (exactly these 5 files, matching the disclosed list precisely) and all missing an `errorWidget`/`placeholder`.

**Investigation before writing anything** (per the user's explicit "establish a precise direction before executing" instruction): confirmed no `errorWidget`/`placeholder`-equivalent field exists anywhere on this DSL's `Image`/`Avatar`/`FFImage` proto surface that's actually wired by codegen — the one candidate field, `showErrorImage`, has zero references anywhere in this SDK's compiler/codegen, so it wasn't gambled on. Split the 5 into 2 classes: **ProfileEdit and MacchaChats** bind a single page-level State field (`editProfileImageUrl`, `counterpartPhoto`) — a simple `ensureReplaced` on one node. **HomePage, MacchaPage, KycReviewPage** bind a PER-ITEM photo URL inside a ListView/GridView `itemBuilder` — confirmed via `GridView.buildItem() => itemBuilder(const ItemRef())` in the SDK source that `item` is a marker object created fresh only when `itemBuilder` executes, so it cannot be referenced from outside that closure. This meant reconstructing the ENTIRE itemBuilder (not just the inner Image) for all 3 — done by locating and copying each one's exact original structure from this file's own already-landed frozen `ensureReplaced` blocks (HomePage's `DiscoveryCastGrid`, MacchaPage's `MatchaItemsListView`, KycReviewPage's original `ensurePage` body — the last one explicitly flagged in an earlier round's own comment as "real added risk" to reconstruct, so extra care was taken to reproduce it exactly: both buttons' `callAdminApproveKyc` calls, both `If`/`SetState`/`Snackbar` branches, verbatim).

**New SDK finding: `ConditionalBuilder` is broken — fails FlutterFlow's own server-side validation regardless of how it's used.** First attempt used `ConditionalBuilder(children: [Image(..., visible: cond), Image(..., visible: Not(cond))])` — the natural reading of its DSL signature and the obvious tool for "show A or show B." `flutterflow ai run` correctly caught this at the validation stage (no push happened): `Every condition in the conditional builder must have a child` — on all 6 uses. `FFConditionalBuilder`'s own proto message carries zero fields of its own (confirmed by reading the generated proto class directly), and no working example of this widget exists anywhere in this project's history, the reference set, or `flutterflow ai docs`. Rather than iterate blindly against an undocumented, unproven widget, replaced every use with a plain `Stack` holding the same two children with the same opposite `visible:` conditions — the exact same underlying conditional-visibility mechanism already proven working elsewhere in this file (the frozen `Divider(visible: Not(Equals(...)))` pattern, and `ensureEmptyState`'s own `bindVisible` from §62). Second push succeeded on the first attempt with this fix; `generated_code` confirms correct `if (cond) ... if (!cond) ...` pairs at all 5 locations, with zero regression to any surrounding onTap/button/text logic in the 3 reconstructed itemBuilders.

**Also found, not yet exercised**: `CustomFunctionHandle(name:, args:, returnType:)` can be constructed directly (its `==`/`hashCode` are name-only) to reference an ALREADY-declared custom function whose original `app.customFunction(...)` call didn't capture a handle into a local variable — avoids the `CustomCodeDuplicateError` that re-calling `app.customFunction` with the same name would throw. Used for `discoveryCastId`/`Nickname`/`PhotoUrl`/`IsOnline` and all 5 `kycReviewItem*` functions, none of which had been captured when first declared.

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` before both pushes; first push correctly failed at `flutterflow ai run`'s validation stage (no partial push, per this SDK's own validate-then-push design) rather than silently landing something broken; second push succeeded. Read `generated_code` for all 5 files directly — confirmed the fallback path renders under the correct negated condition in each, and for the 3 reconstructed itemBuilders specifically confirmed the surrounding card content (Navigate targets, button `CallCustomAction` args, text bindings) is byte-for-byte equivalent to before, not just visually similar.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (5 no-photo-fallback locations — 2 pushes, `ConditionalBuilder`→`Stack` fix on the 2nd), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside with the `ConditionalBuilder` finding.

**Remaining open items, unchanged from §61/§62**: `PaymentConfirm`/`ExtensionPayment`'s broken `image0.png` reference (no replacement asset delivered) and the 4 unclassified `その他_image/` files pending client direction. With this round, every other style-folder asset-application item is now complete.

### §64: Comprehensive review round 4 — 4 parallel fresh-eyes audits, 12 findings, all fixed or deliberately disclosed (2026-08-13)

**Context.** User requested another full-project review, the same "pause, inspect everything thoroughly, one by one" instruction that has triggered prior deep review rounds (§54-58) each of which found real bugs. Dispatched 4 parallel background agents, each scoped to a different slice: (1) fresh-eyes re-verification of §60-63's asset-wiring/UI work specifically, since that's the most recent and most structurally complex work; (2) a reachability re-sweep of everything touched since §58's original sweep; (3) a fresh backend/Firestore-rules/indexes audit; (4) a documentation-accuracy cross-check of all three knowledge files against actual code state. 12 genuine findings surfaced across all 4 — all fixed except one deliberately deferred (see below).

#### Finding 1 (asset-wiring audit) — a previously-fixed visual bug was silently reintroduced

HomePage's `DiscoveryCastGrid` had its `childAspectRatio` fixed from a buggy `0.75` (visible ~65px blank gap under every card) to `1.05` earlier this session (a raw `mutateNode` patch). §63's no-photo-fallback rollout later reconstructed the SAME node from scratch via `ensureReplaced` (to add the fallback image) with a fresh literal that still hardcoded `0.75` — silently overwriting the earlier fix, since the later reconstruction runs after the mutateNode in file order and replaces the whole node. Confirmed live via `generated_code`. Fixed by correcting the literal to `1.05` directly in the reconstruction and freezing/superseding the now-redundant `mutateNode`.

**A second, harder-to-diagnose layer surfaced while fixing this**: the FIRST attempt at this fix (same 1.05 value, but targeting the GridView's key from BEFORE this review round started) compiled and pushed with ZERO error, yet did NOT apply — `generated_code` still showed `0.75` afterward. That key turned out to already be stale (superseded by an earlier push in this same review round, itself part of freezing 11 other landed one-shot ops — see Finding 2). Unlike `KycReviewPage`'s `ListView`→`ListView` stale-key case in the same push (which DID hard-fail with `findByKey(...) found no matches`), this `GridView`→`GridView` stale-key case silently succeeded WITHOUT applying the new property value. Re-running with the corrected current key fixed it — new finding for `project_rules.md`: a same-type `ensureReplaced` on a stale key can succeed silently without applying the new literal, so "it compiled and pushed" is not proof a property change actually landed — always verify the specific value in `generated_code`.

#### Finding 2 (asset-wiring audit) — 11 landed one-shot structural ops were never frozen, and this caused a real, reproduced hard failure

None of §61-63's new `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` calls (9 of them: SupportLegalHub ×3, HomePage banner, HomePage DiscoveryCastGrid, ProfileEdit avatar, MacchaChats avatar, MacchaPage MatchaItemsListView, KycReviewPage ListView) had been frozen after landing, despite this project's own extensively-proven freeze discipline. Two more PRE-EXISTING instances of the same lapse were found too (`ReservationListNavButton`, `BlockedUsersListView`) — not part of this session's own recent work, but caught by the same sweep. **Attempting to push this review round's own fixes immediately reproduced the exact predicted failure**: `flutterflow ai run` hard-failed with `Bad state: page KycReviewPage findByKey("ListView_ohcx81fg") found no matches` — direct, first-hand confirmation (not just theoretical risk) that leaving one of these live blocks ANY future unrelated push, not just a silent no-op. All 11 blocks (plus a 12th found mid-fix — a second `mutateNode` on KycReviewPage's same stale `ListView_ohcx81fg` key, superseded the same way as Finding 1) were frozen with full documentation of their landed replacement keys/names.

#### Finding 3 (reachability audit) — `TutorialPage` unreachable, pre-existing but undisclosed

`TutorialPage` (5-slide onboarding carousel) has had zero incoming navigation since before this session started — `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s own original as-is audit already documented this ("Orphaned: no other page links to TutorialPage; only reachable via direct route"). §61 wired real client assets into its 5 slides without anyone noticing or disclosing that the reachability gap predates that work — the same "built/updated but nobody can get there" bug class as `ReservationListPage` (§58), just missed this time. Investigated the deeper question (when should a tutorial auto-trigger — first launch only? every launch?) and found no persisted "hasSeenTutorial"-style flag anywhere in the schema — inventing one would be a product decision, not a bug fix, the same caution already applied to `RegistrationPopupComp`/`WorkFilterSelectComp`. Fixed with a minimal, low-risk addition instead: a "使い方を見る" link on `LoginPage`, structurally matching its 2 existing sibling Text-links (forgot password, new registration) exactly — makes the page discoverable without touching the app's initial-route logic at all.

#### Finding 4 (documentation audit) — IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md never got correction notes for 2 of §58's 3 most severe findings

§58's reachability sweep produced 3 major findings against phases already marked `[DONE]`: the `ReservationListPage` reachability gap (HIGH), the chat-messages-unreadable bug (CRITICAL — chat was completely non-functional for roughly a day after "shipping"), and the cancellation-capture metadata bug (MONEY-CRITICAL — double-paid casts, un-cancelled reservations). Only the least severe of the three (`ReservationListPage`) received a `**Correction, 2026-08-12**` annotation in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; the two more severe ones were fixed in code (confirmed still correct) but never propagated into the phase-tracking document, so a reader trusting the `[DONE]` tags alone would never learn chat was broken or that a real money-duplication bug briefly existed. Fixed by adding matching correction notes to Phase 6 and the Phase 5 cancellation-fee-matrix bullet.

#### Findings 5-10 (backend audit) — all fixed, deployed, confirmed ACTIVE

1. **HIGH — `cancelPayment` had no guard for an empty `payment_intent_id`.** Unlike its sibling `capturePayment` (which guards this exact case), nothing checked whether a PaymentIntent existed before calling Stripe. `reservations.ts` creates every reservation with `payment_intent_id: ""` up front — a guest cancelling before ever reaching `payment_confirm.dart` always has `chargedAmount > 0` (guest cancellation charges 50-100%), so this unconditionally called `stripe.paymentIntents.capture("", ...)`, which Stripe rejects, leaving the guest stuck unable to cancel a reservation they never paid for. Fixed with a `hasPaymentIntent` guard that skips Stripe entirely (pure local cancellation) when nothing was ever authorized — also guards the downstream cast-debt Stripe-fee charge, which would otherwise have charged a cast a "processing fee" for a transaction that never happened.
2. **MEDIUM-HIGH — `applyToWorkPost` had a non-atomic read-modify-write on `applicants`** (`applicants: [...applicants, uid]`). Two casts applying to the same post within the same read window could race, one silently losing its application despite getting `{success:true}`. Fixed with `FieldValue.arrayUnion(uid)`, matching the pattern this codebase already uses correctly elsewhere (`blockUser`/`unblockUser`).
3. **MEDIUM — `respondToReservation`'s accept/decline path was a read-then-write with no transaction.** Two near-simultaneous calls (double-tap, client retry) could both pass the status guard before either write landed, both creating a `chat_rooms` doc and duplicate `work_posts` entries. Fixed by wrapping the read-check-write in `db.runTransaction`, so only one caller can ever win the status transition.
4. **LOW-MEDIUM — `submitReview` had the same read(query)-then-write(add) race** on its duplicate-review check. Fixed by switching from an auto-ID `.add()` to a deterministic doc ID (`${res_id}_${uid}_${cast_id}`) + `.create()`, which Firestore rejects atomically (ALREADY_EXISTS) if the doc already exists — a single-document atomic operation, simpler and more idiomatic than wrapping the whole function in a transaction.
5. **LOW-MEDIUM — `adminToggleFreeze` wrote caller-supplied `freeze` to `is_frozen` with no type check.** Fixed with a `typeof freeze !== "boolean"` guard, matching this session's own established discipline for boolean-typed inputs (`respondToReservation`'s `accept`, `submitReview`'s `rating`).
6. **LOW — `adminUpdateAffiliateRate` validated `new_rate`'s range/step but never checked `typeof === "number"`**, so a numeric string would pass every `<`/`>`/`%` comparison (JS coercion) and get written to Firestore as a string. Fixed with an explicit `typeof new_rate !== "number"` guard.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean; all 6 functions (`cancelPayment`, `applyToWorkPost`, `respondToReservation`, `submitReview`, `adminToggleFreeze`, `adminUpdateAffiliateRate`) deployed and confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list --json`. DSL: `flutterflow ai test` before every push; 4 pushes total for the DSL side (childAspectRatio fix attempt #1 — silently failed to apply, diagnosed; freeze pass + TutorialPage link — succeeded after also freezing a 12th previously-missed stale mutateNode; childAspectRatio fix attempt #2 with the corrected key — confirmed applied via `generated_code`; final freeze of the now-confirmed-correct block). Every fix re-verified against `generated_code` directly, not just "pushed without error."

**Deliberately not re-litigated**: this round's agents were explicitly told not to re-flag anything already disclosed in PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md's own "investigated and NOT fixed" sections (§35/§37/§39/§49/§58's own disclosed-not-fixed lists) — none of those were re-reported, confirming the existing disclosure list is still accurate and complete.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (childAspectRatio fix, TutorialPage link, freeze of 12 landed one-shot ops — 4 pushes), `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (`cancelPayment` guard), `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (`applyToWorkPost` arrayUnion), `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (`respondToReservation` transaction, `submitReview` deterministic-ID create), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (`adminToggleFreeze`/`adminUpdateAffiliateRate` type guards), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 6 + cancellation-matrix correction notes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated alongside with the stale-key-silent-success finding.

### §65: Cast work-calendar built (§3.7.2) — the single largest remaining Phase 11 item, now done

**Context.** MyPage's and CastProfile's 3rd tabs had been a decorative, unbound month-view `Calendar` widget since before this session even started — flagged repeatedly (§56-§59) as "the single largest remaining Phase 11 item: no data model, no backend, no UI." Built via `EnterPlanMode` (a fresh-eyes Explore agent researched the exact spec/schema/current-state facts, a Plan agent turned that into a concrete design, both reviewed before execution) given the size and number of real design decisions involved.

**Spec** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:148`, §3.7.2, the one authoritative sentence): weeks 1-4 selectable (one week shown at a time), tapping a date reveals a 24-hour/30-minute grid (48 cells), tap toggles ○ (available) / × (unavailable) — "subtractive": default state is available, a slot only gets a real Firestore document once it's non-default (cast-blocked or booked). From the guest side, tapping a ○ deep-links into the reservation form.

**Backend** — new `firebase/functions/src/schedule.ts`, 3 Cloud Functions: `getMySchedule({date})` (cast's own 48-slot day, gaps filled `"available"`), `toggleScheduleSlot({date, slot_index})` (deterministic doc ID `${uid}_${startAtMillis}`, wrapped in a transaction, refuses to touch a `"reserved"` slot), `getCastScheduleForGuest({cast_id, date})` (guest-facing read — required because the pre-existing, already-correct `schedule_slots` firestore rule restricts direct client reads to the owning cast, so a guest genuinely cannot query another cast's slots without going through a callable). New composite index `schedule_slots: cast_id ASC, date ASC`. Deployed, all 3 confirmed `ACTIVE`.

**DSL — Push 1 (MyPage, cast side).** 4 shared customFunctions (`weekDayDate`/`weekDayLabel`/`slotTimeLabel`/`slotGlyph` — reused verbatim by Push 2), 2 custom actions (`callGetMySchedule`/`callToggleScheduleSlot`, same `FirebaseFunctions.instanceFor(region: 'asia-northeast1')` shape as every other `call*` action in this file), 4 new page-state fields, extended the existing `ON_INIT_STATE` chain (not a second call on the same trigger — this project's own confirmed-breakage pattern). Replaced `Calendar_5lwqxdoh` with a week-selector row, day-of-week row, and a 48-cell `GridView` — tap toggles via **act-then-refetch** (call `toggleScheduleSlot`, then immediately re-fetch via `getMySchedule` and re-`SetState` the list), matching this project's own proven `BlockList` unblock-then-refetch convention rather than inventing an optimistic-local-mutation pattern with no revert-on-failure precedent here.

**DSL — Push 2 (CastProfile guest view + ReservationForm deep-link).** `callGetCastSchedule` (reuses Push 1's shared functions), same 4 state fields on CastProfile, extended its existing `ON_INIT_STATE`. Replaced `Calendar_pgw6bzqc` with the read-only mirror of Push 1's grid — tapping a ○ slot `Navigate`s into `ReservationForm` with `castId`/`prefillDate`/`prefillStartTime`. `ReservationForm`'s start-time field (`DropDown_ggrglg4w`) was hardcoded to 5 hourly options (18:00-22:00) — widened to all 48 half-hour values (purely additive) since that's the only way a tapped slot's exact time survives into a valid pre-selected value; added `prefillDate`/`prefillStartTime` params + a new `ON_INIT_STATE` (none existed on this page before) seeding `resDate`/`resStartTime` from them.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during the build, both via the established "verify against generated_code, don't trust a clean push" discipline:**
1. **`outputAs` name collision across widgets.** The first Push 1 attempt reused the same `outputAs` string (`'scheduleFetchResult'`) across all 11 week/day buttons — `flutterflow ai run` correctly rejected this: `Action in Button has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget`. Fixed by parameterizing a unique tag per button (`scheduleFetchResult_week0`, `_day3`, etc.) — a new, previously-undocumented validation rule for this project (each widget's action-chain output names must be unique across the whole page, not just within one action chain).
2. **`patch.dropdownOptions([...])` silently renders every option as an empty string.** Widening `ResStartTimeDropdown` via the typed `EditWidgetPatch.dropdownOptions(...)` patch compiled and pushed with zero error, but `generated_code` showed `options: ['', '', ...]` — all 48 entries empty. Root-caused by reading `_applyDropdownOptionsPatch`'s own source: it only ever sets `FFParameterValue.serializedValue`, never `.translatableText` — while the DropDown's own sibling patch (`_applyDropdownInitialOptionPatch`, for the single default-selected value) uses `FFText(textValue: ...)`, strongly suggesting codegen actually reads `translatableText` for option labels, not `serializedValue`. Same class of gap as `ConditionalBuilder`/`ensureEmptyState` found earlier this session — a typed patch method whose write doesn't match what codegen reads. Fixed via the raw `mutateNode` escape hatch, setting BOTH `serializedValue` and `translatableText: FFText(textValue: FFStringValue(inputValue: label))` per option — confirmed all 48 values (`00:00`...`23:30`) render correctly afterward.

**Also reconfirmed** (third occurrence this session, same as HomePage's `childAspectRatio` in §64): re-running an already-landed SAME-TYPE `ensureReplaced` (Column→Column here, CastProfile's calendar section) on a now-stale key does not throw and does not destroy the existing correct content — it silently no-ops, leaving whatever the first successful run produced intact. Confirmed by deliberately checking `generated_code` after a later, unrelated push (the dropdown fix) that re-executed this still-unfrozen block.

**Deliberately out of scope, disclosed not silently dropped:**
- **Authorize-time slot locking** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:225`, §3.9 item 9) — flipping a slot to `"reserved"` the instant a booking is authorized. Not built. `toggleScheduleSlot` refuses to touch a `"reserved"` slot, but nothing in this build ever creates one.
- **Guest-side booking validation against availability** — a guest can still submit a reservation request for a time the cast marked ×; nothing server-side cross-checks `schedule_slots` at booking time. A known, accepted gap for a future pass — both of the above require touching the live Stripe Authorize/booking flow, a materially larger and riskier task than this one.
- Any further "automation" on top of the toggle mechanic (recurring templates, bulk-set) — explicitly phase-2 per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:474`, undefined beyond a name.
- The pre-existing `cancelPayment` slot-revert query's own lack of date/time scoping (`stripe-payments.ts:1087-1096` — reverts EVERY `"reserved"` slot for a cast on any cancellation, not just the ones tied to that specific reservation) — real, but belongs to the same separately-tracked Authorize-locking gap, not this feature; not touched.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean; all 3 backend functions confirmed `ACTIVE`. `flutterflow ai test` before every push; 5 DSL pushes total (Push 1 build, Push 1 outputAs fix, Push 1 freeze, Push 2 build + param-ordering fix, Push 2 dropdown fix + freeze). Every push verified against the actual `generated_code` output, not just push success — this is what caught both bugs above. Not live-device-tested (standing session-wide limitation, no FlutterFlow Desktop pairing) — structural/backend correctness verified, interactive runtime behavior (actually tapping through the grid on a device) was not.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/schedule.ts` (new), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts`, `firebase/firestore.indexes.json`, `dsl/edit.dart` (5 pushes), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry); `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` updated with the `outputAs`-collision and `dropdownOptions` findings; `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s Phase 11 line updated to reflect this as the last major item closed.

### §66: Skeptical re-review of the cast work-calendar (§65) — read-only audit, no code changes

**Ask:** "leave nothing out" re-audit of `firebase/functions/src/schedule.ts`, cross-checked against `work-posts.ts`/`reservations.ts`/`auth.ts`/`firestore.rules`, explicitly excluding anything already disclosed in §65 as an intentional scope decision (Authorize-time locking, guest-side booking validation, `cancelPayment`'s unscoped revert query). Read-only — nothing in `schedule.ts` was changed.

**New findings (not previously disclosed):**

1. **Medium — day-of-month overflow silently rolls to a different real calendar day, no error.** `parseDayStart`'s only validation is `isNaN(new Date(dateStr).getTime())`. Verified live (node): `parseDayStart("2026-02-30")` returns `2026-03-02` with no throw (V8 normalizes out-of-range *days* via calendar arithmetic, while it correctly rejects out-of-range *months*, e.g. `"2026-13-01"` does throw). Concrete effect: any caller — malicious API probe or a latent client-side date-formatting bug — that sends `"2026-02-30"` gets silently redirected to March 2nd's slots on read (`getMySchedule`/`getCastScheduleForGuest`, 200 success) or write (`toggleScheduleSlot` flips a slot on the wrong day), with nothing signaling the mismatch. Note: `reservations.ts`'s `createReservation` has the exact same validation shape (`isNaN(new Date(date).getTime())`, no round-trip check) — this is a pre-existing codebase pattern, not something `schedule.ts` uniquely introduced, but it hadn't been called out anywhere before. Not reachable through normal UI flow (Dart `DateTime` arithmetic can't itself produce an invalid calendar date); relevant only as an API-abuse/hardening gap.
2. **Low-Medium — `toggleScheduleSlot`/`getMySchedule` don't check `account_type === "cast"`.** Any authenticated user (including a guest) can create `schedule_slots` docs under their own uid. Real inconsistency against this codebase's own strong, repeated convention — `work-posts.ts`'s `applyToWorkPost` gates on `account_type === "cast" && approval_status === "approved"`; `reservations.ts`'s `createReservation` even documents, in its own comments, a **confirmed live bug fix** for this exact class of omission in a sibling function (a guest could previously be named as a reservation's "cast"). However: (a) `firestore.rules`' own `schedule_slots` rule (line 381-388) is **equally permissive** — `create`/`update` only check `cast_id == request.auth.uid`, no `account_type` check — so the Cloud Function isn't introducing exposure beyond what a direct client SDK write already could; (b) grepped the whole backend — the only other reader of `schedule_slots` is `cancelPayment` (`stripe-payments.ts:1087`), which filters via a reservation's own `cast_ids` (already `account_type`-gated at `createReservation` time), and `admin.ts` has zero references to the collection. No concrete exploit chain found today; flagged as a real gap worth closing for consistency/defense-in-depth, not as an active vulnerability. Fixing the Cloud Function alone wouldn't fully close it — the rule itself would need the same gate, since a client can bypass the callable entirely via a direct write that satisfies the current rule.
3. **Low — `getCastScheduleForGuest` doesn't check the target `cast_id`'s `account_type`.** Matches the framing already anticipated in the audit ask: worst case a non-cast target just returns 48 "available" (or self-toggled) slots. Real but minor: functions as a weak existence-oracle (not-found vs success) for arbitrary uids to any signed-in caller, and if finding #2 above ever gets exploited, low-sensitivity availability-toggle data about an unrelated account becomes readable. No PII exposure.
4. **Low — `toggleScheduleSlot` never deletes the doc when a slot is toggled back to "available."** It always `tx.set(..., {merge:true})`s, even on the restore-to-default transition, contradicting this file's own and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s stated "subtractive" model ("a slot only gets a real Firestore document once it's non-default"). Harmless for reads (`readDaySlots` treats an existing `status:"available"` doc identically to a missing one) and doesn't break the ≤48-doc-per-query bound, but `schedule_slots` accumulates a permanent, never-cleaned-up doc for every slot any cast has ever toggled — storage/hygiene gap, not a correctness or security issue.
5. **Informational, verified neutralized in production — local-vs-UTC parsing branch for non-canonical date strings.** Confirmed via node testing that any date string deviating from strict zero-padded `"YYYY-MM-DD"` (e.g. `"2026-8-9"`, `"2026/08/09"`) takes JS's "local time" parsing branch rather than the ISO "UTC midnight" branch, which in principle could shift the bucketed day by the runtime's UTC offset. Confirmed the actual deployed runtime is UTC (`index.ts`'s `setGlobalOptions({region: "asia-northeast1", ...})` doesn't set process TZ; `affiliate.ts`'s own comment states explicitly "Cloud Functions run in UTC" as an already-learned fact in this codebase), so local time == UTC there and this is currently a no-op as long as callers keep sending the established zero-padded convention. Zero-margin footgun, not an active bug — no action taken.

**Reconfirmed safe (no new finding):**
- **Transaction correctness (`toggleScheduleSlot`):** handles the very first toggle of a slot correctly (no doc → `current="available"` → `next="unavailable"`). The read-check-write happens inside a single Firestore transaction, which is safe by construction against a future Authorize-time-locking system: Firestore's optimistic-concurrency control retries the whole transaction if the document was modified by *any* concurrent write (transactional or not) between this transaction's read and commit, and on retry the code correctly re-observes a newly-set `"reserved"` status and throws. No rework needed here — **provided** the future locking system writes to the same `slotRef` document (any write model that writes elsewhere would sidestep this protection).
- **Query scale (`readDaySlots`):** bounded to ≤48 docs per call regardless of which (accepted) date value is used — doc IDs are deterministically 1:1 per (cast, slot start time) and the query is an exact-equality match on `cast_id` + `date`, so no malformed-but-accepted date can produce a large or incorrect result set.
- **Rule/function consistency:** `cast_id` is always written as the caller's own `request.auth.uid`, matching `firestore.rules`' `create`/`update` conditions exactly (`firestore.rules:381-388`) — no divergence found.
- **Build/deploy health:** `npm run build` clean (no `tsc` errors); `firebase functions:list --json` confirms `getMySchedule`, `toggleScheduleSlot`, `getCastScheduleForGuest` all `state: "ACTIVE"` in `asia-northeast1`.

**Disposition:** read-only audit per explicit request — no code, rules, or docs (other than this log entry) changed. Findings #1-#4 above are candidates for a future hardening pass; none block the feature as currently scoped/disclosed in §65.

### §67: Cast work-calendar, round 3 — §66's findings fixed (not just disclosed) + a real guest-facing display bug found and fixed in ReservationForm

**Ask:** "analyze it thoroughly once more... identify and perfectly correct any oversights... leaving nothing out." Unlike §66 (explicitly read-only), this round both re-audits and fixes. Ran 3 parallel fresh-eyes agents (DSL re-verification, backend security/correctness, client-intent alignment) rather than one pass, matching this session's established pattern for large review tasks.

**Backend — §66 findings #1, #2, #3, #4 all fixed and deployed, confirmed `ACTIVE`.**
1. **Date round-trip validation.** `parseDayStart` now rejects any string that isn't strict `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` AND round-trips identically after being parsed and re-formatted from UTC components — `"2026-02-30"` now throws `invalid-argument` instead of silently operating on 2026-03-02. Verified with a standalone node script across 7 cases (valid dates, Feb 30, month 13, non-padded, real leap day 2028-02-29 accepted, fake leap day 2026-02-29 rejected) before deploying.
2. **`requireCastAccount(uid)` helper**, checking `account_type === "cast"` only (deliberately NOT `approval_status === "approved"` — unlike `applyToWorkPost`'s active-marketplace-action gate, setting up one's own calendar is closer to profile setup; requiring full approval would block a legitimate not-yet-approved cast from preparing their calendar before review completes, trading one gap for a worse one). Wired into both `getMySchedule` and `toggleScheduleSlot`.
3. **`getCastScheduleForGuest`** now checks the target `cast_id`'s `account_type === "cast"` too, folded into the SAME `not-found` response as a genuinely-missing doc (not a distinct error) so it doesn't leak account-type information to a prober.
4. **`toggleScheduleSlot`** now `tx.delete(slotRef)` when a slot toggles back to `"available"` (previously always `.set(..., {merge:true})`, contradicting the file's own stated subtractive model) — restores the true "no document == available" baseline instead of leaving a permanent doc behind for every slot any cast has ever touched.

Deployed via `firebase deploy --only functions:getMySchedule,functions:toggleScheduleSlot,functions:getCastScheduleForGuest`; `firebase functions:list --json` confirmed all 3 `ACTIVE` in `asia-northeast1` post-deploy. §66's finding #5 (local-vs-UTC parsing) was already confirmed neutralized in production, no action needed.

**DSL — a real, previously-undetected guest-facing display bug in `ReservationForm`, found and fixed.**

CastProfile's guest-facing calendar (§65 Push 2) deep-links a tapped ○ slot into `ReservationForm` with `prefillDate`/`prefillStartTime` params, and an `ON_INIT_STATE` action correctly sets `_model.resDate`/`_model.resStartTime` from them — confirmed this part was ALREADY correct; a submitted reservation would carry the right values even completely untouched. What was broken: the guest never SAW that. The date `TextField` always rendered blank and the start-time `Dropdown` always showed "19:00", regardless of the deep link — because both widgets capture their on-screen initial value via a `??=` chain that locks in on the WIDGET'S OWN first build, which runs before `ON_INIT_STATE`'s `addPostFrameCallback` resolves the prefill (`SchedulerBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback`, confirmed in `generated_code`). A guest could see a blank/wrong-looking form and "correct" it, silently overwriting the correct prefilled value. This exact bug class (`??=`-captured-before-load) is already documented in `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (inherited from the sister admin-dashboard project) with an established `isConfigLoaded`-style visibility-gate fix.

**Fix taken instead of the visibility-gate pattern:** both widgets' initial values now bind directly to the PAGE PARAMS (`prefillDate`/`prefillStartTime`) rather than to page state. A page param is a constructor-level field, available synchronously from the very first frame — no async dependency, so no `??=`-before-load race is possible regardless of whether codegen evaluates the binding in `initState()` or lazily in `build()`. This is simpler and strictly safer than adding a new page-state flag and gating 2 widgets' visibility, and avoids a visible flash-of-empty-form on every page open that the gate approach would introduce.
- **Date field** (`TextField_amemivlw`): had NO initial-value binding at all before this fix (confirmed via `generated_code` — a bare `TextEditingController()`). `TextField`'s DSL constructor has no value/initialValue param, and `EditWidgetPatch.initialValue` only supports bool/num (Switch/Slider) — the only way to bind a TextField's dynamic initial text is the raw proto field `FFTextField.initialText` (not the deprecated `legacyInitialText`), the same shape already proven in the sister project (`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md:113`). Bound via raw `page.mutateNode` to a hand-built `FFVariable(source: WIDGET_CLASS_PARAMETER, baseVariable: FFBaseVariable(widgetClass: FFWidgetClassVariable(paramIdentifier: ...)))`, replicating the SDK's own internal (non-exported) `varFromPageParam` helper using only public proto types. Confirmed in `generated_code`: `_model.resDateFieldTextController ??= TextEditingController(text: widget!.prefillDate);` — correct in both the deep-link and plain-invite-button cases (blank was already `resDate`'s own default, so no regression for the 2 existing "誘う" buttons that don't pass a deep link).
- **Start-time dropdown** (`DropDown_ggrglg4w`): already had a *pre-existing* binding to `State('resStartTime')` (from before this session) — correct in principle, just broken by the same timing race. Needed a NEW custom function, `resolveInitialStartTime(prefillStartTime)`, returning `prefillStartTime` if non-empty else the literal `'19:00'` (matching `resStartTime`'s own state default exactly — the two must stay in sync if either default ever changes), so the no-deep-link default stays identical to today's behavior.

**A genuine SDK gap found while wiring the dropdown fix: `page.bindValue(...)` is a silent no-op for `DropDown` widgets.** First attempt used the public, typed `page.bindValue(dropdown, CustomFunction(...))` API — compiled and pushed with zero error, but neither `generated_code` nor a follow-up `flutterflow ai inspect --tree` showed ANY change; the server-side proto still had the original `State('resStartTime')` binding, byte-for-byte. Root-caused by reading the SDK source: `_BindStringValueOp.apply` (`edit.dart:5248`) short-circuits via `_nodeSemanticallyEqualsAfterMutation`, which mutates a throwaway copy of the node and compares `buildDslNodeSnapshot(...)` before/after — if they're "semantically equal," the real write is skipped as a no-op optimization. But `_snapshotWidgetDetails`'s per-widget-type switch (`snapshot.dart:434-449`) has cases for `Container/Card/Column/Row/Stack/Text/TextField/Button/Icon/IconButton/ListView/ProgressBar/Switch/Divider/Spacer` — **no case for `DropDown` at all** — so a DropDown's `initialOption` (or any other DropDown-specific prop) is completely invisible to the snapshot, every mutation looks identical to no mutation, and the real write never happens. Distinct from the previously-documented `component.root`/`dropdownOptions` bugs (those wrote correctly server-side but codegen ignored the write) — here the write itself never reaches the real node. **Workaround:** bypass `bindValue`'s operation pipeline entirely — compile the same expression with the lower-level but still-public `compileDslStringValueForExistingWidgetClass(project, widgetClassName:, targetNodeKey:, expression:, app:)` and write the result directly onto the node found via `findPage`/`findByKey` inside `app.raw((project) {...})`. Confirmed working in `generated_code`: `_model.resStartTimeDropdownValue ??= functions.resolveInitialStartTime(widget!.prefillStartTime)`.

**Client-intent re-check (3rd independent pass): no gaps found requiring a fix.** Re-verified against `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` and the client's own spec wording: rolling 7-day weeks from today (not calendar-aligned) — well-supported, consistent with every other relative-time mechanic in this app; "subtractive"/default-available — independently corroborated by pre-existing spec wording, the schema's own status enum, and `cancelPayment`'s revert-to-available logic; ○=available/×=unavailable — directly derivable from the spec sentence describing the guest tapping ○ to deep-link into booking; guest deep-link prefilling only date+time (not more) — matches the existing "誘う" invite buttons' own prefill-what's-known precedent. One soft, spec-silent inference noted but not changed: the "－" (reserved) glyph is a reasonable extrapolation (the spec only describes a binary ○/× toggle) — low risk, left as-is.

**Dormant, documented, deliberately not touched:** two independent `If(prefillDate...)`/`If(prefillStartTime...)` calls in `ReservationForm`'s `ON_INIT_STATE` compile into a NESTED conditional in generated code (the 2nd `if` sits inside the 1st's true-branch), not two sibling `if`s as authored — a compiler-level quirk. Harmless today because the only call site (CastProfile's ○-tap `Navigate`) always passes both params together or neither, so the nesting never changes observable behavior. Not restructured — the restructuring risk outweighs a currently-zero-impact quirk.

**Verification:** backend — `npm run build` clean, standalone node date-validation test (7 cases), `firebase functions:list --json` confirms all 3 `ACTIVE` post-deploy. DSL — `flutterflow ai test` before each of 2 pushes, both verified against actual `generated_code` output (not just push success) — this is exactly what caught the `bindValue`-on-DropDown no-op, which compiled and pushed clean with zero indication anything was wrong. Not live-device-tested (same standing session-wide limitation as §65 — no FlutterFlow Desktop pairing).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/schedule.ts` (4 fixes, deployed), `dsl/edit.dart` (2 pushes: date-field binding + dropdown binding), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (new entries: the page-param-fed variant of the `??=`-timing bug, the raw-`FFTextField.initialText`-plus-hand-built-`FFVariable` technique, and the `bindValue`-on-`DropDown` SDK gap).

### §68: Authorize-time slot locking + guest-side booking validation — the two gaps §65/§67 disclosed and deferred, now built

**Ask:** "Build the Authorize-time slot locking and guest-side booking validation" — the two pieces explicitly scoped out of the original calendar build (§65) as "a materially different and riskier task" since they touch the live Stripe Authorize/booking flow. Given the stakes (payment-critical code, six different reservation cancel/expire paths), researched the full reservation/Stripe lifecycle via 3 Explore passes before writing any code, then had an independent Plan-agent critique the design before implementing — the critique found a critical bug (see below) that would have shipped invisibly.

**What was actually wrong before this change:** nothing anywhere in the codebase ever set a `schedule_slots` doc to `"reserved"`. `toggleScheduleSlot` already refused to touch a `"reserved"` slot, and `cancelPayment` already had revert-to-available logic — both built defensively in §65 in anticipation of this, but nothing upstream ever created the state they were guarding against. A guest could request, pay for, and get confirmed on a time slot a cast had already blocked or that another guest already booked, with the calendar never reflecting reality.

**Key architecture fact that shaped the whole design:** "Authorize" is 100% webhook-driven in this codebase, not synchronous in any callable. `createPaymentIntent` only initiates the Stripe PaymentIntent (`capture_method:"manual"`) and deliberately does NOT write `status:"authorized"` (an already-fixed, earlier bug guards against that). The only place a reservation becomes `"authorized"` is `handleAmountCapturableUpdated` (`stripe-webhooks.ts`), fired from Stripe's `payment_intent.amount_capturable_updated` event — the one point in the whole backend with server-confirmed, Stripe-verified proof funds are actually held. That is where locking had to go.

**New shared helpers, `firebase/functions/src/schedule.ts`:**
- `dayStartOf(d)` — extracted from `parseDayStart`'s inline UTC-midnight construction, so every writer of a slot's `date` field computes it identically.
- `computeSlotStartTimes(startAt, durationMinutes)` — every 30-min-aligned slot start covering the booked window; floors a misaligned start defensively (over-covers, never under-covers).
