### §78: Task #26 — traced all 22 §9 test scenarios against actual code, found and fixed 2 real bugs

**Ask:** "execute as much of the 22-scenario test plan as possible."

**Environment check before starting**: confirmed via `ToolSearch` that no `testpilot.*` (FlutterFlow AI's own vision-agent e2e test runner) or `live.*` (FlutterFlow Desktop pairing) MCP tools are connected in this session — this environment genuinely cannot execute a live run of any kind, consistent with every prior verification disclosure this session has made. Scoped the task honestly rather than either skip it or pretend a code trace is equivalent to a live test: dispatched 5 parallel background agents, each tracing a batch of scenarios against real `firebase/functions/src/*.ts` bodies, real `dsl/edit.dart` wiring, and real `generated_code/lib/**/*_widget.dart` output (not DSL intent — what actually compiled and landed), each required to cite file:line evidence rather than infer from function/action names.

**Result: `TEST_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` (new file, matches the deliverable IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §10 already names).** Of 22 scenarios: 13 TRACED-CORRECT, 7 TRACED-PARTIAL (each with precisely-named gaps, most already-disclosed elsewhere in this project — search/filter, affiliate-network-pull, bulk-invite's group_invite auto-hide, admin work-post hire-UI — re-confirmed still-true rather than newly discovered), 2 that started as TRACED-BROKEN and are now fixed (below), 0 remaining open bugs. Full scenario-by-scenario table is in the report file, not duplicated here.

**Two genuine, previously-undiscovered bugs found by tracing scenarios that no prior review round (including the exhaustive §64/§70/§71 passes) happened to exercise this specific way — both fixed in this same pass, not just reported:**

1. **`cancelPayment` never touched extension PaymentIntents** (`stripe-payments.ts`). Found tracing scenario 11 (cancellation matrix): a cast can cancel an `in_progress` reservation (no status guard blocks this — extensions themselves require exactly that status to request), but `cancelPayment` has only ever acted on the reservation's OWN `payment_intent_id`. Any extension PaymentIntents (`reservations/{res_id}/extensions/{ext_id}`) were silently left untouched — an already-`captured` extension charge stayed captured forever, and "cast cancels → 100% refund to guest" did not literally hold once an extension had been captured. Confirmed this is genuinely unhandled elsewhere too: `cancelExtensionPayment` (same file) only ever handles the "authorized, not yet captured" case (its own early `return` on `status !== "authorized"`) and isn't called from `cancelPayment` anyway. **Fixed**: `cancelPayment` now sweeps every non-`cancelled` extension on the reservation — `authorized` → `paymentIntents.cancel` (release the hold, same as the base PI's own release path); `captured` → `refunds.create` (a real refund, not silently left charged) — and releases each extension's own locked `schedule_slots` row via the same `extensionSlotsQuery` helper `cancelExtensionPayment` already established. One extension's Stripe error is caught and logged rather than aborting the whole reservation cancellation (matches this function's own existing failure-isolation philosophy for its other cleanup steps).
2. **`status: "retrying"` ledger entries were a dead end, not a retry queue** (`stripe-payments.ts`). Found tracing scenario 14 (debt offset): `transferPendingCastRewards` correctly flips a failed Stripe Transfer's ledger entry to `status: "retrying"` on failure — but grepped every `.where(` across the whole backend before concluding: nothing, anywhere, ever queries that status back out. The money itself was never lost (the ledger entry is an accurate, correct record, and any `logical_debt` offset already applied stands correctly) — but the actual payout was permanently stranded with zero path back to the cast's Stripe account. **Fixed**: new scheduled function `retryFailedCastTransfers` (`onSchedule("every 1 hours", ...)`, mirroring this backend's own existing `autoCancelExpiredAuth`/`autoCompleteReviews` sweep pattern — same transactional-claim-then-attempt shape as `transferPendingCastRewards` itself, just re-scoped from a single `resId` to a global `status == "retrying"` query, bounded to 50/run). A cast still lacking a Stripe account on retry reverts to `"retrying"` (not stuck at a claimed-but-abandoned `"transferring"` status) so a later run can pick it up again once they onboard.

**Both fixes verified**: `npm run build` clean, not yet deployed (added to the standing undeployed-functions list — `retryFailedCastTransfers` specifically needs a real deploy to ever run at all, since it's a brand-new scheduled function, inert until live). No DSL/frontend change needed for either — both are pure backend correctness fixes with no new client-facing surface.

**Process note**: this is the first time this session used PARALLEL AGENTS specifically for a read-only VERIFICATION sweep across a fixed, externally-defined checklist (§9's 22 items), rather than for building or for an open-ended comprehensive review (§70's own 7-agent pattern). The batching (by feature-area, ~4-5 scenarios each) and the explicit "cite file:line, don't infer from names" instruction in every agent's prompt were deliberate, given this session's own §70 process note about not trusting agent-reported findings without independent verification — here, every agent's own report already carried its file:line citations, so the citations themselves stand as the verification trail rather than requiring a second independent re-check pass on top (a lighter-weight but still evidence-backed process, appropriate for a trace task as opposed to §70's security-critical hunt).

**Standing limitation, explicitly restated in the report itself, not just here**: no live Stripe test-mode run, live-device testing, or real calendar-boundary execution was possible — every TRACED-CORRECT verdict is necessary evidence toward a passing live run, not a substitute for one. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §11's "re-run the full §9 list against live-mode Stripe with real transactions before launch" requirement is unchanged and unfulfilled by this pass.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/stripe-payments.ts` (2 real bug fixes: extension-refund-on-cancel, retry-queue sweep), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (export `retryFailedCastTransfers`), `TEST_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` (new), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§10 deliverable status), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §80: Fresh-eyes adversarial re-review of the entire Tasks #19-27 body of work — found and fixed a CRITICAL live compile-breaking bug, its own root cause, a money-safety regression in an earlier fix, and 5 more pre-existing compile errors

**Ask:** "Please review this once again. Please identify any missed parts, areas where errors occur, parts that were implemented incorrectly, and various other errors and bugs, and complete the project to achieve the best possible outcome." — an explicit instruction to NOT trust the prior "done" report at face value and instead adversarially re-verify it.

**Method:** dispatched 5 parallel fresh-eyes agents, each told to assume something was broken until proven otherwise (not to confirm the work): (1) audit every admin page's ACTUAL generated output for duplicate widgets/broken wiring; (2) exhaustively grep and group every `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` call in the whole file by (page, anchor) to hunt for more instances of the §72 duplication-bug class; (3) adversarial correctness review of the 5 backend functions added/modified this session; (4) audit every Firestore rule/index touched this session; (5) fact-check every claim in `TEST_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`/`CUTOVER_CHECKLIST.md`/§72-79 against the actual code. Every finding below was independently re-verified by direct file reads / `dart analyze` before being acted on, not fixed on an agent's word alone — several agent findings turned out to need correction themselves (see below).

**CRITICAL — the generated Flutter app currently could not build at all, confirmed via `dart analyze`.** `sysConfigCurrentValueFn`/`serviceAreaFlagFn` (custom functions backing `SystemConfigPage`/`ServiceAreaPage`, built in Tasks #21/#23) compared a nullable `index` parameter (`int?`, this DSL's standard compiled type for a declared arg) directly with `>=`/`<` with no null check — a real null-safety compile error (`unchecked_use_of_nullable_value`), not a false positive; reproduced directly with `dart analyze` against the actual generated project before believing the agent. This is a whole-project-blocking bug: Dart type-checks the entire compilation unit regardless of whether a broken top-level function is ever called, so this alone meant NOTHING in the app could build, not just these two pages.
- **First fix attempt was itself incomplete and had to be corrected.** Tried `CustomFunctionHandle` + `app.raw(updateCustomFunction(...))` (the established pattern for fixing an already-live custom ACTION in place) — this triggered a reproducible, identical-across-2-retries validation cascade on a totally unrelated block (UserManagementPage's per-row buttons: "Custom action argument X is not set properly", "Invalid value for argument item"). Rather than accept that surface explanation, isolated it via 4 separate targeted test pushes (reverting the checkbox fix alone, removing the new page alone, reverting to a renamed-function approach alone) — **all 3 ruled out individually**, proving the apparent cause was a red herring. Kept digging rather than accept "it's flaky" — see the ROOT CAUSE finding below, which was the real explanation all along.
- **A "rename to V2" workaround was tried next and was ALSO wrong** — it avoided the DSL-level "different payload" validation error by declaring brand-new `sysConfigCurrentValueV2`/`serviceAreaFlagV2` functions and repointing call sites, leaving the original buggy functions "orphaned." This is NOT actually a fix: `dart analyze` on the resulting generated project showed the SAME 6 errors still present, because the orphaned original functions' broken bodies were still physically compiled into `custom_functions.dart` — Dart doesn't skip type-checking an unused top-level function. Caught this by re-running `dart analyze` after every fix attempt rather than trusting a successful `flutterflow ai run` push as proof of a working build (this project's own established discipline, applied to itself this time).
- **Once the REAL root cause (below) was found and fixed, the original `CustomFunctionHandle`+`updateCustomFunction` approach was retried and worked correctly** — reverted the V2-rename, updated the ORIGINAL functions in place, confirmed via `dart analyze`: zero errors for these two functions.

**ROOT CAUSE of the validation cascade above, found via careful bisection rather than accepted as unexplained flakiness: a stale, never-frozen duplicate `ensureReplaced` block on `UserManagementPage`, dating back to Task #24's own edit.** Task #20 built a 4-button per-row reconstruction (`ensureReplaced` targeting key `ListView_03wxfdpi`); Task #24 added a 5th button by writing a SECOND, separate `ensureReplaced` call (targeting `ListView_ezz6iswp`) — but never froze/removed the Task #20 block, so BOTH were live simultaneously. On every push, the Task #20 block ran first (rebuilding the list under a fresh key), so by the time the Task #24 block ran second, its hardcoded `ListView_ezz6iswp` no longer matched anything live — `findByKey` silently resolved to nothing, and the itemBuilder's `item` variable and every argument derived from it became invalid, producing the exact validation cascade. **This is the SAME bug class as §72's `AdminDashboardPage` nav-menu duplication (multiple structural mutations targeting one widget across separate pushes) — just manifesting as a hard validation failure instead of silent content duplication this time**, because `ensureReplaced` (unlike `ensureInsertedAfter`) doesn't leave the earlier content behind, it just breaks the LATER call's own anchor resolution. Confirms and generalizes the §72 lesson: this bug class isn't specific to `ensureInsertedAfter`, or even to widgets accumulating — ANY pair of structural mutations sharing a target across separate pushes is unsafe, regardless of which primitive is used or what the visible symptom turns out to be. Fixed by freezing the entire Task #20 block; the Task #24 5-button block is now the sole source of truth. Verified via `generated_code`: `UserManagementPage` has exactly 5 buttons, no duplicates.

**Real logic bug found and fixed: `ServiceAreaPage`'s 10 prefecture checkboxes ignored the actual click direction.** Every checkbox's `onChanged` was wired as `SetState(state.saX, Not(State(state.saX)))` — a pure toggle of whatever the state already was, regardless of which way the admin actually clicked. Confirmed in generated code: both the `if (newValue)` and `else` branches computed the identical `_model.saX = !_model.saX!` — an admin could tap "activate Tokyo" and, depending on race/timing, actually deactivate it, with the checkbox showing the WRONG resulting state. Root cause: this DSL requires a widget-value literal (`const WidgetValue()`) to bind an `onChanged` to the actual new value, exactly the same pattern already proven working elsewhere in this file for `Dropdown`/an existing `Checkbox` (`CocomisePage`'s `activeOnly` toggle) — `Not(State(...))` was a wrong guess that happened to compile (no type error, since both are valid boolean expressions) but was semantically wrong. Fixed by switching all 10 to `const WidgetValue()`, confirmed in generated code: `onChanged: (newValue) async { ...; _model.saX = newValue; ... }`. The save path was never at risk (it already read from page state, populated correctly by this same onChanged) — only the live toggle behavior was broken.

**Backend bug found and fixed: `adminSendNotification`'s "send to all users" query silently excluded legacy users.** `.where("is_active", "!=", false)` — Firestore's `!=`/`not-in` filters exclude any document where the field is ABSENT, not just documents where it's explicitly `true`. Every other consumer of this exact field in this codebase (`getFavorites`/`getDiscoveryCasts`, auth.ts) already filters `is_active !== false` IN-MEMORY specifically to treat a missing field as active — this one function alone pushed the exclusion into the query layer instead, contradicting the established convention and silently dropping any legacy/manually-seeded user doc from every broadcast. Fixed: removed the query-level filter, added in-memory filtering matching the established pattern, and removed the `.limit()`-free full fetch's dependency on the now-unnecessary `(account_type, is_active)` composite index (left the index in place — harmless, just no longer load-bearing for this specific query).

**Money-safety bug in the PRIOR turn's own extension-refund fix (§78), found and corrected before it ever reached this project's real users.** §78's fix for "extension PaymentIntents not refunded on whole-reservation cancel" refunded BOTH `authorized` (never captured) AND `captured` (already charged) extensions. This re-review traced `reportCompletion`'s actual write order (reservations.ts) and confirmed: `status: "completion_pending"` is written BEFORE `captureAuthorizedExtensions` is ever called — meaning an extension can only reach `captured` status once the reservation has ALREADY progressed past `in_progress`, i.e. once service has genuinely been substantially rendered. This is the exact same condition the base payment's own `castHasArrived` check already uses to justify "no refund, service was delivered." §78's fix would have refunded real, already-paid-for, already-consumed service time with no ledger entry and no clawback of any reward already transferred to the cast — introducing a NEW money-safety bug while fixing an old one. Corrected: the sweep now only ever releases `authorized` (never-captured) extensions; a captured extension needing a genuine refund (e.g. a real dispute) should go through `adminManualRefund` — a deliberate, reasoned admin action — not an automatic side effect of cancelling the parent reservation. This is a direct, concrete demonstration of why this project's own "verify, don't just trust the last fix" discipline matters even applied to THIS SESSION's own immediately-prior work, not just inherited code.

**4 more genuine, pre-existing (not introduced this session) compile errors found via the same `dart analyze` sweep and fixed:**
- `checkEmailVerified`, `sendFirebaseEmailVerification`, `verifyPhoneCodeAndLink`, `verifyPhoneCodeAndUpdate` (all in `dsl/edit.dart`, all from early Phase 2/6 auth work) each used `FirebaseAuth`/`PhoneAuthProvider`/`FirebaseAuthException` with no `import 'package:firebase_auth/firebase_auth.dart';` anywhere in the file — `auth_util.dart` (which some of these also import) itself imports but does not `export` that package, so nothing transitively provided these symbols. Fixed all 4 via `updateCustomAction` (2 needed freezing first, since they'd never been touched via this pattern before; 2 already used it and could be edited in place).
- `shareReferralLink` used `Share.share(...)` from `package:share_plus/share_plus.dart`, which was never actually declared as a project pub dependency — confirmed absent from `generated_code/pubspec.yaml`. Adding it turned into a genuine, unresolvable transitive-dependency dead end for this project's specific pinned package set: every `share_plus` version either needs a `win32` incompatible with this project's own `package_info_plus ^10.2.1`, or (`>=13.0.0`) needs `url_launcher_windows ^3.1.5` against this project's own pinned `3.1.4` — 3 version attempts (`^7.2.1`, `^10.1.4`, `^13.0.0`) all failed `flutter pub get` for one of these two reasons. Rather than keep fighting Windows-desktop-platform dependency resolution for a mobile-first app's minor SNS-share fallback, reverted the dependency entirely (`removePubDependency`) and rewrote the one branch that needed it (the generic/no-specific-platform fallback) to use `Clipboard.setData` instead — no new dependency, same technique already proven this session for `LedgerOversightPage`'s CSV export.

**1 remaining pre-existing compile error, confirmed genuinely unfixable from this DSL workspace, same class as the already-documented Android package-path finding (§77).** `auth_util.dart`'s `currentUserDisplayName`/`currentPhoneNumber` getters and `backend.dart`'s `createUsersRecordData(...)` call site (both native FlutterFlow auth-scaffolding files, not DSL-authored custom code) reference `UsersRecord.displayName`/`.phoneNumber` — fields that don't exist on this project's actual schema (real fields are `nickname`/`phone`; confirmed by reading `createUsersRecordData`'s own real, schema-generated signature in `users_record.dart`, which correctly uses `nickname`/`phone` — proving the CALLING code in `backend.dart` is what's wrong, not the schema). Searched the whole SDK for any DSL-level primitive controlling Firebase-Auth-integration field mapping — none exists. This is platform auth-scaffolding boilerplate outside this workspace's reach, not something `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` can touch (those only operate on registered custom-code artifacts, not core auth integration files). Disclosed, not silently left unmentioned or guessed at with a risky patch.

**Result: `dart analyze` on the full generated project went from 14 errors (before this pass — the app genuinely could not build) to 4 (the one disclosed-unfixable finding above), with zero new errors introduced.** Every fix verified against `dart analyze`'s own output directly, not just against `flutterflow ai run`'s own (looser) `compileDslApp` validation, which is exactly what let the original bug ship in the first place — `compileDslApp` does not fully type-check custom-code Dart bodies against the rest of the generated project (confirmed, matches CLAUDE.md's own disclosed custom-code validation limits).

**Documentation-accuracy correction, found by the fact-checking agent and independently confirmed:** §76's "closes the full 13-task admin-panel block... covering all 19 items... no item was silently dropped" was false as stated — items 6, 8-13 carried no status tag at all in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, and item 6 (home-ranking monitoring) genuinely had zero admin surface. Corrected: struck through the false claim inline in §76 (rather than silently editing history), added accurate tags to items 8, 9, 10, 11, 13 (`[DONE]`, work that was actually built in Tasks #13-18 but never got its checklist tag) and item 12 (`[PARTIAL]`, matching what §3.8a's own precedent analysis had already predicted), and built the genuinely-missing item 6 (`HomeRankingDiagnosticsPage`, new `adminGetHomeRankingDiagnostics` — see below).

**New admin page built to close the one genuine feature gap found: `HomeRankingDiagnosticsPage` (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md item 6).** New `adminGetHomeRankingDiagnostics` (admin.ts) deliberately does not just call `getDiscoveryCasts` — that function's own response discards the raw `dist`/`lastLoginMs` values it computes internally, returning only the final sorted order, so a caller could never verify WHY a cast landed where it did. The new function mirrors the exact same filter + haversine distance formula + 3-tier sort comparator but returns every raw computed value (rank position, online status, distance in km or "fallback" when the cast has no location, last-login ISO timestamp), giving an admin genuine verification power, not just a re-display of the same opaque order.

**Process note, worth internalizing:** this pass caught THREE distinct classes of self-correction opportunity that a less adversarial re-review would have missed: (1) a fix that "worked" by the tool's own success signal (`flutterflow ai run` pushed clean) but was objectively still broken (`dart analyze` proved it); (2) a fix for one bug that quietly introduced a different, more severe bug in the same code path (the extension-refund money-safety regression); (3) a documentation claim that read as confident and well-cited but was contradicted by the primary source document it claimed to summarize. None of these would surface from re-reading the PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md prose alone — all three needed direct re-inspection of the actual current code/generated-output state, which is precisely why this project's own "verify against generated_code, not just push success" discipline exists, and why it needs to be applied recursively to this session's own work, not just to inherited code.

**Verification:** every backend change — `npm run build` clean (not deployed, added to the standing undeployed list — none of this pass's backend changes are new functions requiring deploy except what §78 already flagged). Every DSL change — `flutterflow ai test` before every push (12 push attempts total in this pass, most iterating toward the root-cause fix and the `share_plus` version resolution); `dart analyze` run against the FULL generated project (not just the one file with the known bug) after the final push, confirming the error count and identifying exactly which 4 remain and why. Every `generated_code` claim in this entry (button counts, checkbox behavior, function presence) verified by direct grep/read after the final push, not assumed from the DSL source alone.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/{admin,stripe-payments}.ts` (`adminSendNotification` in-memory-filter fix, `cancelPayment` extension-refund-scope correction — no new deploy needed beyond what §78 already listed), `dsl/edit.dart` (sysConfigCurrentValue/serviceAreaFlag real fix, ServiceAreaPage checkbox fix, HomeRankingDiagnosticsPage new page, UserManagementPage stale-block freeze — the actual root-cause fix, 4 firebase_auth import fixes, share_plus revert + Clipboard rewrite), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (items 6/8-13 status tags), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry + §76 correction).

### §81: Comprehensive project-wide review round 2 — 8 parallel adversarial agents covering the WHOLE project (not just admin panel), 19 confirmed real bugs found and fixed, 1 disclosed as genuinely unfixable from this workspace

**Ask (verbatim, twice):** "deploy the backend functions now" (executed — see below), then: "Please conduct a comprehensive review of the project starting now... Despite multiple rounds of checks and corrections, errors have continued to occur. This time, please examine every single aspect in meticulous detail." An explicit statement that §70/§78/§80's prior rounds — each already fairly thorough — were STILL not enough, and a direct instruction to widen scope beyond admin-panel/recently-touched code to the entire project.

**Method:** dispatched 8 parallel `general-purpose` background agents in one batch, each scoped to a distinct area of the ENTIRE project and told to "assume something is broken until proven otherwise": (1) exhaustive `dart analyze` sweep, (2) guest-facing reservation/payment flow, (3) cast-facing + onboarding/auth pages, (4) CocoMise/Favorites/misc pages, (5) full backend TS correctness review, (6) full Firestore/Storage rules re-audit, (7) whole-file DSL `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureReplaced` anchor-collision hunt, (8) deployment-consistency + documentation-accuracy check. Every CONFIRMED finding was independently re-verified by direct file read (never fixed on an agent's word alone) before any code changed — same discipline as §80, now applied at full-project scope.

**CRITICAL — `ReservationDetailPage`'s entire guest-info/schedule card was 100% hardcoded fake data, shown identically for every single reservation, confirmed on two separate cards.** `reservation_detail_widget.dart`: date ("2026年 4月 15日（水）"), time ("20:30~21:30"), reward amount ("2,750円"), and counterpart name ("アルク さん") were all static literals baked into the widget tree at authoring time — `fetchReservationVisibility` (the page's only existing data fetch) only ever resolved status/role for button gating, nothing fetched the reservation's own content. A second, independent bug in the same tree: the 場所 (location) row's OWN value showed the exact same hardcoded DATE string — a copy-paste artifact, not a real address. On follow-up verification AFTER the first fix landed, found a SECOND card ("リクエストメッセージ") with the identical defect: duplicate static counterpart name, a static "お食事" purpose value, and a literal filler string of ~200 repeated "あ" characters standing in for the reservation's real message. Fixed by adding a new backend callable `getReservationDetailInfo` (reservations.ts) — needed because the counterpart's `nickname` lives on `users/{other_uid}`, which `firestore.rules` locks to strictly owner-only read (a guest can never read their cast's own doc directly, same constraint `getChatRoomInfo` already solves the same way) — returning date/duration/location/total_amount/counterpart_nickname/details, plus a client-side action (`fetchReservationDetailData`) that formats date/time/amount labels and parses the `【目的：$purpose】\n$freeText` convention `callCreateReservation` already writes. All 9 affected Text widgets (5 on the first card, 4 on the second, 2 of which reuse the same partner-name/role functions) rebuilt via `ensureReplaced`, confirmed zero hardcoded strings remain via direct `generated_code` grep after push. Counterpart PHOTO deliberately left as its existing static fallback avatar (a prior, deliberate design decision, not a dynamic binding — confirmed via the DSL's own `CircleImage_wer97dj4` patch comment) — not scope-crept into a photo-binding feature.

**Money-safety: `processTip` and the staff-fee transfer both charged/computed money and then could silently lose it with no record if the payout `stripe.transfers.create` call failed.** Both wrote their `ledger` entry only AFTER a successful transfer — a transfer failure (restricted Connect account, transient network error) left NO record at all, and (for `processTip` specifically) the outer catch threw an error back to the guest despite their card already being successfully charged. Fixed by adopting the SAME `pending -> transferring -> confirmed/retrying` pattern §70 already established for `transferPendingCastRewards`: pre-allocate the ledger doc, write it `"pending"` BEFORE calling Stripe, never let a transfer failure surface as a guest-facing error (the charge succeeded; only the payout is deferred/retryable). `retryFailedCastTransfers` (the hourly sweep) broadened from `type == "reward"` to `type in ["reward", "staff_fee", "tip"]` — all three ledger shapes share the same `user_id`/`net_transfer`/`res_id` fields the retry loop already reads, so no other change to its body was needed; its notification text made type-aware (`報酬`/`スタッフ報酬`/`チップ`) so a staff member doesn't get a notification labeled as if it were a cast reward.

**Money-safety: no `stripe.transfers.create`/`refunds.create` call anywhere outside `affiliate.ts` used an `idempotencyKey`, despite `retryFailedCastTransfers` existing specifically to re-fire a failed transfer.** A transfer that actually succeeded on Stripe's side but errored/timed-out on ours before the ledger update committed would be marked `"retrying"` and genuinely double-sent on the next hourly sweep. Fixed by keying every transfer call (`transferPendingCastRewards`, `retryFailedCastTransfers`, the staff-fee loop, the tip transfer) on `transfer_${ledgerDocId}` — the SAME key across every retry attempt for that same logical entry, since the ledger doc id is stable and allocated before the first attempt. `adminManualRefund` similarly keyed on `refund_${res_id}_${amount||"full"}` against admin double-click/client-retry double-refund.

**Money-correctness: `adminUpdateReservationLocation` had no status guard and always stamped `updated_at`, silently corrupting `affiliate.ts`'s date-based payout/stat aggregations.** A routine "fix a typo in the meeting address" admin edit on an already-`completed` reservation shifted `updated_at` to "now" — `affiliate.ts`'s `countUniqueWorkDays` (the min-work-days payout gate) and `getAffiliateDashboard`'s stats both range-query `status == "completed"` reservations BY `updated_at` as a completion-date proxy. The edited reservation would vanish from the JST month/day it actually completed in (risking an affiliator missing `minDays` for a payout already earned) and reappear as "completed today" in whatever month the edit happened. Fixed: this function no longer touches `updated_at` at all (the field isn't completion-relevant to what it edits).

**Real exploitable vulnerability, not just disclosed debt: Storage's `/banners/**` path allowed write from ANY signed-in user, not just admins.** `storage.rules` gated read publicly (correct, matches the `banners` Firestore collection) but write only on `request.auth != null` — any authenticated non-admin could upload/overwrite arbitrary files under the public banners path via the Storage SDK directly (e.g. defacing a live promotional banner). Fixed to mirror `firestore.rules`' own `isAdmin()` check via a cross-service `firestore.get(/databases/(default)/documents/users/$(uid)).data.role == 'admin'` call — the same admin definition used everywhere else in this project, not a separate one to drift out of sync. Also tightened the dead-but-wide-open `invitions` Firestore collection (`allow create/read: if true`) to deny-all — two independent audits (this one and the original 2026-07-31 Tier-4 pass) both found zero backend/client references anywhere; a collection nothing reads or writes has no functionality to preserve by staying open, only attack surface (arbitrary-document spam at minimum) to close.

**`payment_confirm`'s price breakdown didn't sum to the real charged total, and its "30分ルール適用" row always claimed the discount applied.** `fetchPaymentConfirmDetails` computed `staffFee` but never included it in the returned delimited string — a guest whose reservation included staff cost saw 基本料金+タクシー代 that silently didn't add up to 合計, with no explanation. `thirty_min_rule_applied` was never fetched at all — the row's static "適用済み" literal claimed the rule applied for every reservation regardless of the real field. Fixed: both appended to the action's return string (indices 5/6, preserving the already-landed index-0..4 bindings unchanged), a new conditionally-visible row inserted for staff fee (`ensureInsertedAfter`, visible only when `staffFee > 0` since it's an optional per-reservation cost), and the static label rebuilt bound to the real value.

**`callCreateReservation`/`callCreateReservationWithStaff` both interpolated a nullable `date` param with no guard, unlike every other field on the same line.** A null `date` doesn't throw — Dart interpolates it as the literal string `"null"` (e.g. `"nullT19:00:00"`), which then reaches `createReservation`'s `new Date(date)` parse on the backend as an Invalid Date instead of failing loudly at the source. Fixed both (same guard `castId` already had: no real date, no reservation — `return null` early).

**Deployment: `adminGetHomeRankingDiagnostics`, `retryFailedCastTransfers`, and (newly found, not on any prior known-blocked list) `removeFavorite` were in Cloud Functions' `FAILED` state, not merely stale** — for the first two, apparent maiden deploys with likely zero working revision at all, meaning §80's own new diagnostics page and the retry-safety-net this whole round depends on for the money-leak fixes above may have been completely non-functional. Retried as a small, targeted 13-function deploy (everything actually touched by this round, by name) rather than the full ~93-function bulk deploy that previously exhausted the region's Cloud Run CPU quota — **succeeded 13/13**, confirmed via `firebase functions:list --json` (all `ACTIVE`, including both prior `FAILED` entries). A follow-up full `firebase deploy --only functions,firestore:rules,firestore:indexes` was then attempted to opportunistically reconcile the remaining ~80 functions left on stale pre-session code by an earlier, unrelated partial deploy — **this re-hit the identical CPU-quota-exceeded wall for ~52 of those unrelated functions**, confirming the quota constraint is still live in general (only enough headroom existed for this round's own targeted 13, not a full bulk redeploy). Re-confirmed via `firebase functions:list --json` immediately after that this did NOT regress any of this round's 13 functions — all 13 remained `ACTIVE` on the fresh code untouched by the failed bulk attempt (Cloud Run never cuts traffic away from a function's last successfully-served revision just because a later, unrelated deploy attempt fails). The ~52 still-stale functions are a pre-existing, disclosed condition from before this session, not something this round's changes touch or depend on.

**Systemic UX defect, 8 pages: the AppBar back arrow (`Icon(Icons.arrow_back, ...)`) had no tap handler at all — confirmed via `grep -rl "Icons.arrow_back" generated_code/lib/`, exactly 8 files.** The only tappable element nearby is the adjacent 40×40 logo, which on several of these pages actually signs the user out (confirmed separately, not fixed in this pass — see below). A user mid-flow (KYC, an active reservation, a payment) taps the visually obvious back arrow expecting to go back a step; nothing happens. Fixed by wiring each icon's `ON_TAP` to `NavigateBack()` on all 8: `kyc`, `paymentConfirm`, `extensionPayment`, `affiliate`, `castProfile`, `macchaChats`, `reservationDetail`, `reservationForm` — confirmed via `generated_code` that each is now wrapped in an `InkWell` calling `context.pop()`.

**`email_verification`'s message showed a hardcoded placeholder email regardless of the real signup address.** `Text_akk82e0o` read the literal `"example@email.comに認証メールを送信しました！"` for every user. Fixed via a new action reading `auth_util.dart`'s `currentUserEmail`, wired onto the page's existing `ON_INIT_STATE` trigger (edited in place, not a second call — this project's own standing rule: exactly one `ensureActions` call per trigger).

**`CocoTenDetailPage`'s "このお店で待ち合わせるキャストを探す" button navigated to plain `HomePage` with zero params — a dead-end CTA.** `HomePageWidget` has no venue/shop concept at all. A fully correct fix (rank Home's discovery-cast query by proximity to the venue) isn't safely buildable right now: `cocoten_shops.location` (geopoint) is schema.md's own documented "未実装、書き込まれることはない" (no geocoding mechanism exists), and HomePage's existing GPS/prefecture-fallback discovery fetch is wired to a delicate ON_INIT_STATE chain this file's own comments already flag as risky to touch a second time. Disclosed scope reduction, not silently dropped: replaced the misleading silent navigation with an honest "この機能は近日公開予定です" Snackbar so the CTA no longer looks broken; the real fix (venue-proximity-ranked discovery) is flagged here as follow-up work for a session that can safely rework that chain.

**Not fixed, disclosed: SignupPage's password-mismatch Snackbar shows the hardcoded English "Passwords don't match!"** — the only non-Japanese string found anywhere in the guest/cast-facing app. Attempted the same `_retargetNavigate`-style raw walk (recursing `followUpAction`/`conditionActions.trueActions`/`.falseAction` to find and rewrite the `FFAction.snackBar.textMessage` literal) already proven for this exact button's Navigate target — it silently found no match; re-verified via `generated_code` after a real push that the string is unchanged. The mismatch branch is very likely a native FlutterFlow conditional predating any DSL involvement, and if it uses `FFConditionActions`' DEPRECATED `legacy_condition`/`legacy_true_action` fields (not the modern `true_actions` shape this walk checks), this project's own standing rule makes those fields off-limits to read OR write, full stop. Reproducing the whole native chain via `ensureActions` was already ruled out by this block's own original comment (real risk of silently dropping the password check / `createAccountWithEmail` / Firestore write logic). Left as a disclosed, low-severity (i18n-only) known gap rather than forced through a path this project has explicitly ruled out — the dead helper function written to attempt this was reverted, not left as silently-no-op'ing live code.

**SUSPECTED risks closed defensively (not confirmed exploited, but real and cheap to close):** `adminUpdateSystemConfig` validated only `affiliate_payment_day`; every other numeric field (`default_cast_rate`, `tax_rate`, `max_total_hours`, `extension_limit_count`, staff fees, thresholds) was written verbatim with zero bounds checking — an admin-trust-boundary issue, but `max_total_hours: 0` would reject every booking outright and a negative `tax_rate` would produce negative tax. Fixed with per-field range/type validation grouped by shape (rates 0-1, fees/thresholds non-negative, count fields positive-integer-with-a-sane-ceiling). Separately, `processMonthlyAffiliatePayments` grouped pending rewards by `data.month` with no validation — a doc with a missing/malformed `month` (the same class of stray placeholder doc already documented elsewhere in this codebase) would group under the literal string `"undefined"`, and `countUniqueWorkDays("undefined")` throws (`"undefined".split("-")` → NaN → `Timestamp.fromDate(new Date(NaN))`), permanently wedging that reward as `"pending"` since the per-group try/catch only aborts that one group and it reruns identically every day. Fixed: malformed docs are now skipped and logged instead of silently wedging the whole group forever.

**Not fixable from this workspace, disclosed: `backend.dart`'s `queryCollectionCount` swallows real errors via a bare `catchError`.** Confirmed this is pure native FlutterFlow-generated backend plumbing (the generic count helper underlying every typed `query<X>RecordCount` wrapper across the whole project) with zero DSL-editable surface — same class of finding as §80's `auth_util.dart`/`UsersRecord` disclosure. `generated_code/` is read-only per this project's own standing rule; there is no `app.raw`/`updateCustomAction`-equivalent mechanism for touching native FlutterFlow codegen internals, and no confirmed live bug traced to it (the review agent itself flagged "fixability unconfirmed," not a proven defect) — not worth a risky direct edit to generated output for an unconfirmed, unfixable-from-here finding.

**8th agent (DSL anchor-collision hunt) found nothing new — a clean result, itself worth recording.** Exhaustive grep of all 138 `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureReplaced` mentions in the whole file (11 active, 124 frozen, 3 prose-only), grouped by (page, anchor): zero duplicate targets among active calls, guest-facing pages included, not just admin. Also surfaced a genuinely new, useful clarification for future audits: `ensureReplaced` self-heals via `_ReplaceAlreadyAppliedNoop` (re-locates by name+type when its literal key no longer resolves) — only `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` hard-fail on a stale anchor via `.expectOne()`. §80's `UserManagementPage` fix remains intact and correctly self-healing.

**Verification:** every DSL push went through `flutterflow ai run` (not validate-first, per this project's own established discipline); 3 of the pushes in this round hit "ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action... with a different payload" (the standard create-if-missing-only trap) for `callCreateReservation`/`fetchPaymentConfirmDetails`/`fetchReservationDetailData` — all three converted to the established freeze-original + `app.raw(updateCustomAction(...))` pattern, not worked around. One push hit "State field not found" from a state-field `ensureField` declared AFTER (later in the top-to-bottom script) the trigger that referenced it via `SetState` — moved earlier, confirming (again) that this DSL's execution order is real and matters, not just declaration presence anywhere in the file. Final `dart analyze` on the full regenerated project: 0 errors (only pre-existing style-lint `info`/`warning` noise across the whole codebase, none in any file this round touched). Every `generated_code` claim above (hardcoded-string removal, back-arrow `InkWell`, email message binding, Snackbar text unchanged) verified by direct grep/read after the final push, not assumed from the DSL source. Backend: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every edit; `firestore.rules`/`storage.rules` deployed and confirmed compiled by `firebase deploy` itself; 13 changed/previously-failed functions deployed and confirmed individually via `firebase functions:list --json` (all `ACTIVE`, including both prior `FAILED` entries), followed by a full `functions,firestore:rules,firestore:indexes` deploy to reconcile the remaining functions left stale by the earlier partial deploy.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (ReservationDetail full data-binding fix across 2 cards + `getReservationDetailInfo`-backed action, PaymentConfirm staff-fee row + 30-min-rule fix, `callCreateReservation`/`WithStaff` date guard, 8-page back-arrow wiring, EmailVerification message fix, CocoTenDetailPage honest-Snackbar fix, signup Snackbar fix attempted-and-reverted); `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (new `getReservationDetailInfo`), `stripe-payments.ts` (`processTip`/staff-fee-transfer pending-ledger-before-transfer fix, idempotency keys), `admin.ts` (`adminUpdateReservationLocation` updated_at fix, `adminManualRefund` idempotency key, `adminUpdateSystemConfig` validation), `affiliate.ts` (malformed-month guard), `index.ts` (new export); `firebase/firestore.rules` (`invitions` tightened), `firebase/storage.rules` (`/banners/**` admin-only write); `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §82: Task #39 — Admin cast-to-cast recruitment chat moderation (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §3.8 item 12's remaining gap)

**Ask:** "Please list the features that have not yet been implemented and implement them one by one... with a high level of precision to avoid the need for rework." First item taken from that session's own fresh audit of IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md against actual code: item 12's post-removal half was already `[DONE]` (`WorkPostManagementPage`'s stop/close action), but the report/chat-log path scoped specifically to cast-to-cast recruitment chat (as opposed to the existing reservation-scoped viewer on `AdminReportReviewPage`) was confirmed `[MISSING]`.

**Built:** `chat_room_id` was already returned by `selectWorkApplicant` (work-posts.ts) when a `partner_recruit` post's applicant is selected, but never persisted onto the `work_posts` doc itself — fixed with `await postRef.update({ chat_room_id: chatRoomId })` right after room creation, closing the only gap standing between "a recruitment chat exists" and "an admin can find it from the post." New `adminGetRecruitmentChatLog(post_id)` (admin.ts) mirrors `adminGetReportChatLog`'s existing shape (admin-gated, resolves sender nicknames server-side, same `nickname|||text|||` delimited convention `fetchReportChatLog`/`splitFieldFn` already use client-side) — looks up `work_posts/{post_id}` → `chat_room_id` → reads `chat_rooms/{id}/messages`. `fetchAdminWorkPosts`'s own return string extended (index 5: `chat_room_id`) and a new `adminWorkPostHasChat(item)` customFunction gates visibility on `type == 'partner_recruit' && chat_room_id.isNotEmpty` — a "停止する" post with no recruitment chat (most posts) shows no chat button at all. New dedicated page `AdminRecruitmentChatPage` (params: `postId`; matches this project's established "focused drill-down page navigated to with a param" shape, e.g. `ConsentRecordPage`/`HomeRankingDiagnosticsPage`, rather than an inline-expanding section) reached via a new "チャットを見る" button inserted into `WorkPostManagementPage`'s existing post-list `ListView` (full `ensureReplaced` reconstruction, not `ensureInsertedAfter` — the new button needs the itemBuilder closure's own `item` variable, which a sibling insertion can't reach, same established pattern as every other per-row-button addition this session). Disclosed scope reduction: no inline "freeze this participant" action on this page (unlike `AdminReportReviewPage`'s freeze button) — shows sender nicknames so an admin can identify who to moderate, then freezes them from the already-existing `UserManagementPage`; a second independent freeze entry point wasn't judged worth the added complexity.

**Bug class found and permanently documented (`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`):** changing an already-live custom action's argument list AND rewiring its calling widget to the new argument names in the SAME `flutterflow ai run` push fails with "Custom action argument X is not specified" for every argument (not just new ones), even though the argument-list mutation itself silently persists server-side despite the push reporting failure — `compileDslApp` resolves the calling widget's argument-name-to-parameter-key mapping against a pre-mutation snapshot within the same push. Cost 3 failed push attempts before root-caused via a deliberate revert-and-observe probe. Fix, now the standing rule for this class of change: split into two separate pushes — the argument-list change alone first (confirm it lands), then wire the calling widget in a follow-up push referencing the now-already-live names.

**Cleanup (this session, after the feature itself had already landed pre-compaction):** froze the `AdminRecruitmentChatPage` `ensurePage(...)` declaration and the `WorkPostManagementPage` list's `ensureReplaced(...)` call (both per this project's established "freeze after confirmed-landed" convention for structural DSL calls); fixed one dangling reference — the page's own `Navigate(...)` call from the list button still pointed at the now-frozen local `adminRecruitmentChatPage` variable instead of the typed `ff.Pages.adminRecruitmentChatPage` handle available now that the page had actually landed in an earlier push.

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` clean; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded; confirmed via direct `generated_code/lib/work_post_management_page/work_post_management_page_widget.dart` read that the "チャットを見る" button is gated on `adminWorkPostHasChat` and navigates to `AdminRecruitmentChatPageWidget.routeName` with `postId` correctly serialized. Both backend functions (`adminGetRecruitmentChatLog`, `selectWorkApplicant`) deployed via `firebase deploy --only functions:adminGetRecruitmentChatLog,functions:selectWorkApplicant --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` — both `ACTIVE`.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (state/action/component/page additions across the earlier pre-compaction portion of this session, plus this session's freeze+Navigate-fix cleanup), `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (`chat_room_id` persistence), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (new `adminGetRecruitmentChatLog`), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (new export), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (new bug-class section), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §83: Task #40 — Admin historical activity-aggregation reporting (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §3.8 item 19's last open sub-item)

**Ask:** same governing instruction as §82. Item 19's own text: rank/tier was already `[WON'T BUILD]` (§4.3), CSV export already `[PARTIAL]` (LedgerOversightPage, §76) — "activity-count aggregation reporting: still `[MISSING]` — `adminGetDashboardStats` provides same-day dashboard tiles only, not a real historical aggregate."

**Built:** new `adminGetActivityReport(granularity, periods)` (admin.ts), inserted directly after `adminGetDashboardStats` — buckets by admin-chosen granularity (`daily`/`monthly`, default daily) over an admin-chosen period count (default 30/12, hard-capped 31/24 to bound the query fan-out: each bucket issues 6 concurrent queries). Every query shape reuses an already-proven, already-indexed combo from elsewhere in this codebase rather than inventing a fresh one: `account_type`(==) + `created_at`(range) for new-guest/new-cast counts (the same combo `adminGetUsers` already issues when both filters are supplied — confirmed via `firestore.indexes.json`'s `users` composite index); `status`(==) + `updated_at`(range) for completed/cancelled reservation counts, reusing `affiliate.ts`'s own established completion-date proxy (`updated_at`, since reservations have no separate `completed_at` field — see §81's `adminUpdateReservationLocation` fix, which exists specifically to protect this same proxy from corruption); `created_at` alone and `last_capture_at` alone for reservation-created counts and captured revenue (single-field ranges, no composite index needed, same as `adminGetDashboardStats`'s own existing queries). JST bucket-boundary math is a direct generalization of `adminGetDashboardStats`'s own already-live `monthRanges` computation (same `+9h` offset technique), not reinvented.

Frontend: new custom action `fetchActivityReport` (calls the callable, flattens each bucket into a `|||`-delimited row matching this project's established list-state convention), new customFunction `activityReportRowLabel(item, index)` (single flexible extractor keyed by column index, same shape as `consentRecordLabelFn` from §76 rather than one function per column), new page `ActivityReportPage` (two granularity-toggle buttons that re-fetch and replace the row list, a lightweight 7-column table via a header `Row` + a `ListView` of `Row`s, and a CSV-to-clipboard export reusing the exact `Clipboard.setData` pattern already proven on `LedgerOversightPage`, §76 — client-side, currently-displayed-rows-only, same disclosed scope as that precedent). Nav button added to `AdminDashboardPage`'s existing consolidated `ensureReplaced` block (the single canonical insertion point this project settled on after §72's cascading-duplication incident) rather than a new `ensureInsertedAfter` call.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `flutterflow ai test` clean; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded on the first attempt (no argument-list/same-push race risk here — every custom action referenced was brand-new in this same push, not a rewire of an already-live one, the specific condition §82's bug class requires). Confirmed via direct `generated_code/lib/activity_report_page/activity_report_page_widget.dart` read that both granularity buttons call `actions.fetchActivityReport(...)` with the correct literal args and that the CSV button calls `actions.callExportActivityReportCsv(...)`; confirmed via `generated_code/lib/admin_dashboard_page/admin_dashboard_page_widget.dart` that the new nav button reaches `ActivityReportPageWidget.routeName`. `dart analyze` on every new/changed file: 0 errors, only pre-existing style-lint `info`/`warning` noise. `adminGetActivityReport` deployed via `firebase deploy --only functions:adminGetActivityReport --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, confirmed `ACTIVE`.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (new customFunction/2 custom actions/page/nav-button-in-place), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (new `adminGetActivityReport`), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (new export), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §84: Task #41 — CSV export expanded from LedgerOversightPage to 5 more admin list pages (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §3.8 item 19's CSV-export sub-item, now fully closed)

**Ask:** same governing instruction as §82/§83. Item 19's own text flagged this explicitly: "Not yet added to other list pages (`UserManagementPage`, `AuditLogPage`, etc.) — the pattern is now proven and reusable if broader coverage is wanted later."

**Scope decision:** added to `UserManagementPage`, `AuditLogPage`, `ProcessedEventsPage`, `WithdrawalQueuePage`, `ReservationOversightPage` (5 pages) — the two explicitly named in the spec plus 3 more natural fits (money-adjacent or log-shaped list views). Deliberately **excluded `StripeLogsPage`**: its row format's 4th field is the raw Stripe webhook JSON payload (arbitrary external data), which could itself contain the literal `|||` substring this project's delimited-row convention relies on — a naive `row.split('|||')` CSV builder (the same one every other page below uses safely, since none of their other fields carry arbitrary external content) would silently mis-split and corrupt that page's export. Confirmed by reading `fetchAdminStripeLogs`/`stripeLogRawDataFn` directly — they already avoid `.split('|||')` for the same reason, using `indexOf`-based substring extraction instead. Disclosed, not silently dropped; a safe version would need the same indexOf-based extraction, out of proportion for this pass.

**Built:** one `callExport<Thing>Csv(rows: listOf(string))` custom action per page (identical shape to `callExportLedgerCsv`/`callExportActivityReportCsv` — quoted-CSV header + per-row `.split('|||')` re-join), one `AdminExport<Thing>CsvButton` wired to it, per page.

**Non-trivial DSL structural finding, verified from SDK source before acting (not guessed):** 4 of the 5 target pages (`UserManagementPage`/`AuditLogPage`/`ProcessedEventsPage`/`WithdrawalQueuePage`) have their `ListView` as the page's ENTIRE `Scaffold.body` — no wrapping `Column`, unlike `LedgerOversightPage`/`ActivityReportPage` which both already had a `Text` header to `ensureInsertedAfter`. Adding a button above the list meant wrapping into `Column(children: [Button, Divider, Expanded(ListView)])` — the exact same live layout `ReservationOversightPage` already used for its own reason-field + list. Before doing this, read the SDK's own `_ReplaceSpec`/`_ReplaceOp`/`_ReplaceAlreadyAppliedNoop` source directly (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/src/dsl/edit.dart`) to confirm `ensureReplaced` is safe to use even when the REPLACEMENT's type differs from the original anchor's type (ListView → Column): the first run replaces by key unconditionally regardless of type; every later run (once the original key no longer resolves) self-heals by searching the WHOLE tree for a node matching the REPLACEMENT's own `name`+`type` — not the original anchor's — so giving the new `Column` a stable, distinct name (e.g. `AdminUsersWithExportColumn`) guarantees idempotent reruns indefinitely. `ReservationOversightPage` itself needed no reconstruction at all — it already had the Column+Divider+Expanded shape, so its CSV button is a single `ensureInsertedBefore` on its existing `Divider_pjhq7dzq`, confirmed via the SDK's `_InsertBeforeOp` source to already carry its own idempotency check (`_adjacentSiblingMatchBefore`), safe for a single stable call reused across pushes (the cascading-duplication bug class from §72 only applies when MULTIPLE INDEPENDENT insertion calls target the SAME anchor across different tasks — not the case here, this is the only insertion on this anchor).

Every page's itemBuilder (existing action buttons, `Card`/`Container`/`Column` structure) was reproduced byte-for-byte from a fresh direct read of its current live source immediately before writing the replacement — not from memory or a prior research summary — specifically to avoid dropping an existing button while restructuring.

**One real validation catch, not a bug:** the same push also touched the `ActivityReportPage` block built in §83 (no relation to CSV expansion) — `compileDslApp` rejected 3 lines there (`SetState`/`State` still using raw string state names) with "Use `ff.Pages.activityReportPage.state.X` instead," since that page's typed SDK handles only became available after §83's OWN push landed (at authoring time in §83, the page didn't exist yet in the same push, so raw names were the only option — exactly this project's own established same-push rule). Fixed by switching those 3 lines to the now-available typed references; re-ran clean. Not the same bug class as the §82 argument-list race (a different custom action's argument LIST wasn't touched here) — just the normal, expected "typed refs unlock after the page's own first successful push" sequencing already documented as standard practice throughout this project.

**Verification:** `flutterflow ai test` clean both attempts; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded on the second attempt (first attempt's only failure was the `ActivityReportPage` typed-ref validation above, fixed and re-pushed — no argument-list/same-push race risk, no partial-state concern, a clean compile-time rejection with zero server mutation per this project's own established "failing `run` == failing `validate`" guarantee). `dart analyze` across all 5 changed pages + all 5 new custom-action files: 0 errors. Confirmed via direct `generated_code` grep, per page, that (a) the new CSV button and its `actions.callExport<Thing>Csv(...)` call are present and (b) every pre-existing action button (凍結/解除, 強制退会, コンテンツ管理, 退会ブロック状況, 同意記録 on `UserManagementPage`; 承認/保留/却下 on `WithdrawalQueuePage`; 強制キャンセル/手動返金 on `ReservationOversightPage`) survived the restructuring unchanged.

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (5 new custom actions + 4 `ensureReplaced` Column-wraps + 1 `ensureInsertedBefore` + 3-line `ActivityReportPage` typed-ref fix), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §85: Task #43 — CocoTen venue photo upload + genre/tag master (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §3.8 item 7's remaining gap)

**Ask:** same governing instruction as §82-§84. Pre-task audit found item 7's own text stale in two ways, confirmed by reading the actual current code rather than trusting the old status tag: (1) `guest_benefits`/`menu` were already being persisted by `adminUpsertCocotenShop` (fixed in an earlier, unrelated task building `CocoTenDetailPage`) but never reachable from any admin-UI field — the backend capacity existed with zero UI wired to it; (2) `schema.md` documented `photos` as `array<string>`, but the real live FlutterFlow-side schema (`schemas.dart`) only ever declared it as a single `ImagePath` — a documentation-vs-reality drift, not a missing feature, that would have led to over-building a multi-photo gallery against a field type that doesn't exist.

**Photo upload:** new `pickAndUploadShopPhoto()` custom action, a direct copy of the already-proven `pickAndUploadImage` (image_picker + firebase_storage, already-provisioned bucket) with a shop-scoped Storage path (`cocoten_shops/shop_<timestamp>.jpg` instead of `users/$uid/...`) rather than inventing a new upload mechanism. New `storage.rules` entry for `cocoten_shops/**` mirrors `banners/**` exactly (public read, admin-only write via the same cross-service `firestore.get(...).data.role == 'admin'` check). `adminUpsertCocotenShop` extended to accept `photo_url`, written to the existing single-value `photos` field — deliberately NOT a new array field, matching what the live schema actually supports (see above).

**Genre/tag master:** a real admin-editable list added to `system_config/settings.cocoten_genres` (config.ts's `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS`, same "living config" shape as `service_areas`), seeded with the 7 values `CocomisePage`'s own guest-facing filter chips already hardcode — closing the loop an earlier task flagged ("these chip target strings were never verified against real Firestore documents"). New `adminGetCocotenGenres`/`adminUpdateCocotenGenres` (admin-only; no guest-facing wiring in this pass) plus a new `CocotenGenreMasterPage` (add/remove). `adminUpsertCocotenShop` now validates any submitted `genre` against this list server-side, rejecting unknown values with a Japanese error listing the valid set.

**Deliberate design choice, made explicit before writing any code, not discovered by trial and error:** the "structured taxonomy" requirement is enforced via SERVER-SIDE validation against the master list, not via a `DropDown`/picker widget bound to it. `project_rules.md` documents 3 separate confirmed FlutterFlow `DropDown` binding bugs in this exact codebase (`page.bindValue` silent no-op for DropDown, `patch.dropdownOptions` writing empty-string labels, `Switch`-class `initState` eager-capture issues on related widgets), and task #30's own prefecture picker already had to abandon `DropDown` for a bottom-sheet component for the same class of reason. Enforcing the taxonomy server-side gives the identical guarantee (every stored `genre` value is one of the master list's values) without touching that same risky widget surface again — the admin form stays a plain `TextField` with a live reference list of valid values shown above it.

**Design choice avoiding the argument-list/same-push race (§82's bug class):** rather than extending `callAdminUpsertCocotenShop`'s own argument list with `menu`/`guestBenefits`/`photoUrl` (which would require the freeze+`app.raw(updateCustomAction(...))` pattern and risk the exact same-push race §82 root-caused and fixed), a SEPARATE new action `callAdminUpdateCocotenShopDetails(shopId, menu, guestBenefits, photoUrl)` was added instead, calling the SAME `adminUpsertCocotenShop` backend endpoint with just those 4 fields — the backend's pre-existing "blank means unchanged" merge logic already supports this partial-update shape perfectly, with zero backend or existing-action changes needed. `callAdminUpsertCocotenShop` itself was never touched.

**UX shape, matching an established precedent rather than inventing one:** menu/guest_benefits/photo inputs are SHARED page-state fields (not per-row `TextField`s inside the `ListView.itemBuilder`) — the same pattern `ReservationOversightPage` already uses for its own reason/refund-amount fields. This project's own `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` documents that a native FlutterFlow dynamically-repeated row can't host a `TextField` at all (needs a wrapping component, since `TextField` needs a persistent per-instance controller a dynamic row can't provide) — while this DSL's own `ListView.itemBuilder` is a different (DSL-authored) mechanism, `ReservationOversightPage`'s existing shared-field precedent was followed rather than risk finding out the hard way whether the same constraint applies. Each shop row gets a "この店舗に反映" button that applies the CURRENT shared form values to that specific shop.

**Two real bugs caught and fixed before landing, both confirmed via direct compiler feedback, not assumed:**
1. **Same-push typed-SDK-reference compile error** (the by-now-familiar class from §84's `ActivityReportPage` fix): every NEW state field added in this same push (`cocotenGenresList`/`detailsMenu`/`detailsGuestBenefits`/`detailsPhotoUrl` on the already-live `CocotenManagementPage`, plus every field on the brand-new `CocotenGenreMasterPage`) was initially referenced via typed `ff.Pages.X.state.Y` — which doesn't compile locally (`dart` static analysis against the CURRENT, not-yet-regenerated typed SDK file) until AFTER a successful push regenerates it. Fixed by converting every new-field reference to raw string `State('x')`/`SetState('x', ...)`, while correctly leaving every PRE-EXISTING field reference (`newShopName`, `adminShopsList`, etc.) on its already-working typed form.
2. **`If(ActionOutput(...), then:, orElse:)` requires a BOOLEAN condition — passing a nullable `String` action output (the uploaded photo URL, or `null` on failure) fails `compileDslApp` with "Conditional execution for action is improperly set."** Caught by the push's own validation error naming the exact button (`AdminUploadShopPhotoButton`), not discovered via generated-code inspection. Fixed by inserting the same `isNonEmptyString` custom-action check the KYC page's own upload flow already uses (`pickAndUploadImage` → `isNonEmptyString` → `If(ActionOutput('...Succeeded'), ...)`) — the established, already-proven pattern for this exact "did the upload return something real" check, not a fresh invention.
3. **Also invented two DSL expressions that don't exist in this SDK (`JoinedText(...)`, `IsNotEmpty(...)`) while first drafting the page** — caught locally before ever pushing, by grepping the SDK source directly (`widgets.dart`/`references.dart`) rather than trusting the guess. Fixed: the genre-list display uses a real `app.customFunction` (`genreListDisplay`, matching every other "format this data for display" function in this codebase); the photo-preview visibility uses the already-proven `Not(Equals(State(...), ''))` idiom (confirmed via an existing live usage on `AdminReportReviewPage`), not an invented helper.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `flutterflow ai test` clean throughout. `flutterflow ai run` took 3 attempts — attempt 1 failed on the typed-SDK-reference compile error (fixed, no server round-trip needed since this failed locally before any push), attempt 2 failed the SAME push's server-side `compileDslApp` validation on the `If`-condition-type bug (a clean compile-time rejection, zero server mutation per this project's established "failing `run` == failing `validate`" guarantee), attempt 3 succeeded. `dart analyze` on every new/changed page and custom-action file: 0 errors. Confirmed via direct `generated_code` grep that `CocotenManagementPage`'s new detail form, photo upload button, and per-row "この店舗に反映" button all call the correct actions with the correct arguments, and that `CocotenGenreMasterPage`'s add/remove buttons call `callAdminAddCocotenGenre`/`callAdminRemoveCocotenGenre` correctly. Backend: `adminUpsertCocotenShop` (updated), `adminGetCocotenGenres`/`adminUpdateCocotenGenres` (new) all deployed via `firebase deploy --only functions:...,storage --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list`; `storage.rules` deployed and compiled successfully in the same pass. `schema.md`'s stale `photos: array<string>` documentation corrected to match the real single-`ImagePath` field, with a note explaining the drift so a future session doesn't get misled into over-building a multi-photo array feature against a field type that was never real.

**Disclosed, not silently dropped:** `CocoTenDetailPage` (guest-facing) does not yet DISPLAY the uploaded photo — this pass only built the admin WRITE path. `CocomisePage`'s genre filter chips remain hardcoded, not wired to the new `cocoten_genres` master list — that page's relevant block is separately flagged elsewhere in this file as too fragile to touch outside a dedicated pass, and wiring it wasn't attempted here. Venue GPS coordinates (`location`) remain unbuilt — no map/geocoding widget exists anywhere in this app, a real, standing, unchanged limitation from before this task.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/config.ts` (`SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.cocoten_genres`), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (`adminUpsertCocotenShop` extended, 2 new functions), `firebase/functions/src/index.ts` (2 new exports), `firebase/storage.rules` (`cocoten_shops/**`), `firebase/firestore/schema.md` (`photos`/`genre` field docs corrected), `dsl/edit.dart` (6 new custom actions, 1 new customFunction, 1 new page, `CocotenManagementPage` state+body extension), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§3.4 items 1/3, §3.8 item 7), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §86: Task #44 — Municipality-level service areas + GPS coordinates (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §3.8 item 5's remaining gap)

**Ask:** same governing instruction as §82-§85. Item 5's own text: prefecture-level activate/deactivate was already `[PARTIAL]`-done (`ServiceAreaPage`); "municipality-level entries + GPS coordinates remain `[MISSING]` — zero precedent anywhere in this project or the sister project."

**Research before writing any code, to avoid building something inert**: read §3.2.2/§3.3's exact text to find out WHY GPS coordinates matter here. Found the real consumer: `fetchDiscoveryCasts` (HomePage's discovery-grid custom action) already has a working 3-tier GPS fallback (device GPS → prefecture centroid → Tokyo), but the prefecture-centroid tier was a **hardcoded static Dart map compiled into the app**, not admin-editable data — its own comment explicitly flagged this as an interim choice: *"Replace this table with a real lookup once the service-area master collection exists."* This task closes exactly that gap, not just building inert admin CRUD.

**Backend**: `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.service_areas` (config.ts) extended per-entry with `lat`/`lng` (seeded from the SAME 10 coordinates already proven live in `fetchDiscoveryCasts`'s own static table — a manually-verified-good source, not a fresh guess) and an empty `municipalities: []`. New guest-callable `getServiceAreaCoordinates` (auth.ts) — deliberately a SEPARATE function from the existing `getServiceAreas` (whose `string[]`-of-prefecture-names contract is a live, working consumer, the registration-flow prefecture picker — not touched, to avoid any risk to it). New admin-gated `adminAddServiceAreaMunicipality`/`adminRemoveServiceAreaMunicipality` (admin.ts) — dedicated, VALIDATED functions (prefecture must already exist in the list, lat/lng bounds-checked to Japan's rough range 20-46°N/122-154°E) rather than routing through the generic `adminUpdateSystemConfig`, same reasoning already applied to `adminUpdateCocotenGenres` in §85: this data feeds a guest-facing ranking calculation, worth real validation.

**A real data-destruction bug found and fixed BEFORE it could ship, not after**: `ServiceAreaPage`'s existing save action (`callAdminUpdateServiceAreas`) rebuilt the `service_areas` array from scratch using only `{name, prefecture, active}` on every save — since `adminUpdateSystemConfig`'s `set(settings, {merge:true})` replaces the WHOLE array field wholesale (Firestore doesn't deep-merge array elements, already documented as a known constraint on this exact field), simply toggling one checkbox and saving would have silently wiped every prefecture's brand-new `lat`/`lng`/`municipalities` data on the very next admin save — a real, would-have-shipped regression, caught by tracing through the actual consequence of the existing code before touching anything, not discovered after the fact. Fixed via the established freeze + `app.raw(updateCustomAction(...))` pattern (code-only change, same 10-bool argument list, so no argument-list/same-push race risk per §82's own documented bug class): the action now fetches the CURRENT full array first, and only overwrites `active`/`name`/`prefecture` per entry, spreading every other existing field through unchanged.

**A second, less obvious correctness gap found while reasoning about migration safety**: `getSystemConfig()`'s existing merge logic (`{...SYSTEM_DEFAULTS, ...doc.data()}`) is a SHALLOW spread — the instant the live Firestore document has its OWN `service_areas` key (true for any project where `ServiceAreaPage` has ever been saved even once, since `active` predates `lat`/`lng`/`municipalities`), the document's array silently and completely REPLACES `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS.service_areas` in the merge, meaning the new `lat`/`lng`/`municipalities` fields would never appear for ANY consumer no matter what was seeded into `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS`, regardless of the actual current state of that document (which could not be directly inspected in this environment — no `gcloud`/service-account credential available, confirmed by checking). Rather than guess at or attempt to work around not knowing the live document's exact contents, fixed the underlying mechanism to be correct either way: new shared `backfillServiceAreas()` helper (config.ts, exported) that per-entry-matches stored `service_areas` items against `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` by `prefecture`, backfilling only genuinely-missing `lat`/`lng`/`municipalities` while always preferring the stored document's own real values when present. Applied in BOTH places that independently read `service_areas` from Firestore — `getSystemConfig()` (used by the new guest-facing `getServiceAreaCoordinates`) AND `adminGetSystemConfig`'s own separate direct raw doc read (used by the admin UI, confirmed via direct code read to be a SEPARATE, non-`getSystemConfig()`-based path that would otherwise have missed the fix entirely).

**Frontend**: `ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPage` (add/remove municipalities + lat/lng per prefecture; prefecture selection is a validated `TextField`, not a `DropDown` — same reasoning as §85's genre master), reached via a new nav button on `ServiceAreaPage`. `fetchDiscoveryCasts` (already an `app.raw(updateCustomAction(...))`-managed action from an earlier session) updated in place — code-only change, GPS-permission-request logic left completely untouched, only the fallback-coordinate-resolution block replaced: now calls `getServiceAreaCoordinates`, tries a municipality match against the guest's own `city` field first (more precise than the prefecture centroid), falls back to the prefecture's own point, falls back to Tokyo — same 3-tier shape as before, now data-driven instead of compiled-in. The Tokyo fallback is set FIRST, unconditionally, before any lookup attempt, so any failure (network, malformed response, no match) safely leaves it in place — matching the original action's own defensive style.

**Verification**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `flutterflow ai test` clean throughout. `flutterflow ai run` took 2 attempts — attempt 1 failed on the by-now-familiar same-push typed-SDK-reference validation (task #43's `CocotenGenreMasterPage` had landed in the immediately-prior push, so its fields needed converting from raw `State('x')` to typed `ff.Pages.cocotenGenreMasterPage.state.x`, unrelated to this task's own new page which correctly still used raw refs since IT was new in this same push); fixed, attempt 2 succeeded. `dart analyze` on every new/changed page and custom-action file: 0 errors. Confirmed via direct `generated_code` grep that `fetchDiscoveryCasts` now calls `getServiceAreaCoordinates` and reads `municipalities`, that `ServiceAreaPage`'s new nav button reaches `ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPageWidget`, that `ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPage`'s add/remove buttons call the correct actions, and that `callAdminUpdateServiceAreas`'s generated code shows the fetch-then-preserve fix (`adminGetSystemConfig` call + `currentAreas`/`existing` spread) in place. Deployed in two passes: the 3 new functions first, then a second pass re-deploying all 4 together once the backfill fix (found DURING this task, after the first deploy) was written — the first deploy's 3 functions were briefly live without the backfill fix for the span between the two deploy commands, closed within the same task before being reported complete.

### §87: Task #45 — Native push notifications (FCM) infrastructure (the last item of the unimplemented-features pass)

**Ask:** same governing instruction as §82-§86. From the original outreach message (never formally withdrawn, per §1.2's "as much as possible" instruction) — genuine OS-level device push, distinct from the existing `users/{uid}/notifications` IN-APP-ONLY system. §73 (2026-08-13) had deliberately scoped the admin panel's own "push-send" feature as a broadcast into that in-app system specifically because no native push infrastructure existed at the time — this task builds that missing infrastructure and closes the gap §73 itself flagged as future follow-up.

**Investigated before writing any code, not assumed**: CLAUDE.md documents `app.pushNotifications`/`Actions.triggerPushNotificationToUser` as this SDK's own native push mechanism. Traced the SDK package's actual export graph directly (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/flutterflow_ai.dart` and everything it exports) and confirmed `Actions` (the class carrying `triggerPushNotificationToUser`/`triggerPushNotificationToUsers`, defined in `src/ui/actions.dart`) is **not reachable from this project's `dsl/edit.dart`** — it's exported only via `src/internal_sdk.dart`, whose own doc comment reads "Internal FlutterFlow AI surface for package tests and maintainer tooling," and that file itself isn't re-exported anywhere in the public barrel this project actually imports. Confirmed as a genuine SDK-version gap, not a usage mistake — CLAUDE.md's own documented `Actions.*` recipient type (`FFVariable`, resolving to "a user document") also has no producing construct anywhere in this project's DSL vocabulary (no `docRef`/`GetDocument`-style value-producing expression exists), which would have been a second blocker even if the export problem weren't there.

**Architecture decision, made from first principles once the native path was ruled out**: even if `Actions.triggerPushNotificationToUser` HAD been reachable, it's a client-side action — usable only from a live, authenticated client's own action chain. A full audit of every `users/{uid}/notifications` writer in the backend (13 call sites across 6 files) found that a meaningful fraction — every Stripe webhook handler (`stripe-webhooks.ts`, 11 sites) and the scheduled `retryFailedCastTransfers` cron — are structurally server-only, with no live client present under any circumstance. A client-side-only mechanism could never have reached those regardless of the export-graph problem. Built instead: client-side `registerFcmToken` (new pub dep `firebase_messaging`, requests permission, gets the device's FCM token, saves to a new `users/{uid}.fcm_token` field) + server-side `sendPushNotification`/`admin.messaging()` (config.ts — `firebase-admin` already had this capability as a dependency, confirmed unused anywhere in the codebase before this task, zero new backend dependency). This is architecturally the MORE correct choice, not a fallback forced by the SDK gap — it works uniformly for both client-triggered and server-only events, which the native DSL path never could have.

**Client-side registration point**: wired into `MyPage`'s `ON_INIT_STATE` (not `HomePage`'s) — `HomePage`'s own `ON_INIT_STATE` chain is flagged elsewhere in this file/`dsl/edit.dart`'s own comments as delicate/risky to touch a second time; `MyPage` carries no such warning and is visited routinely by every logged-in guest/cast.
