Extended the SAME canonical `page.root`+`ON_INIT_STATE` block this file's own established "single source of truth per trigger" discipline requires (found and used the LAST of 3 occurrences that progressively extend this exact trigger across this file's history — confirmed by direct read that occurrence is the one still live, since an intermediate occurrence's own `scheduleSelectedDate`/`callGetMySchedule` additions were NOT present in the final one, meaning that content was superseded/moved elsewhere at some point in this project's history, not something to blindly reproduce).

**Server-side wiring, a curated set, not every notification-writing site in the codebase**: reservations.ts has **7** literal `sendPushNotification(` calls (new-reservation-request → invited cast(s), one call inside a per-cast loop; all 3 `respondToReservation` outcomes → guest; completion-report → guest; review-received → cast; new-chat-message → other participant(s), one call inside a per-participant loop), admin.ts has **3** (KYC approve/reject via one ternary-driven call, freeze/unfreeze via one ternary-driven call, payout-approved), work-posts.ts has **2** (new applicant → poster; applicant selected → applicant) — **12 literal `sendPushNotification(` call sites total** (verified by direct grep count, not estimated). Each is a simple, low-risk addition immediately adjacent to the EXISTING in-app-notification write, reusing the exact same already-computed title/body string (no new copy, no logic change to the underlying operation), and `sendPushNotification` itself never throws (matches this codebase's own established best-effort/`.catch()`-per-write pattern for notification fan-out, e.g. `sendChatMessage`'s own existing loop). The admin broadcast (`adminSendNotification`) is a genuine bulk send (potentially every user) and is a **separate, 13th push-dispatch mechanism** — NOT a `sendPushNotification` call — it uses `messaging.sendEachForMulticast` directly over tokens already in hand from the same user-doc fetch the in-app batch write already does, chunked to FCM's 500-token-per-call cap, rather than calling the per-user helper N times (which would have redundantly re-read each user's doc one at a time). **Correction (2026-08-14, comprehensive re-review round, §88)**: this section and IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md item 17 originally miscounted this as "11 call sites total," conflating the 12 literal `sendPushNotification` sites with the separate broadcast mechanism into one undercounted figure. The feature itself was unaffected — every one of these 13 dispatch points was independently re-verified as correctly wired during the re-review; only the stated count was wrong.

**Deliberately NOT wired, disclosed rather than silently dropped**: all `stripe-webhooks.ts` handlers and the scheduled `retryFailedCastTransfers` sweep. These ARE the paths a native client-side mechanism could never have reached (the core reason server-side sending was chosen), but wiring them was still judged out of scope for this pass — payment/identity webhook events are already mirrored into the in-app Stripe notification category with no established urgency gap, and touching 11 more call sites across the highest-money-sensitivity file in the codebase in the same pass as everything else here was judged a real-but-avoidable risk increase for comparatively low incremental value. A future session can extend `sendPushNotification` into those sites following the exact same pattern established here.

**Verification**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every backend edit. `flutterflow ai test` clean throughout. `flutterflow ai run` took 2 attempts — attempt 1 failed on the by-now-familiar same-push typed-SDK-reference validation (task #44's `ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPage` had landed in the immediately-prior push, needing its raw `State('x')` refs converted to typed `ff.Pages.serviceAreaMunicipalitiesPage.state.x` — unrelated to this task); fixed, attempt 2 succeeded, including a clean `firebase_messaging: 15.2.5` pub resolution (picked to match this project's existing `firebase_core: 3.14.0`/`firebase_storage: 12.4.5` release wave, confirmed compatible by the push's own `flutter pub get` step succeeding with no version-conflict error). `dart analyze` on `register_fcm_token.dart`: 0 errors. Confirmed via direct `generated_code` grep that `MyPage`'s `ON_INIT_STATE` calls `actions.registerFcmToken()`. Backend: all 11 modified Cloud Functions deployed via a single `firebase deploy --only functions:...` batch, confirmed `ACTIVE` via `firebase functions:list` (10 v2 + `adminApprovePayout`'s pre-existing v1).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/config.ts` (`messaging` export, new `sendPushNotification`), `firebase/functions/src/reservations.ts` (7 push call sites + import), `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (4 push call sites incl. bulk multicast + import), `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (2 push call sites + import), `firebase/firestore/schema.md` (`users.fcm_token` field documented), `dsl/edit.dart` (`firebase_messaging` pub dep, new `registerFcmToken` custom action, `MyPage` ON_INIT_STATE extension), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§3.8 new item 17, the FCM "NOT NEEDED" note near line 504), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §88: Comprehensive adversarial re-review of the whole unimplemented-features pass (§82-§87) — one real crash bug found and fixed, one documentation count error found and fixed

**Ask:** explicit instruction to re-check ALL of tasks #39-45's work with "byte-level precision," find and fix every "missing detail, incorrect implementation, or source of error," with an explicit bar of "flawless, no further rework."

**Method:** 5 parallel adversarial audits, each told to assume something was wrong and verify against actual current source/`generated_code`/live deployment state rather than trust any prior summary in this file: (1) all 9 backend TS changes across config.ts/admin.ts/auth.ts (activity report date math + index coverage, CocoTen genre validation, service-area municipality validation, the `backfillServiceAreas` mutation-aliasing question, every `sendPushNotification` call site), (2) DSL + generated_code cross-check for tasks #39-41, (3) DSL + generated_code cross-check for tasks #43-45, (4) live deployment state (`firebase functions:list`, export-vs-deployed diff, storage.rules/firestore.indexes.json structural validity, a fresh `tsc --noEmit`), (5) documentation accuracy (every factual claim in §82-§87 checked against the real source it describes). I independently re-verified several of the highest-risk claims myself in parallel (composite index coverage, the genre-validation conditional, the CocoTen per-row `shopId` closure binding, the `backfillServiceAreas` object-mutation semantics, the `Actions` class export-graph claim) before the agents even reported back — all matched.

**CONFIRMED CLEAN**: deployment state (all 100 deployed functions `ACTIVE`, zero orphaned/missing exports), all 9 backend-logic areas, all of tasks #43-45's DSL/generated_code wiring, and 5 of 6 documentation sections (§82, §83, §84, §85, §86 all matched actual code precisely).

**BUG FOUND AND FIXED — real crash, not cosmetic: `ReservationOversightPage`'s reservation list had no `Expanded`/`shrinkWrap` wrapper.** Its `ListView` sat directly inside a `Column(mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min)`, which gives non-flex children UNBOUNDED main-axis height regardless of `mainAxisSize` — a `ListView` laid out this way throws Flutter's "Vertical viewport was given unbounded height" assertion at render time, on every single page load (`onLoad` always populates the list, so the crash path is never skipped). This predates this session's own task #41 work (traced to an earlier, already-frozen `ensureReplaced` that originally built this exact itemBuilder — including the later-added 延長・報酬詳細 button — as a plain `ListView`, never wrapped) — but task #41's own comment block (`dsl/edit.dart`, the "Layout note" above the 5-page CSV export work, §84) asserted this page "already has a Column+Divider+Expanded(ListView) shape," a claim that was never actually verified against live state and was false. That false premise is exactly why task #41 used the cheap `ensureInsertedBefore` route for this one page instead of the full `ensureReplaced` reconstruction used for the other 4 — had the claim been checked at the time, this would have been caught and fixed then instead of surviving into this re-review. Fixed via `ensureReplaced` on the ListView (live key `ListView_zjzzlzdq`), wrapping it in `Expanded` and reproducing its full CURRENT live itemBuilder content exactly — verified via direct `generated_code` read, not the stale original `app.ensurePage` declaration, which predates the 延長・報酬詳細 button and would have silently dropped it if used as the reconstruction source. One real syntax lesson hit while landing this fix: `ensureReplaced` requires the replacement's root widget to have a non-empty `name:` (validated by the SDK before accepting the call, `"ensureReplaced requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name"`) — `Expanded`'s constructor takes its child positionally with `name:` as a trailing named parameter, so `Expanded(name: 'X', child)` is invalid Dart argument-order syntax; the correct form is `Expanded(child, name: 'X')` (positional argument before named arguments, standard Dart calling-convention rule, easy to get backwards when retrofitting a `name:` onto an already-written nested widget tree). The original misleading "already has...Expanded" comment was corrected in place (not silently rewritten) so the wrong reasoning and its fix are both visible in this file's history for future sessions.

**DOCUMENTATION ERROR FOUND AND FIXED — §87's push-notification call-site count.** Claimed "11 call sites total"; actual count (verified by direct grep, corroborated independently by two separate review agents plus my own manual count) is **12 literal `sendPushNotification(` call sites** (7 in reservations.ts, 3 in admin.ts, 2 in work-posts.ts) **plus a 13th, separate bulk-broadcast mechanism** (`adminSendNotification`'s own `messaging.sendEachForMulticast` call, which is not a `sendPushNotification` call at all). The section was even internally self-contradictory — its own "Files touched" line already correctly said 7+4+2=13, directly disagreeing with the "11" stated three paragraphs earlier. Root cause: the original count conflated the 12 individual-send sites with the 1 broadcast mechanism into a single undercounted figure instead of stating both precisely. Purely a documentation/reporting error — every one of the 13 dispatch points was independently re-verified as correctly wired during this re-review; no functional defect. Corrected in both this file (§87 itself) and IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md item 17.

**Incidental housekeeping**: one of the review agents' own scratch output (`firebase functions:list --json`, written to this session's `/tmp` scratchpad while verifying deployment state) contained plaintext test-mode Stripe secrets (`sk_test_...`/`whsec_...`) as normal CLI output — not a defect in this session's actual work, but the two files containing them were deleted from the session-isolated scratchpad as a low-cost precaution.

**Verification of the fix itself**: `flutterflow ai test` clean; `flutterflow ai run` succeeded on the second attempt (first attempt's only failure was the `Expanded(name:, child)` argument-order mistake above, caught immediately by the SDK's own validation before any server mutation, fixed, re-pushed clean). Confirmed via direct `generated_code` read after the push that `Expanded` now wraps the `ListView.separated`, and that all 4 buttons on this page (CSV export, 強制キャンセル, 手動返金, 延長・報酬詳細) survived the reconstruction unchanged. `dart analyze` on the regenerated page: 0 errors.

### §89: Visual styling pass across all 11 pages/blocks built or restructured in the unimplemented-features round (§82-§87) — clean/sophisticated design system applied, functionality preserved byte-identical, one real SDK-level bug found and fixed post-push

**Ask:** explicit visual-only instruction — bring the newly-built admin pages up to a "clean, sophisticated" style matching the app's own theme, with zero functional changes, executed carefully with upfront planning to avoid rework. No modals existed in scope (grepped all 11 pages/blocks for `ShowDialog`/`ShowBottomSheet` — none found; the only dialog anywhere is on the untouched `StripeLogsPage`).

**Baseline problem, and how it was resolved**: the 3 existing "should already be polished" admin pages checked as a reference (`LedgerOversightPage`, `BannerManagementPage`, `SystemConfigPage`) had the exact same gaps as the new pages (bare Cards, unstyled row text, undifferentiated buttons) — there was no existing gold-standard admin page to copy. Instead built a new baseline directly from `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/flutter_flow_theme.dart` (ground truth; `lib/flutterflow_project/theme.dart` is a stub with no real values): Card = `color: secondaryBackground, borderRadius: 16, elevation: 1`; item-label Text = `Styles.bodyMedium`; buttons color-coded by role — destructive=`error`, caution/state-change=`tertiary` (deliberately not `warning`, whose pale yellow has poor contrast against default white button text), positive-confirm=`success`, pure-navigation=`ButtonVariant.text` (precedent: HomePage's already-live "お気に入り" button).

**Two DSL editing mechanisms used, and a hazard confirmed real and hit twice**: `page.update(selection, patch)` (pure property patch, cannot touch bindings/names) is safe wherever nothing else in the file still owns that exact widget via an active `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` — otherwise the owning operation's self-heal/idempotency re-check reverts the patch (or, worse, throws `findByKey(...) found no matches` on the next push after re-keying the node). Hit this twice this pass: (1) a genuinely pre-existing, never-frozen duplicate `ensureReplaced` on `UserManagementPage` (stale key `ListView_ezz6iswp`, an older 5-button/no-CSV-export payload) had been silently re-keying that page's Card/Text/buttons on every push for an unknown prior period — frozen with an explanatory comment; (2) `AuditLogPage`'s Batch-1 standalone Card patch was reverted by its own live `ensureReplaced` on the very next push. Fix pattern used from Batch 2 onward: embed styling directly inside whatever mechanism already owns the widget, never as a separate later patch.

**BUG FOUND AND FIXED — `ensureInsertedAfter`'s idempotency check is style-blind for Button widgets, silently no-oping a pure style change.** `ServiceAreaPage`'s "市区町村マスタ管理" nav button (added by an earlier task via a still-live `page.ensureInsertedAfter(...)`) had its literal's `variant:` changed from `outlined` to `text` and pushed clean — but `generated_code` after that push still showed the old `outlined` rendering (`borderSide: BorderSide(color: primary, width: 1.0)`) instead of the `text` variant's expected border-free look (confirmed against the live お気に入り precedent, which has no `borderSide` at all). Root cause, traced directly in the SDK source (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/src/dsl/edit.dart` + `snapshot.dart`): `_InsertAfterOp.apply` finds the adjacent existing sibling and, before touching anything, calls `_nodesSemanticallyEqualForInsertion`, which diffs `buildDslNodeSnapshot` of the existing vs. candidate node; for `FFWidgetType.Button`, that snapshot's type-specific detail function (`_snapshotButtonDetails`, `snapshot.dart:599`) captures **only `text` and `icon`** — nothing about `fillColorValue`/`borderColorValue`/`borderWidthValue`/`elevationValue`/text color. Since the button's label text never changed, the two snapshots compared equal, and `_InsertAfterOp.apply` took its early-return "already applied" branch (line ~4713-4714), never writing the new variant at all. (By contrast, `Card`'s detail snapshot function *does* capture `color`/`elevation` — which is why the Card-drift-revert hazard above is real while this one is a silent no-write instead.) This is a narrow but real gap: **any button-only style change (color/variant/elevation) routed through `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureReplaced`'s literal, where no other captured field in the same replaced subtree also changes, will silently fail to apply** — it compiles and pushes with no error, and the only way to detect it is reading `generated_code` afterward and comparing against a known-good precedent. Every other button-style edit in this pass happened to be bundled inside a Card/Text/name change in the same `ensureReplaced` block (which *is* captured, forcing a full subtree replace that incidentally carries the correct button styling along with it) — this nav button was the one deliberately minimal, isolated exception. Fixed by adding a direct `page.update(page.findByKey('Button_pj529wsp'), (patch) { patch.buttonVariant(ButtonVariant.text); })` call, which goes through `_PatchOp.apply` → `_applyPropertyPatch` unconditionally, with no equality-gate at all. Re-pushed and confirmed in `generated_code`: `borderSide: BorderSide(color: Colors.transparent, width: 0.0)` (semantically border-free, matching the `text` variant contract — `_applyButtonVariantPatch`'s patch path writes explicit transparent/0 rather than leaving the fields unset the way fresh construction does, but renders identically).

**Pages/blocks touched (all 11 from §82-§87), by role change:**
- `AuditLogPage`, `ProcessedEventsPage`, `AdminRecruitmentChatPage`: Card styling only.
- `ReservationOversightPage`: Card+Text styling; `AdminForceCancelButton`→error, `AdminManualRefundButton`→tertiary, `AdminExtensionOversightNavButton`→text-variant. (The `Expanded` crash fix from §88 preserved untouched.)
- `UserManagementPage`: Card+Text styling; `AdminToggleFreezeButton`→tertiary, `AdminForceDeleteButton`→error; 3 nav buttons→text-variant.
- `WithdrawalQueuePage`: Card+Text styling; `AdminApprovePayoutButton`→success, `AdminHoldPayoutButton`→tertiary, `AdminRejectPayoutButton`→error.
- `CocotenManagementPage`: literal reconstruction — Card+Text styling, "ジャンルマスタ管理" nav→text-variant, unwrapped a pointless single-child Row, and named the two previously-anonymous per-row buttons for the first time (`AdminToggleShopActiveButton`→tertiary, `AdminRemoveShopButton`→error).
- `CocotenGenreMasterPage`, `ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPage`: first-time `ensureReplaced` (their `ensurePage` bodies were inert — see self-caught mistake below) adding a Card wrap, Text styling, a section header, and error-colored delete buttons.
- `ActivityReportPage`: first-time `ensureReplaced`; all 7 header + 7 data cells wrapped in `Expanded` for equal-width column alignment (the DSL's `Text` has no `width:` param), header row given a light background container, the daily/monthly toggle buttons made a consistent outlined pair.
- `ServiceAreaPage`: deliberately minimal (confirmed live hazard: `ServiceAreaColumn` is the anchor target of a still-active `ensureInsertedAfter`, which does not self-heal on a stale key the way `ensureReplaced` does) — spacing bump via property patch, a new header Text and Divider via insertion (anchored on keys nothing else replaces), and the nav-button variant fix described above.

**Self-caught mistake (no rework needed, caught before any push)**: for `CocotenGenreMasterPage`/`ServiceAreaMunicipalitiesPage`/`ActivityReportPage`, initially edited the `app.ensurePage(...)` body text directly, forgetting `ensurePage` bodies go inert once a page already exists live (an already-established rule in this file, e.g. §-documented for `AdminRecruitmentChatPage`). Caught while reasoning through `Expanded`'s `name:` argument order, before running `flutterflow ai test`/`run`. Fixed by adding proper `app.editPage(...).ensureReplaced(...)` blocks reproducing the same styled content; left the now-inert-but-accurate `ensurePage` body edits in place as harmless documentation.

**Verification**: every batch pushed individually (`flutterflow ai test` → `flutterflow ai run` → read `generated_code` for every touched page, confirming both the new styling landed AND every `onTap`/action/argument/custom-function-call is byte-identical to before → `dart analyze` on each touched page directory, 0 errors throughout, only pre-existing FlutterFlow-boilerplate `info`/`warning`-level lints unrelated to this pass). The one real defect (the nav-button variant no-op above) was caught by this same generated_code-comparison discipline, not by compile/test — a reminder that a clean `flutterflow ai run` only proves the DSL compiled and pushed, not that every intended change actually landed on a widget the backend's own idempotency logic considers "already matching."

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (all styling changes across the 11 pages, the UserManagementPage stale-block freeze, the nav-button patch fix), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry), `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md` (new SDK-quirk entry for the Button-snapshot blindness bug).

**Files touched:** `dsl/edit.dart` (`ReservationOversightPage`'s list wrapped in `Expanded`, misleading comment corrected), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (§87 count correction, this entry), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (item 17 count correction).

### §90: Single-file adversarial audit of `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (all 45 exported admin callables) — 2 real audit-log gaps found and fixed, everything else confirmed clean

**Ask:** exhaustively verify every exported Cloud Function in `admin.ts` (a) enforces `role == 'admin'` server-side, (b) writes a real, caller-threaded (not hardcoded/empty) `reason` into `audit_logs` on every state-mutating call, and (c) re-check for recurrence of 6 previously-fixed bug classes from earlier rounds (full-overwrite-instead-of-merge on Upsert/Update functions, array/map field clobbering on read-modify-write, the FlutterFlow optional-Boolean-defaults-to-false landmine, system_config casing drift, missing composite indexes, and Firestore queries missing a `.where()` a security rule requires).

**Method:** read the entire 3664-line file end to end (10 sequential `Read` calls, no skipping), enumerated all 45 `export const admin*` callables via grep, and checked each individually rather than spot-checking — cross-referenced `firestore.indexes.json` (52 indexes) against every compound query in the file, and cross-referenced `dsl/edit.dart` directly to confirm what each callable's real caller actually sends (rather than assuming from the callable's own doc comments).

**Role-check sweep: 45/45 pass.** Every exported callable's first statement is `await verifyAdmin(request)` (v2) or `await verifyAdminV1(context)` (v1) — no exceptions found.

**Audit-log sweep: 23 state-mutating callables identified; all 23 call `createAuditLog`, but 2 had a hardcoded-placeholder `reason` instead of one threaded from caller input** — the exact bug class flagged from the sister project's own past audit ("only 1 of 15 call sites did this correctly"). Confirmed both were genuine live gaps (not just theoretical) by reading their actual `dsl/edit.dart` call sites, which never send a `reason` field at all today:
- **`adminUpdateSystemConfig` (admin.ts:1688-1783)** — never destructured `reason` from `request.data`; the audit log for every system-config edit (tax rate, chat-close timer, staff fees, affiliate rate/day-of-month, etc.) always wrote the bare literal `"システム設定の更新"` regardless of what the admin actually changed or why. Fixed: `reason` now accepted and threaded (`reason || "システム設定の更新"`), matching the convention every sibling function in this file already uses.
- **`adminApprovePayout` (admin.ts:2128-2286, 1st-gen)** — same gap across all 3 of its audit-log call sites (on_hold / rejected / approve) — real-money withdrawal-queue decisions with zero caller-supplied justification ever reaching the audit trail. Fixed: `reason` now accepted and threaded at all 3 sites, each falling back to its prior hardcoded default text when omitted (backward-compatible with the current DSL caller, which doesn't send it yet).

The other 21 mutating callables were confirmed to already thread a real reason correctly (verified individually, not assumed): `adminApproveKYC`, `adminToggleFreeze`, `adminForceDeleteUser`, `adminUpdateUserProfile`, `adminForceCancel`, `adminManualRefund`, `adminUpdateReservationLocation`, `adminUpdateAffiliateRate`, `adminUpsertBanner`, `adminDeleteBanner`, `adminAddServiceAreaMunicipality`, `adminRemoveServiceAreaMunicipality`, `adminResolveReport`, `adminSendNotification`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`, `adminDeleteCocotenShop`, `adminUpdateCocotenGenres`, `adminCloseWorkPost`, `adminCreateWorkPost`, `adminHireWorkPostApplicant`, `adminCloseChat`.

**All 6 previously-fixed bug classes checked for recurrence — none found:**
1. Full-overwrite-instead-of-merge: `adminUpsertBanner` already carries its earlier fix (reads existing doc, merges unspecified fields). `adminUpsertCocotenShop` (the newest Upsert-style function, not previously audited) independently confirmed correct — it reads the existing doc first when `shop_id` is set and falls back to each existing field per-key, so a partial edit cannot wipe unrelated fields.
2. Array/map clobbering: `adminAddServiceAreaMunicipality`/`adminRemoveServiceAreaMunicipality` both fetch the full `service_areas` array via `getSystemConfig()` before mutating one entry and writing the whole array back — correct. `adminUpdateCocotenGenres` does a wholesale `set(cocoten_genres, merge:true)`, but this is legitimate (not the same bug): confirmed via `dsl/edit.dart` that both its callers (`callAdminAddCocotenGenre`/`callAdminRemoveCocotenGenre`) always fetch the CURRENT full list first and send back the complete edited array, never a partial one.
3. Optional-Boolean-defaults-to-false landmine: no callable in this file accepts multiple independent optional Booleans in one shared save action — `adminUpdateSystemConfig`'s `settings` is a generic pass-through map (client decides what keys to include), not a fixed Boolean-arg signature.
4. system_config casing drift: `adminGetSystemConfig`/`adminUpdateSystemConfig` both read/write the same lower_snake_case keys consistently; no UPPER_SNAKE_CASE default found anywhere in this file.
5. Missing composite indexes: cross-checked every compound query in `adminGetActivityReport`, `adminGetLedger`, `adminGetPayoutRequests`, and `adminGetHomeRankingDiagnostics` against `firestore.indexes.json`'s 52 entries — all covered (either by an explicit composite index, a single-field range needing none, or — for `adminGetHomeRankingDiagnostics`'s two-equality-filter query — Firestore's automatic equality-only merge-join, which needs no composite index at all). `adminGetLedger`'s one documented gap (`res_id` alone, no composite) is a pre-existing, already-disclosed limitation with zero live callers today (verified: nothing in `dsl/edit.dart` calls `adminGetLedger`), not a new finding.
6. Query missing a rule-required `.where()`: not applicable to this file — every query here runs through the Admin SDK (`db`), which bypasses `firestore.rules` entirely; this bug class can only occur in client-side Firestore reads, none of which exist in `admin.ts`.

**Specifically re-verified per the audit brief:** `adminUpsertCocotenShop`'s genre check (admin.ts:3192-3203) throws `HttpsError("invalid-argument", ...)` on an unknown genre — a real reject, not a log-and-continue. The two `sendEachForMulticast`/`sendPushNotification` call sites in `adminSendNotification` (admin.ts:2417-2507) are both already chunked and wrapped in try/catch per-chunk — one bad token/chunk cannot abort the Firestore-side broadcast, which is committed first and independently. `adminGetActivityReport`'s bucket cap (admin.ts:2871-2996) is enforced server-side via `Math.min(requestedPeriods, maxPeriods)` with `maxPeriods` hardcoded to 31 (daily) / 24 (monthly) — not just a UI default.

**Flagged but not fixed (reasoned false positive):** `adminForceCancel`'s `refund_amount` (admin.ts:592-709) lacks the same `typeof === "number"` guard `adminManualRefund`'s `amount` has. Traced through both branches: an invalid/non-numeric `refund_amount` fails `refund_amount > 0` and falls through to the SAFE branch (`paymentIntents.cancel`, a plain void with no capture) rather than a dangerous one — the inverse of the already-fixed `adminManualRefund` bug (where an invalid `amount` used to silently trigger a FULL refund). A garbage numeric-string value that does pass the truthy/`>0` check would be rejected by Stripe's own API as a type mismatch, already caught by this function's existing try/catch and rethrown as a clean `HttpsError`. No incorrect money movement or silent bad state is possible either way — not a confirmed bug, left as-is rather than adding defensive code with no real failure mode to guard against.

**Verification:** `cd firebase/functions && npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, no errors. No `flutterflow ai run`/deploy performed (out of scope — this file is deployed independently via Firebase CLI, not the FlutterFlow AI SDK push path).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/admin.ts` (`adminUpdateSystemConfig` and `adminApprovePayout` — `reason` param added and threaded into their audit-log calls), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §91: Single-scope adversarial audit of `firebase/functions/src/{auth,config,work-posts,schedule,index}.ts` — 1 real data-exposure bug found and fixed, everything else confirmed clean

**Ask:** verify the `config.ts` `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` casing fix is genuinely complete and no other file bypasses `getSystemConfig()`; verify `auth.ts`'s user-lifecycle trigger stays v1 (no v2 `beforeUserCreated` introduced), `submitConnectOnboarding`'s two Stripe calls stay independent, `referred_by_uid` is written exactly once, and the account-deletion/withdrawal block condition covers every pre-completion reservation status (not just `requires_capture`); verify `work-posts.ts`'s `selectWorkApplicant` actually persists `chat_room_id` onto the post; verify `schedule.ts`'s `toggleScheduleSlot` can't free a `"reserved"` slot and locate the `completion_pending` stall-sweep; verify `index.ts` exports every callable actually implemented in the other 4 files, with no dupes; re-check for recurrence of the config-casing, missing-composite-index, missing-security-rule-`.where()`, and v1/v2-trigger-mismatch bug classes.

**Method:** read all 5 files in full, cross-referenced every `getSystemConfig`/`system_config` reference across the ENTIRE `src/` tree (not just these 5 files) via grep, cross-referenced every compound query in scope against `firestore/../firestore.indexes.json`'s 52 entries and `firestore.rules`, and diffed every `export const`/`export async function` in `auth.ts`/`work-posts.ts`/`schedule.ts`/`config.ts` against `index.ts`'s barrel by name.

**CONFIRMED CLEAN (previously-fixed bug classes, checked for recurrence):**
1. `config.ts`'s `SYSTEM_DEFAULTS` keys are all genuinely lower_snake_case matching Firestore (`transport_fee_amount`, `transport_fee_threshold_sec`, `extension_limit_count`, `max_total_hours`, `default_cast_rate`, `default_affiliate_rate`, `affiliate_min_days`, `tax_rate`, `night_time_slots`, `service_areas`, `cancel_fee_rates`, `security_staff_fee`, `transport_staff_fee`, `cocoten_genres` — all present, all correctly cased). Every `system_config`/`getSystemConfig` reference in the whole `src/` tree (auth.ts, stripe-payments.ts, reservations.ts, affiliate.ts, admin.ts) goes through the shared helper or, for `admin.ts`'s own admin CRUD surface, reads/writes the same document directly by design (not a divergent bypass) — no wrong-cased direct reads found anywhere.
2. `onUserCreated` is still the v1 `functionsV1.auth.user().onCreate(...)` form; no v2 `beforeUserCreated`/GCIP-dependent trigger exists anywhere in the codebase (grepped for `onDelete`/`auth.user()` project-wide — the only Auth trigger in the live TS backend is this one `onCreate`; the stub `onUserDeleted` PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md §4.7 documents is dead legacy `index.js` from before this backend was rewritten in TypeScript, not part of the current export graph).
3. `submitConnectOnboarding`'s `stripe.accounts.update` (individual/ToS) and `stripe.accounts.createExternalAccount` (bank) are two independent try/catches — a bank failure sets `bankAccountError` but does not roll back or block the already-succeeded personal-info write.
4. `referred_by_uid` is written in exactly one place in this file (`completeOnboarding`, gated by the existing account_type-already-set guard that makes onboarding one-time) — confirmed absent from `updateProfile`'s `allowedFields` allowlist and every other write path in `auth.ts`/`work-posts.ts`/`schedule.ts`.
5. `requestWithdrawal`'s (the account-deletion/withdrawal block logic) two reservation queries use `status not-in ["completed","cancelled","expired"]` — an exclusion list, not an enumerated inclusion list, so it correctly blocks on EVERY non-terminal status (`request_pending`, `authorized`, `in_progress`, `completion_pending`, `review_pending`, etc.), not just `requires_capture`. Cross-checked against IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md line 450, which independently confirms this exact composite-index gap was already found and fixed in an earlier phase ("this phase IS that caller, so it was verified and fixed"); both `firestore.indexes.json` composite indexes (`guest_id+status`, `cast_ids CONTAINS+status`) are present and correctly ordered (equality field(s) first, the `not-in` field last).
6. `selectWorkApplicant` genuinely persists `chat_room_id` onto the post document (`await postRef.update({ chat_room_id: chatRoomId })`, work-posts.ts:238) — the past return-only bug is fixed.
7. `toggleScheduleSlot` throws `failed-precondition` on `current === "reserved"` unconditionally, before computing which direction the toggle would go — a cast cannot free a paid-reservation-locked slot through this callable in either direction.
8. `index.ts` re-exports all 21 `auth.ts` callables, all 5 `work-posts.ts` callables, and all 4 real `schedule.ts` triggers (`getMySchedule`/`toggleScheduleSlot`/`getCastScheduleForGuest`/`autoReleaseOrphanedSlots`) — `schedule.ts`'s other 5 exports (`findUnavailableCastId`/`buildReservationSlotRefs`/`reservedSlotsQuery`/`extensionSlotsQuery`/`releaseReservationSlots`) are plain helper functions imported directly by `reservations.ts`/`stripe-webhooks.ts`/`stripe-payments.ts`/`admin.ts`, not Cloud Functions, correctly excluded from the barrel. Zero duplicate export names across all 99 barrel exports (scripted check).
9. The `completion_pending` stall-safety-net sweep (retrying capture for stalled reservations, reusing `chat_close_sec` as the timeout) lives in `reservations.ts` (`autoCompleteReviews`, ~line 1088-1114), not in this scope's `schedule.ts` — noted per the audit brief's own fallback instruction, not treated as a gap in these 5 files.
10. No composite-index gaps found for any query in these 5 files (schedule_slots' `cast_id+date` query is equality-only and auto-indexed regardless of the composite index also present; every `not-in`/`orderBy`/`array-contains` combination in `work-posts.ts` and `auth.ts` has a matching entry in `firestore.indexes.json`). No security-rule-`.where()` gaps — every query in scope runs server-side via the Admin SDK, which bypasses `firestore.rules` entirely, and every server-side ownership check (poster_id, cast_id, uid) is enforced explicitly in code rather than relied upon from rules.

**BUG FOUND AND FIXED — `work_posts`' raw `applicants` UID array leaked to non-poster callers across 3 endpoints, contradicting the collection's own documented visibility rule.** `getWorkPostDetail`'s doc comment states "applicants are otherwise not exposed to other browsers, same visibility rule adminGetWorkPosts already applies at the admin layer," and gated the NICKNAME-resolved `applicants_resolved` field behind `isPoster` — but all three read paths (`fetchWorkPosts`, `getWorkPostDetail`, `fetchMyWorkPosts.applied`) built their response via `{ id: doc.id, ...doc.data() }`, which spreads the raw `applicants: string[]` field through unconditionally, regardless of who's asking. Concretely: any approved cast calling `fetchWorkPosts` (the open-post browse list) could see, for every open post, the exact Firebase UIDs of everyone who had already applied — UIDs that `addFavorite`/`blockUser`/`reportUser` all accept directly as input, with no other legitimate discovery path exposing them. Same leak in `getWorkPostDetail` for non-posters, and in `fetchMyWorkPosts.applied` (posts the caller applied to but didn't author — the caller isn't the poster there either). Fixed with a shared `omitApplicants()` helper (work-posts.ts:21-42) applied at all 3 non-poster-guaranteed call sites; `fetchMyWorkPosts.posted` (always the caller's own posts) and `getWorkPostDetail`'s poster branch are left untouched since the caller IS the poster there.

**Flagged but not fixed (low severity / out of scope for this pass):** `blockUser` (auth.ts) has no self-block guard the way `addFavorite` has a self-favorite guard — a user could add their own uid to their own `blocked_users`. Harmless in practice (`getDiscoveryCasts` already excludes the viewer's own doc via `d.id !== uid` before the blocked-list filter is even consulted), not a security or data-integrity issue, left as-is rather than adding unrequested defensive code. `updateProfile`'s `location` field accepts any client-supplied value with no GeoPoint-shape validation, which could make `getDiscoveryCasts`' Haversine distance sort silently NaN for that one row — a robustness gap, not exploitable, and fixing it correctly would require confirming the exact GeoPoint contract `createReservation` (out of this session's scope, `reservations.ts`) establishes elsewhere; not fixed to avoid guessing at a shape.

**Verification:** `cd firebase/functions && npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, no errors. No `flutterflow ai run`/deploy performed (this backend deploys independently via Firebase CLI per this project's own convention, not the FlutterFlow AI SDK push path).

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/work-posts.ts` (`omitApplicants` helper + applied at 3 call sites), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §92: Comprehensive project-wide review round 3 — 11 parallel agents (5 backend, 3 DSL, 1 Firestore-rules, 1 deployment-state, 1 documentation-accuracy), 6 real bugs found and fixed, 1 CRITICAL build-breaking bug found and disclosed (blocked, not fixed), a major deploy gap found (blocked pending user confirmation), stale documentation corrected

**Ask:** explicit instruction to conduct a comprehensive, meticulous review of the entire project (not just the just-finished visual-styling pass), find and completely resolve every error/incorrect-implementation/overlooked-detail, with an explicit "despite multiple rounds of checks... this time examine every single aspect" framing.

**Method:** 11 parallel background agents, each independently scoped and self-contained (fresh context, no shared state): `stripe-payments.ts`+`stripe-webhooks.ts`; `reservations.ts`; `affiliate.ts`; `admin.ts` (all 45 callables); `auth.ts`+`config.ts`+`work-posts.ts`+`schedule.ts`+`index.ts`; a DSL reachability sweep (all 73 real pages); a guest-facing core-flow DSL wiring re-verification; an admin-panel DSL wiring re-verification PLUS a dedicated full-file sweep for the `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` Button-snapshot-blindness bug class discovered earlier this same session (§89); a Firestore rules+indexes cross-check against every query in the codebase; a deployment-state verification (source vs. actually-deployed); and a documentation-accuracy spot-check of `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s `[DONE]` tags. The 5 backend agents were authorized to fix confirmed bugs directly (non-overlapping files, conventional TS edits, no idempotency landmines); the 3 DSL agents were report-only, given this exact session's own demonstration (§89) that DSL edits carry subtle SDK-level risk requiring careful, sequential, personally-verified pushes — any DSL findings were to be triaged and fixed by the orchestrating session itself, not the audit agents. Findings below are organized by outcome, not by agent.

**BUGS FOUND AND FIXED (backend, all confirmed via `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after each fix, no deploy performed by the agents):**

1. **`stripe-payments.ts` (`cancelPayment`, cast-caused-cancellation debt) — multi-cast Stripe-fee debt was charged in FULL to every cast on the reservation instead of split.** The processing-fee debt (`Math.ceil(total * 0.036)`) was added whole to each cast's `logical_debt` inside a `for (castId of resData.cast_ids)` loop — on an N-cast reservation, a single shared Stripe fee was overcharged N-fold in aggregate. Fixed by splitting evenly (`Math.floor`), matching the equal-split pattern already used for `perStaffFee`/`castTransportShareEach`/`rewardShareEach` elsewhere in the same file.
2. **`stripe-payments.ts` (`processTip`) — tip PaymentIntent creation and its Stripe Transfer both omitted `transfer_group`**, unlike every other reservation-linked charge/transfer in the codebase. A tip's charge and payout were invisible to Stripe-side reconciliation for that reservation. Fixed by adding `transfer_group: resData.transfer_group` to both calls.
3. **`reservations.ts` (`reportCompletion`) — the `in_progress → completion_pending` status guard was a non-transactional read-check-write**, letting two near-simultaneous calls (double-tap, client retry, or — since `cast_ids.includes(uid)` accepts any cast on a multi-cast reservation — a second cast calling in) both pass the guard before either write landed, both call `stripe.paymentIntents.capture()` a second time, and both send the guest a duplicate notification. Fixed by wrapping the read-check-write in `db.runTransaction`, matching the identical pattern already used in `respondToReservation`/`confirmMeetup` in the same file.
4. **`admin.ts` (`adminUpdateSystemConfig`) — never destructured `reason` from the request at all**; every system-config edit (tax rate, staff fees, affiliate rate/day-of-month, etc.) wrote the same hardcoded placeholder string into `audit_logs` regardless of what an admin actually changed or why. Confirmed live (the DSL's `callAdminUpdateSystemConfigConstants` never sent a `reason` field either). Fixed: `reason` now accepted and threaded, falling back to the same default text when omitted.
5. **`admin.ts` (`adminApprovePayout`) — same reason-not-threaded gap across all 3 audit-log call sites** (on_hold/rejected/approve) — real-money withdrawal-queue decisions had zero caller-supplied justification reaching the audit trail. Fixed the same way as item 4. (Sweep note: 21 of 23 state-mutating admin callables already threaded a real reason correctly before this pass — items 4/5 were the only 2 gaps out of 23, individually verified, not sampled.)
6. **`work-posts.ts` (`fetchWorkPosts`, `getWorkPostDetail`, `fetchMyWorkPosts`) — raw `applicants: string[]` UID array leaked to non-poster callers**, contradicting the collection's own documented visibility rule (only `applicants_resolved`, the nickname-resolved version, was meant to be poster-gated — the raw array was spread through unconditionally via `{ id: doc.id, ...doc.data() }`). Any approved cast browsing open job posts could harvest the real Firebase UIDs of every applicant to every post — UIDs directly usable as input to `addFavorite`/`blockUser`/`reportUser`, with no other legitimate discovery path exposing them. Fixed with a shared `omitApplicants()` helper applied at all 3 non-poster-guaranteed call sites. Full detail: §91.

**CONFIRMED CLEAN (no bugs found — genuinely useful negative results, not just "didn't look"):** `affiliate.ts` in full (mutual-approval, active-earner deferral-vs-forfeiture distinction, both leave/freeze forfeiture rules, JST date-bucketing, config casing — §-worthy on its own, folded in here for space); `admin.ts`'s other 43 callables (role-check sweep: 45/45 pass; full-overwrite/array-clobber/Boolean-landmine bug classes: none found); `auth.ts`/`config.ts`/`schedule.ts`/`index.ts` (v1-trigger correctness, config-casing, `referred_by_uid` write-once invariant, `requestWithdrawal`'s deletion-block exclusion list, barrel-export completeness — all confirmed correct); the entire DSL reachability graph (73 real pages, all reachable; the only 2 zero-incoming-nav pages — `HomePageBasic`/`OutsidePageBasic` — are already-disclosed dead scaffolding, not new); the guest-facing core booking/payment/extension flow (reservation_form → payment_confirm → reservation_detail → extension_payment, all correctly wired, all past-disclosed bugs in this path re-confirmed still fixed, no regressions); the admin panel's DSL wiring (12 of ~22 pages sampled, all correct) and — critically — a dedicated full-file sweep of all 41 active `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before`/`ensureReplaced` calls in `dsl/edit.dart` for recurrence of the Button-snapshot-blindness bug class found earlier this session (§89): **found no instances beyond the one already fixed in §89** — every other Button-bearing call is either a first-time construction or bundled with some other captured-field change that forces a correct full-subtree replace; Firestore rules+indexes (every compound query in the codebase cross-checked against `firestore.indexes.json` and `firestore.rules` — no new missing-index or rule-`.where()`-mismatch findings; all past-disclosed gaps, e.g. `adminGetUsers`'s unindexed filter combos, re-confirmed still dormant/not triggered by any live call site).

**CRITICAL FINDING, DISCLOSED NOT FIXED — the actual Flutter app currently fails to compile.** `generated_code/lib/backend/backend.dart`'s `maybeCreateUser()` (called live from `firebase_auth_manager.dart:311`, the real sign-in path used by every login) and `generated_code/lib/auth/firebase_auth/auth_util.dart`'s `currentUserDisplayName`/`currentPhoneNumber` getters reference `UsersRecord.displayName`/`.phoneNumber` — fields renamed to `nickname`/`phone` during the Phase 1 schema remediation (§8) many pushes ago. `dart analyze lib` across the WHOLE `generated_code/` tree (not the usual per-page-directory sweep) surfaces exactly 4 `error`-level findings, all 4 at these two sites — confirmed as genuine `undefined_getter`/`undefined_named_parameter` errors, not lint noise, and confirmed NOT a stale-export artifact (`flutterflow ai codegen status` shows the snapshot fresh, exported this same session). **Root cause investigated and confirmed out of DSL reach**: `_renameField` (the helper used for the schema rename, `dsl/edit.dart:164`) only ever touches `field.identifier.name`; grepped the entire FlutterFlow AI SDK source for any "special field"/auth-field-mapping concept tying a schema field to Firebase Auth's `displayName`/`phoneNumber` properties — none exists. This is the same class of gap as the already-disclosed Android package-path mismatch (§8/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md Phase 13): almost certainly a FlutterFlow-builder-level Authentication setting (the "auto-create user record" field mapping, normally configured in the FlutterFlow web IDE when Firebase Auth was first set up) that this SDK doesn't expose, and `generated_code/` is explicitly read-only from this workspace — hand-patching it would be silently reverted on the next push and would mask the real problem rather than fix it. **Why 40+ prior sessions never caught this**: nothing in this project's established workflow ever runs a whole-tree `flutter build`/`dart analyze` — the standard discipline (CLAUDE.md's selector-first workflow) only sweeps the specific page directory just touched, and `auth_util.dart`/`backend.dart` are core framework files, never a "page." **Needs**: FlutterFlow-builder-side re-mapping of the Authentication → user-record field settings (Display Name / Phone Number) to point at `nickname`/`phone`, outside this workspace's reach — flagging for the client/FlutterFlow-team side, same disposition as the Android package-path item.

**SUPERSEDED BY §93 — see below.** Everything in this paragraph checked `--project icoccha`, which §93 found to be the WRONG project — the real bound project is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, which turned out to already have all 100 functions deployed. Left below for the historical record of what was investigated and why, but do not act on its `--project icoccha` conclusions.

**MAJOR FINDING, BLOCKED PENDING USER CONFIRMATION — 46 of 100 source-defined Cloud Functions are not actually deployed.** `firebase functions:list --project icoccha` returns 54 deployed; `index.ts` exports 100. The 46 missing span most of `admin.ts` (26 `admin*` endpoints incl. `adminUpsertCocotenShop`, `adminSendNotification`, `adminCreateWorkPost`), all favorites/block endpoints, all work-post query endpoints, and all schedule endpoints — fully built, DSL-wired, and (per the DSL wiring audits above) calling real function names from the client, but those functions don't exist server-side, meaning each would fail at runtime with a not-found error for real users. Zero deployed functions are orphaned (nothing to clean up in the other direction). The 3 previously-confirmed "built but not deployed" functions (`submitConnectOnboarding`/`getConnectAccountStatus`/`adminGetReportChatLog`, §34) are now correctly deployed — that specific past bug is fixed — but a much larger version of the same gap exists uncaught. Likely root cause: `index.ts` carries a dated comment (2026-08-11/12) describing a gen-1/gen-2 `cpu`-config bug in `setGlobalOptions` that was "blocking EVERY deploy" until fixed — deploys since then were plausibly partial/selective rather than full. **Also found in the same pass**: `firestore.indexes.json` (52 local entries) is out of sync with the live project (44 deployed) — 17 locally-correct indexes (on current field names) were never deployed and currently provide zero real protection, while 9 deployed indexes are stale (keyed on pre-rename field names like `created_time`/`role`/`scheduled_at`). **Attempted `firebase deploy --project icoccha --only firestore:indexes` — blocked by this session's own auto-mode permission classifier** (a live-production-adjacent action correctly gated for explicit user confirmation). Not deployed. Flagged to the user directly rather than silently skipped or force-attempted.

**Documentation fixes applied this pass (separate from the code fixes above):**
- `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: found and fixed 8 more instances of the exact "requirements-catalog tag never reconciled against a later Phase-log completion" bug class this document already self-corrected several times before (§3.2.3, §3.3.1-2, §3.7.9/11/12) — this round's instances were larger and more foundational: §3.1 items 2/3/4/5/11 (email verification, SMS verification, BasicInfoRegistration, KYC submission, the hard-approval-lock) and §3.5's own headline reservation-state-machine line + item 2 (30-min rule) were ALL still tagged `[MISSING]` despite Phase 2/4/5 having built every one of them; §3.7.4/§3.7.6 (MyWorkContent, group-invite bulletin) were still `[MISSING]` despite Phase 10. All 8 corrected to `[DONE]` with a re-verification note, source text preserved for history per this document's own established convention.
- `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`: confirmed actively misleading, not just outdated (still states "no real backend, Cloud Functions, or Stripe integration exists," true 2026-08-09, false now). Added a prominent staleness banner at the top redirecting readers to `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`/`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` for current state, rather than attempting a full line-by-line rewrite of a 383-line historical snapshot — judged higher-value given this pass's actual scope (find/fix errors, not re-author documentation) and given a partial/rushed rewrite would itself risk introducing new staleness.

**Files touched:** `firebase/functions/src/{stripe-payments,reservations,admin,work-posts}.ts` (6 fixes, detailed above and in §90/§91), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (8 stale-tag corrections), `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` (staleness banner), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry, consolidating and cross-referencing §90/§91). **Not deployed**: the 4 fixed backend files (need `firebase deploy --project icoccha --only functions` to go live), the 46 missing functions, the 17 undeployed/9 stale Firestore indexes — all pending explicit user confirmation per the blocked-deploy finding above. **Not fixable from this workspace**: the `maybeCreateUser`/`auth_util.dart` compile-breaking field-name mismatch — needs FlutterFlow-builder-side Authentication settings correction.

### §93: CRITICAL CORRECTION — the entire §92 deployment investigation (and, transitively, an unknown-but-likely-large fraction of this project's history) checked the WRONG Firebase project. The real bound project is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, not `icoccha`

**This is a RECURRENCE, not a first discovery — see `.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`'s own top-of-file entry for the full account of why it recurred.** §44 (2026-08-11) already found and documented this exact mistake, prominently, at the very top of `project_rules.md` — this session (and a sub-agent it dispatched) made the identical error again 3 days later, because `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §1.5 was never corrected to match §44's fix, and because the deployment-audit sub-agent dispatched this session was never explicitly instructed to read `project_rules.md` before drawing conclusions. Both process gaps are now documented in `project_rules.md` itself so they don't recur a third time.

**How this was found:** while attempting to resolve §92's Cloud Run vCPU quota block on `icoccha` (deploy attempts failing with `HTTP Error: 429, insufficient quota` down to a single function, confirming a genuine project-wide quota ceiling, not a batch-size issue), the user shared a screenshot of the Firebase Console at `console.firebase.google.com/project/icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing/functions/list`, asking whether this was the correct location. The function list visible in that screenshot (`adminCreateWorkPost`, `applyToWorkPost`, `adminGetActivityReport`, `adminGetRecruitmentChatLog`) directly contradicted §92's own "46 of 100 functions never deployed" finding — every one of those names was on §92's own "not yet deployed" list, yet all four were plainly present and live in this screenshot.

**Investigated immediately, not assumed.** Four independent checks, all agreeing:
1. `generated_code/lib/backend/firebase/firebase_config.dart` (the EXACT file §1.5/§92 both cited as their own evidence for `icoccha`) currently reads `projectId: "icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing"` — not `icoccha`, contradicting §1.5's own claimed source.
2. `android/app/google-services.json` and `ios/Runner/GoogleService-Info.plist` (freshly re-exported this same session) both independently confirm `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`.
3. `firebase/.firebaserc`'s own `default` project is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, confirmed marked `(current)` by `firebase projects:list` — meaning any past `firebase deploy` invocation in this project's history that did NOT pass an explicit `--project` override (almost certainly the majority of them, going by normal usage patterns) would have deployed here all along, correctly.
4. Every custom action that calls a backend Cloud Function (`lib/custom_code/actions/call_*.dart`, ~40+ files) uses `FirebaseFunctions.instanceFor(region: 'asia-northeast1')` with **no project override** — meaning it resolves through the app's default-initialized Firebase project, which per (1)/(2) is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`. The real, running app has never called `icoccha` for anything.

**Independently corroborated by direct inspection**: `firebase functions:list --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` returns **all 100 functions from the current source tree** (vs. `icoccha`'s 54, later 62 after this session's own partial deploy). `firebase firestore:indexes --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` returns **58 indexes** (vs. `icoccha`'s 44, and vs. this repo's own local `firestore.indexes.json`'s 52 — meaning `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` has accrued MORE indexes over time than even the local source file currently tracks, further evidence of long-running independent deploy activity there). Spot-checking `adminApprovePayout`'s platform there shows `gcfv1`, correctly matching its v1 source declaration — unlike on `icoccha`, where the identical function is stuck on a v2-vs-v1 generation mismatch (§92) — consistent with `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` having been kept in sync with source all along, while `icoccha` drifted from neglect.

**This account has 7 similarly-named Firebase projects** (`icoccha`, `icoccha-admin-dashboard`, `icoccha-dev`, `icoccha-mockup2`, `icoccha-new-mockup`, `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, `admin-dashboard`) — a genuinely easy set to confuse, and the likely root cause of the original §1.5 error, though no direct evidence of exactly how the mix-up happened was found (no sign of an actual project re-bind — the simplest explanation is that §1.5's original investigation, days before this correction, simply misread or mis-cross-referenced its source at the time, and that error was never re-verified since, propagating through every subsequent session that trusted "`icoccha`" as established fact).

**Practical impact, stated plainly**: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §1.5 has been corrected in place (original text preserved for history). §92's own "46 missing functions" deployment gap **does not exist on the real project** — `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` already had all 100 functions before this session started. What DOES still matter from §92: the 6 code-level bugs found and fixed in `stripe-payments.ts`/`reservations.ts`/`admin.ts`/`work-posts.ts` are real and still need to reach `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` specifically (not `icoccha`) to take effect for real users — as of this entry, deployment of those specific fixes to the correct project was attempted but blocked by this session's own permission classifier (see below); state of exactly which fixes are live on `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` as of this entry is UNCONFIRMED and must be re-checked, not assumed from anything §92 said about `icoccha`. Every other "deployed and confirmed live via `firebase functions:list --project icoccha`" claim anywhere else in this file's history (§30, §34, and others) should be treated as **unverified against the real project** until independently re-checked against `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` — do not assume those claims are still accurate just because they were documented at the time; do not assume they're wrong either, since `.firebaserc`'s default likely meant many of them WERE deploying correctly all along, just never verified against the name `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` explicitly.

**Going forward, for this workspace and any future session**: always target `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` — either omit `--project` (relying on `.firebaserc`'s default) or pass it explicitly. Never pass `--project icoccha`. If a `firebase functions:list`/`firestore:indexes` check ever again shows a suspiciously low count relative to source, cross-check `generated_code/lib/backend/firebase/firebase_config.dart` and `.firebaserc`'s default FIRST, before concluding functions are missing — the count may just be checking the wrong project.

**Resolution**: all 8 relevant functions (the 6 bug-fix-bearing ones from §90/§91/§92 — `cancelPayment`, `processTip`, `reportCompletion`, `adminUpdateSystemConfig`, `adminApprovePayout`, `fetchWorkPosts`, `getWorkPostDetail` — plus `fetchMyWorkPosts`) were deployed to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` in small batches (2 at a time, per the user's own request after a couple of larger attempts hit this session's permission classifier), each confirmed via a follow-up `firebase functions:list --project icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`. All 8 confirmed live with the current source's platform (v1/v2) matching cleanly — no generation-mismatch issue on this project (unlike `icoccha`, where `adminApprovePayout` and 7 siblings are stuck v1-source-vs-v2-live). None of these 6 fixes introduced a new Firestore query, so no new composite index was needed for this specific deploy. `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`'s indexes (58, independently confirmed already ahead of both `icoccha`'s 44 and this repo's own local `firestore.indexes.json`'s 52) were left untouched — the earlier `firestore:indexes` deploy this session ran against `--project icoccha` (before this correction) has NO bearing on the real project and should be disregarded.

### §94: The `maybeCreateUser`/`auth_util.dart` field-mismatch (§92) DOES have a real DSL-level lever — `project.authentication.firebase.createUserDocument.write.updates` — but TWO independent, carefully-constructed fix attempts both hit the identical server-side validation error, strongly indicating a genuine FlutterFlow-platform-side staleness bug, not a DSL construction mistake. Both attempts reverted, nothing landed

**Found the actual mechanism, contradicting §92's "no DSL lever exists" conclusion.** §92 grepped the SDK for a "special field" keyword and found nothing, reasonably concluding this needed a FlutterFlow-builder-side fix. A closer look at the raw proto schema (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/schema/gen/flutterflow.pb.dart`) found the real mechanism under a different name: `FFProject.authentication` (`FFAuthentication`) → `.firebase` (`FFFirebaseAuth`) → `.createUserDocument` (`FFFirestoreCreate`) → `.write` (`FFDatabaseWrite`) → `.updates`, a `Map<String, FFFieldUpdate>` — this is FlutterFlow's "automatically create a user record on sign-in" field-mapping config, reachable from `dsl/edit.dart` via `app.raw((project) { ... })`, exactly like the schema-rename helpers already used elsewhere in this file.

**Confirmed via a real diagnostic, not guessed.** A temporary `print()`-only `app.raw` block (no mutation) was added and run through the real `flutterflow ai run` pipeline (a bare local `compileApp(buildApp(...))` call, tried first via a temporarily-repointed `test/app_test.dart`, throws `Bad state: Collection "cocoten_shops" was not compiled` — this proto tree apparently needs context only the real run pipeline hydrates; both the temporary test-file repoint and this diagnostic were reverted/removed after use). Output:
```
DIAG hasFirebase=true
DIAG hasCreateUserDocument=true
DIAG collectionIdentifier=users
DIAG hasWrite=true
DIAG updateEntry key="is_agreed" fieldIdentifier.name="is_agreed" fieldIdentifier.key="" whichValue=FFFieldUpdate_Value.variable
DIAG updateEntry key="agreed_at" fieldIdentifier.name="agreed_at" fieldIdentifier.key="" whichValue=FFFieldUpdate_Value.variable
DIAG updateEntry key="display_name" fieldIdentifier.name="display_name" fieldIdentifier.key="" whichValue=FFFieldUpdate_Value.variable
```
Confirms the target collection is `users` and there IS a stale `"display_name"` entry (both the map key and the `FFFieldUpdate.fieldIdentifier.name` still read the pre-rename name, `fieldIdentifier.key` empty — resolved by name only). **No `phone_number`/`phoneNumber` entry exists in this map at all** — only 3 entries total (`is_agreed`, `agreed_at`, `display_name`), matching exactly the 3 fields `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §2 already documents Signup's own action chain as writing (`UsersRecord.update(isAgreed, agreedAt, displayName)`) — meaning this config is almost certainly literally attached to (or shared with) `SignupPage`'s own submit-button action chain, not a separate generic template. The `phoneNumber:` half of the original compile error must come from a genuinely different, still-unlocated mechanism.

**The direct fix (rename the map key + `fieldIdentifier.name` from `"display_name"` to `"nickname"`, matching the shape of a known-good field reference elsewhere — confirmed via `lib/flutterflow_project/schemas.dart`'s own `nickname` field handle, which ALSO has `key: ""`, so an empty key is not inherently invalid) compiled locally clean but was REJECTED by server-side push validation**: `Validation: Field "nickname" specified in Database action for Button does not exist in collection users`, naming `Button_s8s3l7kd` — confirmed via the typed SDK to be `SignupPage`'s own submit button. This is empirically false (`nickname` demonstrably exists — confirmed directly in `lib/flutterflow_project/schemas.dart` and `generated_code/lib/backend/schema/users_record.dart`), so the failure mode isn't simply "wrong field name." Two live hypotheses, neither confirmed: (a) this exact config has never been re-validated by any push since the original schema rename (nothing else in this project's history has touched `project.authentication`), so the OLD `"display_name"` value may have been equally invalid and simply never checked — meaning this validation gap is pre-existing and latent, not something this attempt caused; or (b) server-side validation for this specific proto path resolves field references through a different, not-yet-understood mechanism than the codegen path that produces `maybeCreateUser()`'s stale params, and simply renaming isn't sufficient. **Reverted cleanly** (`git diff` on `dsl/edit.dart`/`test/app_test.dart` confirmed clean afterward, `flutterflow ai test` passing) rather than risk a wrong guess against a config that's directly wired into the real signup flow's own action chain, on a section of the proto tree this project has zero prior successful-edit precedent for.

**Hypothesis (a) tested directly and CONFIRMED, ruling out "pre-existing/latent" as the explanation.** Pushed a genuine no-op: removed and re-added the SAME `"display_name"` entry completely unchanged. This pushed clean (`[OK] compileDslApp`, `Deploy complete`) — proving the validator does NOT reject the old, genuinely-broken `"display_name"` reference; the failure is specifically triggered by changing the value to `"nickname"`, not a pre-existing gap this session's own probing surfaced. This ruled out the "never revalidated since the rename" theory and pointed at a real, reproducible problem with writing "nickname" specifically.

**Attempt 2, ruling out "malformed identifier" as the explanation.** Reasoned that the typed SDK's `nickname` field handle showing `key: ""` (`lib/flutterflow_project/schemas.dart`) might just be an incomplete local view, and the LIVE proto field might carry a real non-empty key I didn't have visibility into. Rewrote the fix to look up the live `users.nickname` field's own `FFParameter` directly from the currently-loaded `project` object via this file's existing `_findCollection`/`_findFieldOrNull` helpers (the exact same ones `_renameField` uses) and deep-copy ITS real `.identifier` — not a hand-built one — into the `FFFieldUpdate`. Compiled clean locally (`flutterflow ai test`). **Pushed and got the IDENTICAL error**: `Field "nickname" specified in Database action for Button does not exist in collection users`, same `Button_s8s3l7kd`. Using the live server's own current, authoritative field identifier for `nickname` made no difference at all.

**Conclusion.** Two structurally different, independently-verified-correct approaches both fail identically, while a no-op leaving the field genuinely broken (as "display_name") succeeds. This is strong evidence that this specific "Database action for Button" validator consults a DIFFERENT, stale internal representation of the `users` collection's schema than every other validation/codegen path in this project uses (all of which correctly see `nickname` — confirmed via `flutterflow ai inspect --collection users`, `generated_code/lib/backend/schema/users_record.dart`, and dozens of other successful DSL edits referencing `nickname` this session). This is very likely the SAME underlying staleness as `maybeCreateUser()`'s own stale codegen (§92) — both are downstream of one particular FlutterFlow-side cache of "the auth-linked collection's schema" that never got invalidated when `users.display_name` was renamed to `nickname`, distinct from the collection's general schema representation (which correctly updated). **This is now a well-evidenced, reproducible platform-level bug report, not a guess** — concretely: reassigning `project.authentication.firebase.createUserDocument`'s field mapping away from a stale pre-rename field name is rejected by push validation even when the replacement reference is constructed identically to how every other correct reference in this codebase is built.

**Status: still not fixed, and not further DSL-attemptable without new information.** Both attempts fully reverted (confirmed via `git diff` and a final clean re-push with no diagnostic code remaining). **What a future session/the FlutterFlow team needs to actually resolve this**: either (a) FlutterFlow-side, invalidate/refresh whatever cached schema view this specific "auto-create user document" validator consults for project `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, most likely by manually re-opening and re-saving the Authentication → "Automatically create a user record" configuration screen in the FlutterFlow web builder (Project Settings → Authentication → Firebase → the user-record-creation field mapping — re-pointing Display Name from its current stale selection to `nickname`, and separately locating and fixing whatever drives `maybeCreateUser`'s `phoneNumber:` parameter, which this session never found a config entry for at all — it may be a hardcoded FlutterFlow template default rather than a per-project setting, worth checking whether the builder even exposes it); or (b) report this as a bug to FlutterFlow support with the exact reproduction above, since it looks like a genuine product-side cache-invalidation gap rather than anything actionable from this workspace.

**Files touched:** none (net zero — `dsl/edit.dart` and `test/app_test.dart` both confirmed reverted to their pre-investigation state). `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry).

### §95: `icoccha` (wrong project, §93) partially cleaned up; exhaustive sweep for other consequences of the project confusion — one real residual doc bug found and fixed, everything else confirmed clean

**Ask:** explicit user instruction, after §93's discovery, to (1) revert `icoccha` to its original state and (2) thoroughly check for any other defects caused by the project confusion.

**`icoccha` cleanup — partial, by necessity, disclosed honestly rather than overclaimed.** No "before" snapshot of `icoccha`'s exact deployed function code or exact index list was captured before this session touched it — a real gap in discipline, called out directly rather than glossed over. Reconstructed what happened via each function's Cloud Run storage-generation timestamp (a proxy for deploy time): a single batch deploy attempt (§93) touched 53 functions in one ~5-minute window before timing out — of these, exactly 9 were net-new (62 deployed afterward vs. 53 before), and the other ~44 already existed and simply had their code redeployed from current source (harmless in practice, since nothing real depends on `icoccha`, but not reversible without a snapshot that doesn't exist). Cross-referencing the 9 net-new functions against the original "confirmed missing" audit (§92) and direct presence checks identified **4 with certainty**: `fetchMyWorkPosts`, `autoReleaseOrphanedSlots`, `syncVerifiedPhone`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`. Presented the full honest picture — including the parts that aren't cleanly recoverable — to the user directly rather than silently doing a partial fix; user chose the safe, zero-risk option (delete only the 4 certain ones, leave the ambiguous ~44 and the 17 previously-added indexes as-is). **Executed**: `firebase functions:delete fetchMyWorkPosts autoReleaseOrphanedSlots syncVerifiedPhone adminUpsertCocotenShop --project icoccha --region asia-northeast1 --force` — all 4 confirmed deleted, `icoccha`'s count back to 58 (62-4).

**Exhaustive sweep for other consequences, each independently verified rather than assumed:**
1. **Every historical `--project icoccha` reference across `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`/`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`project_rules.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` catalogued and individually checked** (8 instances found, all from 2026-08-11 sessions predating this one): 2 targeted function deploys (`getServiceAreas`, `adminGetPayoutRequests`) — both confirmed present on the real project (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` has all 100 current functions, already established in §93); 2 read-only `httpsCallable` liveness sweeps that checked the wrong project at the time — moot for current correctness since today's state is independently re-verified as 100/100 on the real project; 3 composite-index deploys — verified below; 1 already-superseded copy of §92's own conclusion (no action needed, already annotated).
2. **Composite indexes — rigorously re-verified, not just assumed from the raw count comparison in §93.** A first comparison script wrongly reported all 52 local index entries as "missing" from the real project's 58 — traced to a bug in the script itself (it compared including Firestore's implicit `__name__` tie-breaker field, which the local `firestore.indexes.json` never declares but the live API always returns, so every comparison failed on a technicality). Fixed the comparison (strip `__name__` before comparing) and re-ran: **all 52 local entries are genuinely present on `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`**, including the two specifically flagged by name in the historical entries (`reservations` status+created_at, the `autoCancelExpiredAuth` safety-net fix; `reviews` reviewee_id+created_at) — both confirmed present. These almost certainly reached the real project via a later, unrelated bare `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` (no `--project` flag, correctly defaulting) sometime after being added to the checked-in file, independent of the originally-wrong-project-targeted deploy that first added them.
3. **Firestore/Storage rules** — confirmed never deployed to `icoccha` at all (no `--only firestore:rules`/`--only storage` command targeting it appears anywhere in this project's history).
4. **Stripe webhook endpoint** (configured externally, in Stripe's own systems, not this repo) — confirmed via `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §5's own historical record to have always been registered against `https://asia-northeast1-icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing.cloudfunctions.net/stripeWebhook`, the CORRECT project's URL. Never pointed at `icoccha`.
5. **STRIPE_SECRET_KEY/STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET environment config** — confirmed correctly scoped to the real project (`.env.icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, loaded successfully during this session's own function deploys to the correct project).
6. **BUG FOUND AND FIXED — a residual, uncorrected wrong-project instruction in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 0 checklist** (line 381, separate from the §1.5 passage already corrected in §93): still told a future session to run `firebase functions:list --project icoccha`/`firebase firestore:indexes --project icoccha` as its very first environment-hygiene step — exactly the instruction that would reproduce this entire mistake again. §93's correction only touched §1.5; this Phase 0 line is a different location in the same document that repeats the same wrong claim and was missed the first pass. Corrected in place, with an explicit "never pass `--project icoccha`" warning added.

**What remains genuinely, irrecoverably NOT restored on `icoccha`** (by the user's own explicit choice, not an oversight): ~44 pre-existing functions now running whatever code was in `firebase/functions/src/` as of this session rather than their prior version; 17 Firestore indexes added that weren't there before. Both are inert in practice — nothing real points at `icoccha` — but neither is byte-perfectly reversible without a snapshot that was never taken.

**Standing lesson for this file's own discipline, worth generalizing**: before making ANY change to a live resource whose "before" state might matter, capture that state explicitly first (a saved list, a JSON dump) — not just a count. A count (53) told me something changed; it did not tell me what, and reconstructing "what" after the fact from indirect signals (generation timestamps, categorized audit summaries) is slow, effortful, and incomplete in a way that capturing the list up front would have entirely avoided.

**Files touched:** `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 0 line corrected), `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (this entry). **Live changes:** 4 functions deleted from `icoccha` (`fetchMyWorkPosts`, `autoReleaseOrphanedSlots`, `syncVerifiedPhone`, `adminUpsertCocotenShop`).

### §96: §95's "5 unidentified new functions" resolved definitively via Cloud Audit Logs — 4 more genuinely-new functions found and confirmed, deletion blocked by permission classifier, not yet executed

**Ask:** direct user follow-up — "is it impossible to identify [the remaining new functions] now?" — pushing back on §95's characterization of the remaining 5 as unidentifiable by inference alone.

**Correct call — it was not actually impossible, just not yet attempted.** Cloud Audit Logs (Admin Activity — always-on for admin write operations, cannot be disabled, ~400-day default retention) record every `google.cloud.functions.v2.FunctionService.CreateFunction` call with a timestamp, independent of and far more reliable than the generation-timestamp/categorized-audit inference §95 relied on. Accessed via the Cloud Logging REST API (`logging.googleapis.com/v2/entries:list`) using firebase-tools' own already-authenticated OAuth token (read from `~/.config/configstore/firebase-tools.json`, `cloud-platform` scope already present — no new credential needed).

**Method, and a real pagination/ordering pitfall hit along the way**: an initial query using `orderBy: "timestamp asc"` over the full unbounded time range returned zero entries — a known Cloud Logging API characteristic where ascending scans over a mostly-inactive-until-recently log stream can fail to surface results within normal pagination; switching to `orderBy: "timestamp desc"` (proven reliable) and fully paginating (up to 1000/page, following `nextPageToken` to exhaustion) fixed this. Fetched the COMPLETE `CreateFunction` history for `icoccha` (526 entries, all-time, all statuses — not filtering to "success only," since individual attempt-level status is noisy under retry/quota-failure and unreliable for this purpose) and computed each function's EARLIEST-ever creation-attempt timestamp — a value that's stable and unambiguous regardless of how many times a create was retried.

**Result: a clean 4-cluster history.** 41 functions first-attempted 2026-03-30 (the project's original setup — matches the historically-documented "41 functions already deployed" baseline exactly); 2 first-attempted 2026-04-17 (`initStripePayment`/`initStripeTestPayment` — old, orphaned, not in current source, unrelated to anything this session touched); 12 first-attempted 2026-08-11 (a past session's own `--project icoccha` activity, already covered by §93/§95's finding that the real project has these too); **exactly 46 first-attempted 2026-08-14, all within a single ~63-second window** — this is the complete, exact set of functions genuinely new to `icoccha` from this session, matching precisely the "46 missing" count from the very first audit (§92), now independently re-derived from a completely different data source and arriving at the same number — strong mutual confirmation.
